# TIMELINE · Cross-Yuga & Recurring Myths

> **Period 9 of 9 on the cosmic clock.** One slice of the corpus-wide [TIMELINE hub](../TIMELINE.md); each entry links to the file where the event is detailed. Back to the [master index](../README.md).
> ← Prev: [Future Manvantaras](07-future-manvantaras.md) · [↑ TIMELINE hub](../TIMELINE.md)

**1627 events.**

*Episodes the texts do not pin to one fixed age (recurring deva–asura wars, Śaiva/Śākta cycles).*

---

- **"As in dance, so in painting"** — the Citrasūtra's governing dictum yathā nṛtte tathā citre makes the painted body a frozen moment of dance, fixing gesture, proportion, and rasa as the shared grammar of the visual and performing arts _(actors: Mārkaṇḍeya, Vajra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vishnudharmottara-purana.md) — (Viṣṇudharmottara Purāṇa III.35–43)
- **"Hence I depart not" — the refusal of heaven** — when Dharma and Indra arrive with the celestial car, Vipaścit declines to ascend while thousands cry to him for relief, holding compassion for the distressed above the joys of Svarga _(actors: Vipaścit, Dharma, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vipashchit-in-hell.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa XV)
- **"I am lord only of the rest"** — Yama confesses he governs all mankind by Brahmā's appointment save the Vaiṣṇavas, over whom Hari alone rules — prabhur aham anya-nṛṇāṃ na vaiṣṇavānām _(actors: Yama, Viṣṇu, Brahmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yama-gita.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa III.7)
- **"If you are cut, do you not bleed?"** — the thoṭṭam's charter verse: Brahmin and Pulaya bleed the same red blood, so caste has no ground in the body _(actors: the Pulaya-Śiva, the Brahmin)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/pottan-theyyam.md) — (Poṭṭan thoṭṭam-pāṭṭu, Pulappoṭṭan samvāda)
- **"The air that clings to thy body"** — the tortured souls beg the king to stay, revealing that the cool wind was his own presence, whose radiated merit dispels their burning and pain _(actors: Vipaścit, the damned)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vipashchit-in-hell.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa XV)
- **"The whole earth is one family"** — the Hitopadeśa's friendship-book preserves the famed verse vasudhaiva kuṭumbakam, holding that to the noble-hearted no one is stranger _(actors: Nārāyaṇa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/hitopadesha.md) — (Hitopadeśa I, subhāṣita)
- **"We drink the same water"** — the argument extends to shared sun, water and toddy, dissolving the fictions of tīṇḍal and high/low caste _(actors: the Pulaya-Śiva, Śaṅkarācārya)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/pottan-theyyam.md) — (Poṭṭan thoṭṭam)
- **A brahmin refuses Indra's throne** — Indra descends to Kālikā-grāma to check a mortal whose recitation of Gītā chapter 18 rivals his merit, and the reciter spurns Indra's seat to choose Viṣṇu-sāyujya _(actors: Indra, a brahmin reciter)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavad-gita/gita-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Gītā-Māhātmya 18)
- **A charter for shared karmic fruit** — Somaka's insistence on shouldering a sin committed for his sake stages the epic's loophole of loyalty, prefiguring Yudhiṣṭhira's own refusal to enter heaven alone _(actors: Somaka, the purohita, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/jantu-somaka.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva CXXVIII; Svargārohaṇa Parva)
- **A lifetime of sons and grandsons** — over many years Suśīlā bears the king many sons who grow, marry, and give her grandsons — an entire ripening life of saṃsāric love lived inside the illusion _(actors: Suśīlā, Tāladhvaja)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-maya-taladhvaja.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa VI.30)
- **Abhijit slips from the sky** — the 28th nakṣatra Abhijit falls from heaven while contesting seniority with Rohiṇī, the mythic charter behind the star and its victorious midday muhūrta _(actors: Abhijit, Rohiṇī, Skanda, Indra)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/muhurta-and-auspicious-timing.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana/Āraṇyaka Parva)
- **Agastya brings Tamil and founds the Siddhar lineage** — The sage Agastya, crossing south of the Vindhyas, is held in Tamil tradition to be the ādi-guru of the Siddhars and patron of Siddha medicine _(actors: Agastya (Agathiyar))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tamil-siddhars.md) — (Akattiyam; Siddha-vaidya corpus; Wikipedia (Agastya, Siddhar))
- **Agni reveals the Agni Purāṇa to Vasiṣṭha** — The fire-god Agni reveals the encyclopedic Agni Purāṇa, the essence of all sciences, to the sage Vasiṣṭha, who transmits it down the chain to Vyāsa and the Sūta _(actors: Agni, Vasiṣṭha, Vyāsa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/agni-purana.md) — (Agni Purāṇa 1)
- **Ajamila's Fall through the Dasi** — The virtuous brahmana Ajamila of Kanyakubja is overpowered by lust at the sight of a drunken dasi, casts off his wedded wife, and descends into a life of theft and deceit _(actors: Ajamila, the dasi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ajamila.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6.1.20-27)
- **Akūpāra the tortoise remembers** — the oldest being on earth weeps and knows Indradyumna, for the lake he dwells in was scooped out by the hooves of the countless cattle the king gave away in charity _(actors: Akūpāra, Indradyumna)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indradyumna-akupara-oldest-beings.md) — (Mahābhārata 3.191 / Ganguli Vana CXCIX)
- **Alakṣmī expelled by the Kārtika lamps** — Jyeṣṭhā-Alakṣmī, risen from the churning before Lakṣmī and assigned to dwell in filth and strife, is driven out by the swept house and rows of lamps of the Kārtika/Dīpāvalī vow that invite Śrī in _(actors: Alakṣmī/Jyeṣṭhā, Lakṣmī, Duḥsaha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kartika-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma P.; Ṛgveda Khila, Śrī-Sūkta)
- **Alarka's 66,000-year youthful reign** — Alarka, son of Pratardana and (in the Mārkaṇḍeya splice) of Madālasā, rules the earth 66,000 years while remaining young, then renounces after Dattātreya's instruction _(actors: Alarka, Madālasā, Dattātreya)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kashi-line-divodasa.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa IX.17.7–8; Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa ~chs. 16–44)
- **Anaraṇya renounces his throne for Śivaloka** — grieving the loss of his favourite daughter, King Anaraṇya of the Indra-Sāvarṇi line abandons the kingdom for forest penance and attains Śivaloka, leaving his son Kīrtimān to rule _(actors: Anaraṇya, Kīrtimān)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/padma-pippalada.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Pārvatī-khaṇḍa 34)
- **Annam Brahma — food revealed as Brahman** — Varuṇa sends his son Bhṛgu into austerity, who first realizes that food (anna) itself is Brahman before ascending through breath, mind and bliss _(actors: Bhṛgu, Varuṇa)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/anna-naivedya-and-prasada.md) — (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 3.1–3.6)
- **Annapūrṇā feeds Śiva at Kāśī** — in the poem's Deva-frame the Great Goddess as Annadā fills the begging Śiva's bowl, the anna-theology that the giver of food sustains even the Absolute _(actors: Annapūrṇā/Annadā, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/annadamangal-bharatchandra.md) — (Annadāmaṅgal, Annadā-maṅgal khaṇḍa)
- **Annapūrṇā feeds Śiva in Kāśī** — Pārvatī as the goddess who is full of food gives alms to Śiva himself, who begs at her ladle; no one in her city goes hungry, and Śaṅkara enshrines her in the Annapūrṇā-stotra _(actors: Annapūrṇā, Śiva)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/anna-naivedya-and-prasada.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kāśī Khaṇḍa; Devī-Bhāgavata)
- **Āpaduddhāraka — the rescuer from calamity** — invoked through the Vaṭuka-Bhairava Stotra and kavaca, the boy-Bhairava lifts devotees out of disease, enemies, debt, fear, and untimely death _(actors: Vaṭuka Bhairava, devotees)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vatuka-bhairava-origin.md) — (Tantric stotra tradition)
- **Apotheosis of the impaled god** — his wrongful, violent death on the stake generates godhood, making Kāttavarāyaṉ a kāval teyvam whose stake becomes his throne and emblem — the South Indian death-and-deification engine _(actors: Kāttavarāyaṉ)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kattavarayan-katai.md) — (Masilamani-Meyer, The Tale of Kāttavarāyaṉ) [scholarly] [folk]
- **Aśokasundarī born of the Kalpavṛkṣa** — lonely Pārvatī voices her wish before the wish-fulfilling tree, which produces a maiden of surpassing beauty named for ending the goddess's grief (śoka), reckoned a Śaiva daughter beside Gaṇeśa and Skanda _(actors: Pārvatī, Śiva, Aśokasundarī, Kalpavṛkṣa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ashokasundari-nahusha-hunda.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Bhūmi-khaṇḍa 103)
- **Atri rescued from the burning pit** — cast into a fiery chasm (ṛbīsa) by his enemies, Atri Saptavadhri is cooled and freed unharmed by the Aśvins with a coolness-giving draught _(actors: Aśvins, Atri Saptavadhri)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ashvins.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.116.8; 5.78.4–6)
- **Atri restores the darkened Sun** — the Āsura Svarbhānu strikes Sūrya with darkness and the ṛṣi Atri alone finds and re-fixes the sun in the sky by the fourth brahman-formula — the Vedic prototype of the Rāhu eclipse-myth _(actors: Atri, Svarbhānu, Sūrya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/brahmana-legends-catalog.md) — (Ṛgveda 5.40.5–9; Pañcaviṃśa Brāhmaṇa 4.5.2)
- **Aṭṭa-vīraṭṭāṉam — the eight feat-shrines** — a closed Tamil set of eight Śiva temples, each fixing one of his fierce cosmos-ordering deeds to a named delta town, the presiding liṅga called Vīraṭṭāṉeśvarar _(actors: Śiva, Nāyanār saints)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-virattanam-eight-feat-shrines.md) — (Tēvāram; sthala-purāṇas)
- **Bala consents to be the gods' sacrificial victim** — The devas ask the truth-bound asura to serve as the pashu of their yajna, and Bala freely offers his own body to his enemies' rite _(actors: Bala/Vala, the devas)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/bala-asura-navaratna-origin.md) — (Garuda Purana, Purva-khanda gem chapters; Buddhabhatta, Ratnapariksha)
- **Bala's ascendancy over the gods** — In the primordial deva–asura war the asura Bala (Vala) grows invincible and the devas, unable to slay him in battle, resort to stratagem _(actors: Bala/Vala, Indra, the devas)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/bala-asura-navaratna-origin.md) — (Garuda Purana, Purva-khanda ch. ~68)
- **Bala's blood and eyes fall on Lanka** — The asura's blood falls into the Ravanaganga river of Simhala and his eyes on the same island, chartering Ceylon's ruby-gravels and blue-sapphire beds _(actors: Bala/Vala, the devas)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/bala-asura-navaratna-origin.md) — (Buddhabhatta, Ratnapariksha, ruby and sapphire sections)
- **Bala's dismembered body becomes the gem-seeds** — Slain as a sanctified victim, Bala's members turn into the seeds of the gems: blood→ruby, teeth→pearl, bile→emerald, eyes→sapphire, bones→diamond _(actors: Bala/Vala, the devas)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/bala-asura-navaratna-origin.md) — (Garuda Purana, Purva-khanda chs. ~68–70; Agni Purana 246)
- **Bāṇāī the Dhangar shepherdess** — Khaṇḍobā wins his second wife, the Dhangar shepherdess Bāṇāī, in pastoral guise, writing the pastoral layer into the high god's own biography _(actors: Khaṇḍobā, Bāṇāī, Mhāḷsā)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/dhangar-oral-epics-biroba-mhaskoba-dhuloba.md) — (Bāṇāī ovī; Sontheimer)
- **Between ghost and god** — a fierce spirit or fallen pastoral hero, bound to a devotee and propitiated by possession and blood-sacrifice, is progressively deified into a temple-god and equated with Bhairava _(actors: the bhūt/vīragaḷ, the devotee-medium, Bhairava)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/dhangar-oral-epics-biroba-mhaskoba-dhuloba.md) — (Sontheimer, Between Ghost and God)
- **Bhadrā bound into the karaṇa cycle** — the fierce daughter of Sūrya and Chāyā, sister of Śani, is assigned by Brahmā a fixed seat as the Viṣṭi karaṇa so her destructive hours can be known and avoided _(actors: Bhadrā, Sūrya, Chāyā, Brahmā, Śani)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/muhurta-and-auspicious-timing.md) — (astrological/purāṇic lore [late text] [folk])
- **Bhadrāyu revived with holy ash** — the poisoned, forest-abandoned infant of queen Sumati dies and is resuscitated by the yogin Ṛṣabha with Śiva's bhasma, named Bhadrāyu, and taught the Śiva-kavaca _(actors: Bhadrāyu, Sumati, Ṛṣabha, Vajrabāhu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/brahmottara-khanda-vrata-tales.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Brāhmottara-khaṇḍa 10–11)
- **Bhakti above the sacrificial fire** — the Bhāgavata makes the yajña-patnī episode its charter that loving surrender outranks Vedic ritualism, with disqualified women reaching the Lord where learned twice-born ritualists fail _(actors: yajña-patnīs, the brāhmaṇa sacrificers, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yajna-patni-mokshana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.23)
- **Bhṛgu curses Viṣṇu to mortal births** — After Viṣṇu beheads Śukra's mother sheltering the war-broken asuras, Bhṛgu curses him to be born again and again among mortals — the Matsya's charter for the human avatāras _(actors: Bhṛgu, Viṣṇu, Śukra's mother, Indra)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/twelve-devasura-battles.md) — (Matsya Purāṇa, 47)
- **Bhṛgu revives Kāvyamātā** — Bhṛgu sprinkles water from his kamaṇḍalu and restores his beheaded wife to life, though the curse on Viṣṇu still stands _(actors: Bhṛgu, Kāvyamātā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigus-curse-on-vishnu.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata IV.14)
- **Bhṛgu's curse dooms Viṣṇu to mortal births** — finding his wife slain, Bhṛgu curses Viṣṇu to be born again and again among men and to live long separated from his consort — the charter of the avatāras _(actors: Bhṛgu, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigus-curse-on-vishnu.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara 51.14–17; Devī-Bhāgavata IV.14)
- **Bhṛgu's word makes Vītahavya a Brāhmaṇa** — the defeated king takes refuge with Bhṛgu, who tells the pursuing Pratardana "there are only Brāhmaṇas here" — and the true word of the ṛṣi transmutes the Kṣatriya into a Brāhmaṇa on the instant _(actors: Vītahavya, Bhṛgu, Pratardana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matanga-vitahavya-varna-upakhyanas.md) — (Anuśāsana Parva ~31)
- **Bhṛṅgī cursed to be fleshless** — the Śiva-only gaṇa defends his lord as cheated, and the furious Goddess curses him to skin and bone, the etiology of his skeletal three-legged form _(actors: Bhṛṅgī, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shiva-parvati-dice-game.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa 34.115–116)
- **Bhujyu saved from the shipwreck** — the Aśvins pluck Bhujyu, son of Tugra, from the midst of the ocean and bear him home over three days in self-propelling winged ships _(actors: Aśvins, Bhujyu, Tugra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ashvins.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.116.3–5; 1.117.14–15)
- **Bhuśuṇḍa the immortal crow recounts the kalpas** — Vasiṣṭha visits the crow-sage nested on the kalpa-taru on Meru, who remembers repeated ocean-churnings and pralayas and survives cosmic dissolution by prāṇa-contemplation _(actors: Bhuśuṇḍa, Vasiṣṭha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yoga-vasishtha-tales-catalog.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha VI.1.14–28)
- **Bhuśuṇḍa the immortal crow survives the pralayas** — The ancient crow on Meru's kalpa-tree tells Vasiṣṭha how he has watched dissolutions and world-ages repeat, abiding in prāṇa-contemplation while creation burns _(actors: Bhuśuṇḍa, Vasiṣṭha)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha, Nirvāṇa prakaraṇa Pūrvārdha)
- **Biroba born of Māyavā** — the Dhangar shepherd-god is miraculously born of, or given by, the goddess Māyavā (Māyakkā) and fostered in a shepherd household, emerging to herd sheep and found his cult _(actors: Biroba, Māyavā, the Dhangar foster-family)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/dhangar-oral-epics-biroba-mhaskoba-dhuloba.md) — (Biroba ovī; Sontheimer, Pastoral Deities)
- **Birth of Bhadrakālī from Śiva's third eye** — Alerted by Nārada to Dārika's outrages, Śiva opens his third eye and from its fire Bhadrakālī is born fully armed as his daughter, with the vetāla given as her mount _(actors: Śiva, Bhadrakālī, Nārada, the vetāla)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bhadrakali-kerala-bhagavati.md) — (Kerala Bhadrakālī-māhātmya; kaḷampāṭṭu invocations)
- **Birth of Sārasvata** — Dadhīca's seed, spilled at the sight of the apsaras Alambuṣā, falls into the Sarasvatī, who preserves it and bears the sage Sārasvata _(actors: Dadhīca, Alambuṣā, river-goddess Sarasvatī, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sarasvata-upakhyana-vriddhakumari.md) — (Mbh Śalya Parva 9.51; Ganguli LI)
- **Birth of the water-child Lauhitya** — Amoghā bears Brahmā's seed, conveyed through Śāntanu, and gives birth to a son in the form of water — the Brahmaputra _(actors: Amoghā, Śāntanu, Brahmā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lauhitya-brahmaputra-origin.md) — (Kālikā Purāṇa, 83; Padma Purāṇa, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 55)
- **Bobbariya, the Muslim mariner made daiva** — a Byāri merchant-sailor who dies at sea is deified as a Tuḷu sea-spirit guarding trade and fishermen, his shrine marked by the mast-like bobbariyana kambo _(actors: Bobbariya)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/panjurli-guliga-tulu-daivas.md) — (Bobbariya paddana) [folk]
- **Both cousins liberated** — Khāṇḍikya entrusts his son with the throne and by steady meditation on Viṣṇu attains release, and Keśidhvaja is freed likewise, closing the Viṣṇu Purāṇa on its mokṣa coda _(actors: Khāṇḍikya, Keśidhvaja)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khandikya-keshidhvaja.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa VI.7)
- **Brahmā enthrones Ripuñjaya as Divodāsa** — In an age of anarchy Brahmā persuades the ascetic Ripuñjaya to rule the earth from Kāśī on condition that all gods withdraw to heaven _(actors: Brahmā, Ripuñjaya/Divodāsa)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kashi-line-divodasa.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kāśī-khaṇḍa, Pūrvārdha ch. 43)
- **Brahmā's desire for Amoghā** — Brahmā, smitten by Amoghā, wife of the sage Śāntanu, is rebuked and departs in shame, his seed fallen at the hermitage _(actors: Brahmā, Amoghā, Śāntanu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lauhitya-brahmaputra-origin.md) — (Kālikā Purāṇa, 82)
- **Brahmā's ten aśvamedhas at Kāśī** — Testing Divodāsa, Brahmā demands ten simultaneous horse-sacrifices, which the king executes flawlessly — the etiology of Daśāśvamedha Ghāṭ; Sūrya likewise stays as the twelve Ādityas of Kāśī _(actors: Brahmā, Divodāsa, Sūrya)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kashi-line-divodasa.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kāśī-khaṇḍa, Uttarārdha ~chs. 54–55)
- **Brahmā's yajña at Puṣkara and Sāvitrī's curse** — Brahmā sacrifices at Puṣkara with substitute bride Gāyatrī when Sāvitrī delays, and her curse limits his worship to this one tīrtha _(actors: Brahmā, Sāvitrī, Gāyatrī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/padma-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa, Puṣkara-māhātmya)
- **Brahmā, Śatarūpā, and the fifth head** — the Purāṇic reflex: Brahmā grows extra heads to keep gazing at his own created daughter Śatarūpā/Sandhyā, and Kāla Bhairava shears off the fifth head, incurring the brahmahatyā skull-penance _(actors: Brahmā, Śatarūpā, Kāla Bhairava)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/prajapati-ushas-rudra-piercing.md) — (Matsya Purāṇa 3–4; Śiva Purāṇa, Śatarudra-saṃhitā)
- **Bṛhaspati impersonates Śukra and deludes the asuras** — While Śukra endures his vow for Śiva's mantras and weds Jayantī, Bṛhaspati teaches in his form and turns the asuras from their true guru _(actors: Śukra, Bṛhaspati, Jayantī, the asuras)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/twelve-devasura-battles.md) — (Matsya Purāṇa, 47)
- **Bṛhaspati impersonates the asura-guru** — during Śukra's hidden decade the deva-guru takes Śukra's very form, preaches a heterodox dharma to disunite the Daityas, and gets the returning true Śukra rejected as the fraud _(actors: Bṛhaspati, Daityas, Śukra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigus-curse-on-vishnu.md) — (Matsya Purāṇa 47; Devī-Bhāgavata IV.13)
- **Brihaspati's curse blinds Dirghatamas in the womb** — The embryo in Mamata's womb blocks Brihaspati's intruding seed, and the enraged god-priest curses him to be born into 'long darkness', giving the blind seer his name _(actors: Brihaspati, Mamata, Utathya, Dirghatamas (in womb))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dirghatamas.md) — (Mahabharata Adi Parva ~104; Brihaddevata 4)
- **Caṇḍikeśvara, guardian of the nirmālya** — as Śiva's appointed officer (adhikārin), Caṇḍeśa becomes the standing Āgamic recipient of all offerings already given to Śiva (nirmālya), from whom alone devotees may receive them _(actors: Caṇḍikeśvara, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/chandesvara-chandikeshvara.md) — (Śaiva Siddhānta Āgamas)
- **Candrāṅgada drowned and rescued by nāgas** — Sīmantinī's husband Candrāṅgada of Niṣadha sinks in a Yamunā whirlpool, is carried to Pātāla by nāga-maidens, honoured by Takṣaka for his Śiva-devotion, and returns alive to reunite with her — the Somavāra-vrata's proof _(actors: Candrāṅgada, Sīmantinī, Takṣaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/brahmottara-khanda-vrata-tales.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Brāhmottara-khaṇḍa 9)
- **Candrāvatī desecrates the prasād** — royal women scorn the Svasthānī offerings, casting the sacred flowers into the river, and lose husbands, sons, and kingdom until they re-perform the vow and are wholly restored _(actors: Candrāvatī, Svasthānī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/swasthani-brata-katha.md) — (Svasthānī Vrata Kathā, Goma cycle)
- **Chastity restores Pippalāda's youth** — pleased by her proven fidelity, Dharma reveals himself and grants boons: the aged Pippalāda is made young and vigorous, Padmā eternally youthful, with ten virtuous sons and boundless wealth _(actors: Dharma, Pippalāda, Padmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/padma-pippalada.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Pārvatī-khaṇḍa 35)
- **Ciṟuttoṇḍar's extreme naivedya** — The Nāyanār cooks and offers his own son as food to a Bhairava-guest, the limit-case of food-devotion, before the child is restored _(actors: Ciṟuttoṇḍar/Siruthondar, Śiva as Bhairava)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/anna-naivedya-and-prasada.md) — (Periya Purāṇam)
- **Consecration of Sthāṇu-tīrtha** — the ground where Śiva stood by the sage as the immovable Sthāṇu becomes Sthāneśvara (Thanesar) at Kurukṣetra, a tīrtha granting Śiva-sāyujya _(actors: Śiva, Dadhīca)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kshupa-dadhicha-dispute.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ~35–36)
- **Cūḍālā appears as the sage Kumbha** — Disguised as a young brahmin sage claiming to be Nārada's pot-born son, Cūḍālā becomes her husband's guru and exposes his false renunciation through the cintāmaṇi and Vindhya-elephant parables _(actors: Cūḍālā/Kumbha, Śikhidhvaja, Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/chudala-shikhidhvaja.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha VI.1, ~chs. 84–96)
- **Cūḍālā liberates King Śikhidhvaja as the sage Kumbha** — The realized queen Cūḍālā, disguised as the brahmin youth Kumbha, teaches and tests her renunciate husband until both rule as jīvanmuktas _(actors: Cūḍālā, Śikhidhvaja)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha, Nirvāṇa prakaraṇa Pūrvārdha)
- **Cūḍālā's awakening in the palace** — Queen Cūḍālā of Mālava attains Self-knowledge through self-inquiry (ātma-vicāra) while living as a reigning queen, without external renunciation _(actors: Cūḍālā, Śikhidhvaja)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/chudala-shikhidhvaja.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha VI.1, ~ch. 78)
- **Curds bought with a gold ring** — thirsty on the march, the two disguised Lords drink the milkmaid Māṇikā's curds and, having no coin, pay her with a gold ring, bidding her collect the debt from the king coming behind _(actors: Jagannātha, Balabhadra, Māṇikā the milkmaid)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kanchi-kaveri-jagannatha-legend.md) — (Kāñcī-Kāverī) [regional] [folk]
- **Cyavana initiates the gleaner** — Dharmāraṇya seeks out Cyavana of Bhṛgu's line for formal saṃskāra into the Uñcha vow, then takes up Yama and Niyama and withdraws into another forest to live it _(actors: Dharmāraṇya, Cyavana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/unchavritti-brahmin-naga-padmanabha.md) — (Śānti Parva CCCLXV)
- **Dadhīca's curse foretelling the Dakṣa-yajña** — victorious, Dadhīca curses the gods for siding with the king and prophesies that Rudra will scatter and burn them all, Viṣṇu included, at the future sacrifice of Dakṣa _(actors: Dadhīca, the Devas, Rudra, Dakṣa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kshupa-dadhicha-dispute.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ~35–36)
- **Dakṣa's sacrifice and Satī's fire (Svasthānī telling)** — Satī, humiliated at her father Dakṣa's Śiva-excluding sacrifice, immolates herself, and Śiva's hosts wreck the rite _(actors: Satī, Dakṣa, Śiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/swasthani-brata-katha.md) — (Svasthānī Vrata Kathā, Śaiva chapters)
- **Damanaka sunders lion and bull** — the jackal Damanaka poisons the friendship of lion-king Piṅgalaka and the bull Sañjīvaka with insinuation until the lion kills his friend, the exemplum of Suhṛdbheda _(actors: Damanaka, Karaṭaka, Piṅgalaka, Sañjīvaka)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/hitopadesha.md) — (Hitopadeśa II, Suhṛdbheda)
- **Dambhodbhava's daily boast** — the universal emperor Dambhodbhava asks each morning whether anyone on earth is his equal in battle, until brāhmaṇas name the ascetics Nara and Nārāyaṇa as his superiors _(actors: Dambhodbhava, the brāhmaṇas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dambhodbhava-nara-narayana.md) — (Mahābhārata CE 5.94, Udyoga Parva)
- **Dārika's boon from Brahmā** — The asura Dārika wins invincibility against every male being — god, asura, or human — leaving only a woman able to kill him, and tyrannizes the worlds _(actors: Dārika, Brahmā, Dānavēndra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bhadrakali-kerala-bhagavati.md) — (Kerala Bhadrakālī-māhātmya; mudiyettu tōṟṟam tradition)
- **Dārika-vadham — the beheading of Dārika** — After a long war in which the vetāla drinks the demon blood before it seeds new demons, Bhadrakālī severs Dārika's head and holds it aloft, the defining Kerala Bhagavati icon _(actors: Bhadrakālī, Dārika, Kūḷi, the vetāla)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bhadrakali-kerala-bhagavati.md) — (Kerala Bhadrakālī-māhātmya; mudiyettu tradition)
- **Death teaches Naciketas the yogic Song** — the Agni Purāṇa's Yama-Gītā, framed on the Kaṭha archetype, has Yama expound jñāna, yoga, and the chariot-of-the-body path to release _(actors: Yama, Naciketas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yama-gita.md) — (Agni Purāṇa 381)
- **Deva Śarmā's tapas at Devprayag** — the sage's austerities and divine vision at the confluence give Devprayag its name [regional] _(actors: Deva Śarmā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-prayag-alaknanda-confluences.md) — (Wikipedia, Devprayag; Kedāra-khaṇḍa tradition)
- **Devaśarman and the goatherd Mitravat** — a brahmin seeking supreme knowledge learns from a forest goatherd that steady duty ripens into wisdom, the lesson of Gītā chapter 2 _(actors: Devaśarman, Mitravat, Sukarman)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavad-gita/gita-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Gītā-Māhātmya 2)
- **Devaśayanī and Prabodhinī — Viṣṇu's four-month cosmic sleep** — Viṣṇu lies down on Śeṣa at Āṣāḍha śukla Ekādaśī and wakes at Kārttika śukla Ekādaśī, the two tithis bounding Cāturmāsya and the Tulasī-vivāha _(actors: Viṣṇu, Śeṣa, Tulasī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ekadashi-mahatmyas-catalog.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa; pan-purāṇic calendar lore)
- **Dhaneśvara saved by the company of Kārttika-vratins** — a lifelong cheat dying among Kārttika observers on the Narmadā is spared Yama's hells by their contagious merit _(actors: Dhaneśvara, Yama, Kārttika-vratins)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/masa-mahatmyas-kartika-magha-vaishakha.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Kārttika-māhātmya)
- **Dharma disguised tests Padmā's chastity** — the god Dharma takes the guise of a lustful king and, meeting Padmā at the celestial river, urges her to cast off the ruthless old brahmin, but she refuses utterly _(actors: Dharma, Padmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/padma-pippalada.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Pārvatī-khaṇḍa 35)
- **Dharmāraṇya converted to the gleaner's life** — his doubt dispelled, the brāhmaṇa declares he has learnt the value of the Uñcha vow and resolves to abandon acquisition for the humblest, harm-minimal livelihood _(actors: Dharmāraṇya, Padmanābha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/unchavritti-brahmin-naga-padmanabha.md) — (Śānti Parva CCCLXV)
- **Dharmāraṇya seeks the Nāga Padmanābha** — a righteous but doubt-gnawed brāhmaṇa, unsure which dharma most surely wins liberation, journeys to the serpent-city in the Naimiṣa forest on the Gomatī to question the virtuous Nāga _(actors: Dharmāraṇya, Padmanābha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/unchavritti-brahmin-naga-padmanabha.md) — (Śānti Parva CCCLV, CCCLXI)
- **Dhuloba, the dust-lord of the flock** — the fierce Bhairava-type Dhangar god of the Vīr–Phalṭaṇ tract is born, marries and founds his cult in his own sung ovī _(actors: Dhuloba, his Dhangar devotees)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/dhangar-oral-epics-biroba-mhaskoba-dhuloba.md) — (Dhuloba ovī; Sontheimer)
- **Dīrghajihvī the soma-licker trapped** — Indra, unable to catch the demoness who licks up the gods' soma with her long tongue, fashions the youth Sumitra Kutsa to seduce and destroy her _(actors: Indra, Dīrghajihvī, Sumitra Kutsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/brahmana-legends-catalog.md) — (Jaiminīya Brāhmaṇa 1.161–163)
- **Dundubhinirhrāda hunts the brahmins of Kāśī** — the Daitya uncle of Prahlāda, avenging Hiraṇyākṣa, resolves to starve the gods by devouring their sacrificing brahmins and comes to Kāśī, ambushing them in the shapes of forest-dweller, water-beast, invisible form, and tiger, leaving not even a bone _(actors: Dundubhinirhrāda, the brahmins of Kāśī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yuddha-khanda-minor-vadhas.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṃhitā, Yuddha-khaṇḍa 58)
- **Durgama steals the Vedas** — the asura Durgama, son of Ruru of Hiraṇyākṣa's line, wins the four Vedas as a boon from Brahmā, extinguishing all yajña so that the rains fail and a hundred-year drought grips the earth _(actors: Durgama, Brahmā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shakambhari-shatakshi-durgamasura.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.28)
- **Even Mārkaṇḍeya is not old enough** — the deathless sage who survives the deluge confesses he does not know Indradyumna and sends him to an elder still, the owl Prāvārakarṇa _(actors: Mārkaṇḍeya, Indradyumna, Prāvārakarṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indradyumna-akupara-oldest-beings.md) — (Mahābhārata 3.191 / Ganguli Vana CXCIX)
- **Faith held before vindication** — Nandabhadra refutes the materialist on scripture and reason, defends the devas and Liṅga-worship, then walks to the Bahūdaka tīrtha at Mahīsāgara-Saṅgama to end his life _(actors: Nandabhadra, Satyavrata)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nandabhadra-tested-faith.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa 45)
- **Gaṇeśa settles in Kāśī as Ḍhuṇḍhirāja** — Disguised as an aged brāhmaṇa astrologer, Gaṇeśa wins the city's trust, foretells the counselor to come on the eighteenth day, and remains as Ḍhuṇḍhi-Vināyaka, chief guardian Gaṇeśa of Kāśī _(actors: Gaṇeśa, Śiva, Divodāsa)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kashi-line-divodasa.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kāśī-khaṇḍa, Uttarārdha ~chs. 56–57)
- **Garuḍa humbled and restored at Garuḍa-tīrtha** — Garuḍa imprisons the Śiva-protected serpent Maṇināga, is crushed under Viṣṇu's little finger, and rises stronger after bathing in the Gautamī _(actors: Garuḍa, Maṇināga, Viṣṇu, Śiva, Nandin)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautami-mahatmya-tales-catalog.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa, Gautamī-māhātmya, vulgate adh. 90)
- **Garuḍa reconciled and the wedding of Sumukha and Guṇakeśī** — the humbled Garuḍa makes peace with Sumukha, who weds Guṇakeśī, laying to rest the bird-and-serpent enmity in this instance _(actors: Garuḍa, Sumukha, Guṇakeśī, Mātali)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matali-sumukha-naga-bridegroom.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva 5.103)
- **Garuda seizes the bile of Bala, seeding the emeralds** — Bala's bile, taken by the serpent-king Vasuki, is snatched by Garuda and dropped in a remote mountain land, whence the emerald mines _(actors: Garuda, Vasuki, Bala/Vala)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/bala-asura-navaratna-origin.md) — (Buddhabhatta, Ratnapariksha, emerald section; Garuda Purana emerald chapter)
- **Garuḍa's one-month death-term revealed** — Nārada discloses that Garuḍa devoured Sumukha's father Cikura and has fixed a term one month hence to eat Sumukha too, dismaying the grandfather Āryaka _(actors: Nārada, Sumukha, Garuḍa, Āryaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matali-sumukha-naga-bridegroom.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva 5.101)
- **Gaurī wins half of Śiva's body by the Kedāra vrata** — Pārvatī keeps the 21-day Kedāra Gaurī vow and is granted the left half of Śiva, the vrata-charter for Ardhanārīśvara _(actors: Pārvatī/Gaurī, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vrata-kathas-catalog.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa [attrib.], Kedāra-vrata-kathā) [late text] [regional]
- **Gautama's curse of Devī-aversion** — outraged at the framing, Gautama curses his benefactees to become averse to Gāyatrī and Mūla-Prakṛti Śrī Devī, to Veda, sandhyā and yajña, and to turn instead to Kāpālikas, Bauddha śāstras and pāṣaṇḍa heresy _(actors: Gautama, the brāhmaṇas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautama-curse-on-the-brahmins.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata XII.9)
- **Gāyatrī's inexhaustible vessel** — in a twelve-year famine the Sage Gautama worships Gāyatrī Devī, who grants him a brim-full vessel that yields whatever he wishes, so no starving brāhmaṇa is turned away _(actors: Gautama, Gāyatrī Devī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautama-curse-on-the-brahmins.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata XII.8)
- **Ghoṣā cured and wed** — the seer Ghoṣā Kākṣīvatī, disfigured by skin disease and unmarried into old age, wins from the Aśvins both a cure and a husband in her own hymns _(actors: Aśvins, Ghoṣā Kākṣīvatī, Kakṣīvat)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ashvins.md) — (Ṛgveda 10.39–40)
- **Gobhila's curse of idiocy** — insulted when Devadatta of Kosala rebukes his Sāma-singing at a Putreṣṭi on the Tamasā, the sage Gobhila curses the brahmin's son-to-be to be born dull, illiterate, and dumb _(actors: Gobhila, Devadatta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/satyavrata-aim-bija.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa III.10)
- **Godhā the lizard saved by a bath's transferred merit** — Rukmāṅgada's horse crushes a she-lizard (once a sorceress-wife of Śākala, punished in the hell Tāmrabhrāṣṭra); over Mohinī's objection the king gifts her his Vijayā-rite merit from the Gaṅgā–Sarayū confluence and she ascends to Viṣṇu's realm _(actors: Rukmāṅgada, Godhā, Mohinī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-purana-ganga-tales.md) — (Nārada Purāṇa, Uttarabhāga 14)
- **Goma married to the seventy-year-old brahmin** — the young brahmin girl Goma is given by fate and Śiva's will to Śiva Śarma, a brahmin of seventy, dramatising a wife's dharma under the harshest marital fortune _(actors: Goma, Śiva Śarma)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/swasthani-brata-katha.md) — (Svasthānī Vrata Kathā, Goma cycle)
- **Goma takes the Svasthānī vow** — in her misery Goma undertakes the month-of-Māgh vow of the goddess Svasthānī, and by its power her fortunes turn and her son thrives _(actors: Goma, Svasthānī, Śiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/swasthani-brata-katha.md) — (Svasthānī Vrata Kathā, Goma cycle)
- **Gopāla agrees to walk south** — pleaded to testify in a hostile assembly, Kṛṣṇa's Deity consents to follow the youth on one condition — that he never look back while the ankle-bells sound behind him _(actors: young brāhmaṇa, Gopāla)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sakshi-gopal-witness-krishna.md) — (Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya 5)
- **Guliga, the insatiable one born of ash and stone** — a devouring goblin-daiva born from a stone Pārvatī finds in ash is handed to Viṣṇu, who curbs his world-swallowing hunger and fixes him as Kṣetra-pāla, guardian of the precinct _(actors: Guliga, Pārvatī, Śiva, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/panjurli-guliga-tulu-daivas.md) — (Guliga paddana) [folk]
- **Gulikan sprung from Śiva's toe** — when Śiva strikes down Yama to save Mārkaṇḍeya, he brings forth the death-being Gulikan from his left great toe and deputizes him over death, making him North Malabar's Yama-aspect and, once propitiated, a guardian against untimely death _(actors: Gulikan, Śiva, Yama)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/theyyam-deities-and-thottam-myths.md) — (Gulikan thoṭṭam) [folk]
- **Guṇanidhi's unwitting Śivarātri vrata** — Starving on Śivarātri night, Guṇanidhi enters a Śiva shrine to steal the food-offerings and, feeding his torn garment as a wick to the dying lamp before the liṅga, unknowingly completes the fast-vigil-lamp vrata before being killed as a thief _(actors: Guṇanidhi, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gunanidhi-kubera.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṁhitā, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 18)
- **Gurudruh's unwitting Śivarātri vigil** — a sinful hunter, having caught nothing all day (an accidental fast), spends the night in a bilva tree above a hidden liṅga, unknowingly showering it with bilva leaves and water in each of the four watches _(actors: Gurudruh, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shivaratri-hunter-legend.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṁhitā 40)
- **Guruvayurappan comes to taste the payasam** — legend holds the Lord of Guruvayur visits Ambalappuzha daily to taste its pal-payasam, binding the two Kerala Krishna-kshetras _(actors: Guruvayurappan, Ambalappuzha Krishna)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ambalappuzha-krishna-palpayasam.md) — (Kerala oral tradition) [folk] [oral tradition]
- **Harmukh–Gaṅgabal ash-immersion** — Kashmiri Pandits carry the ashes of their dead to the alpine lake Gaṅgabal below Śiva's peak Harmukh, the valley's northern Gaṅgā, in the Harmukh-Gaṅga death-rite yātrā _(actors: Kashmiri Pandit pilgrims, Śiva of Harmukh)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kashmir-tirthas-nilamata-geography.md) — (living Kashmiri Pandit tradition) [regional]
- **Hayagrīva reveals the Rahasya-nāma-sāhasra to Agastya** — having withheld the secret litany, the horse-headed Viṣṇu-form yields to Agastya's pressing and transmits the Lalitā Sahasranāma, chartering the Śrī-Vidyā guru-transmission _(actors: Hayagrīva, Agastya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/lalita-sahasranama.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa, Lalitopākhyāna, pūrva-bhāga)
- **Hayagrīva transmits the Lalitā Triśatī** — in the same frame Hayagrīva also gives Agastya the 300-name hymn, its names arranged twenty to each syllable of the Pañcadaśī mantra _(actors: Hayagrīva, Agastya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/lalita-sahasranama.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa, Lalitopākhyāna)
- **Hemalekhā teaches that pleasure lies in the mind** — the dispassionate wife awakens her besotted husband by showing that joy and sorrow reside not in objects but in the mind's own imagining, turning him to inquiry into the witnessing Self _(actors: Hemalekhā, Hemacūḍa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tripura-rahasya.md) — (Tripurā Rahasya, Jñāna-khaṇḍa)
- **Hiraṇyahasta given to Vadhrimatī** — to Vadhrimatī, whose husband was impotent, the Aśvins grant a son, Golden-hand (Hiraṇyahasta), restoring the line _(actors: Aśvins, Vadhrimatī, Hiraṇyahasta)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ashvins.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.116.13; 6.62.7)
- **Iḍā, Manu's flood-daughter** — in the oldest stratum Manu's post-deluge offering yields the woman Iḍā, the offering-goddess and progenitrix of Manu's race, with no sex-change — the Vedic root of the later Sudyumna myth _(actors: Manu, Iḍā, Mitra-Varuṇa)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/ila-sudyumna.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa I.8.1)
- **Indra cleansed at the Aruṇā tīrtha** — pursued by the guilt of the treacherous slaying, Indra bathes at the Aruṇā-saṃgama on the Sarasvatī and is freed of the sin-stain _(actors: Indra, Brahmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indra-namuchi.md) — (Mahābhārata Śalya Parva 9.43)
- **Indra defeats the tyrant Mayadenawa (Galungan origin legend)** — the god strikes forth the Tirta Empul spring to revive his poisoned troops and slays the worship-banning tyrant whose blood curses the Petanu river, commemorated every 210 days at Galungan [regional] [folk] _(actors: Indra, Mayadenawa)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/balinese-hinduism-agama-hindu-dharma.md) — (Usana Bali)
- **Indra distributes the Brahmahatyā** — after slaying the brāhmaṇa Vṛtra/Viśvarūpa, Indra hides in a lotus-fibre and is purified by dividing the great sin into four — earth, water, trees, and women — each taking a share for a boon, then by the Aśvamedha _(actors: Indra, Vṛtra, Earth, the waters)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/papa-punya-and-prayaschitta.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 6; Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva)
- **Indra grants Sumukha long life** — carried to heaven before Indra and Viṣṇu, Sumukha is granted length of days rather than the amṛta, escaping Garuḍa's appointed term _(actors: Mātali, Sumukha, Indra, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matali-sumukha-naga-bridegroom.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva 5.102)
- **Indra plants Jayantī at Śukra's side** — fearing the completed tapas, Indra sends his daughter Jayantī to serve the penancing sage, who rewards her with a ten-year union behind a veil of māyā _(actors: Indra, Jayantī, Śukra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigus-curse-on-vishnu.md) — (Matsya Purāṇa 47; Devī-Bhāgavata IV.12)
- **Indra refuses Brāhmaṇahood** — Indra appears again and again to tell Mātaṅga that Brāhmaṇahood, the highest station, cannot be won in one life by the Caṇḍāla-born; varṇa rises only gradually across many births _(actors: Mātaṅga, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matanga-vitahavya-varna-upakhyanas.md) — (Anuśāsana Parva ~29)
- **Indra throws the Yatis to the salāvṛkas** — one of the canonical sins of Indra: he gives the ascetic Yatis to hyenas to be devoured, a transgression the tradition itself flags _(actors: Indra, the Yatis, salāvṛkas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/brahmana-legends-catalog.md) — (Aitareya Brāhmaṇa 7.28)
- **Indra's apsara counter-insurgency** — the recurring dispatch of Menakā, Rambhā and Pramlocā to break world-threatening tapas, yielding Śakuntalā and a stone-cursed Rambhā _(actors: Indra, Menakā, Viśvāmitra, Rambhā)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tapas-boons-and-curses.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva; Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa 1.63–64)
- **Indra's crosswise birth dialogue** — the unborn Indra refuses the 'ancient proven path' of birth and emerges crosswise through his mother's side, with Vamadeva's famine-verse attached _(actors: Indra, Indra's mother, Vamadeva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/rigveda-samvada-and-secular-hymns.md) — (Rigveda 4.18)
- **Indra's six decoy Vitaṅkas** — Indra makes six identical replicas to trick Muchukunda, but by Śiva's grace the king picks the true image and Indra yields all seven emerald Somāskanda icons _(actors: Indra, Muchukunda, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruvarur-thyagaraja-kamalalaya.md) — (Kṣetra-māhātmya)
- **Indradyumna falls from heaven for want of a rememberer** — the celestials cast the royal sage back to earth because his store of merit lapses once no living being recalls his deeds, and bid him find one witness who knows him _(actors: Indradyumna, the devas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indradyumna-akupara-oldest-beings.md) — (Mahābhārata 3.191 / Ganguli Vana CXCIX)
- **Indrani's quarrel over Vrishakapi** — Indrani rages at Indra's pet bull-ape Vrishakapi in a ribald three-way dialogue closing with the refrain 'Indra is supreme over all' _(actors: Indra, Indrani, Vrishakapi)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/rigveda-samvada-and-secular-hymns.md) — (Rigveda 10.86)
- **Installation of Bālāditya** — the boy discharges his karma through solar penance and voluntary death; Nandabhadra cremates him at Barkarikā Tīrtha and founds the Bālāditya Sun-shrine on his instruction _(actors: Nandabhadra, the boy)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nandabhadra-tested-faith.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa 46)
- **Īśvarī Pāṭunī ferries the goddess** — Annadā in disguise as a radiant woman asks the low-caste boatman Īśvarī Pāṭunī to row her across the Bhāgīrathī at night, then reveals her four-armed form and offers a boon _(actors: Annadā, Īśvarī Pāṭunī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/annadamangal-bharatchandra.md) — (Annadāmaṅgal, Pāṭunī episode)
- **Jalandhara, Vṛndā and the birth of Tulsī** — Viṣṇu deceives the chaste Vṛndā to break the asura Jalandhara's invincibility, and she is reborn as the tulsī plant, charter of Tulsī Vivāha _(actors: Jalandhara, Vṛndā, Viṣṇu, Śiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/padma-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa, Kārttika-māhātmya)
- **Jaya-Vijaya's three-birth curse drives the avatāras** — the cursed doorkeepers choose three births as Viṣṇu's enemies, necessitating Varāha, Narasiṃha, Rāma and Kṛṣṇa _(actors: Jaya, Vijaya, the Four Kumāras, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tapas-boons-and-curses.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 3.15–16, 7.1)
- **Jyotibā born of the Trimūrti** — Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva combine their aṃśas to bring forth the radiant warrior-god Jyotibā (Kedāreśvara), taking birth to the sage Paugaṇḍa to answer the harassed ṛṣis of Karavīra _(actors: Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Śiva, Paugaṇḍa, Jyotibā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jyotiba-kedarling-kolhapur.md) — (Kedāra Vijaya)
- **Jyotibā takes Wadi Ratnagiri** — with the demons dead, Jyotibā liberates Karavīra and establishes his permanent guardian-seat on the Wadi Ratnagiri hill above Kolhapur as the male counterpart of the enthroned Goddess _(actors: Jyotibā, Mahālakṣmī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jyotiba-kedarling-kolhapur.md) — (Kedāra Vijaya)
- **Kāla comes for the rājarṣi Śveta** — At the end of his allotted span, Death-as-Time arrives to seize the royal sage Śveta while he is absorbed in worship of the Śiva-liṅga _(actors: Śveta, Kāla)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sage-shveta-and-yama.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ch. 30)
- **Kālī saves Sundara at the śmaśāna** — caught as a thief and condemned to death, Sundara hymns Kālī at the execution-ground; the goddess manifests, stays the executioners, and reveals him as the prince of Kāñcī, winning his proper marriage to Vidyā _(actors: Sundara, Kālī, Vidyā, Vīrasiṃha, the kotwāl)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/annadamangal-bharatchandra.md) — (Annadāmaṅgal, Vidyā-Sundar khaṇḍa)
- **Kāmodā's tears of joy become Gaṅgā lotuses** — Each spring at her seat ten yojanas above Gaṅgādvāra, the meditating Kāmodā laughs with delight and her tears fall into the Gaṅgā as fragrant yellow lotuses, with which devotees worship Śiva for their desires _(actors: Kāmodā, Viṣṇu, Śiva, the Gaṅgā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-purana-ganga-tales.md) — (Nārada Purāṇa, Uttarabhāga 68)
- **Kandalī the ogress regains human form by a righteous deed** — Cursed by Durvāsas into rākṣasī shape, Kandalī helps Kāṣṭhīlā's wandering husband slay the demon Gobhila with a śakti weapon, wins back her benign form, and weds him alongside Ratnāvalī of Vārāṇasī _(actors: Kandalī, Gobhila, Kāṣṭhīlā's husband, Ratnāvalī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-purana-ganga-tales.md) — (Nārada Purāṇa, Uttarabhāga 28–30)
- **Kandiyūr — the severing of Brahmā's fifth head** — the shrine of Brahmaśiraḥ-Kaṇḍeśvarar where Bhairava shears off Brahmā's insolent fifth head; a local variant has Viṣṇu, not Kāśī, free Śiva of the brahmahatyā _(actors: Śiva/Bhairava, Brahmā, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-virattanam-eight-feat-shrines.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa Śatarudra-saṃhitā; sthala-purāṇa)
- **Karabhājana's yuga-avatāra doctrine** — the Lord descends white in Satya (worshipped by meditation), red in Tretā (by sacrifice), dark in Dvāpara (by royal/tantric worship), and in Kali by saṅkīrtana _(actors: Karabhājana, Nimi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nimi-nava-yogendra-samvada.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 11.5.20–32)
- **Karkaṭī becomes the needle-demoness Viśūcikā** — The Himālayan rākṣasī wins from Brahmā the form of a piercing needle-sickness, repents of formless misery, regains her body Self-knowing, and tests a king and minister with riddles on the Ātman _(actors: Karkaṭī, Brahmā, the Kirāta king and minister)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yoga-vasishtha-tales-catalog.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha III.68–81)
- **Karkaṭī the rākṣasī becomes the needle Viśūcikā and repents** — The demoness wins a boon to become a piercing sickness, wearies of formless hunger, regains her body Self-knowing, and tests a king and minister with riddles on the Ātman _(actors: Karkaṭī, Brahmā, a mountain king and his minister)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha, Utpatti prakaraṇa)
- **Karma, the three guṇas and transmigration** — action as mental, verbal and bodily yields specific animal and hellish rebirths, with sattva leading upward to godhood, rajas to human states and tamas downward _(actors: the transmigrating self, Yama's order)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/manusmriti-map.md) — (Mānava-Dharmaśāstra 12.3–51)
- **Kārtika-vrata charter (Dāmodara-māsa)** — the month sacred to Viṣṇu is glorified through the vows of dawn bath, lamp-offering, and Tulasī worship, illustrated by a fixed cluster of devotee tales recited each Kārtika _(actors: Viṣṇu-Dāmodara, the Kārttika-māhātmya narrators)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kartika-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Skanda P., Vaiṣṇava-khaṇḍa, Kārttikamāsa-māhātmya; Padma P., Uttara-khaṇḍa)
- **Kārttikeya founds the Kumārikā-kṣetra & plants Stambheśvara** — the boy-general Skanda, victor over Tāraka, consecrates the Mahī-mouth as his kṣetra and installs the pillar-liṅga Stambheśvara at the sea's edge, giving Khambhāt (Stambhatīrtha) its name _(actors: Kārttikeya, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mahisagara-sangama-kumarika-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa)
- **Kaśmīrā is Pārvatī** — the governing maxim of the valley's sacred geography, that the land itself is the Goddess and its king a portion of Śiva, making every spring and hill a limb of the divine landscape _(actors: Pārvatī, the land of Kashmir)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kashmir-tirthas-nilamata-geography.md) — (Rājataraṅgiṇī 1.42)
- **Kataragama, the multi-faith abode of Skanda** — the deep-south Murugan shrine worshipped at once by Hindus, Buddhists, Muslims and Vedda, centred on the god's marriage to the Vedda maiden Vaḷḷi _(actors: Murugan/Skanda, Vaḷḷi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/greater-india-sacred-geography.md) — ([regional]; [folk])
- **Kathivanūr Vīran deified with Chemmarathi** — the archer-hero Mandappan Chekavar, disowned and settled at Kathivanūr in Coorg, is ambushed and killed in the Kodagu war, whereupon his wife Chemmarathi enters his pyre and the two are deified together as a hero-theyyam _(actors: Mandappan Chekavar, Chemmarathi)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/theyyam-deities-and-thottam-myths.md) — (Kathivanūr Vīran thoṭṭam) [folk]
- **Kauṇḍinya tears the ananta-thread** — the sage scorns his wife Suśīlā's 14-knot cord, burns it, falls to ruin, and after a riddle-quest past mango tree, cow, bull, ponds, donkey and elephant is restored by fourteen years of the vrata _(actors: Kauṇḍinya, Suśīlā, Ananta/Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vrata-kathas-catalog.md) — (Bhaviṣyottara Purāṇa [attrib.]) [late text]
- **Kāvyamātā shelters the routed asuras** — with Śukra hidden, the beaten Daityas flee to Bhṛgu's wife Kāvyamātā, who grants them refuge and moves to burn and stupefy Indra by yogic power _(actors: Kāvyamātā, Daityas, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigus-curse-on-vishnu.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara 51; Devī-Bhāgavata IV.13)
- **Keśidhvaja's yoga discourse** — the jñāna-master teaches that avidyā is false self-identification, defines yoga and its stages, and prescribes contemplation (dhāraṇā) on the embodied form of Viṣṇu withdrawn toward the formless Brahman _(actors: Keśidhvaja, Khāṇḍikya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khandikya-keshidhvaja.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa VI.7)
- **Khāṇḍikya asks for yoga, not a throne** — the deposed karma-master requests as his fee the knowledge that severs rebirth, and Keśidhvaja rejoices that by refusing the kingdom his cousin has truly conquered _(actors: Khāṇḍikya, Keśidhvaja)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khandikya-keshidhvaja.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa VI.6)
- **Khāṇḍikya spares his conqueror** — with the defenceless Keśidhvaja in his power and ministers urging the kill, Khāṇḍikya lays down his bow, reasoning that slaying wins only this world while teaching the expiation wins the world beyond _(actors: Khāṇḍikya, Keśidhvaja)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khandikya-keshidhvaja.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa VI.6)
- **King Mahīratha gives his merit to hell** — the Vaiśākha-vrata king makes over his accumulated merit to hell's sufferers, releasing them, and ascends to Viṣṇu's world _(actors: Mahīratha, hell's sufferers, Nārada, Ambarīṣa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/masa-mahatmyas-kartika-magha-vaishakha.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Pātāla-khaṇḍa 85–103)
- **King Narasiṃha and the half-verse in the rock** — a Gauḍa king finds half a verse of Gītā chapter 15 in a fissure; its mere reading sends a karma-bound horse to heaven and wins salvation for the king, his son, and ministers _(actors: King Narasiṃha, a horse)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavad-gita/gita-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Gītā-Māhātmya 15)
- **King Padma's death** — Padma dies; Līlā preserves his flower-heaped body and invokes her first boon to keep his spirit near, and Sarasvatī reveals the soul is present but dwelling in a world of its own making _(actors: Padma, Līlā, Sarasvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/lila-story-yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha III ~17–19)
- **King Tāladhvaja marries Suśīlā** — the palmyra-bannered king finds the solitary woman by the lake, is smitten, and takes her as his queen, beginning a full married household _(actors: Tāladhvaja, Suśīlā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-maya-taladhvaja.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa VI.29–30)
- **King Vipaścit finds himself in hell** — the just monarch, who sacrificed, ruled fairly and honoured guests, gods and ancestors, walks bewildered into Naraka and protests his innocence _(actors: Vipaścit)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vipashchit-in-hell.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa XIII)
- **Kolhāsura and the naming of Kolhāpura** — the demon Kolhāsura who gives Kolhapur its name is slain in the Karavīra war, credited to Mahālakṣmī in her charter and to Jyotibā as her guardian-general in his _(actors: Jyotibā, Mahālakṣmī, Kolhāsura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jyotiba-kedarling-kolhapur.md) — (Kedāra Vijaya; Karavīra-māhātmya)
- **Koneśvaram, the southern Kailāsa** — the Trincomalee Śiva temple styled Dakṣiṇa-Kailāsa, tied by legend to Rāvaṇa's Śiva-worship and sung in the Tamil Tēvāram as one of the Pāḍal Petṟa Sthalams _(actors: Śiva, Rāvaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/greater-india-sacred-geography.md) — (Tēvāram; Dakṣiṇa Kailāsa Purāṇam; [regional])
- **Kordabbu, the Koraga wonder-worker deified** — a hero of the marginalised Koraga community, able to make a dry stick sprout green and raise water, is worshipped as the daiva Koragajja, giver of lost things and vows _(actors: Kordabbu, Koragajja)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/panjurli-guliga-tulu-daivas.md) — (Kordabbu / Koragajja paddana) [folk]
- **Krishna reveals himself and commutes the debt** — the sage discloses he is Krishna and converts the unpayable rice into an eternal duty to serve pal-payasam to every devotee until repaid _(actors: Krishna, Chempakasseri king)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ambalappuzha-krishna-palpayasam.md) — (Ambalappuzha temple tradition) [folk]
- **Kṣupa's vajra nullified** — the enraged king hurls Indra's thunderbolt at Dadhīca, but Śiva's boon renders it harmless against the sage's body — the kṣatra's ultimate weapon defeated by brahma _(actors: Kṣupa, Dadhīca, Indra's vajra, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kshupa-dadhicha-dispute.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ~35–36)
- **Kubera named and enthroned by Śiva** — Reborn (in the Meghavāhana kalpa) and doing tapas at Kāśī, the future wealth-lord's left eye bursts as he stares at Umā; Śiva names him Kubera and grants wealth-lordship, yakṣa-kingship, Alakā, and his own friendship _(actors: Kubera (Guṇanidhi), Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gunanidhi-kubera.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṁhitā, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 19)
- **Kukura slain in buffalo-form** — the Goddess battles the daitya Kukura, who takes mahiṣa shape, and beheads him on the Bālā-ghāṭ, re-enacting the Mahiṣāsura-mardinī act on Deccan soil _(actors: the Devī, Kukura)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tuljapur-tulja-bhavani.md) — (Tuljā-māhātmya) [regional]
- **Kuṇḍadhara grants dharma, not wealth** — the cloud-spirit Kuṇḍadhara, pleased with a poor Brahmin who begged for riches, gives him instead attachment to righteousness, which carries him past rebirth _(actors: Kuṇḍadhara, the poor Brahmin)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shanti-parva-minor-upakhyanas.md) — (MBh, Śānti Parva 12.263)
- **Kuṇi-Garga's will-born daughter** — the ṛṣi Kuṇi surnamed Garga creates a fair-browed daughter by a fiat of will, then ascends to heaven, leaving her to a lifetime of severe penance unmarried _(actors: Kuṇi-Garga, the maiden)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sarasvata-upakhyana-vriddhakumari.md) — (Mbh Śalya Parva 9.52; Ganguli LII)
- **Kuñjala the pious parrot instructs Cyavana** — the learned parrot teaches dharma and the inner-tīrtha doctrine to his four sons, the sage Cyavana, and Divyadevī _(actors: Kuñjala, Cyavana, Divyadevī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/padma-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Bhūmi-khaṇḍa 85–101, 123)
- **Kurukkai — the burning of Kāma** — the shrine where Śiva's third eye reduced the love-god Kāma to ash, later restored bodiless as Anaṅga at Rati's plea _(actors: Śiva, Kāmadeva, Rati, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-virattanam-eight-feat-shrines.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa Rudra-saṃhitā; Kumārasaṃbhava)
- **Kutsa Aurava, Indra's doppelgänger** — the ṛṣi Kutsa so exactly resembles Indra that, carried to heaven bound by a single shared lock of hair, even Indrāṇī cannot tell god from man _(actors: Kutsa Aurava, Indra, Indrāṇī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/brahmana-legends-catalog.md) — (Jaiminīya Brāhmaṇa 3.199–200)
- **Kuṭṭichāttan slain and multiplied** — the wild boy-imp born of Śiva and Pārvatī and fostered at the Kāḷakāṭṭ Illam slaughters the household's cow, is cut down by the Nampūtiri, and multiplies from his own blood into hundreds of Chāttans who torment the illam until propitiated and enshrined _(actors: Kuṭṭichāttan, Śiva, Pārvatī, Kāḷakāṭṭ Nampūtiri)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/theyyam-deities-and-thottam-myths.md) — (Kuṭṭichāttan thoṭṭam) [folk]
- **Lakṣmī commands Cārumatī in a dream** — Mahālakṣmī visits the virtuous Cārumatī of Kuṇḍina and institutes the Varalakṣmī vratam of Śrāvaṇa's Friday, showering the town's women with fortune at each pradakṣiṇā _(actors: Mahālakṣmī, Cārumatī, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vrata-kathas-catalog.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa [attrib.], Varalakṣmī-vrata-kathā) [late text] [regional]
- **Līlā's two boons against widowhood** — dreading her husband's death, Queen Līlā propitiates the goddess Sarasvatī and wins that Padma's soul stay in the palace when he dies and that the goddess come whenever called _(actors: Līlā, Sarasvatī, Padma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/lila-story-yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha III ~16)
- **Madālasā dies of grief** — hearing the false report and seeing her husband's ornament, the faithful queen at once yields up her life, a death only nāga power can undo _(actors: Madālasā, Tālaketu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kuvalayashva-madalasa.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa, Canto XXII)
- **Madana Vinoda sails and leaves the guardian bird** — the reformed merchant's son departs on a trading voyage, entrusting his wife to the parrot, while in the two-bird recension a blunt myna is nearly killed for rebuking her _(actors: Madana Vinoda, Prabhāvatī, the parrot, the myna)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/shuka-saptati.md) — (Śukasaptati, frame)
- **Mahānandā's rudrākṣa vow** — the pious courtesan of Nandigrāma, sworn on oath to a Vaiśya devotee, follows him into the fire when his jewelled liṅga burns, and Śiva halts her and lifts her whole household to his realm _(actors: Mahānandā, the Vaiśya devotee, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/brahmottara-khanda-vrata-tales.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Brāhmottara-khaṇḍa 20)
- **Mahiṣāsura's gender-clause boon** — invincibility against gods and men forces the devas' pooled tejas to condense into the Goddess who alone can slay him _(actors: Mahiṣāsura, Brahmā, Durgā)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tapas-boons-and-curses.md) — (Devī Māhātmya, Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa 81–83)
- **Mala-māsa adopted as Puruṣottama-māsa** — the reviled personified leap month weeps before Viṣṇu, who leads it to Goloka where Kṛṣṇa bestows his own name Puruṣottama and supremacy among months [late text] _(actors: Mala-māsa personified, Viṣṇu, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/masa-mahatmyas-kartika-magha-vaishakha.md) — (Puruṣottama-māsa-māhātmya, transmitted under the Padma Purāṇa)
- **Māṇabasā Gurubāra (Gurubāra Oṣā) vrata** — every Thursday of Mārgaśira Odia women seat the māṇa of new paddy as Lakṣmī, draw jhoṭi-chitā, and recite the Lakṣmī Purāṇa, annually re-performing the goddess's verdict _(actors: Lakṣmī, Odia householder women)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/lakshmi-purana-shriya-chandaluni.md) — (Lakṣmī Purāṇa as vrata liturgy; Odia household practice)
- **Mānasa-Pūjā Vindicated** — the charter-parable that worship built wholly in the mind outweighs the grandest temple of dressed stone, pairing Pūcalār with Vāyilār of Mylapore as the canon's exemplars of interior devotion _(actors: Pūcalār, Vāyilār, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pusalar-nayanar-mind-temple.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Pūcalār & Vāyilār Purāṇams)
- **Manifestation of Vaṭuka Bhairava** — Śiva brings forth a radiant, fierce boy from his own being as a protective son-form (putra-rūpa), not womb-born, charged with guarding sages and devotees _(actors: Śiva, Vaṭuka Bhairava)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vatuka-bhairava-origin.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Kotirudra-saṃhitā ch. 13)
- **Maṇikuṇḍala's dharma-wager vindicated** — Mutilated and blinded after wagers on dharma vs adharma, the vaiśya Maṇikuṇḍala is healed by the viśalyakaraṇī herb on the Gautamī's bank, becomes king, and pardons his betrayer _(actors: Maṇikuṇḍala, Gautama (the namesake brāhmaṇa), Vibhīṣaṇa and his son)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautami-mahatmya-tales-catalog.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa, Gautamī-māhātmya, vulgate adh. 170)
- **Manu Nīti Cholan's chariot-justice** — the Chola king of Tiruvārūr drives his own war-chariot over his son to avenge a calf the prince's wheel had killed, and Śiva restores both to life _(actors: Manu Nīti Cholan, the prince Vīdhividaṅgan, the cow and calf, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruvarur-thyagaraja-kamalalaya.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Tirumalai-charukkam preamble)
- **March on the hermitage of the twin sages** — Dambhodbhava leads his six-limbed army to Gandhamādana and, spurning the ascetics' hospitality and repeated refusals, demands battle of the emaciated Nara and Nārāyaṇa _(actors: Dambhodbhava, Nara, Nārāyaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dambhodbhava-nara-narayana.md) — (Mahābhārata CE 5.94, Udyoga Parva)
- **Marriage bought with half a lifetime of penance** — the old maid offers half her ascetic merit as bride-price, and Śṛṅgavat of Gālava's line weds her on the condition of a single night together _(actors: the old maid, Śṛṅgavat)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sarasvata-upakhyana-vriddhakumari.md) — (Mbh Śalya Parva 9.52; Ganguli LII)
- **Mātali's quest for a Nāga son-in-law** — Indra's charioteer Mātali, finding no groom worthy of his daughter Guṇakeśī in the upper worlds, resolves to search the netherworld of Pātāla _(actors: Mātali, Guṇakeśī, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matali-sumukha-naga-bridegroom.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva 5.95–96)
- **Mātaṅga's consolation boon and death** — after collapsing from a century on one toe, Mātaṅga is caught by Indra, granted shape-shifting and worship by women in place of the caste he sought, and dies without ever becoming a Brāhmaṇa _(actors: Mātaṅga, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matanga-vitahavya-varna-upakhyanas.md) — (Anuśāsana Parva ~30)
- **Mātaṅga's vow to win Brāhmaṇahood** — shaken by the truth of his mixed birth, Mātaṅga undertakes penances of terrifying severity to propitiate Indra and force his way into the Brāhmaṇa varṇa _(actors: Mātaṅga, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matanga-vitahavya-varna-upakhyanas.md) — (Anuśāsana Parva ~28–29)
- **Māyāmoha frames the tale** — Viṣṇu's emitted deluder strips the Daityas of the Vedas on the Narmadā, creating the pāṣaṇḍa class whose mere conversation dooms Śatadhanu _(actors: Viṣṇu, Māyāmoha, the Daityas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shatadhanu-shaibya.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, III.17–18)
- **Maynāmatī's ordeal-proof** — to prove that yogic deathlessness is real, Maynāmatī passes unharmed through fire and water, demonstrating the kāya-siddhi her son may attain _(actors: Maynāmatī, Gopīcandra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gopichandra-maynamati-nath-epic.md) — (Gopīcandrer Gān) [folk] [oral tradition]
- **Mhaskoba the buffalo-lord** — the Deccan Mhasobā of Vīr is raised into a temple-god and identified with Kāḷ-Bhairava, the fierce skull-bearing form of Śiva _(actors: Mhaskoba, Kāḷ-Bhairava/Śiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/dhangar-oral-epics-biroba-mhaskoba-dhuloba.md) — (Mhaskoba tradition of Vīr; Sontheimer)
- **Mohinī destroys Bhasmāsura through dance** — Viṣṇu's enchantress-form lures the demon into placing his boon-hand on his own head, the namesake charter-myth of Mohiniyāṭṭam _(actors: Viṣṇu as Mohinī, Bhasmāsura, Śiva)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/classical-dance-forms-and-ritual-theatre.md) — (Purāṇic Mohinī cycle)
- **Muchilōṭṭu Bhagavathi immolates and is enshrined** — a learned Brahmin virgin of Perinchellūr, excommunicated on a malicious slur after out-debating the scholars, immolates herself at Karivellūr and is enshrined by a Vāṇiyan oil-presser as the benign bride-goddess Muchilōṭṭu Bhagavathi _(actors: Muchilōṭṭu Bhagavathi, Muchilōṭṭu Paḍanāyar)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/theyyam-deities-and-thottam-myths.md) — (Muchilōṭṭu Bhagavathi thoṭṭam) [folk]
- **Muchukunda wins the Somāskanda** — the Chola king Muchukunda, having aided Indra in a deva–asura war, claims as reward the Tyāgarāja/Somāskanda image worshipped on Viṣṇu's chest _(actors: Muchukunda the Chola, Indra, Viṣṇu, Śiva-Umā-Skanda)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruvarur-thyagaraja-kamalalaya.md) — (Tyāgarāja/Kamalālaya-kṣetra-māhātmya)
- **Mudgala and Mudgalani's chariot race** — Mudgala wins a thousand cattle in a race with a cart yoked to a bull and a wooden club, his wife Mudgalani driving as charioteer _(actors: Mudgala, Mudgalani, Indra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/rigveda-samvada-and-secular-hymns.md) — (Rigveda 10.102)
- **Namuci as prototype loophole-boon** — Namuci's "neither wet nor dry, neither day nor night" is the earliest fully-articulated boon-loophole, the template for Hiraṇyakaśipu, Mahiṣa, and Tāraka _(actors: Namuci, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indra-namuchi.md) — (Purāṇic pattern; Ṛgveda; Brāhmaṇas)
- **Namuci steals Indra's strength with the surā** — the Dānava Namuci carries off Indra's vigour and the essence of food and drink together with the Soma and Surā, leaving the king of the gods powerless _(actors: Indra, Namuci)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indra-namuchi.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 12.7.3.1–4)
- **Namuci's sunbeam refuge and sworn friendship** — in the epic Namuci hides in a ray of the sun and binds Indra by a pact of friendship on the wet/dry, day/night terms _(actors: Indra, Namuci)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indra-namuchi.md) — (Mahābhārata Śalya Parva 9.43)
- **Nandā identified as Pārvatī, daughter of the hills** — Uttarakhand tradition localizes the Purāṇic Pārvatī cycle, worshipping the goddess as the region's married-out daughter (dhiyāṇī) sent yearly toward Kailāsa _(actors: Nandā/Pārvatī, Śiva, Himavat)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/nanda-devi-and-uttarakhand-deities.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa frame; Kumaoni/Garhwali jāgar ballads [regional])
- **Nandabhadra merges into Rudra** — his faith vindicated by the theodicy of karma, the merchant becomes a lifelong worshipper of Śiva and Sūrya and at the end attains mokṣa, merging into Rudra's body _(actors: Nandabhadra, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nandabhadra-tested-faith.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa 46)
- **Nandabhadra tested at the confluence** — the flawless merchant, stripped of son and wife and baited to abandon dharma, holds fast and is answered by a karma-theodicy at the Mahīsāgara-saṃgama — a Book-of-Job analogue _(actors: Nandabhadra, an atheist neighbour)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mahisagara-sangama-kumarika-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa)
- **Nandabhadra the honest merchant** — a vaiśya of flawless piety trades without deceit and worships Śiva's Liṅga daily, keeping only what suffices _(actors: Nandabhadra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nandabhadra-tested-faith.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa 45)
- **Nandikeśvara delivers the Kāśī-fruit on the Narmadā** — Gaṅgā appears to Suvāda as a maiden at Viṃśatigrāma and sends him to cast the bones at Nandikeśvara on Vaiśākha-saptamī; his mother rises in divine form and ascends to Śiva's world _(actors: Suvāda, Gaṅgā, the brahmin widow, Nandikeśvara)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kotirudra-minor-linga-legends.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṃhitā 6)
- **Nara's sermon and Dambhodbhava's reform** — granting the fallen king safety, Nara commands him to honor brāhmaṇas, conquer his senses, and never insult anyone again, and the chastened emperor rules righteously ever after _(actors: Nara, Dambhodbhava)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dambhodbhava-nara-narayana.md) — (Mahābhārata CE 5.94, Udyoga Parva)
- **Nara-Nārāyaṇa manifests Urvaśī** — in Drumila's avatāra recital, the sage Nārāyaṇa at Badarī foils Indra's temptation by producing a nymph lovelier than the apsarases sent against him _(actors: Nara-Nārāyaṇa, Indra, Kāmadeva, Urvaśī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nimi-nava-yogendra-samvada.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 11.4)
- **Nārada asks to see Mahāmāyā** — the deathless sage begs Bhagavān Viṣṇu (Hari) to reveal the Goddess's power of illusion directly, not in words, and Viṣṇu sets out to teach him by experience _(actors: Nārada, Viṣṇu/Hari)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-maya-taladhvaja.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa VI.28)
- **Nārada baits the demons toward Pārvatī** — at Śiva's direction Nārada extols the Goddess's beauty to Vidala and Utpala until, inflamed with lust, they resolve to abduct her from Kailāsa _(actors: Nārada, Vidala, Utpala, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yuddha-khanda-minor-vadhas.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Yuddha-khaṇḍa 59)
- **Nārada denies the old maid heaven** — Nārada rules that the aged virgin ascetic cannot reach the blessed regions unpurified by the rite of marriage, however great her tapas _(actors: Nārada, the old maid)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sarasvata-upakhyana-vriddhakumari.md) — (Mbh Śalya Parva 9.52; Ganguli LII)
- **Nārada proposes the dice-game** — on Kailāsa the sage interrupts Śiva and Pārvatī's embraces and sets them at akṣa-krīḍā, seeding the quarrel _(actors: Nārada, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shiva-parvati-dice-game.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa 34.61)
- **Nārada rises as the woman Suśīlā** — plunging into the water the sage forgets he is Nārada entirely and emerges on the bank as a beautiful young woman with no memory of his prior self — māyā as amnesia, not disguise _(actors: Nārada, Suśīlā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-maya-taladhvaja.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa VI.29)
- **Nārada voices the 84 bhakti aphorisms** — tradition ascribes the Nārada Bhakti Sūtra, defining bhakti as parama-prema, to the timeless wandering devarṣi _(actors: Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhakti-sutras-narada-shandilya.md) — (Nārada Bhakti Sūtra, traditional ascription)
- **Nārada's love for Sukumārī and the concealment** — smitten with Sṛñjaya's daughter Sukumārī and wed to her, Nārada hides his desire from Parvata, breaking their vow of full disclosure _(actors: Nārada, Sukumārī, Parvata, Sṛñjaya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/suvarnashthivin-srinjaya-narada-parvata.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva; Ganguli XXX)
- **Nārada's tour of the seven Pātālas** — Nārada guides Mātali down through the subterranean worlds, cataloguing the Vaḍavāmukha fire, the asura strongholds, Hiraṇyapura, Rasātala with Surabhi, and the serpent-cities _(actors: Nārada, Mātali)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matali-sumukha-naga-bridegroom.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva 5.97–100)
- **Naravāhanadatta consecrated Vidyādhara emperor (Mahābhiṣeka)** — the mortal-born son of Udayana of Vatsa wins the vidyās and brides mortal and celestial and is crowned cakravartin of the Vidyādharas, fulfilling Śiva's decree that frames the whole story-ocean _(actors: Naravāhanadatta, Udayana, Madanamañcukā)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/brihatkatha-kathasaritsagara-and-vikramaditya-cycle.md) — (Kathāsaritsāgara, XV Mahābhiṣeka)
- **Navarāj born, the old husband gone** — Goma bears a son, Navarāj, but poverty drives the aged Śiva Śarma to leave and never return, leaving her a destitute single mother _(actors: Goma, Navarāj, Śiva Śarma)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/swasthani-brata-katha.md) — (Svasthānī Vrata Kathā, Goma cycle)
- **Navarāj crowned by the elephant's garland** — grown by the vow's grace, Navarāj wins an heirless kingdom when the late king's state elephant garlands him, and the beggar-widow's son becomes king _(actors: Navarāj, Goma)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/swasthani-brata-katha.md) — (Svasthānī Vrata Kathā, Goma cycle)
- **Nīli deified as Icakki Ammaṉ** — the blood-taking pēy is placated and enshrined as the far-southern yakṣi-goddess Icakkiyammaṉ, her sung katai the charter of her bow-song cult _(actors: Icakki Ammaṉ, her worshippers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/icakki-amman-nili-katai.md) — (Icakkiyammaṉ Katai; Perundevi Srinivasan)
- **Origin of blinking — Nimi in every eyelid** — the gods set Nimi in the eyes of all creatures as the moving vāyu, so that eyelids open and close on his account; the twinkling (nimeṣa) is named for him _(actors: Nimi, the devas, all living beings)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/nimi-vasishtha-mutual-curse.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara-kāṇḍa 57)
- **Origin of painting: Nārāyaṇa and Urvaśī** — to humble the proud apsarases, the sage Nārāyaṇa paints a surpassingly beautiful woman on his thigh with mango juice, and from the image springs Urvaśī — the first act of citra _(actors: Nārāyaṇa, Urvaśī, apsarases)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vishnudharmottara-purana.md) — (Viṣṇudharmottara Purāṇa, Citrasūtra / Khaṇḍa III)
- **Pacification of Bhadrakālī's fury** — Still blazing after the kill, the goddess is calmed — by darśana of her father Śiva, by the gods' masked kōlam dances (padayani's charter), or by an infant placed in her path — variants that found Kerala's ritual arts _(actors: Bhadrakālī, Śiva, the devas)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bhadrakali-kerala-bhagavati.md) — (Mudiyettu and padayani traditions) [folk]
- **Padma reborn as King Vidūratha** — at death Padma's own vāsanā spins a further nested world in which he lives as the young King Vidūratha beside a second Queen Līlā, a world set inside the space of Padma's death-chamber _(actors: Padma, Vidūratha, the second Līlā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/lila-story-yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha III ~36–41)
- **Padmā's curse fixes dharma's yuga-decline** — enraged, Padmā curses the disguised seducer to lose a leg each age — four legs in Kṛta, one in Kali, restored in the next Kṛta — then, learning she has cursed Dharma himself, refines but keeps the curse _(actors: Padmā, Dharma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/padma-pippalada.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Pārvatī-khaṇḍa 35; cf. Manusmṛti 1.81–86)
- **Panjurli raised on Kailāsa and exiled to guard the fields** — an orphaned wild-boar piglet is reared by Pārvatī in Śiva's household, ravages the divine garden, and is cast to Earth by Śiva as a guardian-daiva of crops and justice _(actors: Panjurli, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/panjurli-guliga-tulu-daivas.md) — (Panjurli paddana) [folk]
- **Pariyalūr — the wrecking of Dakṣa's sacrifice** — the feat-shrine of the Dakṣa-yajña destruction, where Vīrabhadra sprung from Śiva's wrath beheads the Prajāpati Dakṣa _(actors: Śiva, Vīrabhadra, Dakṣa, Satī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-virattanam-eight-feat-shrines.md) — (Bhāgavata IV; Śiva Purāṇa Satī-khaṇḍa)
- **Pārvatī becomes the Śabarī huntress** — the Goddess takes the guise of a dark tribal huntress with bow and quiver to seduce her ascetic husband back _(actors: Pārvatī, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shiva-parvati-dice-game.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa 35)
- **Pārvatī curses Puṣpadanta and Mālyavān to mortal births** — furious at the leak of her 'unheard' tale, Pārvatī curses the eavesdropper and his intercessor, to be freed only when the Great Tale passes through the piśāca Kāṇabhūti and is published to the world _(actors: Pārvatī, Puṣpadanta, Mālyavān, Kāṇabhūti)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/brihatkatha-kathasaritsagara-and-vikramaditya-cycle.md) — (Kathāsaritsāgara, I.1)
- **Pārvatī receives Upamanyu as a son** — The Goddess takes the milk-fed boy-devotee into Śiva's own household and gaṇa-circle _(actors: Pārvatī, Upamanyu, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/upamanyu.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Pārvatī's curse on the plantain-grove boar** — variant paddana in which Śiva creates the boar-being and Pārvatī curses it for destroying her kadaḷi (plantain) grove, softening the curse into a commission to be born on Earth as a redeeming spirit _(actors: Panjurli, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/panjurli-guliga-tulu-daivas.md) — (Panjurli paddana, variant) [folk]
- **Pārvatī's marriage-prophecy** — the goddess blesses Aśokasundarī to wed the Indra-like Lunar king Nahuṣa who will rule the three worlds, a prophecy that marks both bride and groom for the demon Huṇḍa's schemes _(actors: Pārvatī, Aśokasundarī, Nahuṣa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ashokasundari-nahusha-hunda.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Bhūmi-khaṇḍa 103–104)
- **Pārvatī's play-ball slays Vidala and Utpala** — understanding Śiva's signal, the Goddess strikes both disguised demons at once with her kanduka and they drop dead like ripe palmyra fruit, the ball becoming the Kandukeśvara liṅga at Kāśī _(actors: Pārvatī, Vidala, Utpala)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yuddha-khanda-minor-vadhas.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Yuddha-khaṇḍa 59)
- **Pārvatī's tapas, Madana-dahana, and the god-marriage** — the goddess reborn as Himavān's daughter wins the mourning Śiva by austerity; Kāma is burned to ash and the ideal marriage is sealed _(actors: Pārvatī, Śiva, Kāma, Himavān)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/swasthani-brata-katha.md) — (Svasthānī Vrata Kathā, Śaiva chapters)
