- **Pippalāda seizes Padmā by threat of ash** — the aged sage Pippalāda, inflamed after meeting a lustful Gandharva, demands King Anaraṇya's daughter Padmā and threatens to burn all to ashes; on his preceptor's counsel the king surrenders her in marriage _(actors: Pippalāda, Anaraṇya, Padmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/padma-pippalada.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Pārvatī-khaṇḍa 34)
- **Pitṛvartti reborn as King Brahmadatta of Kāmpilya** — the most pious of the seven returns as the yogin-king Brahmadatta, son of Aṇuha and Kṛtvī (Śuka's daughter), who understands the speech of all creatures _(actors: Brahmadatta, Aṇuha, Kṛtvī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pitrikalpa-seven-brahmins-brahmadatta.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Pitṛ-kalpa 24; Matsya Purāṇa 21)
- **Pottan Theyyam rebukes Śaṅkara** — Śiva appears as the Pulaya "Pottan" and blocks Śaṅkarācārya's path, defeating him with the anti-caste argument that the same blood flows from high-born and low-born alike, and is ordained to be worshipped in that casteless form _(actors: Śiva, Pottan, Śaṅkarācārya, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/theyyam-deities-and-thottam-myths.md) — (Pottan Theyyam thoṭṭam) [folk]
- **Prabhāvadatta wins the Nāgara-mantra** — harried by the serpent-folk of Pātāla, the brahmin Prabhāvadatta does penance to Śiva and is granted the Nāgara-mantra that subdues the Nāgas, securing Camatkārapura for the brahmins _(actors: Prabhāvadatta, Śiva, the Nāgas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vadnagar-hatakeshvara-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Nāgara-khaṇḍa 163)
- **Prajāpati becomes Mṛgaśiras** — pierced, the Creator is fixed in the heavens as Mṛga, the deer's-head constellation (Orion), while the hunter Mṛgavyādha (Sirius), the doe Rohiṇī, and the three-barbed arrow (Orion's Belt) complete the sky-picture _(actors: Prajāpati, Rudra as Mṛgavyādha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/prajapati-ushas-rudra-piercing.md) — (Aitareya Brāhmaṇa 3.33)
- **Pratardana the born-avenger** — Bharadvāja's putrakāmeṣṭi yields Pratardana, who matures supernaturally fast, leads Kāśī's army back, and exterminates Vītahavya's hundred sons to the last man _(actors: Divodāsa, Bharadvāja, Pratardana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matanga-vitahavya-varna-upakhyanas.md) — (Anuśāsana Parva ~30)
- **Prince Hemacūḍa marries the sage-reared Hemalekhā** — separated on a hunt and sheltering in Vyāghrapada's hermitage, the Daśārṇa prince Hemacūḍa weds Hemalekhā, foundling daughter of the apsaras Vidyullekhā and already established in Self-knowledge _(actors: Hemacūḍa, Hemalekhā, Vyāghrapada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tripura-rahasya.md) — (Tripurā Rahasya, Jñāna-khaṇḍa)
- **Queen Līlā's journey through nested worlds** — Guided by Sarasvatī, Queen Līlā discovers her whole royal universe exists inside a village brahmin's hut-space, world nested within world with time running at different rates _(actors: Līlā, Sarasvatī, Padma/Vidūratha)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha, Utpatti prakaraṇa)
- **Raden Kusuma given to the Bromo crater (Tengger founding legend)** — Roro Anteng and Joko Seger's prayed-for twenty-five children are granted on condition the youngest returns to the volcano, redeemed yearly at Yadnya Kasada [oral tradition] [regional] _(actors: Roro Anteng, Joko Seger, Raden Kusuma)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/balinese-hinduism-agama-hindu-dharma.md) — (Tengger oral tradition)
- **Rādhāṣṭamī** — Rādhā's appearance day on Bhādrapada śukla aṣṭamī, a fortnight after Janmāṣṭamī, celebrated grandest at Barsānā with the once-yearly caraṇa-darśana of her feet at Vṛndāvana temples _(actors: Rādhā, Braj devotees)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/radha.md) — (Braj/Vaiṣṇava festival calendar) [regional]
- **Raktabhoja the blood-devourer** — Jyotibā destroys Raktabhoja, a Deccan reflex of the Devī Māhātmya's Raktabīja whose blood-drops spawn clones, freeing the Karavīra country _(actors: Jyotibā, Raktabhoja)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jyotiba-kedarling-kolhapur.md) — (Kedāra Vijaya; cf. Devī Māhātmya)
- **Rantideva's supreme annadāna** — After 48 days of famine the king gives his last food and water to a Brāhmaṇa, an outcaste, and a dog-keeper with his dogs — the gods in disguise — seeking only to relieve all beings' suffering _(actors: Rantideva, Viṣṇu-Brahmā-Śiva disguised)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/anna-naivedya-and-prasada.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.21)
- **Rāvaḷnāth and Betāḷ guard the village** — the fierce Bhairava-type tutelaries — Rāvaḷnāth the horseman-protector and Betāḷ the naked lord of ghost-hosts offered new sandals worn out by night-walking — patrol the Goan grāma bounds _(actors: Rāvaḷnāth, Betāḷ/Vetāḷa, the bhūta-gaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/goa-shantadurga-mangeshi-kuladevatas.md) — (gāonkārī tradition)
- **Ṛbhu and Nidāgha become the stock non-dual guru–disciple pair** — The pair migrate from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa parable into later Advaita literature — the Śaiva Ribhu-Gītā of the Śivarahasya Purāṇa and the Tejobindu, Varāha, and Annapūrṇā Upaniṣads _(actors: Ṛbhu, Nidāgha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ribhu-nidagha-vishnu-purana.md) — (Śivarahasya Purāṇa; Tejobindu Upaniṣad)
- **Rebha drawn from the waters** — the Aśvins lift the wounded, bound Rebha, hidden in water for ten nights and nine days, and revive him like soma pressed from the vat _(actors: Aśvins, Rebha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ashvins.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.116.24; 10.39.9)
- **Rebirth as Dama, lamp-king of Kaliṅga** — By the Śivarātri merit Guṇanidhi is reborn as Dama, son of Arindama and king of Kaliṅga, who has lamps kept burning in every Śiva shrine of his realm _(actors: Dama (Guṇanidhi), Arindama, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gunanidhi-kubera.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṁhitā, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 18)
- **Reborn as cakravāka birds on the Mānasa lake** — as ruddy geese renamed Sumanas, Vasu, Citradarśin and the rest, they recite aloud the verse of their past births, watching King Aṇuha disport in worldly splendour _(actors: the seven cakravāka birds, King Aṇuha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pitrikalpa-seven-brahmins-brahmadatta.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Pitṛ-kalpa 23; Shiva Purāṇa 42)
- **Reborn as deer on Kālañjara** — the memory-keeping souls take deer bodies, remain indifferent to the herd, and fast to death at the Prapatana cliff _(actors: the seven deer)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pitrikalpa-seven-brahmins-brahmadatta.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Pitṛ-kalpa 22)
- **Reborn as hunters of Daśārṇa** — the seven return as fowlers on Mount Kālañjara, and sickened by the killing life yet remembering all, they cast off their bodies by yoga _(actors: the seven hunters)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pitrikalpa-seven-brahmins-brahmadatta.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Pitṛ-kalpa 22; Shiva Purāṇa, Umā-saṃhitā 41)
- **Recognition and release** — Brahmadatta and his companions Subālaka and Puṇḍarīka recognise one another through the verse, complete the yoga begun in the hunter-birth, and the memory-bearing souls attain liberation _(actors: Brahmadatta, Subālaka, Puṇḍarīka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pitrikalpa-seven-brahmins-brahmadatta.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Pitṛ-kalpa 24; Shiva Purāṇa 42)
- **Reconciliation and Śiva's coronation** — awakened from his delusion by Nārada, Śiva pardons Pārvatī and is re-enthroned at Gandhamādana by Aṅgiras and the sages _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Nārada, Aṅgiras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shiva-parvati-dice-game.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa 35)
- **Renunciation and the vow of truth** — broken by ridicule, Utathya withdraws to the Ganges bank, builds a hermitage, and takes a strict vow of truth (satya-vrata) — earning the name Satyavrata _(actors: Utathya/Satyavrata)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/satyavrata-aim-bija.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa III.10)
- **Rescue of Madālasā in Purandarapura** — in the subterranean city the prince finds Madālasā, gandharva-king Viśvāvasu's daughter, held captive, and her companion Kuṇḍalā reports Surabhi's prophecy that her rescuer shall wed her _(actors: Kuvalayāśva, Madālasā, Kuṇḍalā, Surabhi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kuvalayashva-madalasa.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa, Canto XXI)
- **Restored by the witness of the land** — the moment the eldest creature affirms his deeds endure, a celestial car descends and Indradyumna is carried back to the heavenly worlds _(actors: Indradyumna, Akūpāra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indradyumna-akupara-oldest-beings.md) — (Mahābhārata 3.191 / Ganguli Vana CXCIX)
- **Restored to Nārada in a moment** — the second bath dissolves the spell: the woman, marriage, sons, and grief all vanish, Nārada stands as himself again, and Viṣṇu smiles on the bank where only a moment has passed _(actors: Nārada, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-maya-taladhvaja.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa VI.31)
- **Return to earth on the horse Kuvalaya** — reunited with his resurrected wife, Kuvalayāśva takes Madālasā up from the nāga-world and rides in splendour back to his own city, closing the netherworld arc _(actors: Kuvalayāśva, Madālasā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kuvalayashva-madalasa.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa, Canto XXIV)
- **Revatī and Kakudmī's return across the ages** — waiting through one Gandharva song in Brahmaloka, twenty-seven catur-yugas pass on earth, and Brahmā directs the out-of-time princess Revatī to wed Balarāma _(actors: Kakudmī, Revatī, Brahmā, Balarāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/balarama.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 9.3; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 4.1)
- **Revelation and the liberated reign** — Cūḍālā reveals herself; Śikhidhvaja salutes her as his guru, and the two return to rule Mālava as jīvanmukta king and queen for ten thousand years before final liberation _(actors: Cūḍālā, Śikhidhvaja)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/chudala-shikhidhvaja.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha VI.1, ~chs. 109–110)
- **Ṛṣabha and the gaunt sage Tanu** — the emaciated ascetic Tanu teaches the royal sage Ṛṣabha that hope (āśā) is thinner than his own wasted frame and the hardest thing to slay _(actors: Ṛṣabha, Tanu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shanti-parva-minor-upakhyanas.md) — (MBh, Śānti Parva 12.125–126)
- **Rukmāṅgada tested by Mohinī** — the Vidiśā king's realm-wide Ekādaśī enforcement empties Yama's hells; Brahmā's mind-born temptress Mohinī demands he break the fast or behead his son Dharmāṅgada, and Viṣṇu intervenes at the sword-fall _(actors: Rukmāṅgada, Mohinī, Dharmāṅgada, Sandhyāvalī, Viṣṇu, Brahmā, Yama)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ekadashi-mahatmyas-catalog.md) — (Nārada Purāṇa, Uttarabhāga — Rukmāṅgada-carita) [late text]
- **Śākambharī feeds the world** — the Goddess brings forth vegetables, greens, roots and fruit from her own body to feed a famished world until the rains return, earning the name Śākambharī, the Herb-Bearer _(actors: the Devī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shakambhari-shatakshi-durgamasura.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.28)
- **Śaktideva finds the Golden City of Kanakapurī** — to win a princess who will wed only one who has seen the golden Vidyādhara city, Śaktideva survives being swallowed by a giant fish, reaches Kanakapurī, weds four cursed princesses, and is renamed Śaktivega _(actors: Śaktideva/Śaktivega, Kanakarekhā, king Śaśikhaṇḍa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kathasaritsagara-famous-tales.md) — (Kathāsaritsāgara V, Caturdārikā)
- **Śambara conjures Dāma-Vyāla-Kaṭa and Bhīma-Bhāsa-Dṛḍha** — Three conjured demons are invincible while ego-less and fall once self-identity breeds death-fear; their knowing successors fight untouched — egohood itself is the vulnerability _(actors: Śambara, Dāma, Vyāla, Kaṭa, the devas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yoga-vasishtha-tales-catalog.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha IV.25–34)
- **Sandhyāvalī frees Kāṣṭhīlā the wood-worm with Māgha merit** — The queen rescues a talking worm (once a brāhmaṇa's wife who refused her husband her wealth) from a crow and deeds her one-fourth of her Māgha-observance merit, sending her by aerial chariot to Viṣṇu's region _(actors: Sandhyāvalī, Kāṣṭhīlā, her brāhmaṇa husband)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-purana-ganga-tales.md) — (Nārada Purāṇa, Uttarabhāga 27–31)
- **Śaṅkara receives the Ānanda-laharī on Kailāsa** — Ascending Mount Kailāsa, Ādi Śaṅkara obtains the first 41 verses (Ānanda-laharī) from Śiva himself, the half held to be revealed rather than authored _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara, Śiva, Nandin (Nandikeśvara), Gaṇeśa/Pārvatī (variant))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/soundarya-lahari.md) — (Saundarya-laharī; Śrīvidyā hagiography)
- **Ashtavakra Curses His Father from the Womb** — From the womb the unborn Ashtavakra corrects his father Kahoda's faulty Vedic recitation, and the wounded father curses him to be born bent in eight places _(actors: Ashtavakra, Kahoda, Sujata)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/ashtavakra.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva CXXXII)
- **Ashvapati's eighteen-year penance and the goddess's boon** — The childless king Ashvapati of Madra propitiates the goddess Savitri with eighteen years of austerity, and she grants him a single radiant daughter named Savitri _(actors: Ashvapati, goddess Savitri, Savitri)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/savitri-satyavan.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva 291)
- **Avadhūta-Gītā taught by Dattātreya** — Dattātreya, the avadhūta-avatāra, declares the ~289-verse, 8-chapter Song of the Free Soul to a disciple as eternal brahma-vidyā with no fixed yuga-date _(actors: Dattātreya (speaker); a disciple (traditionally Kārtika / Svāmī Kārttikeya, or unnamed seeker))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/avadhuta-gita.md) — (Avadhūta Gītā (attributed to Dattātreya))
- **Aśvapati's penance and the boon of Sāvitrī** — The childless king Aśvapati's eighteen-year austerity wins from goddess Sāvitrī a radiant daughter named Sāvitrī _(actors: Aśvapati, goddess Sāvitrī, Sāvitrī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/savitri-satyavan.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva 291)
- **Bhrgu Tests the Trimurti and Kicks Vishnu** — Sage Bhrgu, sent to test which of the Trimurti is supreme, kicks Vishnu on the chest; Vishnu humbly massages the offending foot, crushing Bhrgu's eye of ego _(actors: Bhrgu, Vishnu, Lakshmi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Venkatacala-Mahatmya)
- **Ṛbhu instructs Nidāgha on non-duality** — The sage Ṛbhu delivers a vast ~2,200-verse, 44-chapter Advaita discourse to his disciple Nidāgha, teaching that Śiva, Brahman, and the Self are one and the world is unreal _(actors: Ṛbhu (Ṛibhu), Nidāgha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ribhu-gita.md) — (Śivarahasya Purāṇa (Ribhu-Gītā section, aṃśa 6))
- **Birth and swelling of the cosmic being** — A formless being is born (from Shiva's sweat in the Andhaka battle, or unbidden) and swells until it threatens to blot out the worlds _(actors: the proto-Vastu-purusha, Shiva, the asura Andhaka)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/vastu-shastra-and-the-vastu-purusha-mandala.md) — (Matsya Purana; Brihat Samhita 52)
- **Birth from the cremation ground** — Sudalaimāḍaṉ is born as a son of Śiva and Pārvatī from or into the burning-ground, taking the cuṭalai as his domain _(actors: Sudalaimāḍaṉ, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/sudalaimadan.md) — (Sudalaimāḍaṉ kodai oral tradition; Tamil villuppāṭṭu ballad)
- **Birth of Bharadvaja from Brihaspati and Mamata** — Bharadvaja is born of Brihaspati's union with Mamata (wife of Utathya); rejected by the womb-child Dirghatamas, the seed grows into a separate boy named via the folk-etymology 'bhara dva-jam' _(actors: Brihaspati, Mamata, Utathya, Dirghatamas (in womb), the Maruts)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharadvaja.md) — (Mahabharata Adi Parva; Bhagavata Purana 9.20)
- **Birth of Bhaṇḍāsura from the ash of Kāmadeva** — Citrakarman molds the ash of Śiva-burnt Kāmadeva into a figure that Śiva's glance brings to life; the gaṇas cry 'bhaṇḍa!' and name him Bhaṇḍāsura _(actors: Śiva, Citrakarman, Kāmadeva (as ash), the gaṇas, Bhaṇḍāsura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna))
- **Birth of Durvāsā as the Śiva-portion at Atri's hermitage** — Durvāsā, the irascible Rudra-natured sage, is born as the aṃśa (portion) of Śiva to the Saptarṣi Atri and the chaste Anasūyā, alongside his brothers Soma/Candra (Brahmā-portion) and Dattātreya (Viṣṇu-portion) _(actors: Durvāsā, Atri, Anasūyā, Soma/Candra, Dattātreya, the Trimūrti)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ambarisha-durvasa.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 4.1; Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa (Anasūyā cycle))
- **Bāṇāsura's boon of invincibility** — The demon Bāṇāsura obtains a boon that he can be slain only by a virgin maiden (kanyā), forcing the gods to need an unwed Goddess _(actors: Bāṇāsura; the devas; (boon-grantor))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-kanyakumari.md) — (Sthala-purāṇa of Kanyākumārī)
- **Childless Cāraṇ couple and the penance** — The scorned childless Cāraṇ Mamaḍiyā and Devaḷ do severe penance and are granted that goddesses will be born as their daughters _(actors: Mamaḍiyā (Mamaiyā Gaḍhvī), Devaḷ, Śiva/Devī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khodiyar-mata.md) — (Cāraṇ folk tradition (Khodiyār sākhī))
- **Citrasutra revealed (Markandeya to king Vajra)** — The sage Markandeya instructs king Vajra in the art of painting, the dialogue that forms the Citrasutra of the Vishnudharmottara Purana, the foundational treatise on citra-kala _(actors: Markandeya, king Vajra)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/chitra-kala-painting-traditions.md) — (Vishnudharmottara Purana III.35-43 (Citrasutra))
- **Cākṣuṣī-mantra transmission through Viśvāvasu** — The Cākṣuṣī-mantra of supernatural sight, devised by Manu, passes down the gandharva line Soma → Viśvāvasu → Chitraratha, who finally gifts it to Arjuna _(actors: Soma; Viśvāvasu; Chitraratha; Manu; Arjuna)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/gandharvas-and-kinnaras.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva, Caitrarathaparva)
- **Composition of the body from the five elements** — The Garbha Upaniṣad teaches that the human body is built of earth, water, fire, air and space, with a fivefold function in each element _(actors: Pippalāda (traditional teacher); the human body)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/garbha-upanishad.md) — (Garbha Upaniṣad 1; Muktikā Upaniṣad)
- **Daksha gives his 27 nakshatra-daughters to Candra** — Daksha Prajapati marries all twenty-seven of his nakshatra-daughters to the Moon-god Candra on the condition he treat them equally, personifying the lunar mansions _(actors: Daksha, Candra, the 27 nakshatra-daughters)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/nakshatras.md) — (Vishnu Purana; Bhagavata Purana 6.6)
- **Daksha's curse on Candra** — Daksha curses the Moon-god Soma to waste away for neglecting his other 26 wives in favor of Rohini _(actors: Soma/Candra, Daksha, Rohini, the 27 nakshatra-daughters)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/prabhasa-somnath-tirtha.md) — (Skanda Purana Prabhasa-khanda; Shiva Purana)
- **Devī manifests and Himavat questions her** — After the cosmos longs for the Goddess and Himavat propitiates her, the Devī appears in dazzling form and Himavat asks her to reveal her true nature and the means of liberation _(actors: Bhuvaneśvarī (Mahādevī), Himavat (Himālaya), the gods)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/devi-gita.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.31–32)
- **Dānava birth of Maya** — Maya is born a Dānava, son of Danu and Kaśyapa, brother of Namuci, in the asura genealogy _(actors: Maya, Danu, Kaśyapa, Namuci)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/maya-danava.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva; Purāṇic Dānava-lists)
- **Dream-revelation to Budha-Kauśika** — Śiva appears to the sage Budha-Kauśika in a dream and dictates the Rāma-rakṣā Stotra, which the sage records on waking _(actors: Śiva, Budha-Kauśika (ṛṣi))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/ramaraksha-stotra.md) — (Rāma-rakṣā Stotra (phala-śruti / colophon); devotional dream-tradition)
- **Durga formed from the gods' pooled tejas** — The combined radiance of the gods coalesces into the supreme Goddess Durga/Candika to confront the asura host _(actors: Durgā/Caṇḍikā, the Trimūrti and devas, Mahiṣa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/mahishasura-mardini-stotra.md) — (Devī Māhātmya (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa); Mahiṣāsura-mardinī stotra)
- **Durvasa's curse strips the devas of fortune** — Durvasa gifts Indra a divine garland; Indra carelessly lets Airavata trample it, and the insulted sage curses the devas to lose all sri, weakening them _(actors: Durvasa, Indra, Airavata)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/samudra-manthana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 8.5; Vishnu Purana 1.9)
- **Durvāsas gifts a garland to Indra** — The sage Durvāsas offers Indra a never-fading celestial garland, which Indra, seated on Airāvata, carelessly places on the elephant's head _(actors: Durvāsas, Indra, Airāvata)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/airavata-and-celestial-animals.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8.5–8.6; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.9)
- **Emanation and stationing of the Aṣṭa-Bhairava as directional kṣetrapālas** — From the supreme Kāla-Bhairava emanate eight kṣetrapāla-Bhairavas — Asitāṅga, Ruru, Caṇḍa, Krodha, Unmatta, Kapāla, Bhīṣaṇa, Saṃhāra — each stationed to guard one of the eight directions, each pairing one-to-one with a Mātṛkā Śakti in the Śrī Chakra and presiding over eight further sub-Bhairavas to yield the sixty-four Bhairavas; they anchor the guardian-circuits of Kāśī and Ujjain _(actors: Kāla-Bhairava (Mahākāla Śiva), the eight Bhairavas, the eight Mātṛkās (Brāhmī, Māheśvarī, Kaumārī, Vaiṣṇavī, Vārāhī, Indrāṇī, Cāmuṇḍā, Mahālakṣmī))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa (Kāśī Khaṇḍa); Rudrayāmala Tantra; Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Enumeration of the pañca-sarovara (five sacred lakes)** — Tradition fixes a closed roster of five supreme lakes — Mānasa, Puṣkara, Bindu, Pampā, Nārāyaṇa — binding far-flung sites into one sacred circuit _(actors: Hindu sthala-māhātmya / Purāṇic tradition)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-sarovar-sacred-lakes.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa; regional sthala-māhātmya lists)
- **Establishment of the Ashta Svayam-vyakta class** — Padma and Garuda Puranas fix the closed set of eight self-disclosed Vishnu icons as the apex tier of the Pancharatra five-fold arca-grading _(actors: Vishnu; Pancharatra Agama tradition; Padma & Garuda Purana redactors)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-svayamvyakta-vishnu-kshetras.md) — (Padma Purana (Patala Khanda); Garuda Purana; Pancharatra Agama; Divya Suri Carita)
- **Gautama as Saptarṣi and Gautama-gotra founder** — Gautama is reckoned a Saptarṣi of the present Vaivasvata Manvantara and eponymous ancestor of the Gautama (Gotama) gotra, a Vedic seer whose ṛṣi-status frames the whole episode _(actors: Gautama, Brahmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/ahalya.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa; Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa Saptarṣi lists; Ṛgveda Gotama hymns)
- **Gayāsura's Penance** — The colossal asura Gaya performs terrible austerities until his body becomes so pure that the mere sight or touch of him sends beings straight to Vaikuṇṭha _(actors: Gayāsura)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/gaya-pitru-tirtha.md) — (Vayu Purana, Gaya-mahatmya; Agni Purana)
- **Generation of Murugan from Shiva's Six Sparks** — To produce a deva commander able to slay Surapadma, Murugan is generated from Shiva's six fiery sparks, nursed by the Krittikas and fused into the six-faced Shanmukha _(actors: Shiva, Krittikas, Murugan)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Kanda Puranam; Skanda Purana)
- **Ghoṣā healed by the Aśvins** — The skin-afflicted, long-unmarried seer Ghoṣā praises the physician-twins the Aśvins, who restore her health and youth so she can wed _(actors: Ghoṣā Kākṣīvatī, the Aśvins, Kakṣīvat (father))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/rigvedic-women-seers-brahmavadinis.md) — (Ṛgveda 10.39–40)
- **Ghṛtācī bears the ten sons of Raudrāśva** — The Paurava king Raudrāśva/Rudrāśva unites with the apsarā Ghṛtācī and begets ten sons (the -eyu brothers) from whom the Paurava–Bhārata dynastic stem descends through Matināra toward Saṃvaraṇa, Kuru, and ultimately the Kauravas and Pāṇḍavas _(actors: Ghṛtācī, Raudrāśva/Rudrāśva, the ten -eyu sons)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/apsaras.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa IV.19; Bhāgavata Purāṇa IX.20)
- **Hayagrīva narrates the Lalitā lore to Agastya** — In the Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa's Lalitopākhyāna, the horse-headed Viṣṇu-form Hayagrīva narrates the Lalitā Sahasranāma and Triśatī to the ṛṣi Agastya, the charter-myth of Śrīvidyā transmission _(actors: Hayagrīva, Agastya, Lopāmudrā)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shakta-and-tantra-lineages.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa)
- **Hālāsya founding & the 64 Tiruviḷaiyāḍal** — Śiva-Sundareśvara consecrates Madurā with falling nectar and founds the lotus-plan city in the Kaḍamba forest, the setting of his sixty-four sacred sports _(actors: Śiva (Sundareśvara), Pāṇḍya Kulaśekhara)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/madurai-meenakshi-sundareshwara-kshetra.md) — (Hālāsya Māhātmya; Tiruviḷaiyāḍal Purāṇam (Paranjyoti))
- **Huhū Cursed to Become the Crocodile (Gajendra-mokṣa)** — The gandharva Huhū pulls sage Devala's leg while sporting in water and is cursed to become the crocodile that seizes the elephant Gajendra, released only when Viṣṇu liberates Gajendra _(actors: Huhū, Devala, Gajendra, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/gandharvas-and-kinnaras.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8.2-4)
- **Indra slays Viśvarūpa Triśiras** — Fearing the three-headed Viśvarūpa's divided loyalty, Indra beheads him, committing brahmahatyā; from the severed heads spring birds _(actors: Indra, Viśvarūpa, Tvaṣṭṛ)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vishvakarma-and-tvashtar.md) — (Taittirīya Saṃhitā 2.5.1; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 6.9)
- **Indra slays Vritra** — Fortified by soma, Indra hurls the vajra forged by Tvashtr and smashes the serpent Vritra, releasing the captive cosmic waters and ordering the cosmos _(actors: Indra, Vritra, Tvashtr)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/indra.md) — (Rigveda 1.32)
- **Indra Slays Vritra (Vritrahatya)** — Indra wields the vajra to slay the serpent-dragon Vritra who had dammed the cosmic waters, releasing the seven rivers — the central Vedic act of cosmic order over chaos _(actors: Indra, Vritra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedic-deities-and-suktas.md) — (Rigveda 1.32)
- **Indra Slays Vṛtra and Flees** — After killing the brāhmaṇa-born Vṛtra, Indra is overtaken by brahmahatyā and hides in a lotus-stalk, leaving heaven kingless _(actors: Indra, Vṛtra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nahusha.md) — (Mahabharata Udyoga Parva 9-11; Bhagavata Purana 6.13)
- **Indra teaches Dadhyañc the Madhu-vidyā** — Indra imparts the esoteric honey-doctrine (Madhu-vidyā) to Dadhyañc, binding it with a curse that his head will be struck off if he teaches it to anyone _(actors: Indra, Dadhyañc)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dadhichi.md) — (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2.5; Ṛgveda 1.116–117)
- **Śaṅkukarṇa the merchant-serpent yields his hoard** — a miser reborn a serpent guarding his buried treasure is restored to human form and sent to Viṣṇu-loka when his sons recite Gītā chapter 7 over the gold _(actors: Śaṅkukarṇa, his sons)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavad-gita/gita-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Gītā-Māhātmya 7)
- **Śāntā-Durgā stills the Hari-Hara war** — when Viṣṇu and Śiva battle to the ruin of the worlds, Brahmā appeals to the Great Goddess, who descends between them and pacifies the fight, earning the name Durgā-of-peace _(actors: Śāntā-Durgā, Viṣṇu, Śiva, Brahmā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/goa-shantadurga-mangeshi-kuladevatas.md) — (Gomāntaka māhātmya / Kavlem tradition)
- **Sārasvata re-teaches the Vedas to sixty thousand ṛṣis** — the returning sages, having forgotten scripture, accept the boy-brahmarṣi as preceptor and relearn the lost Vedas from his unbroken memory _(actors: Sārasvata, 60,000 ṛṣis)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sarasvata-upakhyana-vriddhakumari.md) — (Mbh Śalya Parva 9.51; Ganguli LI)
- **Sarasvatī opens the door of knowledge** — pleased by the accidental seed-syllable, the Goddess makes the fool a seer and poet in an instant, equal to the Muni Vālmīki _(actors: Sarasvatī, Satyavrata)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/satyavrata-aim-bija.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa III.11)
- **Sarasvatī's gift of all sound to the nāgas** — moved by the couple's ruin, the nāga king Aśvatara propitiates Sarasvatī, who grants him and his brother Kambala mastery of every musical note, scale, and poetic art _(actors: Aśvatara, Kambala, Sarasvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kuvalayashva-madalasa.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa, Canto XXIII)
- **Śatadhanu's lapse at the Bhāgīrathī** — after the Kārttika full-moon fast, the king courteously converses with a pāṣaṇḍa (his arms-preceptor's friend) while Śaibyā turns away and gazes at the sun _(actors: Śatadhanu, Śaibyā, the pāṣaṇḍa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shatadhanu-shaibya.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, III.18)
- **Śatākṣī weeps the rains** — invoked by the starving Brāhmaṇas, the Great Goddess manifests covered with a hundred eyes and, weeping torrents from them, ends the drought — whence her name Śatākṣī, the Hundred-Eyed _(actors: the Devī, the Brāhmaṇas)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shakambhari-shatakshi-durgamasura.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.28)
- **Sāterī the anthill-mother** — the aniconic earth-goddess is worshipped in her living termite-mound (roeṇ), the oldest stratum of Goan religion, widely identified with Śāntā-Durgā/Śānterī _(actors: Sāterī/Sānterī, the gāonkār servitors)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/goa-shantadurga-mangeshi-kuladevatas.md) — (gāonkārī tradition)
- **Satī's Eye Falls at Tārāpīṭha** — in the Birbhum reckoning the eye / third eye (nayana-tārā) of Satī falls at Tārāpīṭha as Viṣṇu dismembers her corpse, sanctifying the death-ground pīṭha _(actors: Satī, Śiva, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tarapith-tara-vashishtha-bamakhepa.md) — (Tārāpīṭha sthala-māhātmya) [regional]
- **Satyavrata's Lokāyata bait** — the envious atheist neighbour Satyavrata exploits the grief, arguing that Liṅga-worship caused the losses and that virtue is a fraud to be traded for hedonism _(actors: Satyavrata, Nandabhadra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nandabhadra-tested-faith.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa 45)
- **Sauminī's fall from brahmin girl to outcaste** — a widowed brahmin girl, fallen through lust to a Śūdra husband and cāṇḍāla status, eats meat, drinks wine, and drunkenly kills a calf, and is reborn by Yama as a blind, leprous Cāṇḍāla daughter _(actors: Sauminī, Yama)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kotirudra-minor-linga-legends.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṃhitā 9)
- **Seven sons from the dead king** — obeying the boon, the chaste Bhadrā conceives by her dead husband and bears seven children — three Śalvas and four Madras, eponyms of those peoples _(actors: Bhadrā, Vyuṣitāśva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyushitashva-bhadra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva; Ganguli §CXXI)
- **Shiva appears as Rudra to Nārada at Rudraprayag** — the music-proud sage's penance at the Mandākinī confluence draws Shiva's Rudra form, who graces him in music; a tāṇḍava variant also circulates [folk] _(actors: Nārada, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-prayag-alaknanda-confluences.md) — (Wikipedia, Panch Prayag; Kedāra-khaṇḍa tradition)
- **Sight restored to the blind** — the Aśvins give back sight to Ṛjrāśva (blinded by his father) and Kaṇva, and make the blind-and-lame Parāvṛj see and walk _(actors: Aśvins, Ṛjrāśva, Kaṇva, Parāvṛj)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ashvins.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.116.16; 1.117.17–18)
- **Śikhidhvaja dismisses his enlightened wife** — Asked about her new radiance, Cūḍālā says she has found the Self; the king laughs her off as a prattling child, refusing to believe a wife could be enlightened _(actors: Śikhidhvaja, Cūḍālā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/chudala-shikhidhvaja.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha VI.1, ~ch. 79)
- **Sīmantinī takes the Somavāra-vrata** — warned by astrologers she will be widowed young, princess Sīmantinī is taught the Monday fast of Śiva-and-Gaurī by Maitreyī, wife of Yājñavalkya, and keeps it faithfully _(actors: Sīmantinī, Citravarmā, Maitreyī, Yājñavalkya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/brahmottara-khanda-vrata-tales.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Brāhmottara-khaṇḍa 8)
- **Śiva bursts from the liṅga as the tiger-lord** — on Śivarātri, as a meditating devotee is attacked by the tiger-shaped Daitya, Śiva springs from the worshipped liṅga, pins Dundubhinirhrāda under his armpit and kills him with a fist-blow, then vows to lift the torments of all who see him in that form _(actors: Śiva, Dundubhinirhrāda, the devotee)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yuddha-khanda-minor-vadhas.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Yuddha-khaṇḍa 58)
- **Śiva descends as the Pulaya** — to humble Brahmin pride, Śiva takes the body of a drunken untouchable corpse-bearer (with Pārvatī and Gaṇapati as his family) and walks a forest path _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, the Pulaya form)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/pottan-theyyam.md) — (Poṭṭan thoṭṭam-pāṭṭu)
- **Śiva exiled from Kāśī to Mount Mandara** — Bound by Divodāsa's compact, Śiva must forsake his never-forsaken city Avimukta and pine for it on Mandara with the newly-wed Pārvatī _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Divodāsa)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kashi-line-divodasa.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kāśī-khaṇḍa, Pūrvārdha chs. 43, 45)
- **Śiva flees Kailāsa as a deer into the Śleṣmāntaka forest** — Wearied of duty, Śiva abandons Kailāsa and roams the Bāgmatī valley as an antelope, refusing to return _(actors: Śiva (deer/antelope-form), the devas (Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Indra))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pashupatinath-kathmandu-nepal-kshetra.md) — (Nepāla-māhātmya; Paśupati Purāṇa)
- **Jaimini's four Mahabharata questions referred to the four birds** — The Mimamsa master Jaimini brings four unresolved Mahabharata puzzles to Markandeya, who deflects him to four wise birds whose answers open the Purana _(actors: Jaimini, Markandeya, the four birds)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/markandeya-purana.md) — (Markandeya Purana (chs. 1-9))
- **Kacha and Devayānī's mutual curses** — Kacha steals the revival-mantra from Śukra; refusing to wed Devayānī, the two exchange curses sealing her future marriage to a kṣatriya _(actors: Kacha, Devayānī, Śukrācārya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yayati.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva 71–73)
- **Kadrū–Vinatā wager and the slave-bet** — Kaśyapa's co-wives Kadrū and Vinatā bet on the horse Uccaiḥśravas; Kadrū wins by cheating and enslaves Vinatā _(actors: Kadrū, Vinatā, Kaśyapa, Uccaiḥśravas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/astika-sarpa-satra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva (Āstīka) 14–20)
- **Kama burnt to ash by Shiva's third eye** — Sent to break Shiva's meditation and kindle desire for Parvati, Kama looses an arrow and Shiva opens his third eye to reduce him to ash, whence the name Ananga _(actors: Kama, Shiva, Parvati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/kamadeva-and-minor-deities.md) — (Shiva Purana; Matsya Purana)
- **Kāmākṣī's Penance and Marriage to Śiva (Ekāmra Legend)** — At Kāñcī, Kāmākṣī fashions a sand Śiva-liṅga under a mango tree and shields it with her body as the Vegavati floods to test her; Śiva, won, takes her as bride _(actors: Kāmākṣī, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanchipuram-city-of-temples.md) — (sthala-purana; Wikipedia, Ekambareswarar Temple)
- **Kojagari Lakshmi Puja — 'Ko jagarti?'** — Lakshmi roams the Sharad full-moon night asking 'who is awake?' and blesses the wakeful with wealth, kept as the Kojagara/Kaumudi vrata of nightlong vigil _(actors: Lakshmi; devotees keeping vigil (jagarana); regional: Kartikeya/Kumara (Odisha), Indra-Indrani)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sharad-purnima-kojagari-lakshmi-puja.md) — (Skanda Purana; Brahmavaivarta Purana (Kojagara/Kaumudi vrata); regional Bengal/Mithila/Odisha tradition)
- **Kūrma bears Mount Mandara at the Samudra-manthana** — Vishnu becomes the tortoise and dives beneath the sinking Mount Mandara to serve as the churning-pivot for the ocean of milk, the cosmogonic deed Śrīkūrmam localizes _(actors: Vishnu (Kūrma), devas and asuras, Mount Mandara)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/avatara-kshetras-srikurmam-srimushnam.md) — (Kūrma Purāṇa; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8; Mahābhārata Ādi-parva; Śrīkūrma-māhātmya)
- **Kārttikeya departs to Śrīśaila after the Gaṇeśa marriage-contest** — The boy Skanda, slighted when Gaṇeśa wins the marriage-precedence contest, leaves Kailāsa in pique and settles as an ascetic on the southern hill _(actors: Kārttikeya/Skanda, Gaṇeśa, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srisailam-srisaila-mallikarjuna-bhramaramba.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa (Śrīśaila-khaṇḍa); Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Kāverī born as Brahmā's daughter / Lopāmudrā, raised by Kavera** — The river-goddess Kāverī (Kāveramma), daughter of Brahmā and identified with Lopāmudrā, is fostered by the sage-king Kavera at Brahmagiri _(actors: Brahmā, Kāverī/Kāveramma, Kavera, Lopāmudrā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sacred-rivers-and-descent-myths.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kāverī-māhātmya / Agastya-saṃhitā)
- **Lalitā Tripura-Sundarī arises and slays Bhaṇḍāsura** — Bhaṇḍāsura, formed from the ashes of Kāmadeva, conquers the worlds; the gods' fire-offering produces Lalitā Tripura-Sundarī from the Cidagni-kuṇḍa, who weds Kāmeśvara, founds Śrī-Nagara, and annihilates Bhaṇḍa and his city Śūnyaka _(actors: Lalitā Tripura-Sundarī, Bhaṇḍāsura, Kāmeśvara/Śiva, Viśukra, Viśaṅga)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahmanda-purana.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna))
- **Liṅgodbhava — Śiva's infinite pillar of fire** — Disputing Brahmā and Viṣṇu are confronted by Śiva manifesting as a boundless column of fire whose ends neither can find _(actors: Śiva (as jyotir-liṅga), Brahmā, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arunachala-tiruvannamalai-fire-hill.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa (Liṅgodbhava); Aruṇācala-māhātmya (Skanda Purāṇa))
- **Mahakala Bursts Forth to Slay Dushana** — When the demon Dushana torments Avanti, the devotee-king Candrasena (or boy Shrikara) cries out, and Shiva bursts from the earth as Mahakala, incinerates the demon, and stays forever as the jyotirlinga _(actors: Shiva-Mahakala, Dushana, Candrasena, Shrikara)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ujjain-avantika.md) — (Skanda Purana, Avantya-khanda; Shiva Purana)
- **Mahakali slays Madhu-Kaitabha** — As the tamasika aspect, the Goddess slays the demons Madhu and Kaitabha in the first carita of the Devi Mahatmya _(actors: Mahakali, Madhu, Kaitabha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 1; Devi-Bhagavata Purana)
- **Mātṛkās Emanate to Fight Śumbha-Niśumbha** — As Durgā battles the asura generals, the Sapta-Mātṛkās burst forth as śaktis from the bodies of the gods and join the Goddess to annihilate the demon host, re-merging into her at the end _(actors: Mātṛkās, Durgā/Caṇḍikā)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/matrikas-and-64-yoginis.md) — (Devī Māhātmya / Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa 8, 10)
- **Mudgala Purana Eight Incarnations of Ganesha** — The Mudgala Purana narrates Ganesha's eight avataras (Vakratunda through Dhumravarna), each slaying a demon personifying an inner vice such as jealousy, pride, greed, anger, lust, and ego _(actors: Ganesha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashtavinayaka.md) — (Mudgala Purana)
- **Murugan slays Surapadma with the Vel** — Parvati gives Murugan (Kartikeya/Skanda) the divine Vel lance, with which he slays the asura Surapadma/Soorapadman at Tiruchendur; commemorated by Thaipusam on the Pushya nakshatra of Tamil Thai, with penitential kavadi-bearing and vel-piercing _(actors: Parvati, Murugan/Kartikeya, Surapadma/Soorapadman)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Skanda/Tamil Murugan tradition; Wikipedia (Thaipusam))
- **Ānanda Tāṇḍava in the Kanaka/Poṟ Sabhai (Chidambaram)** — Śiva dances the Dance of Bliss in the golden hall at Thillai for the sages Patañjali and Vyāghrapāda, enacting the five cosmic acts (pañca-kṛtya) _(actors: Śiva-Naṭarāja, Patañjali, Vyāghrapāda)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancha-sabhai-nataraja-dance-halls.md) — (Chidambara Māhātmya; Tirumantiram; Tēvāram)
- **Nandi's dual identity (bull vs. gaṇa-lord)** — Tradition carries two figures under one name — Nandī the bull-mount and Nandikeśvara the anthropomorphic bull-faced gaṇa-chieftain — never fully merged _(actors: Nandi/Nandikeśvara, Śiva)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nandi-and-shaiva-mounts.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa; Liṅga Purāṇa; Śaiva Āgamas; temple tradition)
- **Nara–Nārāyaṇa take up eternal tapas at Badarikāśrama** — Viṣṇu descends as the twin sages Nara and Nārāyaṇa, sons of Dharma, performing perpetual penance between the Nara and Nārāyaṇa peaks on the Alaknandā _(actors: Nara, Nārāyaṇa (Viṣṇu), Dharma, Mūrti/Ahiṃsā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/badrinath-badarikashrama-kshetra.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 1.3, 4.1; Viṣṇu Purāṇa; Mahābhārata)
- **Neminātha / Ariṣṭanemi renounces on his wedding day** — The 22nd Tīrthaṅkara Neminātha (Ariṣṭanemi), a Yādava cousin of Vāsudeva Kṛṣṇa, renounces the world on his wedding day on hearing the cries of animals penned for the marriage feast, becoming the clearest narrative bridge between the Jain Tīrthaṅkara roster and the Hindu Kṛṣṇa cycle _(actors: Neminātha/Ariṣṭanemi, Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva), the Yādavas)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Jain tradition; Hemacandra, Triṣaṣṭi-śalākā-puruṣa-carita)
- **Nārada asks Brahmā how to cross Kali** — At the end of Dvāpara, the sage Nārada approaches the creator Brahmā and asks how one may be delivered from the evils of the Kali age _(actors: Nārada, Brahmā)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/kalisantarana-upanishad.md) — (Kālisantaraṇa Upaniṣad, Kṛṣṇa-Yajurveda)
- **Nārada beholds the deformed rāgas and rāgiṇīs and undertakes tapas to master music** — Vain of his musicianship, the celestial singer Nārada is led by Viṣṇu (var. Śiva) through the divine realm, where he sees maimed and crippled figures — the embodied rāgas and rāgiṇīs whose bodies he had broken by singing them out of tune and against rule. Shamed, he abandons his pride and undertakes severe tapas (var. learning from Tumburu) to master music properly and heal what he marred _(actors: Nārada (as celestial vīṇā-player), Viṣṇu, Tumburu, the personified rāgas and rāgiṇīs)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/gandharvas-and-kinnaras.md) — (Later Purāṇic / musicological and rāga-mālā lore; bhakti tradition (folk / devotional — not in the early epics))
- **Nārada frames the tale** — Nārada narrates Gālava's story to dissuade Duryodhana from prideful obstinacy on the eve of the Kurukṣetra war _(actors: Nārada, Duryodhana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/galava-madhavi.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva ~103–105 (Bhagavad-yāna sub-parva))
- **Origin of the Kīrtimukha — Śiva's wrath-being commanded to devour itself** — When Jalandhara's envoy Rāhu demands Pārvatī, Śiva's wrath bursts forth as a ravening lion-faced being; with no other prey it is told to eat its own body, and the surviving jawless face is fixed forever above the temple door as Kīrtimukha, the 'Face of Glory' _(actors: Śiva, Jalandhara, Rāhu, the Kīrtimukha/grāsa-being, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/temple-iconographic-motifs.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa; Śiva Purāṇa (Rudra-saṃhitā Yuddha-khaṇḍa); Coomaraswamy, 'The Face of Glory')
- **Parvati creates Ganesha from her body-dirt** — Parvati forms a boy from the turmeric paste or scurf of her own body and breathes life into him as a guardian while she bathes _(actors: Parvati, Ganesha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Shiva Purana, Rudra-samhita)
- **Pāṇḍava pursuit of Śiva for expiation** — After Kurukṣetra, the Pāṇḍavas pursue Śiva to expiate kin- and Brahmin-slaying; Śiva evades them past Kāśī into the Garhwal Himalaya _(actors: Pāṇḍavas (esp. Bhīma), Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kedarnath-kedara-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa; Mahābhārata-derived sthala-purāṇa)
- **Penance of Sutapas and Pṛśni** — In the Svāyambhuva Manvantara the Prajāpati Sutapas and his wife Pṛśni perform tapas for twelve thousand celestial years until Viṣṇu appears and offers a boon _(actors: Sutapas, Pṛśni, Viṣṇu, Brahmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/prishnigarbha-three-births.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.3.32-36)
- **Piplada curses Shani** — The Shiva-avatar Piplada, abandoned as a child, curses Saturn for the suffering his gaze causes, winning the boon that Shani would not afflict children under sixteen _(actors: Piplada (Shiva), Shani)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Pulastya recites the tīrtha-catalogue to Bhīṣma** — The sage Pulastya enumerates the holy fords of Bhāratavarṣa to a young Bhīṣma, the prototype anukramaṇī of tīrthas with their merits _(actors: Pulastya, Bhīṣma)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mahabharata-tirtha-yatra-fords.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva 82–85)
- **Puṣpadanta granted the boon of invisibility** — Śiva grants the gandharva Puṣpadanta the power of unseen movement wherever he wishes _(actors: Śiva, Puṣpadanta (gandharva))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-mahimna-stotra.md) — (Puṣpadanta frame-legend (Śiva-mahimnaḥ stotra tradition / Purāṇic legend))
- **Recitation of the Bilvashtaka at bilva-leaf offering** — The eight-verse Bilvashtaka is recited as a samarpana-mantra while laying the trifoliate bilva leaf on the Shiva-linga in shiva-puja _(actors: The worshipper (devotee); Shiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bilvashtaka.md) — (Bilvashtaka)
- **Renewal of the cosmic administration at each manvantara turnover** — At every manvantara turnover an entire cosmic administration is recreated: a new Manu, Indra, Devas, Saptarishis, sons of Manu, and a manvantara-avatara of Vishnu _(actors: Manu, Indra, Saptarishis, Vishnu)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/manvantara-system.md) — (Vishnu Purana 3.1-2; Bhagavata Purana 8.1.3-5)
- **Rukmangada's Ekadashi-vrata empties Yama's realm** — King Rukmangada so successfully enforces the Ekadashi fast that the sinful stop dying and Yama's underworld empties, prompting Yama to complain to Brahma _(actors: Rukmangada, Yama, Brahma)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/narada-purana.md) — (Narada Purana (Rukmangada-carita, Uttarabhaga))
- **Sage Kālava Cured by the Navagrahas and Brahmā's Curse** — The leprous sage Kalava is cured by the nine planets at Suryanar Kovil; Brahma curses the planets with leprosy for altering fate, and they regain health by worshipping Shiva there _(actors: Kalava, Navagrahas, Brahma, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/navagraha-temples.md) — (Sthala-purana)
- **Sarasvatī disappears at Vinaśana** — The Sarasvatī river sinks underground and is lost to view (adarśana) in the desert sands at Vinaśana, out of disdain for the Niṣāda/Ābhīra tribes, running hidden before re-emerging; bathing at the vanishing-ford removes sin _(actors: Sarasvatī (river-goddess))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kurukshetra-dharmakshetra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana-parva (Tīrtha-yātrā); Śalya-parva 36–37)
- **Satī's yoni falls on Nīlācala** — In the Daksha-yajña dismemberment, Satī's yoni falls on Nīlācala hill, sanctifying the supreme yoni-pīṭha _(actors: Satī, Śiva, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kamakhya-nilachala-kamarupa.md) — (Kālikā Purāṇa 18; Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII)
- **Sādhyas seek the highest good** — A class of demigods, the Sādhyas, approach seeking instruction in the supreme good (śreyas), the highest dharma, and the conduct of the wise _(actors: the Sādhya gods)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-gita.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva, Mokṣadharma ~12.288–299)
- **Śiva gambles away his regalia** — throw by throw Pārvatī wins his crescent moon, the serpent-necklace Vāsuki, his nāga ear-rings, elephant-hide and loincloth, stripping the ascetic bare _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shiva-parvati-dice-game.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa 34.76 ff.)
- **Śiva grants the Sudarśana Cakra** — the pleased Śiva manifests and bestows the discus 'shining with a mass of splendour,' with which Viṣṇu at once slays the daityas _(actors: Śiva, Viṣṇu, the daityas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vishnu-lotus-eye-sudarshana.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṃhitā 34)
- **Śiva hides one flower** — to test Viṣṇu's devotion Śiva secretly withdraws a single lotus, leaving the offering one short at the final name _(actors: Śiva, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vishnu-lotus-eye-sudarshana.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṃhitā 34)
- **Śiva Kapālin and Kapālamocana** — for severing Brahmā's fifth head Śiva incurs Brahmahatyā, and the skull clings to his hand as kāpālika penance until it drops at Kapālamocana in Kāśī — the mythic charter of the skull-bearing vow _(actors: Śiva, Brahmā)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/papa-punya-and-prayaschitta.md) — (Śiva/Skanda Purāṇa)
- **Śiva narrates the Gītā-Māhātmya to Pārvatī** — in a timeless Śaiva teaching-scene Śiva relays Viṣṇu's dialogue with Lakṣmī, telling one deliverance-parable for each of the Gītā's eighteen chapters _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Viṣṇu, Lakṣmī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavad-gita/gita-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa, Gītā-Māhātmya frame)
- **Śiva seen in all; Viṣṇu stupefied** — Viṣṇu unleashes his māyā, multiplying into countless forms, but Dadhīca perceives Parama-Śiva pervading every form, the Devas flee, and Viṣṇu is left motionless — the Liṅga Purāṇa's Śaiva-supremacy proof _(actors: Viṣṇu, Dadhīca, the Devas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kshupa-dadhicha-dispute.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ~35–36)
- **Śiva settles on Kailāsa for Kubera's sake** — Honouring the friendship, Śiva proceeds with his gaṇas to Mount Kailāsa beside Kubera's Alakā and takes his seat there — the Purāṇic charter of Kailāsa as Śiva's abode _(actors: Śiva, Kubera, the gaṇas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gunanidhi-kubera.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṁhitā, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 20)
- **Śiva slays Kāla to save Śveta** — When Death lays hands on the rapt worshipper in the liṅga's presence, Śiva manifests and strikes Kāla dead, vindicating the devotee's fearlessness as Kālakāla, the Death of death _(actors: Shiva (Kālāntaka/Kālakāla), Kāla, Śveta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sage-shveta-and-yama.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ch. 30)
- **Śiva smitten by the huntress** — the god who fled a woman seizes the vanishing Śabarī's hand and is sent to seek her from her 'father' Himavān, the disguise naming its own truth _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī-as-Śabarī, Himavān)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shiva-parvati-dice-game.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa 35)
- **Śiva storms off to the forest** — shamed and stripped, the god abandons Pārvatī and Kailāsa to sit alone in yoga in the Siddhavaṭa forest _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shiva-parvati-dice-game.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa 34.139–153)
- **Śiva tells Pārvatī the Great Tale on Kailāsa** — Pārvatī demands a story never before heard, and behind barred doors Śiva narrates the tale of the seven Vidyādhara emperors, overheard by the invisible gaṇa Puṣpadanta _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Puṣpadanta)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/brihatkatha-kathasaritsagara-and-vikramaditya-cycle.md) — (Kathāsaritsāgara, I.1)
- **Śiva the tiger and 'trāhi mām gīrīśa'** — Śiva takes tiger-form to frighten Pārvatī, who cries 'trāhi mām gīrīśa!' ('Lord of the mountains, save me!'), and from 'mām gīrīśa' the liṅga takes the name Mangeś _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/goa-shantadurga-mangeshi-kuladevatas.md) — (Mangeśī sthala-tradition)
- **Śiva worships Maṅgala Caṇḍī before the Tripura war** — the beneficent form of Caṇḍī grants Śiva victory over the three cities, after which her Tuesday and household worship spreads _(actors: Śiva, Maṅgala Caṇḍī, the Tripura asuras)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevata-shashthi-cross-links.md) — (Brahmavaivarta, Prakṛti-khaṇḍa 41–44)
- **Śiva worships Maṅgalā Caṇḍī before Tripura** — the destroyer bows to the beneficent fortune-goddess for victory and conquers the three flying cities as Tripurāntaka, spreading her Tuesday cult through the worlds _(actors: Śiva, Maṅgalā Caṇḍī, the Tripura asuras)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/prakriti-khanda-goddess-origins.md) — (Brahmavaivarta, Prakṛti-khaṇḍa 41–44; Devī-Bhāgavata IX.44–47)
- **Śiva's boon: Madālasā reborn from a nāga** — hymning Śiva with their new song, the nāgas win the boon that Madālasā be reborn as Aśvatara's daughter through a śrāddha rite he performs and consumes _(actors: Aśvatara, Kambala, Śiva, Madālasā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kuvalayashva-madalasa.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa, Canto XXIII)
- **Śiva's gaṇas repel Yama's messengers for Guṇanidhi** — Yama's noose-bearing messengers come for the slain sinner but Śiva's gaṇas read out his accidental Śivarātri merit and carry him to Śiva's realm _(actors: Guṇanidhi, Yama's dūtas, Śiva's gaṇas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gunanidhi-kubera.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṁhitā, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 18)
- **Śiva's return to Kāśī** — Śiva re-enters Kāśī in triumphal procession with Pārvatī and the gaṇa-host, never to leave again — the charter of Avimukta and Viśveśvara's eternal residence _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Gaṇeśa)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kashi-line-divodasa.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kāśī-khaṇḍa, Uttarārdha ch. 58)
- **Śiva's wedding-priests settled at Hāṭakeśvara** — Śiva creates a class of Brāhmaṇas to officiate at his marriage to Umā and settles them permanently in the kṣetra, the founding pedigree of the Nāgara Brāhmaṇas _(actors: Śiva, Umā/Pārvatī, the Nāgaras)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vadnagar-hatakeshvara-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Nāgara-khaṇḍa, origin cycle)
- **Śivaśarman tests his five sons** — the yogin-brahmin subjects his five sons to conjured ordeals, including dismembering their mother's illusory corpse, opening the Somaśarman–Suvrata piety cycle _(actors: Śivaśarman, his five sons, Somaśarman, Suvrata)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/padma-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Bhūmi-khaṇḍa 1–22)
- **Slaying of Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa charters the Sandhi Pūjā** — the 48-minute Aṣṭamī–Navamī juncture worships the Goddess as Cāmuṇḍā at the instant Kālī beheaded Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa in the Devī Māhātmya war _(actors: Kālī/Cāmuṇḍā, Caṇḍa, Muṇḍa)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/durga-puja-bengal.md) — (Devī Māhātmya 7)
- **Slaying of Pātālaketu with Tvaṣṭṛ's weapon** — Kuvalayāśva weds Madālasā, and when the Dānava host attacks their departure he looses the divine weapon of Tvaṣṭṛ, reducing Pātālaketu and his demons to heaps of bones _(actors: Kuvalayāśva, Madālasā, Pātālaketu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kuvalayashva-madalasa.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa, Canto XXI)
- **Slaying of Ratnāsura** — Jyotibā kills the demon Ratnāsura on the hill thereafter named Ratnāgiri, where he settles as Vāḍī-Ratnāgirīcā Jyotibā _(actors: Jyotibā, Ratnāsura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jyotiba-kedarling-kolhapur.md) — (Kedāra Vijaya)
- **Soma abducts Tārā** — The Moon-god Soma, infatuated, carries off Tārā the wife of his teacher Bṛhaspati and refuses to return her, triggering a cosmic crisis _(actors: Candra (Soma), Tārā, Bṛhaspati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/the-gurus-brihaspati-and-shukra.md) — (Padma Purāṇa; Viṣṇu Purāṇa; Bhāgavata Purāṇa; Devī Bhāgavata)
- **Somaprabhā's automata of Maya** — the asura-engineer's daughter entertains her friend Kaliṅgasenā with Maya's mechanical dolls that fetch garlands, bring water, dance and guard — a locus classicus of Indian automata _(actors: Somaprabhā, Kaliṅgasenā, Maya Dānava)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kathasaritsagara-famous-tales.md) — (Kathāsaritsāgara VI; [scholarly])
- **Sukalā the pativratā defeats Indra and Kāma** — while her merchant husband is away on pilgrimage, Indra and Kāma conspire to break Sukalā's fidelity and are routed by her pativrata power _(actors: Sukalā, Kṛkala, Indra, Kāma)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/padma-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Bhūmi-khaṇḍa 41–60)
- **Śukra curses the ungrateful Daityas** — spurned in favour of the imposter, Śukra curses the asuras to defeat and lost power; they repent only when Bṛhaspati vanishes, the war already lost _(actors: Śukra, Daityas, Bṛhaspati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigus-curse-on-vishnu.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata IV.15)
- **Śukra revives Dadhīca; Śiva hardens his spine** — struck near death, Dadhīca is restored by Śukra's Mṛta-sañjīvanī, then wins from Śiva a boon making his backbone many times harder than the vajra — the etiology of the bones that will one day slay Vṛtra _(actors: Dadhīca, Śukrācārya, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kshupa-dadhicha-dispute.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ~35–36)
- **Śukra's husk-smoke tapas for the counter-lore** — leaderless in the deva war, Śukra performs Śiva's head-down chaff-smoke penance for a thousand years and wins an invincible mantra, Prajāpati-hood and avadhyatva _(actors: Śukra, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigus-curse-on-vishnu.md) — (Matsya Purāṇa 47; Devī-Bhāgavata IV.11)
- **Śukra's mind-made lifetimes** — Young Śukra follows an apsarā in mind through heaven and a chain of imagined rebirths while his body withers, until Kāla shows Bhṛgu that mind alone transmigrates and the revived Śukra becomes the asuras' preceptor _(actors: Śukra, Bhṛgu, Kāla)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yoga-vasishtha-tales-catalog.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha IV.5–17)
- **Sukumārī's fidelity and the reconciliation** — Sukumārī cleaves to her ape-faced husband unflinching, and Parvata, moved, releases Nārada from the curse while Nārada frees Parvata to ascend to heaven _(actors: Sukumārī, Nārada, Parvata)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/suvarnashthivin-srinjaya-narada-parvata.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva; Ganguli XXXI)
- **Sulabhā defeats Janaka in debate** — the woman renouncer Sulabhā enters King Janaka's court and refutes his claim that a woman cannot attain liberation, as narrated by Bhīṣma _(actors: Sulabhā, Janaka, Bhīṣma)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sannyasa-and-the-renunciate-tradition.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva, Sulabhā-Janaka-saṃvāda)
- **Sumati's hell-birth for thwarting cattle** — in his seventh former birth the wise dullard Sumati (Jaḍa), born a merchant, blocks thirsty cattle from water and is cast for a century into a hell of iron-beaked birds and rivers of blood _(actors: Sumati/Jaḍa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vipashchit-in-hell.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa XIII)
- **Sumukha chosen from Airāvata's line** — Mātali selects the young Nāga Sumukha, son of Cikura and grandson of Āryaka, as Guṇakeśī's husband _(actors: Mātali, Sumukha, Āryaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matali-sumukha-naga-bridegroom.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva 5.100–101)
- **Sundarar's descent from Kailāsa** — the gana Ālāla Sundarar is sent to earthly birth for loving two of Pārvatī's attendants, framing the whole epic _(actors: Śiva, Ālāla Sundarar, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/periya-puranam-63-nayanmar-legend-map.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Tirumalai carukkam)
- **Sūrapadma's boon of Śiva** — the daitya Sūrapadma, son of Kaśyapa and Māyā, wins from Śiva sovereignty over the worlds and invulnerability to all but a son of Śiva _(actors: Sūrapadma, Kaśyapa, Māyā, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/surapadma-samharam-kanda-puranam.md) — (Kanda Purāṇam, Asura-kāṇḍam)
- **Sūrapadma's defiance and the burning of the city** — the asura mocks Skanda as a child and refuses; Vīrabāhu routs the guards, lays waste the assembly-hall and gardens, and returns to report _(actors: Sūrapadma, Vīrabāhu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/surapadma-samharam-kanda-puranam.md) — (Kanda Purāṇam, Mahēndra-kāṇḍam)
- **Sūrasaṃhāram and the split into peacock and cock** — Sūrapadma rises from the sea as a world-spanning mango tree; the Vēl splits it into Skanda's peacock-mount and cock-banner, the enemy converted to service _(actors: Sūrapadma, Skanda)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/surapadma-samharam-kanda-puranam.md) — (Kanda Purāṇam, Yuddha-kāṇḍam)
- **Suratha and Samadhi meet at Medhas's hermitage** — A king who lost his kingdom and a betrayed merchant, both still attached to those who wronged them, meet at the forest hermitage of sage Medhas, framing the Devi Mahatmya _(actors: Suratha, Samadhi, Medhas)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 1)
- **Sureśvara — Śiva tests Upamanyu as Indra** — Śiva appears as Indra offering boons and reviling Śiva; Upamanyu refuses any gift but Śiva's and would rather die than hear the blasphemy _(actors: Śiva (Sureśvara/Indra), Upamanyu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/upamanyu.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Śatarudra-saṃhitā 32; Mahābhārata, Anuśāsana Parva)
- **Suryaa's wedding to Soma** — Suryaa, daughter of Surya/Savitr, is wed to Soma with the Ashvins as wooers, the hymn becoming the charter of the Hindu vivaha rite _(actors: Suryaa, Soma, Surya/Savitr, Ashvins)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/rigveda-samvada-and-secular-hymns.md) — (Rigveda 10.85)
- **Suśarman the bullock freed by a parrot's chapter** — a sinful brahmin reborn an oil-press ox is delivered when a courtesan gifts him merit earned by overhearing a caged parrot recite Gītā chapter 1 _(actors: Suśarman, a courtesan, a parrot)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavad-gita/gita-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Gītā-Māhātmya 1)
- **Svayaṃvara reunion and ascent beyond Indra** — Śaibyā chooses her former lord at her svayaṃvara; he reigns, sacrifices, dies in battle, she mounts his pyre, and both rise beyond Indra's sphere _(actors: Śatadhanu, Śaibyā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shatadhanu-shaibya.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, III.18)
- **Śveta defies Death's universal boast** — Kāla claims nothing in the three worlds escapes him, even the gods; the sage answers that one whose heart holds Maheśvara has nothing to dread, and continues his liṅga-pūjā _(actors: Śveta, Kāla)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sage-shveta-and-yama.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ch. 30)
- **Śveta granted deathlessness** — With Death destroyed, the sage is freed from mortality altogether — the Liṅga Purāṇa's proof that liṅga-worship confers mṛtyuṃjaya, distinct from the Mārkaṇḍeya telling _(actors: Shiva, Śveta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sage-shveta-and-yama.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ch. 30)
- **Śvetaketu binds the marriage-vow** — outraged to see a Brahmana lead his mother away, Śvetaketu son of Uddālaka institutes the rule of wifely fidelity, the charter Pāṇḍu cites for controlled niyoga _(actors: Śvetaketu, Uddālaka Āruṇi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyushitashva-bhadra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva; Ganguli §CXX)
- **Tālaketu's false death-report** — the slain demon's brother Tālaketu, disguised as a Yamunā-bank ascetic, tricks the prince out of his neck-jewel and carries a lie to the capital that Kuvalayāśva has been speared and burned _(actors: Tālaketu, Kuvalayāśva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kuvalayashva-madalasa.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa, Canto XXII)
- **Tāpoi and the Khudurukuṇī Oṣā** — the tormented merchant's daughter is saved by goddess Maṅgaḷā, re-performed by unmarried girls each Bhādraba Sunday in coastal Odisha [folk] [oral tradition] _(actors: Tāpoi, Maṅgaḷā, the seven sādhaba brothers)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/odia-literature-sarala-and-panchasakha.md) — (Tāpoi kathā, Odia oral tradition)
- **Tārā Suckles the Poison-Burnt Śiva** — after Śiva swallows the halāhala at the Samudra Manthana and his throat burns, the goddess Tārā takes him as an infant to her breast and nurses away the venom's fire, the icon enshrined at Tārāpīṭha _(actors: Tārā, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tarapith-tara-vashishtha-bamakhepa.md) — (Tārāpīṭha sthala tradition; cf. Samudra Manthana) [regional]
- **Tarakasura's boon and the liṅga at his throat** — The boon-protected asura Tārakāsura wears a Śivaliṅga that makes his body proof against death, slayable only by a son of Śiva _(actors: Tārakāsura, Śiva, the devas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancharama-kshetras-andhra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa; Śiva Purāṇa (Rudra-saṃhitā, Kumāra-khaṇḍa); local Andhra sthala-purāṇa)
- **Ten thousand brāhmaṇas fed through the famine** — Gautama sustains ten thousand brāhmaṇas and munis for the whole twelve famine-years, his refuge-āśrama swelling to a hundred-yojana boundary and his fame reaching Indra's court _(actors: Gautama, the brāhmaṇas, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautama-curse-on-the-brahmins.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata XII.8)
- **The 1,008-world empire from Vīramahendrapuram** — Sūrapadma conquers the three worlds, rules 1,008 aṇḍas from the sea-fortress Māyaṉ built, and enslaves the devas _(actors: Sūrapadma, Māyaṉ, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/surapadma-samharam-kanda-puranam.md) — (Kanda Purāṇam, Mahēndra-kāṇḍam)
- **The 64 Yoginīs stay in Kāśī** — Sent by Śiva to find a flaw in Divodāsa, the 64 Yoginīs find none, fall in love with the city, and settle there permanently _(actors: 64 Yoginīs, Śiva, Divodāsa)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kashi-line-divodasa.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kāśī-khaṇḍa, Uttarārdha ~ch. 53)
- **The ajapā-naṭanam** — Tiruvārūr's Vīdhividaṅkar sways in the "dance without chanting," borne on the rhythm of breath alone as Somāskanda rocks on Viṣṇu's chest _(actors: Tyāgarāja/Vīdhividaṅkar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruvarur-thyagaraja-kamalalaya.md) — (Tiruvārūr sthala tradition)
- **The amma-theyyam raised at the kāvus** — across 100+ Muchilōṭṭu kāvus of North Malabar the possessed dancer becomes the living Bhagavatī, the grand Perunkaḷiyāṭṭam held only at long intervals _(actors: theyyam performer, Muchilottu Bhagavathy)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/muchilottu-bhagavathy.md) — (North Malabar Kaḷiyāṭṭam / theyyam tradition)
- **The Aṅkāḷammaṉ and Kerala recensions** — the Nīli myth is absorbed into the fierce goddess Aṅkāḷaparamēcuvari's cult, while the Malayalam Yakṣi-Nīli of Kalliyaṅkāḍu is subdued by the sorcerer Kaṭamaṟṟattu Kattanār _(actors: Nīli/Icakki, Aṅkāḷammaṉ, Kaṭamaṟṟattu Kattanār)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/icakki-amman-nili-katai.md) — (Meyer, Aṅkāḷaparamēcuvari; Kalliyaṅkāṭṭu Nīli tradition) [regional]
- **The anna-dāna charter of self-consumption** — the recurring parable that a king who fed only himself must in death feed on himself, twinning the Rāmāyaṇa Śveta with the Mahābhārata's dāna-upākhyāna and the half-golden mongoose _(actors: Śveta, Agastya, the gleaner-family)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shveta-corpse-king-and-agastya.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa Uttara Kāṇḍa 77–78; Mahābhārata Vana & Āśvamedhika Parvas)
- **The Anna-vows — never disparage food** — The injunctions annam na nindyāt, na paricakṣīta, bahu kurvīta and to turn no guest from one's door establish food-reverence and hospitality as spiritual attainment _(actors: Bhṛgu)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/anna-naivedya-and-prasada.md) — (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 3.7–3.10)
- **The armed ascetics of Badarī humble a conqueror** — the recurring motif in which a proud challenger mistakes Nara-Nārāyaṇa for weaklings and is undone: Prahlāda in a thousand-year duel, Dambhodbhava by a fistful of grass-blades _(actors: Nara-Nārāyaṇa, Prahlāda, Dambhodbhava)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/prahlada-nara-narayana-battle.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa IV.9; Vāmana Purāṇa; Mahābhārata 5.94)
- **The Āṣāḍhī–Kārttikī Wārī cycle** — twice yearly, at the Ekādaśīs of Viṣṇu's cosmic sleep (Śayanī) and waking (Prabodhinī), the Jñāneśvar (Āḷandī) and Tukārām (Dehu) pālkhīs with hundreds of thousands of Vārkarīs converge on Pandharpur _(actors: Vārkarī pilgrims, saints' pādukās, Viṭṭhala)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pandharpur-vitthala-wari.md) — (Vārkarī practice; Ekādaśī-māhātmya cycle) [regional]
- **The Aśvins and Sarasvatī restore Indra** — the twin healers and the goddess supply the loophole-foam and cure Indra, drawing his stolen strength back out through the surā _(actors: Indra, the Aśvins, Sarasvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indra-namuchi.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 12.7.3.1–4)
- **The Bhārgava gotra seeded from a king** — Vītahavya, now a Brahmarṣi, begets Gṛtsamada (seer of Ṛgveda Maṇḍala II), founding the line to Śunaka and Śaunaka and the Vītahavya/Gṛtsamada Bhārgava gaṇa of the pravara-lists _(actors: Vītahavya, Gṛtsamada, Śaunaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matanga-vitahavya-varna-upakhyanas.md) — (Anuśāsana Parva ~31)
- **The birth of Skanda from six sparks** — Śiva emits six fiery sparks, the Kṛttikās nurse them into the six-faced Ṣaṇmukha, armed with Pārvatī's Vēl as Devasenāpati _(actors: Śiva, Kṛttikās, Skanda-Murugan, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/surapadma-samharam-kanda-puranam.md) — (Kanda Purāṇam, Utpatti-kāṇḍam)
- **The bleeding stone in the well** — three disabled brothers digging a well in drought-parched Kāṇipākam strike a hard object that wells up blood, and the blood-mingled spring-water instantly cures all three of blindness, deafness, and muteness/lameness _(actors: the three brothers, the svayambhū Gaṇeśa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanipakam-varasiddhi-vinayaka.md) — (Kāṇipākam Sthala-Purāṇa)
- **The boar's cry and the half-uttered bīja** — a boar wounded by a fowler flees into Satyavrata's hermitage, and in pity the illiterate ascetic cries "Aiṃ! Aiṃ!" — the vāg-bīja of Sarasvatī voiced by pure accident, its meaning unknown to him _(actors: Satyavrata, the boar, the fowler)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/satyavrata-aim-bija.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa III.11)
- **The bonfire of the hermitage and the king's awakening** — Śikhidhvaja burns hut, staff, rosary and deer-skin; Kumbha teaches that the mind alone is the 'all' to be renounced, and the king awakens from samādhi a jīvanmukta _(actors: Śikhidhvaja, Cūḍālā/Kumbha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/chudala-shikhidhvaja.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha VI.1, ~chs. 97–99)
- **The boon of a gold-voiding son** — pleased with Sṛñjaya, Nārada grants him a son whose every excretion turns to gold, to be named Suvarṇaṣṭhīvin, warning that the very gold will make the boy short-lived _(actors: Nārada, Sṛñjaya, Suvarṇaṣṭhīvin)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/suvarnashthivin-srinjaya-narada-parvata.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva; Ganguli XXX–XXXI)
- **The boy-guardian of the threshold** — as kṣetrapāla and dvārapāla, Vaṭuka is propitiated first at temple boundaries before the fiercer Bhairavas and the main shrine _(actors: Vaṭuka Bhairava, Tantric practitioners)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vatuka-bhairava-origin.md) — (Tantric/Āgamic tradition)
- **The brahma-vs-kṣatra debate** — King Kṣupa and the sage Dadhīca, once friends, quarrel over whether the kṣatriya or the brāhmaṇa is supreme, until the sage strikes the king on the head with his fist _(actors: Kṣupa, Dadhīca)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kshupa-dadhicha-dispute.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ~35–36)
- **The Brahmakuṇḍa formed** — the water-child is penned amid the four mountains Kailāsa, Gandhamādana, Jārudhi and Saṃvartaka, growing as the sacred red lake Brahmakuṇḍa _(actors: Lauhitya, Śāntanu, Brahmā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lauhitya-brahmaputra-origin.md) — (Kālikā Purāṇa, 83–84)
- **The Brihadaranyaka fuses rta, satya and dharma** — Brihadaranyaka Upanishad 1.4.14 declares satya and dharma to be one and nothing higher than dharma, the pivotal late-Vedic teaching bridging the old cosmic order (rta) into the new ethical-legal order → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/rta-and-cosmic-order.md) — (Brihadaranyaka Upanishad 1.4.14)
- **The caṇḍāla frees the brahmarākṣasa Somaśarmā** — An ekādaśī-keeping outcaste devotee of Viṣṇu keeps his pledge to return to be eaten, then gifts part of his vigil-merit to free the demon Somaśarmā from his rākṣasa state _(actors: the caṇḍāla of Avanti, Somaśarmā, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautami-mahatmya-tales-catalog.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa, dharma-discourse chapters)
- **The Cāndrāyaṇa lunar fast** — the penitent's food waxes and wanes with the moon: mouthfuls decrease each day of the dark fortnight to a total fast on new-moon, then increase through the bright fortnight to the full-moon peak _(actors: the penitent)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/papa-punya-and-prayaschitta.md) — (Manusmṛti 11.217–222)
- **The catalogue of sins and torments** — at the king's asking the officer recites which crimes earn which hells — eye-torture for covetousness, the torn tongue for false teachers, the saw for sowers of discord — with the maxim that a trifling sin still pricks like a thorn _(actors: Vipaścit, Yama's messenger)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vipashchit-in-hell.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa XIV)
- **The chain of ignorant sages** — Keśidhvaja's priests, then Kaśeru, then Śunaka each confess they do not know the expiation, leaving only his exiled enemy Khāṇḍikya able to purify the rite _(actors: Keśidhvaja, Kaśeru, Śunaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khandikya-keshidhvaja.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa VI.6)
- **The chain of the arts** — Mārkaṇḍeya declares that image-making cannot be learned without painting, painting without dance, dance without instrumental music, and that without vocal song — binding all the arts into one dependent tree _(actors: Mārkaṇḍeya, Vajra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vishnudharmottara-purana.md) — (Viṣṇudharmottara Purāṇa III.2)
- **The champion who dies by treachery becomes a god** — the Northern Songs run the North-Malabar vīra-ārādhana engine: a martial hero martyred in valour or betrayal is venerated at shrines and embodied as theyyam, the deified-hero logic shared with Kathivanur Veeran _(actors: the Cēkava/Nāyar hero-dead, the theyyam cult)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vadakkan-pattukal-northern-ballads.md) — (Vaṭakkan Pāṭṭukaḷ; Kurup, Cult of Teyyam)
- **The Chandra river heads the Chandrabhāgā at the Moon Lake** — Chandra Tāl sits at the source-basin of the Chandra ("moon") river, which joins the Bhāga to form the Chandrabhāgā, the Chenab of the Punjab five-rivers, giving the Moon Lake a place at the head of a great Indus tributary _(actors: Chandra river, Bhāga, Chandrabhāgā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/chandra-taal-yudhishthira-svargarohana.md) — (Lāhaul regional tradition; river geography) [regional]
- **The chase into Pātāla** — Kuvalayāśva pursues the boar-shaped Dānava Pātālaketu across thousands of yojanas until it plunges into a chasm, and horse and rider fall in after it into the netherworld _(actors: Kuvalayāśva, Pātālaketu, the horse Kuvalaya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kuvalayashva-madalasa.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa, Canto XXI)
- **The chaste lady raises her fallen husband to heaven** — A śūdra wife nurses her courtesan-keeping, disease-stricken husband, follows him onto the pyre, and both ascend to heaven — Dharmāṅgada's exemplum for honouring Mohinī without jealousy _(actors: the chaste śūdra wife, her husband, Dharmāṅgada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-purana-ganga-tales.md) — (Nārada Purāṇa, Uttarabhāga 16)
- **The Chiranjīvī Hanumān localised as Bālājī** — the deathless Tretā-Yuga Rudrāvatāra, son of Vāyu, is worshipped as the Rajasthani folk healer Bālājī, doctrinally present in Kali Yuga by his own immortal nature _(actors: Hanumān, Vāyu, Rāma)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mehandipur-salasar-balaji-hanuman-shrines.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa; folk tradition) [folk]
- **The city of jīvanmuktas** — realization spreads from Hemacūḍa to King Muktacūḍa, prince Maṇicūḍa, ministers and citizens until the whole Daśārṇa city becomes a community of the liberated who still farm, rule, and worship _(actors: Hemacūḍa, Muktacūḍa, Maṇicūḍa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tripura-rahasya.md) — (Tripurā Rahasya, Jñāna-khaṇḍa)
- **The color-omen spring of Tulmul** — the hexagonal spring of Kṣīr Bhavānī changes color as an omen for Kashmir, milky-white to rose in peace but blackening before calamity, receiving milk and kheer offerings _(actors: Kṣīr Bhavānī/Rājñā, Kashmiri Pandit devotees)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kashmir-tirthas-nilamata-geography.md) — (living Kashmiri Pandit tradition) [folk]
- **The contest of Veda versus the lute** — the Gandharvas woo Vāc by reciting the Veda while the gods invent the vīṇā and win her with song and dance _(actors: the Gandharvas, the devas, Vāc)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vac-and-the-soma-purchase.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 3.2.4.6)
- **The cow offered to the ancestors — the saving reflex** — before eating, the seven dedicate the flesh as a śrāddha to the pitṛs, and that single act of ancestor-piety earns them unbroken memory of former births through everything that follows _(actors: the seven brahmins, the pitṛs)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pitrikalpa-seven-brahmins-brahmadatta.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Pitṛ-kalpa 21; Matsya Purāṇa 21)
- **The cow returns as Queen Sannati** — the soul of the slaughtered cow is reborn as Sannati, who weds Brahmadatta and bears his son Viṣvaksena, closing the karmic circle of the founding sin _(actors: Sannati, Brahmadatta, Viṣvaksena)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pitrikalpa-seven-brahmins-brahmadatta.md) — (Padma Purāṇa III.10; Harivaṃśa)
- **The deer family's pleas and pledges** — a pregnant doe, a second doe, and the stag each come to the water in successive watches, plead in human speech for reprieve, and vow to return to be killed _(actors: Gurudruh, the deer family)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shivaratri-hunter-legend.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṁhitā 40)
- **The deity who comes out to the excluded** — across the Purī triad the same grace-pattern recurs: shut out as yavana, untouchable, or too poor, the devotee loves anyway and Jagannātha comes out to meet him, teaching that his grace is casteless, creedless, and cannot be timetabled _(actors: Jagannātha, Sālabega, Dāsiā Baurī, Bandhu Mohanty)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/jagannatha-bhakta-legends.md) — (Odia bhakta tradition) [regional] [scholarly]
- **The devas restored and the deva-bride** — the prison is opened, Jayanta and the gods freed, Indra restored, and Skanda weds Indra's foster-daughter Deivānai at Tirupparaṅkuṉṟam _(actors: Jayanta, Indra, Skanda, Deivānai)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/surapadma-samharam-kanda-puranam.md) — (Kanda Purāṇam, Deva-kāṇḍam)
- **The Devī re-charters a dynasty** — the Śākta Devī-Bhāgavata rewrites the Haihaya eponym's origin as a mantra-driven divine descent, giving the marauding Haihaya house a Goddess-devotee founder _(actors: Devī, Ekavīra/Haihaya)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/ekavira-ekavali-haihaya-origin.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VI) [late text]
- **The Devī rests at Saptaśṛṅgī after slaying Mahiṣa** — the eighteen-armed goddess of the seven-peaked hill at Vaṇī is read as the Devī-Māhātmya's Mahiṣa-slayer resting after battle, with Mārkaṇḍeya reciting her Māhātmya from the facing hill _(actors: Saptaśṛṅgī Devī, Mahiṣāsura, Mārkaṇḍeya)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/tulja-bhavani.md) — (Saptaśṛṅgī sthala-tradition; cf. Devī-Māhātmya 2–4) [regional] [folk]
