- **The Diamond-Needle thesis posed** — The Upaniṣad announces the vajra-sūcī teaching that destroys ignorance and poses its central question: what makes one a brāhmaṇa? _(actors: Anonymous Upaniṣadic teacher (traditionally Śaṅkara))_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/vajrasuchika-upanishad.md) — (Vajrasūcikā Upaniṣad 1)
- **The diptych's opposed morals** — set adjacent and unreconciled, the pair teaches both that varṇa is fixed by birth (Mātaṅga refused by Indra) and that it is moved by a sage's fiat (Vītahavya raised by Bhṛgu) — a Brāhmaṇa the gatekeeper either way _(actors: Mātaṅga, Vītahavya, Indra, Bhṛgu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matanga-vitahavya-varna-upakhyanas.md) — (Anuśāsana Parva 27–31)
- **The disguised sage's chess wager** — Krishna, arriving as a travelling sage, challenges the chess-loving Chempakasseri king and asks only for rice doubled square by square across the board _(actors: Krishna-in-disguise, Chempakasseri king)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ambalappuzha-krishna-palpayasam.md) — (Ambalappuzha temple tradition) [folk]
- **The dismembered goddess and the pīṭhas** — grief-mad Śiva bears Satī's corpse across the worlds; her body is cut apart and its pieces fall as the Śakti pīṭhas, localised in the Kathmandu Valley _(actors: Śiva, Satī, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/swasthani-brata-katha.md) — (Svasthānī Vrata Kathā, Śaiva chapters)
- **The doctrine of united brahma-kṣatra** — the upākhyāna's charter: the Brahmaṇa's energy and the Kṣatriya's energy are twin pillars of sovereignty that conquer joined and fail divided _(actors: Bhīṣma, Yudhiṣṭhira, Mucukunda)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mucukunda-vaishravana-shanti.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva 12.75, Rājadharma)
- **The dog of Vaidiśā garlanded** — Śaibyā, reborn a jātismarā princess of Kāśī, refuses all suitors, recognizes her husband reborn as a dog at Vaidiśā, garlands him and names his fault; remembering, he dies in the desert _(actors: Śaibyā, Śatadhanu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shatadhanu-shaibya.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, III.18)
- **The doves feed the hunter at Kapota-tīrtha** — A male and female pigeon cast themselves into the fire to feed a starving hunter-guest and both ascend to heaven, chartering the ford _(actors: the kapota pair, the hunter)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautami-mahatmya-tales-catalog.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa, Gautamī-māhātmya, vulgate adh. 80)
- **The dudhe-bhāte boon** — the humble ferryman asks not for wealth or heaven but only that his children live ever "in milk and rice," a line that passed into Bengali speech as the archetypal blessing _(actors: Īśvarī Pāṭunī, Annadā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/annadamangal-bharatchandra.md) — (Annadāmaṅgal, Pāṭunī episode)
- **The eight forms of marriage** — the code grades marriage from the meritorious Brāhma gift of a daughter down through Gāndharva to the Rākṣasa and Paiśāca abduction/rape, assigning each to its varṇas _(actors: bride-givers, the four varṇas)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/manusmriti-map.md) — (Mānava-Dharmaśāstra 3.20–44)
- **The eighteen titles of law (vyavahāra)** — Manu enumerates the eighteen grounds of litigation, from non-payment of debt through assault, theft and adultery, systematising civil and criminal procedure _(actors: king, judge, litigants)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/manusmriti-map.md) — (Mānava-Dharmaśāstra 8.4–7)
- **The encounter on the narrow path** — the Pulaya meets the ācārya on a way too narrow for two and refuses to step aside for the purity code, opening the dispute _(actors: the Pulaya-Śiva, Śaṅkarācārya)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/pottan-theyyam.md) — (Poṭṭan thoṭṭam)
- **The eponymous charter for Gāndhāra** — the Uttara-kāṇḍa ties two real northwestern cities into the Ikṣvāku line by deriving their names from Bharata's sons, a foundation-legend claiming Gandhāra for the Solar dynasty _(actors: Bharata, Takṣa, Puṣkala)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharata-gandharva-conquest-takshashila.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara-kāṇḍa 100–101) [late text]
- **The equivocal-oath ordeal** — a suspected wife passes a Yakṣa chastity-ordeal by having her lover embrace her disguised as a madman, then swearing truthfully that no man touched her but her husband and this lunatic _(actors: the clever wife, her lover, the Yakṣa image)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/shuka-saptati.md) — (Śukasaptati, simplicior)
- **The errand to the lake** — near a city Viṣṇu feigns thirst and sends Nārada down to fetch water from a tank, the threshold between the sage's true self and the coming dream _(actors: Viṣṇu, Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-maya-taladhvaja.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa VI.28–29)
- **The ever-growing Ganesha** — the Kāṇipākam svayambhū is held to be slowly enlarging as a living stone, the stock proof being a donated silver kavaca that no longer fits the grown image _(actors: Varasiddhi Vināyaka, temple priests)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanipakam-varasiddhi-vinayaka.md) — (temple tradition) [folk]
- **The fall of Guṇanidhi** — The brahmin Yajñadatta of Kāmpilya discovers via a gem-ring on a gambler's hand that his son Guṇanidhi has ruined himself at dice, and disowns him _(actors: Guṇanidhi, Yajñadatta, Guṇanidhi's mother)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gunanidhi-kubera.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṁhitā, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 17)
- **The fall of the asura champions** — over the six-day war Sūrapadma's son Bānukōpaṉ and his brothers Taraka and Siṃhamukha are cut down by Skanda's Vēl _(actors: Skanda, Bānukōpaṉ, Taraka, Siṃhamukha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/surapadma-samharam-kanda-puranam.md) — (Kanda Purāṇam, Yuddha-kāṇḍam)
- **The Father and the Daughter (RV 10.61)** — the earliest, veiled attestation: the Father unites with the Daughter and spills his seed on the earth, and the gods fashion the brahman and form Vāstoṣpati to set the deed right _(actors: the Father/Prajāpati, the Daughter, Vāstoṣpati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/prajapati-ushas-rudra-piercing.md) — (Ṛgveda 10.61.5–7)
- **The fed brāhmaṇas turn envious** — when good rains return and harvests abound, the sheltered brāhmaṇas grow jealous of the honour heaped on their host and resolve to abandon his āśrama _(actors: the brāhmaṇas, Gautama)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautama-curse-on-the-brahmins.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata XII.9)
- **The five further births awakened** — Śaibyā tracks Śatadhanu as jackal on mount Kolāhala, wolf, vulture, crow, and peacock, reminding him each time of the heretic-conversation until he abandons each body _(actors: Śaibyā, Śatadhanu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shatadhanu-shaibya.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, III.18)
- **The five gandharva maidens delivered** — cursed into a piśāca-state, five gandharva maidens are restored when a passing vratin gifts them a share of his Māgha-snāna merit _(actors: five gandharva maidens, a Māgha vratin)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/masa-mahatmyas-kartika-magha-vaishakha.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Māgha-māhātmya)
- **The five great sins and their penances (prāyaścitta)** — brahmin-murder, drinking liquor, stealing gold, violating the guru's bed and association with such sinners are graded as mahāpātaka, expiated by Kṛcchra and Cāndrāyaṇa fasts _(actors: the sinner, expiators)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/manusmriti-map.md) — (Mānava-Dharmaśāstra 11.55–71, 11.217ff)
- **The five mahāpātakas codified** — Manu names the five mortal sins — brahmahatyā, surāpāna, gold-theft, violating the guru's bed, and association with those who do — each demanding caste-loss and the heaviest expiation _(actors: Manu, the twice-born)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/papa-punya-and-prayaschitta.md) — (Manusmṛti 11.55)
- **The foam of the waters at twilight** — Indra beheads Namuci in the dawn-mist with the foam of the waters (apāṃ phena), the one instrument that is neither wet nor dry, no weapon, and struck at neither day nor night _(actors: Indra, Namuci)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indra-namuchi.md) — (Ṛgveda 8.14.13; Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 12.7.3)
- **The foot-dipping taboo of Chandra Tāl** — because the moon-lake is held to be the threshold of Yudhiṣṭhira's heavenward departure, Spiti locals revere it as a doorway between earth and Svarga and traditionally refuse to dip their feet in its water _(actors: Yudhiṣṭhira, Spiti pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/chandra-taal-yudhishthira-svargarohana.md) — (Spiti/Lāhaul oral tradition) [folk]
- **The forms foretold** — in the Nārāyaṇī Stuti the Goddess prophesies she will become Śatākṣī, Śākambharī and Durgā in a future age of hundred-year drought — the older text's seed of the whole episode _(actors: the Devī, the gods)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shakambhari-shatakshi-durgamasura.md) — (Devī Māhātmya 11 = Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa 91)
- **The four friends of Mitralābha** — the mouse Hiraṇyaka, crow Laghupatanaka, tortoise Mantharaka, and deer Citrāṅga cooperate to free one another, proving alliance among the weak defeats the strong _(actors: Hiraṇyaka, Laghupatanaka, Mantharaka, Citrāṅga)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/hitopadesha.md) — (Hitopadeśa I, Mitralābha)
- **The four Kumāras seek liberation** — The aged primordial sages Sanaka, Sanandana, Sanātana, and Sanatkumāra wander seeking the truth of the Self _(actors: Sanaka, Sanandana, Sanātana, Sanatkumāra (the four Kumāras))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dakshinamurti.md) — (Sūta Saṃhitā (Skanda Purāṇa); Śiva Purāṇa)
- **The four roots of dharma** — Manu ranks the sources of law as Veda, Smṛti, the conduct of the good and the satisfaction of one's own conscience, the charter of Dharmaśāstra authority _(actors: Manu, Bhṛgu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/manusmriti-map.md) — (Mānava-Dharmaśāstra 2.6, 2.12)
- **The fourteen-birth ledger** — the boy reveals he was, fourteen births earlier, Dharmajālika of Vidiśā, a hypocrite-preacher whose descent through hell, worm, crow and jackal explains suffering as karma across many lives _(actors: the boy / Dharmajālika, Vyāsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nandabhadra-tested-faith.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa 46)
- **The gaṇa-disguised infiltration of Kailāsa** — descending by Śāmbarī illusion in the guise of Śiva's attendants, the two Daityas approach the ball-playing Pārvatī, but Śiva reads the tremulousness of their eyes and warns her with a silent glance _(actors: Vidala, Utpala, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yuddha-khanda-minor-vadhas.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Yuddha-khaṇḍa 59)
- **The Gandharvas claim Vāc back** — following after, the Gandharvas demand "the Soma is yours, let Vāc be ours," and the gods agree to a wooing-contest rather than let her be seized by force _(actors: the Gandharvas, the devas, Vāc)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vac-and-the-soma-purchase.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 3.2.4.5)
- **The Gautamī serpent dialogue on karma** — A bereaved mother Gautamī refuses to let a fowler kill the snake that killed her child; the serpent, Death, and Time each deflect blame until karma is named the true cause, teaching that vengeance is futile _(actors: Gautamī, Arjunaka the fowler, the serpent, Mṛtyu, Kāla)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/13-anushasana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 13.1)
- **The Gayasura legend** — The daitya Gayasura's body grows so holy by devotion to Vishnu that touching it liberates anyone, threatening to empty hell; the gods perform a yajna on his recumbent body, Vishnu pins him with his foot, and dying he is granted the boon that his body become the eternal tirtha of Gaya where ancestral offerings free the dead _(actors: Gayasura, Vishnu, Brahma)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/shraddha-and-pitr-rites.md) — (Garuda Purana; Vishnu Purana; Vayu Purana)
- **The ghost Jaḍa released at Kāśī** — a brahmin turned trader and murdered by robbers becomes a preta until his son's recitation of Gītā chapter 3 at Vārāṇasī lifts him and his ancestors to Vaikuṇṭha _(actors: Jaḍa, his son)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavad-gita/gita-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Gītā-Māhātmya 3)
- **The gleaner who reached the solar disc** — Sūrya's own tale within the Nāga's account: a brāhmaṇa living by the Uñcha vow, subsisting on gleaned grain, fallen leaves, water, and air, won a station above the gods and to this day circles the Earth within the disc of the Sun _(actors: Sūrya, the gleaner-brāhmaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/unchavritti-brahmin-naga-padmanabha.md) — (Śānti Parva CCCLXIII)
- **The Goddess behind the sword** — the folk hero-song touches the Purāṇic frame where the champion's power flows from Lōkanārkāvu Bhagavatī, a Durgā/Bhadrakāḷī form, tying the Malabar kaḷari cult into the pan-Keralan Goddess complex _(actors: Lōkanārkāvu Bhagavatī, the kaḷari warriors)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vadakkan-pattukal-northern-ballads.md) — (Vaṭakkan Pāṭṭukaḷ, Tacōḷi cycle; Lōkanārkāvu tradition)
- **The Goddess hastens to Anubhūti (Tvaritā)** — the Devī appears instantly at the widowed sage-wife Anubhūti's cry, slays the demon Kukura in his buffalo-form, and stays enthroned on the Bālā-ghāṭ hill as Tuḷjā Bhavānī _(actors: Devī/Tuljā Bhavānī, Anubhūti, Kardama, Kukura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/tulja-bhavani.md) — (Tuljā-māhātmya, Skanda Purāṇa Sahyādri-khaṇḍa — attributed)
- **The Goddess stays on the Yamunācalā** — at Anubhūti's prayer the Devī consents to remain permanently on the hill, the seat-fixing that turns a māhātmya rescue into a standing Śakti Pīṭha _(actors: the Devī, Anubhūti)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tuljapur-tulja-bhavani.md) — (Tuljā-māhātmya) [regional]
- **The gods scatter the gem-seeds and the mines arise** — Devas, nagas and celestial beings carry off the radiant seeds through the sky and drop them, and wherever a seed falls in ocean, river, mountain or forest a gem-mine (akara) is born _(actors: the devas, nagas, yakshas)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/bala-asura-navaratna-origin.md) — (Garuda Purana, Purva-khanda gem chapters; Buddhabhatta, Ratnapariksha)
- **The gods wipe their blood-guilt onto the Āptyas** — because they knew of Viśvarūpa's killing, the guilt is drained onto Trita, Dvita, and Ekata with the rinse-water of every soma-rite ("For Trita thee!"), and they pass it on to the fee-less sacrificer _(actors: the devas, Indra, the Āptyas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/trita-aptya.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 1.2.3.2–5)
- **The grass-blade rout** — Nara hurls a handful of iṣīkā grass-blades that fill the sky and pierce the eyes, ears, and noses of the emperor's host, and Dambhodbhava falls at the sage's feet begging protection _(actors: Nara, Dambhodbhava, Dambhodbhava's army)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dambhodbhava-nara-narayana.md) — (Mahābhārata CE 5.94, Udyoga Parva)
- **The Guptakṣetra and its phala-śruti** — the Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa styles the Mahī-mouth a concealed 'hidden holy ground' and closes its 66 chapters with the fruit-of-hearing promised to whoever bathes there or hears its glory _(actors: Sūta, the Naimiṣa ṛṣis)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mahisagara-sangama-kumarika-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa 66)
- **The Haihaya war on Kāśī** — Vītahavya and his hundred sons crush three generations of Kāśī kings — Haryaśva slain, Sudeva beaten, Divodāsa routed despite fortifying Vārāṇasī at Indra's command _(actors: Vītahavya, Divodāsa, Haryaśva, Sudeva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matanga-vitahavya-varna-upakhyanas.md) — (Anuśāsana Parva ~30)
- **The Hāṭakeśvara liṅga rises from Pātāla** — a self-manifest Śiva-liṅga whose seat was in Nāgaloka surfaces at Vaḍnagar, named Hāṭakeśvara after the golden (hāṭaka) nether-world, and is received by the Nāgaras _(actors: Śiva as Hāṭakeśvara, the Nāgas, the Nāgara Brāhmaṇas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vadnagar-hatakeshvara-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Nāgara-khaṇḍa 1)
- **The household stripped away** — in old age Nandabhadra loses his son and then his chaste wife Kanakā, and the ever-composed merchant is at last driven to grief and doubt _(actors: Nandabhadra, Kanakā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nandabhadra-tested-faith.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa 45)
- **The hunter outshines the brāhmaṇa Veda** — An unlettered hunter's pure-faith worship of Śiva pleases the god more than the learned brāhmaṇa Veda's correct ritual _(actors: the hunter, Veda, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautami-mahatmya-tales-catalog.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa, Gautamī-māhātmya, vulgate adh. 169)
- **The illusion-test and recovery from the nāga's body** — invited to Pātāla, Kuvalayāśva refuses Aśvatara's treasures, is warned "it is illusion, touch her not" as Madālasā is shown, faints, and learns she is truly restored from death _(actors: Kuvalayāśva, Aśvatara, Aśvatara's sons, Madālasā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kuvalayashva-madalasa.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa, Canto XXIV)
- **The illusory dying cow (māyā-dhenu)** — the ingrate brāhmaṇas fashion an aged phantom cow by māyā and drive her into Gautama's homa-hall; when he shoos her she drops dead and they charge him with go-hatyā _(actors: the brāhmaṇas, Gautama)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautama-curse-on-the-brahmins.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata XII.9)
- **The impossible doubled-grain debt** — the doubling reaches 2^64 − 1 grains of rice, an unpayable mountain that no granary can meet, and the king despairs _(actors: Chempakasseri king)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ambalappuzha-krishna-palpayasam.md) — (wheat/rice-and-chessboard reckoning) [folk]
- **The imprisonment of Jayanta and the devas** — Indra is expelled from Amarāvatī and Jayanta and the gods are cast into the dungeon of Vīramahendrapuram and made menial servants _(actors: Jayanta, Indra, Sūrapadma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/surapadma-samharam-kanda-puranam.md) — (Kanda Purāṇam, Mahēndra-kāṇḍam)
- **The Indo-Iranian dragon-slayer — Thraētaona / Thrita** — the same figure survives in the Avesta: Āthwya's son Thraētaona (Fereydun) slays the three-headed dragon Aži Dahāka, and Thrita, the third Haoma-presser, becomes the first physician _(actors: Thraētaona, Āthwya, Thrita, Aži Dahāka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/trita-aptya.md) — (Yasna 9.7–10; Yašt 5.33–34)
- **The Indra temptation and staged infidelity** — Cūḍālā conjures Indra inviting the king to heaven, then an illusion of Madanikā with a lover; Śikhidhvaja remains desireless and unangered, passing every test _(actors: Śikhidhvaja, Cūḍālā, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/chudala-shikhidhvaja.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha VI.1, ~chs. 106–108)
- **The jīva returns and Padma revives** — Vidūratha's soul travels back along the nesting into Padma's preserved corpse, which stirs to life, exposing the chain brahmin→Padma→Vidūratha→Padma as mere changes of mental scene _(actors: Padma, Vidūratha's jīva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/lila-story-yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha III ~53–54)
- **The justice-bell of Tiruvārūr** — Manu Nīti Cholan hangs a bell at his palace gate for any wronged being to ring, and the bereaved mother-cow tolls it for judgment against the king's own heir _(actors: Manu Nīti Cholan, the cow)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruvarur-thyagaraja-kamalalaya.md) — (Tiruvārūr sthala-māhātmya; Periya Purāṇam)
- **The Karṇikī widow's dying wish for Kāśī** — a pious brahmin widow of Karṇikī on the Revā, unable to reach Kāśī in life, bids her elder son Suvāda immerse her bones in the Gaṅgā after death _(actors: the brahmin widow, Suvāda)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kotirudra-minor-linga-legends.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṃhitā 5)
- **The Keshin muni drinks the poison-cup with Rudra** — the long-haired, wind-girdled ecstatic rides the winds and shares Rudra's poison-cup, the earliest proto-yogi image _(actors: the Keshin muni, Rudra, Vayu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/rigveda-samvada-and-secular-hymns.md) — (Rigveda 10.136)
- **The king gives his merit and empties the hell** — Vipaścit asks Indra that all his lifelong good deeds be spent to deliver the sinners from torment; the damned are freed even as he at last mounts the chariot to heaven _(actors: Vipaścit, Indra, the damned)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vipashchit-in-hell.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa XV)
- **The king's midnight flight and the queen's regency** — Śikhidhvaja secretly abandons his throne for austerities on Mount Mandara; Cūḍālā, following by yogic flight, judges him unripe and rules the kingdom in his stead for eighteen years _(actors: Śikhidhvaja, Cūḍālā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/chudala-shikhidhvaja.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha VI.1, ~chs. 82–84)
- **The kingdom inside a dead brahmin's hut** — Sarasvatī reveals that Padma is the projection of an unfulfilled kingship-wish of a dead village brahmin Vasiṣṭha, and that the whole realm exists within a corner of his death-hut, where decades pass in days _(actors: Sarasvatī, Līlā, the brahmin Vasiṣṭha, Arundhatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/lila-story-yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha III ~23–28)
- **The kolam re-enacts the combat** — annually in the theyyam season the possessed dancer fights Kathivanur Veeran's mock-battle, dies, and searches for his severed fingers before rising as the living god _(actors: theyyam performer, Kathivanur Veeran)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/kathivanur-veeran.md) — (Kannur theyyam / Kaḷiyāṭṭam tradition)
- **The kṣaṇa-mukti exemplum** — Khaṭvāṅga becomes the standing proof that the quantum of remaining time is irrelevant to salvation, dramatising the Gītā's teaching that whoever remembers the Lord at death attains Him _(actors: Khaṭvāṅga)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khatvanga-one-muhurta.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 2.1.13; Bhagavad-gītā 8.5–6)
- **The Kumaras barred at Vaikuntha's gate** — Visnu's doorkeepers Jaya and Vijaya block the four child-sage Kumaras from the seventh gate of Vaikuntha, the seed-offence of the avatara-cycle _(actors: Jaya, Vijaya, Sanaka-kumaras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/jaya-vijaya.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 3.15)
- **The Kumbh of the South** — the twelve-yearly Mahāmaham bath fires when Jupiter enters Siṃha and the Sun enters Kumbha on Māśi-Magha full moon, an amṛta-pot festival paralleling the northern Kumbh Mela _(actors: Bṛhaspati/Jupiter, Sūrya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kumbakonam-adi-kumbheshwara-mahamaham.md) — (pañcāṅga; festival tradition) [regional]
- **The ladder of ever-older witnesses** — the owl Prāvārakarṇa passes the king to the crane Nāḍījaṅgha, who passes him to the tortoise Akūpāra, each too young to remember and naming an elder _(actors: Prāvārakarṇa, Nāḍījaṅgha, Akūpāra, Indradyumna)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indradyumna-akupara-oldest-beings.md) — (Mahābhārata 3.191 / Ganguli Vana CXCIX)
- **The leprous boy as teacher** — at Bahūdaka a seven-year-old wracked with leprosy consoles the despairing merchant, teaching mastery of attachment and the difference between bodily affliction and mental suffering _(actors: the leprous boy, Nandabhadra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nandabhadra-tested-faith.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa 46)
- **The lesson of Mahāmāyā** — Viṣṇu declares that the whole lived lifetime was the Goddess's Mahāmāyā, the power Nārada asked to see — provable only by being suffered and woken from, showing even the gods are bound by illusion _(actors: Viṣṇu, Nārada, Mahāmāyā/the Goddess)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-maya-taladhvaja.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa VI.31)
- **The look-back roots the Witness-Gopāla** — nearing Vidyānagara the brāhmaṇa turns to point out the Deity and looks back, whereupon Gopāla halts, becomes fixed at the spot, and speaks to confirm the marriage-vow before the assembly _(actors: young brāhmaṇa, Gopāla, sabhā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sakshi-gopal-witness-krishna.md) — (Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya 5)
- **The Lubdhaka saved from Yama** — at the hunter's death Yama's noose-bearing messengers are barred and repelled by Śiva's gaṇas, who bear the Śivarātri-purified sinner to Śiva's world _(actors: the lubdhaka, Yama's dūtas, Śiva's gaṇas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shivaratri-hunter-legend.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Māheśvara-khaṇḍa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa 33)
- **The lullaby and the raising of Alarka** — restored to earth, Madālasā sings non-dual Vedānta to her infants so Vikrānta, Subāhu and Śatrumardana renounce, while the fourth son Alarka is reared for kingship with a ring of secret counsel _(actors: Madālasā, Kuvalayāśva, Alarka, Subāhu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kuvalayashva-madalasa.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa, Cantos XXV–XXVII)
- **The Madanikā marriage test** — Claiming Durvāsas cursed him to become a woman each night, Kumbha becomes Madanikā and weds the king, who accepts with perfect equanimity — ascetic friends by day, spouses by night _(actors: Cūḍālā/Kumbha/Madanikā, Śikhidhvaja, Durvāsas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/chudala-shikhidhvaja.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha VI.1, ~chs. 100–103)
- **The maker crippled by his patron — a charter of the artisan's grievance** — the Kalkuḍa–Kallurṭi paddana enshrines the recurring protest of the wronged craftsman, telescoping the Kārkaḷa (1432) and Vēṇūr (1604) statue-events into one biography of genius punished for its excellence _(actors: Kalkuḍa, Kallurṭi, Vīra Pāṇḍya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kalkuda-kallurti.md) — (Kalkuḍa–Kallurṭi paddana; Viveka Rai, Claus) [scholarly]
- **The Man-Lion recension** — the Nṛsiṃha Purāṇa retells Yama's order to shun Viṣṇu's and Narasiṃha's devotees, adding that souls in the narakas are eased of torment on hearing Hari's names _(actors: Yama, the Yamadūtas, Nṛsiṃha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yama-gita.md) — (Nṛsiṃha Purāṇa 8)
- **The marks the noose cannot bind** — Yama lists for his servant the signs of the true devotee (even-minded, harmless, truthful, fixed on Kṛṣṇa) whom the pāśa must pass by _(actors: Yama, the Yamadūta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yama-gita.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa III.7)
- **The marriage upheld and the shrine raised** — the assembly, awed by the speaking Deity, enforces the promise; the marriage is performed and a temple built over the spot, the Deity taking the name Sākṣi-Gopāla, 'Gopāla the Witness' _(actors: two brāhmaṇas, Gopāla)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sakshi-gopal-witness-krishna.md) — (Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya 5)
- **The Mātaṅga slaying at Tuljapur** — at the appeal of Brahmā and the gods the goddess destroys the demon Mātaṅga, a second demon-cycle attached to the Tuljapur seat _(actors: Tuljā Bhavānī, Mātaṅga, Brahmā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/tulja-bhavani.md) — (Tuljapur sthala-tradition) [regional] [folk]
- **The metres fetch Soma from heaven** — the metres led by Gāyatrī fly to the far heaven for the Soma, framing the Vāc-purchase in the Kṛṣṇa-Yajurvedic account _(actors: Gāyatrī, the metres, the devas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vac-and-the-soma-purchase.md) — (Taittirīya Saṃhitā 6.1.6.1–6)
- **The mind-journey through cid-ākāśa** — Līlā and Sarasvatī abandon their bodies in trance and travel through the space of pure consciousness to the brahmin's cottage, where Vasiṣṭha and Arundhatī lie dead with Līlā's whole empire dreaming inside the room _(actors: Līlā, Sarasvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/lila-story-yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha III ~30–35)
- **The mouse that lit the lamp** — nibbling at a dying temple lamp's ghee in Kārttika, a mouse drags the wick and rekindles the flame, and is freed from its animal birth by the accidental dīpa-sevā _(actors: the mouse, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/masa-mahatmyas-kartika-magha-vaishakha.md) — (Kārttika-māhātmya, Padma/Skanda recitation tradition)
- **The Nāga absent on the Sun's car** — reaching the city Dharmāraṇya finds Padmanābha away on his monthly turn dragging the one-wheeled chariot of Vivasvat; the Nāga's wife honours the guest and is persuaded to send for her husband _(actors: Padmanābha, his wife, Dharmāraṇya, Sūrya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/unchavritti-brahmin-naga-padmanabha.md) — (Śānti Parva CCCLVII–CCCLX)
- **The Navarātra-vidhi ordained** — Vyāsa codifies the twice-yearly nine-night vrata of Caitra and Āśvina with ghaṭa-sthāpana, homa, and kumārī-pūjā of the nine named virgins, open to rich and poor alike _(actors: Vyāsa, Janamejaya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sudarshana-shashikala-navaratri.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa, III.26–27)
- **The need for a war-god against Taraka** — The boon-protected asura Taraka, killable only by a son of Shiva, oppresses the devas, prompting the gods to engineer Parvati's tapas and marriage to the ascetic Shiva _(actors: Tarakasura, devas, Parvati, Shiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/skanda-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Skanda Purana (Maheshvara/Kedara-khanda))
- **The nine rivers converge at the Mahāmaham** — once in twelve years the nava-nadī (Gaṅgā, Yamunā, Godāvarī and the rest) descend in person into the tank to shed the sins deposited in them by pilgrims _(actors: Gaṅgā, the nine rivers, the pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kumbakonam-adi-kumbheshwara-mahamaham.md) — (sthala-purāṇa; festival tradition) [regional]
- **The Niṣāda hunter's Śivarātri (Garuḍa parallel)** — a Niṣāda hunter (Sundarasenaka in printed editions), out with his dog and catching nothing, keeps the fast and vigil unknowingly beside a liṅga and escapes Yama's realm _(actors: Sundarasenaka, Śiva, Yama)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shivaratri-hunter-legend.md) — (Garuḍa Purāṇa, Ācāra/Pūrva-khaṇḍa, Śivarātri-vrata chapter)
- **The objection of youth answered** — challenged as too young to teach elders, Sārasvata declares fitness is measured by understanding and conduct, not grey hairs, age, wealth, or lineage _(actors: Sārasvata, the ṛṣis)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sarasvata-upakhyana-vriddhakumari.md) — (Mbh Śalya Parva 9.51; Ganguli LI)
- **The ocean-of-milk boon** — Śiva reveals himself and grants the boy the Kṣīra-sāgara itself, eternal youth, undying devotion, and his abiding presence near the hermitage _(actors: Śiva, Upamanyu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/upamanyu.md) — (Mahābhārata, Anuśāsana Parva XIV; Śiva Purāṇa, Śatarudra-saṃhitā 32)
- **The old brahmin's consolation** — Viṣṇu appears as an aged brahmin beside the grieving woman, teaching that kinship itself is Māyā's knot, and bids her bathe again to make the funeral offering _(actors: Viṣṇu (as brahmin), Suśīlā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-maya-taladhvaja.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa VI.31)
- **The origin of the 'oldest animals' motif** — the parable establishes the Sanskrit archetype of a quest passed up a chain of ever-older creatures to the eldest, the world-bearing tortoise Akūpāra _(actors: Indradyumna, Mārkaṇḍeya, Akūpāra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indradyumna-akupara-oldest-beings.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva)
- **The outcaste woman's inadvertent worship wins Śivaloka** — at a Śivarātri festival dropped bilva-leaves fall on the Mahābala liṅga and the blind Cāṇḍālī unknowingly keeps night-vigil, whereupon Mahābala instantly carries her to Śiva's abode _(actors: the Cāṇḍālī, Mahābala/Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kotirudra-minor-linga-legends.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṃhitā 9–10)
- **The owl–dove war ended at Yama-tīrtha** — A terrible war between owl and dove hosts, propitiating Agni and Yama respectively, is ended only when all worship the Gautamī _(actors: the ulūka, the kapota, Yama, Agni)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautami-mahatmya-tales-catalog.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa, Gautamī-māhātmya, vulgate adh. 125)
- **The pañcagavya purification** — the five cow-products — milk, curd, ghee, urine, and dung, mixed with kuśa-water as brahma-kūrca — are drunk as an internal cleanser central to the sāntapana penance and re-admission after defilement _(actors: the penitent, the cow)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/papa-punya-and-prayaschitta.md) — (Manusmṛti 11.212–213; Parāśara-smṛti)
- **The Panchagni Vidya and the two after-death paths** — The 'doctrine of the five fires' sets out the devayana (path of light, no return) and pitryana (path of smoke, returning via the moon) by which the soul travels at death sorted by knowledge and conduct → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/purusharthas-and-karma-samsara-moksha.md) — (Chandogya Upanishad 5.3-5.10; Brihadaranyaka Upanishad 6.2)
- **The Panjurli–Guliga double-nēma** — the just, forgiving boar-daiva and the fierce blood-taking watcher are worshipped as one complementary pair, Panjurli passing judgement and Guliga enforcing it _(actors: Panjurli, Guliga)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/panjurli-guliga-tulu-daivas.md) — (Tuḷu Bhūta-Kōla liturgy) [regional]
- **The parrot's seventy-night vigil** — a household parrot, entrusted with a merchant's lonely wife, holds her back from adultery by telling one cliff-hanger tale each night for seventy nights until her husband returns _(actors: the parrot, Prabhāvatī, Madana Vinoda)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/shuka-saptati.md) — (Śukasaptati, frame)
- **The parrot's stratagem of indirection** — the parrot grants the legitimacy of desire but breaks each tale off at its crisis, asking whether Prabhāvatī has the wit to escape as its heroine did, so that waiting for the ending saves the night _(actors: the parrot, Prabhāvatī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/shuka-saptati.md) — (Śukasaptati, frame)
- **The pativratā who out-gives a pativratā** — the Mārkaṇḍeya charter-myth resolves one faithful wife's world-freezing vow not by breaking her but through a second faithful wife, Anasūyā, whose compassion earns the Datta boon _(actors: Anasūyā, Kauśika's wife)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/anasuya-halted-sunrise.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa XVI, pativratā-māhātmya)
- **The peacock bathed at the aśvamedha** — at the avabhṛtha ablutions of the Kāśī king's horse-sacrifice Śaibyā bathes her peacock and reminds him of all his births; he resigns the body and is reborn a man of distinction _(actors: Śaibyā, Śatadhanu, the king of Kāśī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shatadhanu-shaibya.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, III.18)
- **The peerless paṇḍit returns home** — Satyavrata becomes a poet and scholar without rival, recites the Satyavrata mantram, and is welcomed back with honour by his estranged father Devadatta _(actors: Satyavrata, Devadatta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/satyavrata-aim-bija.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa III.11)
- **The penance of Triśiras** — a three-headed asura performs severe tapas to Śiva on the rock and wins his boon, giving the town the etymology Trisirapuram → Tiruchirāppaḷḷi (a Śaiva penitent, not the Rāmāyaṇa Triśiras) _(actors: Triśiras, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruchirappalli-rockfort-thayumanavar.md) — (Sthala-purāṇa) [disputed]
- **The plural Avvaiyār tradition** — The title Avvaiyār ('venerable matron') was borne by at least three or four distinct Tamil women poets across the Sangam, Chola, and later eras, later fused into one venerated poet-saint _(actors: Avvaiyār (multiple poets); Tamil tradition)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/avvaiyar.md) — (Auvaiyar — Wikipedia)
- **The Prājāpatya krīcchra** — the standard arduous penance: twelve days of graded fasting — three mornings-only, three evenings-only, three days of unsolicited food, three of total fast _(actors: the penitent)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/papa-punya-and-prayaschitta.md) — (Manusmṛti 11.212)
- **The Puṇyaka rite taught by Umā** — the vow's mythic charter: Umā (Pārvatī) first laid down the Puṇyaka regulations to an assembly of celestial ladies, Arundhatī and the rest, in ages past _(actors: Umā, Arundhatī, celestial women)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/parijata-harana.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva 78–82)
- **The reciprocal curses — Nārada's monkey face** — the wronged Parvata curses Nārada's beautiful face to become an ape's, and Nārada counter-curses Parvata never to reach heaven _(actors: Parvata, Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/suvarnashthivin-srinjaya-narada-parvata.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva; Ganguli XXX)
- **The recognition-verse — 'seven hunters among the Daśārṇas…'** — the reborn birds/brahmins recite a mnemonic gāthā naming every rung of the ladder, and whoever hears it swoons under the flood of recollection — a stanza later quoted across vairāgya literature _(actors: the reborn souls)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pitrikalpa-seven-brahmins-brahmadatta.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Pitṛ-kalpa 23–24; Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha)
- **The rival Janaka cousins** — of the Videha line Keśidhvaja masters Self-knowledge (ātma-jñāna) and Khāṇḍikya the science of works (karma), and Keśidhvaja conquers his cousin and drives him into forest exile _(actors: Keśidhvaja, Khāṇḍikya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khandikya-keshidhvaja.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa VI.6)
- **The rival origins of the discus** — competing traditions derive the Sudarśana instead from Viśvakarmā's forging of Sūrya's pared radiance, from Viṣṇu's seizure of it from Hayagrīva, or as his eternal attribute — sectarian claims held side by side _(actors: Viṣṇu, Śiva, Viśvakarmā, Sūrya, Hayagrīva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vishnu-lotus-eye-sudarshana.md) — (Purāṇic / Rāmāyaṇa traditions)
- **The river feeds her son fish** — Sarasvatī stays Sārasvata from fleeing and sustains him on fish through the drought, so he alone keeps his Vedic study and offerings unbroken _(actors: Sarasvatī, Sārasvata)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sarasvata-upakhyana-vriddhakumari.md) — (Mbh Śalya Parva 9.51; Ganguli LI)
- **The rudrākṣa-wearing monkey and cock reborn as princes** — Mahānandā's pet monkey and rooster, adorned with rudrākṣa, are reborn as Prince Sudharmā and the minister's son Tāraka, retaining their craving for the bead — the rudrākṣa's potency even for animals _(actors: Sudharmā, Tāraka, Parāśara, Bhadrasena)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/brahmottara-khanda-vrata-tales.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Brāhmottara-khaṇḍa 20)
- **The Ṣaḍgarbha curse-chain** — the recurring pattern of fallen divinities passing through successive asura births until a curse is spent and the soul is redeemed, cataloged across Bhāgavata, Harivaṃśa and Devī-Bhāgavata with variant demon fathers (Hiraṇyakaśipu vs Kālanemi) _(actors: the six sons of Marīci, Brahmā, Kaṃsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/devaki-six-sons-shadgarbha.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.85; Harivaṃśa, Viṣṇu Parva 47–48)
- **The Sapta-Vitaṅka distribution** — Muchukunda enshrines the seven Vitaṅka images across the Kāverī delta (Tiruvārūr, Tirunallāṟu, Nāgapaṭṭinam, Tirukkāraiyil, Tirukkuvaḷai, Tiruvaimūr, Vedāraṇyam), each with its own dance of the Tyāgarāja bronze _(actors: Muchukunda)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruvarur-thyagaraja-kamalalaya.md) — (Kṣetra-māhātmya; cultureandheritage.org)
- **The Saptarṣi tell Himavān of Padmā** — to overcome Himavān's fear of marrying young Pārvatī to the aged, fearsome ascetic Śiva, Vasiṣṭha and the seven sages narrate the exemplum of the faithful wife Padmā _(actors: Vasiṣṭha, the Saptarṣi, Himavān)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/padma-pippalada.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Pārvatī-khaṇḍa 34–36)
- **The Satī–Rāma chronological knot and its cyclic resolution** — Tradition harmonizes primordial Satī meeting Tretā-Yuga Rāma by holding that Rāma's līlā recurs in every cycle, as witnessed by the immortal Kākabhuśuṇḍi _(actors: Satī, Rāma, Śiva, Kākabhuśuṇḍi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sati-tests-rama.md) — (Rāmcaritmānas, Bāl- & Uttara-kāṇḍa framing)
- **The Sautrāmaṇī charter** — the beheading and cure become the aetiology of the Sautrāmaṇī surā-offering to Indra Sutrāman with the Aśvins and Sarasvatī, the rare Vedic sanction of the intoxicant _(actors: Indra, the Aśvins, Sarasvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indra-namuchi.md) — (Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa 1.7.1.6–8)
- **The self-emptying dāna-king archetype** — Rantideva stands with Śibi as the paired exemplar of the king who gives away everything and is tested by gods in disguise, a compassion-over-mokṣa ideal quoted across Hindu, Buddhist, and Jain gift-literature _(actors: Rantideva, Śibi, the testing gods)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rantideva.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.21; Mahābhārata Droṇa/Śānti Parvas)
- **The self-revelation of Poṭṭan Daivam** — after out-reasoning the master of scripture, Śiva sheds the disguise; the ācārya bows and the untouchable's body becomes the throne of god _(actors: Śiva, the Brahmin)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/pottan-theyyam.md) — (Poṭṭan thoṭṭam)
- **The serpent Arbuda saves Nandin** — The nāga Arbuda raises his hood to bear Śiva's falling bull Nandin out of an earth-chasm, and the hill is named Arbuda-giri after him _(actors: Arbuda (nāga), Nandin (Śiva's bull), Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mount-abu-arbuda-achaleshwar.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Prabhāsa-khaṇḍa (Arbuda-khaṇḍa); Arbuda sthala tradition)
- **The seven students eat their teacher's cow** — during a famine seven brahmin disciples of the sage Garga slaughter the milch-cow they were set to guard, incurring go-hatyā and betrayal of trust _(actors: the seven brahmins, Garga, the cow)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pitrikalpa-seven-brahmins-brahmadatta.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Pitṛ-kalpa 21; Matsya Purāṇa 20)
- **The seventieth night and the parrot's plea** — on the seventieth night Madana returns with the sin never consummated, Prabhāvatī confesses, and the parrot's last tale intercedes for her forgiveness _(actors: Madana Vinoda, Prabhāvatī, the parrot)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/shuka-saptati.md) — (Śukasaptati, frame)
- **The severed head cries 'friend-betrayer'** — Namuci's foam-severed head rolls after Indra reproaching him as mitra-ghna (friend-slayer) for breaking the sworn oath _(actors: Indra, Namuci's head)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indra-namuchi.md) — (Mahābhārata Śalya Parva 9.43)
- **The she-ass reveals Mātaṅga's birth** — as Mātaṅga whips an ass-colt on the road to a sacrifice, the mother she-ass consoles her calf by disclosing that the driver is no Brāhmaṇa but Caṇḍāla-born, begotten on a Brāhmaṇa woman by a low-caste man _(actors: Mātaṅga, the she-ass)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matanga-vitahavya-varna-upakhyanas.md) — (Anuśāsana Parva ~28)
- **The showcase of bīja-mantra power** — the DBP holds Satyavrata's transformation up as proof that the Goddess's seed-syllable works by its own sound-power even when uttered in ignorance, the Śākta twin of Ajāmila's saving Name _(actors: Sarasvatī, Satyavrata, Ajāmila)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/satyavrata-aim-bija.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa III.11)
- **The signs of unexpiated sin** — Manu teaches that sin not discharged surfaces in the next birth as disease or deformity: the brahmin-killer consumptive, the drinker black-toothed, the gold-thief with diseased nails — the explicit weld of prāyaścitta to karma _(actors: reborn sinners)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/papa-punya-and-prayaschitta.md) — (Manusmṛti 11.49–53)
- **The Śiva-kavaca armours Bhadrāyu** — wearing the unfailing armour-hymn and wielding a divine sword and conch, Bhadrāyu single-handedly routs the Magadha army, rescues Vajrabāhu, and weds Kīrtimālinī, daughter of Candrāṅgada and Sīmantinī _(actors: Bhadrāyu, Kīrtimālinī, Candrāṅgada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/brahmottara-khanda-vrata-tales.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Brāhmottara-khaṇḍa 12–13)
- **The sixfold chain of narrators** — the epic wraps the tale in a deep transmission — Cyavana to Janaka to Nārada to Indra to the brāhmaṇas to Bhīṣma to Yudhiṣṭhira — staging uñchavṛtti as settled, handed-down doctrine _(actors: Cyavana, Janaka, Nārada, Indra, Bhīṣma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/unchavritti-brahmin-naga-padmanabha.md) — (Śānti Parva CCCLXV)
- **The sixth-night destiny-writing** — Ṣaṣṭhī (with Vidhātā) visits the birth-chamber on the sixth night to write the infant's fate on its forehead, the rationale of the sūtikā-ṣaṣṭhī rite _(actors: Ṣaṣṭhī, Vidhātā, the newborn)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/prakriti-khanda-goddess-origins.md) — (folk tradition; Devī-Bhāgavata IX.44–47)
- **The sky-born horse Kuvalaya** — as the Dānava wrecks sage Gālava's sacrifice in beast-shapes, a tireless heaven-sent steed descends and King Śatrujit's son Ṛtadhvaja mounts it, taking the name Kuvalayāśva _(actors: Gālava, Śatrujit, Ṛtadhvaja/Kuvalayāśva, the nāga Aśvatara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kuvalayashva-madalasa.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa, Canto XX)
- **The slaying of Durgama** — Durgama attacks to reclaim the Vedas; the Goddess emits an army of Śaktis, fells him with her bow, and absorbs his tejas — and because she slew Durgama she is famed as Durgā _(actors: the Devī, Durgama, the Śaktis)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shakambhari-shatakshi-durgamasura.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.28)
- **The slaying of Mātaṅga** — appealed to by Brahmā and the devas, Tuljā Bhavānī destroys the demon Mātaṅga who oppressed gods and sages, a second demon-cycle fixed to the seat _(actors: the Devī, Mātaṅga, Brahmā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tuljapur-tulja-bhavani.md) — (sthala-tradition) [folk] [regional]
- **The slaying of Mura and the origin of Ekādaśī** — a śakti born of Viṣṇu's body slays the demon Mura and becomes the goddess of the Ekādaśī fast, founding the year-round vrata cycle _(actors: Viṣṇu, Mura, Ekādaśī-devī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/padma-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa)
- **The Solah Somvār dice-game curse** — Pārvatī beats Śiva at dice, curses the temple priest with leprosy for a partial lie, and apsaras teach him the sixteen-Monday vrata that cures him and chains onward through Pārvatī, Kārttikeya and a brahmin-turned-king _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, the temple priest, Kārttikeya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vrata-kathas-catalog.md) — (Solah-Somvār pothī kathā) [folk] [disputed]
- **The somakrayaṇa and the beaten Soma-seller** — the gods' recovery of the price becomes the ritual buying of King Soma with the tawny cow, followed by reclaiming goods and driving off the Soma-seller _(actors: the sacrificer, the Soma-seller, King Soma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vac-and-the-soma-purchase.md) — (Aitareya Brāhmaṇa 1.27)
- **The son who is the father** — the boy-Bhairava, though called a son, is Śiva himself in juvenile shape — the guardian the god emanates when protection is needed, not a separate deity _(actors: Śiva, Vaṭuka Bhairava)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vatuka-bhairava-origin.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Kotirudra-saṃhitā ch. 13)
- **The sparing at dawn and Śiva's grace** — the whole deer family returns true to its word, each offering to die first; the hunter, purified by the unwitting vrata, spares them all and both hunter and deer receive Śiva's grace _(actors: Gurudruh, the deer family, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shivaratri-hunter-legend.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṁhitā 40)
- **The speaking goat halts its own sacrifice** — a goat about to be immolated reveals it was a brahmin cursed by Caṇḍikā and recounts how Gītā chapter 9 once shielded King Candraśarman from the demons Pāpa and Nindā _(actors: the goat-brahmin, Mādhava, Candraśarman)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavad-gita/gita-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Gītā-Māhātmya 9)
- **The Sthāṇu-Māl-Ayaṉ liṅga** — a single self-manifest liṅga worshipped at once as Śiva (Sthāṇu), Viṣṇu (Māl) and Brahmā (Ayaṉ), making Suchindram a rare joint Hari–Hara–Ayaṉ shrine _(actors: Śiva, Viṣṇu, Brahmā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/suchindram-sthanumalayan.md) — (Sucīndra-sthala-māhātmya; temple usage)
- **The stotra recited against Saturn's afflictions** — the Daśaratha Śani-stotra becomes a pan-Indian remedial hymn chanted on Saturdays, on Śani Jayantī, and during sāḍe-sātī at shrines like Tirunallar and Śani Śiṅgṇāpur _(actors: Śani, devotees)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dasharatha-shani-stotra-legend.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Nāgara-khaṇḍa; stotra tradition)
- **The sudden cool wind in Naraka** — after a hundred years of torment a fragrant cool breeze unaccountably eases every condemned soul, the unexplained prelude to a righteous king's arrival _(actors: the damned, Sumati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vipashchit-in-hell.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa XIII)
- **The svayambhu ice-liṅga forms** — A self-manifest column of ice venerated as Śiva's liṅga forms in the Amarnāth cave and waxes/wanes with the moon _(actors: Śiva (as ice-liṅga))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/amarnath-ice-lingam-and-amar-katha.md) — (Amareśvara sthala-māhātmya; living Amarnāth Yātrā tradition)
- **The svayambhū liṅga of the Rock Fort** — the maternal Lord is worshipped as a self-manifest liṅga on the living Archaean granite, with the consort Mattuvār Kuḻali (Sugandha Kuntaḷāmbikā) in her own shrine _(actors: Śiva, Mattuvār Kuḻali)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruchirappalli-rockfort-thayumanavar.md) — (Sthala-purāṇa)
- **The svayambhū Vināyaka refuses to be moved** — the villagers cannot bale the well dry nor lift the self-manifest Gaṇeśa image, the spring endlessly refilling, so the god fixes himself in situ submerged in his own sanctum-water _(actors: Kāṇipākam villagers, Varasiddhi Vināyaka)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanipakam-varasiddhi-vinayaka.md) — (Kāṇipākam Sthala-Purāṇa)
- **The tapo-bhaṅga pattern (penance broken by an apsaras)** — Māṇḍakarṇi joins the recurring type in which the gods send celestial nymphs to dissolve a threatening mortal austerity, kin to Viśvāmitra–Menakā and Ṛśyaśṛṅga _(actors: the devas, apsaras, tapasvin-sages)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mandakarni-and-panchapsaras-lake.md) — (comparative; Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa)
- **The teacher's fee refused** — returning to discharge the debt of instruction, Keśidhvaja offers any prize, and Khāṇḍikya's counsellors demand the kingdom back, but he declines empire as transient _(actors: Keśidhvaja, Khāṇḍikya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khandikya-keshidhvaja.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa VI.6)
- **The ten sons of Indu become ten Brahmās** — Ten brahmin brothers resolve in meditation "I am Brahmā" and each becomes a world-creating Brahmā, ten complete cosmoses arising from ten sustained convictions _(actors: the Aindava brothers, Brahmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yoga-vasishtha-tales-catalog.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha III, Utpatti prakaraṇa)
- **The terrestrial Vaitaraṇī at Viraja-kṣetra (Jajpur)** — The earthly Baitaraṇī/Vaitaraṇī river of Odisha, flowing past Jajpur (Viraja-kṣetra), is sanctified as a holy ford whose very name doubles the underworld death-river _(actors: the river Vaitaraṇī; Jajpur/Viraja-kṣetra; pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaitarani-jajpur-viraja-kshetra.md) — (Mahābhārata Tīrtha-yātrā Parva; Padma & Skanda Purāṇa māhātmyas; regional gazetteers)
- **The thoṭṭam song draws the god into the dancer** — across the 400-plus open-ended theyyam pantheon of North Malabar, the sung thoṭṭam narrates each deity's birth and deeds and, with the placing of the mudi headdress, marks the god entering the possessed Dalit performer who is then venerated as the living deity across caste lines _(actors: theyyam performers, Vaṇṇān, Malayan, Vēlan communities)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/theyyam-deities-and-thottam-myths.md) — (Theyyam / Kaliyāṭṭam oral tradition) [folk]
- **The thousand lotuses for the thousand names** — Viṣṇu recites Śiva's thousand names and lays down one lotus from the Mānasa lake for each, a perfect thousand-and-thousand pūjā _(actors: Viṣṇu, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vishnu-lotus-eye-sudarshana.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṃhitā 34)
- **The Three Dogs of Dharma** — the Ayodhyā petitioner-dog is distinguished from Yudhiṣṭhira's faithful hound (Dharma/Yama in disguise) and from Yama's four-eyed hounds Śarvara and Śyāma, three separate dog-motifs kept apart _(actors: the dog, Yudhiṣṭhira, Yama, Śarvara, Śyāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramas-justice-the-dog-and-the-mendicant.md) — (Mbh Mahāprasthānika Parva; Ṛgveda 10.14) [scholarly]
- **The tiger and the sacrificial cow** — during Keśidhvaja's rite to conquer death a tiger kills the milch-cow whose milk the offering requires, defiling and halting the sacrifice _(actors: Keśidhvaja, the tiger)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khandikya-keshidhvaja.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa VI.6)
- **The tiger test of Bhadrāyu** — Śiva and Pārvatī, disguised as a tiger, its Brahmin victim, and an aged suppliant, demand Bhadrāyu's queen and then his life; when he surrenders both they reveal themselves and grant Śiva-sāyujya to him, Kīrtimālinī, and their parents _(actors: Bhadrāyu, Kīrtimālinī, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/brahmottara-khanda-vrata-tales.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Brāhmottara-khaṇḍa 14)
- **The traveller and the tiger** — an old tiger feigns piety and dangles a gold bracelet to lure a greedy traveller into quicksand, where he is devoured — greed blinds prudence _(actors: the old tiger, the greedy traveller)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/hitopadesha.md) — (Hitopadeśa I, embedded)
- **The trifling sin revealed** — Yama's officer tells Vipaścit his chariot to heaven waits, and that he burns here only for one lapse: neglecting his wife Pīvarī's fertile season out of infatuation with Kaikeyī _(actors: Vipaścit, Yama's messenger, Pīvarī, Kaikeyī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vipashchit-in-hell.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa XIV)
- **The twelve devāsura saṅgrāmas enumerated** — The Matsya Purāṇa counts the twelve primordial god-demon wars as a fixed roster, from Nārasiṃha to Kolāhala _(actors: the devas, the Daityas and Dānavas, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/twelve-devasura-battles.md) — (Matsya Purāṇa, 47.40–75)
- **The twelve-year drought scatters the ṛṣis** — a dvādaśa-vārṣikī famine drives the starving sages from their hermitages and the Vedas are forgotten from human memory _(actors: the ṛṣis)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sarasvata-upakhyana-vriddhakumari.md) — (Mbh Śalya Parva 9.51; Ganguli LI)
- **The twelve-year skull vow (mahāvrata)** — the brahmin-killer's expiation: twelve years in a forest hut on alms, bearing the slain man's skull as a banner and proclaiming the deed, or dying for a brāhmaṇa in battle _(actors: the penitent, the pariṣad)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/papa-punya-and-prayaschitta.md) — (Manusmṛti 11.73–90)
- **The two apsaras turned to badarī trees** — celestial girls who broke Satyatapas' penance and were cursed to treehood are freed when the saint Bharata recalls Gītā chapter 4 _(actors: Satyatapas, two apsaras, Bharata, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavad-gita/gita-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Gītā-Māhātmya 4)
- **The two daily twilights born** — Sandhyā casts her body into Medhātithi's Jyotiṣṭoma fire; the Sun splits it into Prātaḥ-sandhyā (dawn, for the gods) and Sāyaṃ-sandhyā (dusk, for the ancestors), the origin of sandhyā-vandana _(actors: Sandhyā, Sūrya, Medhātithi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sandhya-arundhati.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Sati-khaṇḍa 7)
- **The two effulgences merge** — the Nāga's most astonishing sight: a radiant Being of equal splendour approaches Sūrya and their two energies fuse so wholly that none can tell which was which _(actors: Padmanābha, Sūrya, the radiant Being)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/unchavritti-brahmin-naga-padmanabha.md) — (Śānti Parva CCCLXII)
- **The two Līlās meet, one liberated** — the first and second Līlās are revealed as one consciousness in two projected forms; knowing every world to be mind, Līlā lives on liberated beside the revived Padma, free of her original fear _(actors: Līlā, the second Līlā, Padma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/lila-story-yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha III ~55–58)
- **The two Śaṅkhacūḍas kept apart** — the corpus distinguishes the minor Vraja Yakṣa slain by Kṛṣṇa's fist from the cosmic daitya Śaṅkhacūḍa of the Tulasī cycle, sharing only the name 'conch-crest' _(actors: Śaṅkhacūḍa the Yakṣa, Śaṅkhacūḍa the daitya, Tulasī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sudarshana-shankhachuda-vyoma.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.34; Brahma-Vaivarta Purāṇa)
- **The two-serpent icon of Kavlem** — Śāntā-Durgā is enshrined holding a nāga in each hand, the two serpents read as Viṣṇu and Śiva whom she grasps and holds apart as mediatrix _(actors: Śāntā-Durgā, the two nāgas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/goa-shantadurga-mangeshi-kuladevatas.md) — (Kavlem temple tradition)
- **The unteachable Utathya** — born as Utathya, the cursed brahmin cannot learn a word in twelve years, fails even the daily sandhyā rites, and becomes the laughing-stock of his village _(actors: Utathya, Devadatta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/satyavrata-aim-bija.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa III.10)
- **The Vedas restored** — with Durgama dead the Goddess recovers the four Vedas and returns them to Brahmā and the Brāhmaṇas, re-establishing yajña, rain and dharma _(actors: the Devī, Brahmā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shakambhari-shatakshi-durgamasura.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.28)
- **The voice from the corpse** — as Bhadrā laments over his body an incorporeal voice — the dead king's own generative will — grants her a boon, bidding her lie beside the corpse on the eighth or fourteenth lunar night _(actors: Vyuṣitāśva (as corpse-voice), Bhadrā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyushitashva-bhadra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva; Ganguli §CXXI)
- **The Void corrected, Hemacūḍa awakens** — reaching a blank thought-free absorption he mistakes for the goal, Hemacūḍa is corrected by Hemalekhā — the knower of the void remains — and realizes the Self as ever-present awareness, becoming a jīvanmukta while still a prince _(actors: Hemacūḍa, Hemalekhā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tripura-rahasya.md) — (Tripurā Rahasya, Jñāna-khaṇḍa)
- **The vow of Nārada and Parvata** — the sky-roaming Devarṣi Nārada and his nephew Parvata wander bound by a compact of total disclosure, concealing no thought from each other, and lodge with King Sṛñjaya _(actors: Nārada, Parvata, Sṛñjaya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/suvarnashthivin-srinjaya-narada-parvata.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva; Ganguli XXX)
- **The Vṛddhakanyā-tīrtha benediction** — regaining youth for the wedding night, the maiden at dawn grants that one night at her ford equals fifty-eight years' brahmacarya, then casts off her body and ascends to heaven _(actors: the old maid, Śṛṅgavat)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sarasvata-upakhyana-vriddhakumari.md) — (Mbh Śalya Parva 9.52; Ganguli LII)
- **The Vṛndāvana promise before Gopāla** — an old brāhmaṇa, moved by a poor youth's faithful pilgrimage-service, vows to give his daughter in marriage, swearing the pledge before the Gopāla Deity as witness _(actors: old brāhmaṇa, young brāhmaṇa, Gopāla)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sakshi-gopal-witness-krishna.md) — (Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya 5)
- **The war and the burning-ground** — a battle between Tāladhvaja and a rival king slays all of Suśīlā's sons and grandsons, and she breaks down wailing over their pyres at the cremation ground _(actors: Suśīlā, Tāladhvaja)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-maya-taladhvaja.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa VI.30–31)
- **The war and Vidūratha's fall** — a rival monarch invades Vidūratha's kingdom; watched unseen from the sky by Sarasvatī and the first Līlā, Vidūratha is mortally wounded and dies _(actors: Vidūratha, Sarasvatī, Līlā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/lila-story-yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha III ~42–52)
- **The war of swans and peacocks** — swan-king Hiraṇyagarbha of Karpūra-dvīpa and peacock-king Citravarṇa go to war, the vulture Dūradarśī counselling the six-fold policy of statecraft _(actors: Hiraṇyagarbha, Citravarṇa, Dūradarśī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/hitopadesha.md) — (Hitopadeśa III, Vigraha)
- **The water-fairy guardian of Chandra Tāl** — Spitian folklore sets a supernatural lake-guardian (a jal-parī or protective spirit) over the moon-lake, said to appear to and mislead travellers — a spirit-lore layer parallel to the Mahābhārata charter _(actors: the lake-guardian, travellers)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/chandra-taal-yudhishthira-svargarohana.md) — (Spiti folklore) [folk]
- **The wet/dry, day/night compact** — to recover his strength Indra swears a pact with Namuci to slay him "neither by day nor by night, neither with the wet nor with the dry" _(actors: Indra, Namuci)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indra-namuchi.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 12.7.3; Mahābhārata Śalya 9.43)
- **The winning of Sūryā** — in the charioteers' race the Aśvins win Sūryā, Daughter of the Sun, who mounts their car as bride — the Indic Sun-maiden myth behind the wedding hymn _(actors: Aśvins, Sūryā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ashvins.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.116.17; 10.85)
- **The wonder of Sūrya** — from his turns bearing the Sun's car Padmanābha recounts that all creatures flow from Sūrya, that ascetics and gods dwell in his rays, and that the Supreme Soul gazes on the worlds from within the solar disc _(actors: Padmanābha, Sūrya, the Supreme Soul)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/unchavritti-brahmin-naga-padmanabha.md) — (Śānti Parva CCCLXII)
- **The wooden brothers ride to war** — Jagannātha on a black horse and Balabhadra on a white ride out of the Purī sanctum as two soldiers ahead of the Gajapati army, the dark and fair images made mounted flesh _(actors: Jagannātha, Balabhadra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kanchi-kaveri-jagannatha-legend.md) — (Kāñcī-Kāverī; Mādaḷā Pāñji) [regional]
- **The wooden Lord's hand reaches out** — before the temple's eyes the carved Jagannātha image stretches out its arm and takes the untouchable Dāsiā's coconut directly, refusing what the high-caste bearer would have offered _(actors: Jagannātha, Dāsiā Baurī, the pilgrim)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/jagannatha-bhakta-legends.md) — (Odia bhakta-caritra) [oral tradition] [folk]
- **Thee Chāmuṇḍi, the goddess who sits in fire** — the fierce Chāmuṇḍi fire-goddess of the North-Malabar kāvus is worshipped through the theyyam's ember-ordeal, her thoṭṭam and rite placing her among the amma-theyyams of the Kerala Bhadrakālī sphere _(actors: Thee Chāmuṇḍi)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/theyyam-deities-and-thottam-myths.md) — (Thee Chāmuṇḍi thoṭṭam) [folk]
- **Tirukkaḍavūr — the conquest of Death** — the Amṛtaghaṭeśvarar shrine where Śiva as Kālāntaka bursts from the liṅga to kick down Yama and grant the boy Mārkaṇḍeya deathless youth; seat of the Mahā-Mṛtyuñjaya _(actors: Śiva, Mārkaṇḍeya, Yama)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-virattanam-eight-feat-shrines.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa; Liṅga Purāṇa)
- **Tirukkōvilūr — the slaying of Andhaka** — the feat-shrine on the Teṉ Peṇṇai where Śiva impales the blind demon Andhaka on his trident while the Mātṛkās drink his cloning blood _(actors: Śiva, Andhaka, Saptamātṛkā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-virattanam-eight-feat-shrines.md) — (Matsya, Kūrma, Liṅga Purāṇa)
- **Tiruvatikai — the burning of Tripura** — the shrine localising Śiva as Tripurāntaka, who incinerated the three flying asura cities with a single arrow; also where Appar reconverted to Śaivism _(actors: Śiva, Tārakākṣa, Vidyunmālī, Kamalākṣa, Appar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-virattanam-eight-feat-shrines.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa Yuddha-khaṇḍa; sthala-purāṇa)
- **Tiruvirkuḍi — the slaying of Jalandhara** — the shrine marking Śiva's decapitation of the asura Jalandhara with the razor-edge of his cakra after Vṛndā's vow is broken _(actors: Śiva, Jalandhara, Vṛndā, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-virattanam-eight-feat-shrines.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa Yuddha-khaṇḍa; Padma Purāṇa)
- **Tripurā as the self-aware Absolute (Cit-Śakti)** — Dattātreya expounds the Goddess Tripurā, who transcends the three states, bodies, worlds, and guṇas, as self-luminous consciousness whose self-knowing throb (vimarśa) projects and reabsorbs the cosmos _(actors: Dattātreya, Tripurā/Lalitā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tripura-rahasya.md) — (Tripurā Rahasya, Jñāna-khaṇḍa)
- **Tripurantaka burns the three flying cities (Kartik Purnima charter)** — Shiva as Tripurantaka destroys the three flying cities (Tripura) of the asuras with a single arrow of the Pinaka bow, commemorated on Kartik Shukla Purnima as Tripurari/Tripuri Purnima _(actors: Shiva (Tripurantaka), the Tripura asuras, Brahma (charioteer))_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa (Rudra Saṃhitā); Mahābhārata (Karṇa Parva))
- **Trita slays the three-headed Triśiras** — urged and aided by Indra, Trita Āptya smites Viśvarūpa Triśiras, the three-headed son of Tvaṣṭṛ, strikes off his heads, and drives out the penned cows _(actors: Trita, Indra, Viśvarūpa/Triśiras, Tvaṣṭṛ)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/trita-aptya.md) — (Ṛgveda 10.8.8–9)
- **Tvaritā comes in haste** — at the cry of the sage-widow Anubhūti, the Goddess descends *instantly* (tvarita) on the Yamunācalā hill, the etymology that names Tuḷjā and her town Tuljapur _(actors: Anubhūti, Kardama, the Devī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tuljapur-tulja-bhavani.md) — (Tuljā-māhātmya, attrib. Skanda Purāṇa Sahyādri-khaṇḍa)
- **Two brahmarṣis reduced to squabbling birds** — the Śākta reading of the Āḍi-Baka war: if masters of tapas can be dragged into a ten-thousand-year animal war by ego, the guṇas of Prakṛti bind every embodied being _(actors: Vasiṣṭha, Viśvāmitra, the Devī as Māyā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/adi-baka-bird-war.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata VI.13)
- **Two Khaṭvāṅgas disambiguated** — the Ikṣvāku king who wins liberation in an hour is distinct from the khaṭvāṅga skull-staff, the bed-post club wielded by Śiva-Bhairava, Kālī and Kāpālika ascetics as conquerors of death _(actors: Khaṭvāṅga, Śiva-Bhairava)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/khatvanga-one-muhurta.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.9; Āyudhapuruṣa iconography)
- **Two māyā-cow legends of Gautama** — the same phantom-cow framing drives two incompatible endings: the Brahma Purāṇa's Godāvarī descent from Śiva's locks (no curse) versus the Devī-Bhāgavata's curse of Devī-aversion (no river) _(actors: Gautama, the jealous sages)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautama-curse-on-the-brahmins.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata XII.8–9; cf. Brahma Purāṇa, Gautamī-māhātmya)
- **Tyranny of Maṇi and Malla** — The daitya brothers Maṇi and Malla ravage the sages of the Deccan forest, who appeal to Śiva for deliverance _(actors: Maṇi, Malla, the ṛṣis, Śiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khandoba-martanda-bhairava.md) — (Mallāri Māhātmya)
- **Umā's annual homecoming (Āgomoni)** — each autumn Umā/Gaurī returns from Kailāsa to her natal home of Himālaya and Menakā with Gaṇeśa, Kārtikeya, Lakṣmī and Sarasvatī for the pūjā days, departing at visarjan _(actors: Umā/Durgā, Menakā, Himālaya, Śiva)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/durga-puja-bengal.md) — (Bengali āgomoni tradition; Śākta padāvalī) [regional]
- **Upamanyu denied milk — the childhood vow** — Given flour paste in water instead of milk, the boy Upamanyu learns from his mother that milk flows only from Śiva's grace and resolves to win Śiva by austerity _(actors: Upamanyu, his mother)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/upamanyu.md) — (Mahābhārata, Anuśāsana Parva XIV)
- **Upamanyu's Pañcākṣara tapas** — The boy withdraws to the Himālaya and performs ferocious penance with ceaseless japa of the five-syllable mantra Namaḥ Śivāya _(actors: Upamanyu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/upamanyu.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Śatarudra-saṃhitā 32)
- **Uṣas carries bad dreams to Trita** — worshippers pray Dawn to bear away their nightmares and evil to Trita Āptya, the scapegoat-god who wears the bad dreams like ornaments on his limbs _(actors: Uṣas, Trita)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/trita-aptya.md) — (Ṛgveda 8.47.13–18)
- **Uttaṅka's daughter freed by Ṛṣi Pañcamī** — a widowed daughter's worm-afflicted body is traced to a former birth's breach of rajasvalā rules and released by the Saptarṣi-honoring vrata _(actors: Uttaṅka, Suśīlā, the Saptarṣis, Brahmā, King Sitāśva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vrata-kathas-catalog.md) — (Bhaviṣyottara Purāṇa [attrib.], Ṛṣi-pañcamī-kathā) [late text]
- **Vāc fashioned into a woman to buy Soma** — lacking the Soma the Gandharvas guard, the gods shape Speech (Vāc) into a woman and send her as the buying-price, and she returns to them bringing the Soma _(actors: the devas, Vāc, the Gandharvas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vac-and-the-soma-purchase.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 3.2.4.3–4)
- **Vāc won back by the dance** — the Taittirīya recension has the gods give Vāc to the Gandharvas and recover her by dance and song, so women are fond of the dancer and singer _(actors: the devas, Vāc, the Gandharvas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vac-and-the-soma-purchase.md) — (Taittirīya Saṃhitā 6.1.6.5–6)
- **Vāg-devatās compose the thousand names** — at Lalitā's own command the eight goddesses of speech headed by Vaśinī compose and sing her secret thousand names before her throne in Śrīpura _(actors: Lalitā Tripurasundarī, Vaśinī, the eight Vāg-devatās)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/lalita-sahasranama.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa, Lalitopākhyāna)
- **Vaiṣṇavī's birth and vow** — The Goddess takes form as the maiden Vaiṣṇavī, an aṃśa of the Tridevī, and vows lifelong virginity and tapas until merging with Viṣṇu at the age's end _(actors: Vaiṣṇavī (Mātā Vaiṣṇo Devī), Ratnākar Sāgar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaishno-devi-trikuta-katra.md) — (Vaiṣṇo Devī sthala/folk legend; regional Trikūṭa-māhātmya)
- **Vaḻuvūr — the flaying of Gajāsura** — the feat-shrine of Gajasaṃhāra-mūrti, Śiva impaling and flaying the elephant-demon to wear its hide as Kṛttivāsas; home of the famed Cōḻa bronze _(actors: Śiva, Gajāsura)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-virattanam-eight-feat-shrines.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa; Liṅga Purāṇa; sthala-purāṇa)
- **Vandana raised from the pit** — the Aśvins draw the aged Vandana out of a pit into the light and restore his youth, like buried gold dug up and made to shine _(actors: Aśvins, Vandana)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ashvins.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.116.11; 1.117.5)
- **Vanmīkanāthar, the anthill-liṅga** — the beginningless swayambhū liṅga of Tiruvārūr manifests within an anthill (puṟṟu), named Puṟṟiḍaṅkoṇḍār by Appar _(actors: Śiva as Vanmīkanāthar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruvarur-thyagaraja-kamalalaya.md) — (Tēvāram; sthala-māhātmya)
- **Variant: Viṣṇu rigs the rematch** — in a parallel telling Śiva meets Viṣṇu, wins everything back, and Viṣṇu reveals his spirit had entered the dice — the game an illusion, the quarrel mere moha _(actors: Śiva, Viṣṇu, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shiva-parvati-dice-game.md) — (retelling tradition; cf. Skanda Purāṇa) [disputed]
- **Varuṇa demoted from Vedic sovereign to devotee** — the archaic Ṛgvedic lord of ṛta and cosmic order appears in Purāṇic form as mere water-guardian bowing to Krishna, dramatizing the relativizing of the Vedic pantheon beneath the Supreme _(actors: Varuṇa, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nanda-varuna-vaikuntha-darshana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.28; cf. Ṛgveda 7.86–89)
- **Varuṇa's noose and the debt of énas** — the earliest Hindu conception of sin: transgression (énas) against cosmic order (ṛta) is a debt (ṛṇa) owed to Varuṇa, who binds the guilty with his pāśa, and the penitent begs release incurred knowingly or unknowingly _(actors: Varuṇa, the transgressor)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/papa-punya-and-prayaschitta.md) — (Ṛgveda 7.86–89)
- **Vasu proclaims the Gaṅgā-māhātmya to the restored Mohinī** — After Brahmā restores Mohinī's body, the brāhmaṇa Vasu declares the Gaṅgā supreme among tīrthas — a mouthful of her water equal to a horse-sacrifice, sins fleeing the bather like a serpent's shed slough _(actors: Vasu, Mohinī, Brahmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-purana-ganga-tales.md) — (Nārada Purāṇa, Uttarabhāga 38–43)
- **Vasu wins eternal residence in Vṛndāvana** — Mohinī's priest-guide reports to Brahmā, gains the boon of dwelling in Vṛndāvana, performs austerities over five thousand years until the Lord grants him Vraja forever, and hears from Nārada the deeds Kṛṣṇa will work on earth _(actors: Vasu, Brahmā, Kṛṣṇa, Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-purana-ganga-tales.md) — (Nārada Purāṇa, Uttarabhāga 81)
- **Vaṭuka distinguished from Kāla-Bhairava** — the youthful guardian-boy carries no brahmahatyā narrative, standing apart from the skull-bearing adult Kāla-Bhairava born of severing Brahmā's fifth head _(actors: Vaṭuka Bhairava, Kāla-Bhairava, Brahmā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vatuka-bhairava-origin.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa; Skanda Purāṇa, Kāśī Khaṇḍa)
- **Vidala and Utpala's boon of immunity from man** — the two Daitya brothers, granted by Brahmā that no man may slay them, defeat the gods, who petition Brahmā and are told the Goddess alone will kill them _(actors: Vidala, Utpala, Brahmā, the gods)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yuddha-khanda-minor-vadhas.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Yuddha-khaṇḍa 59)
- **Vikuṇḍala's merit-transfer debate with the Devadūta** — two accidental Māgha baths in the Yamunā outweigh a wastrel's life, and Vikuṇḍala assigns their fruit to free his brother Śrīkuṇḍala from hell _(actors: Vikuṇḍala, Śrīkuṇḍala, Hemakuṇḍala, the devadūta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/masa-mahatmyas-kartika-magha-vaishakha.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa 125–129)
- **Vīrabāhu's embassy to the asura court** — Skanda sends Vīrabāhu, chief of the Nava-Vīrar, into Vīramahendrapuram with an ultimatum to free the devas — the Tamil parallel to Hanumān's Laṅkā-dūta _(actors: Vīrabāhu, Skanda, Sūrapadma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/surapadma-samharam-kanda-puranam.md) — (Kanda Purāṇam, Mahēndra-kāṇḍam)
- **Vīravara's loyal sacrifice** — the retainer Vīravara offers his consenting son Śaktidhara to goddess Caṇḍikā to save King Śūdraka's life, and the moved goddess restores the whole slain family _(actors: Vīravara, Śaktidhara, Śūdraka, Caṇḍikā, Rājya-Lakṣmī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/hitopadesha.md) — (Hitopadeśa III, embedded)
- **Vishnu appears to Nārada at Vishnuprayag** — the sage's austerities at the Dhauligaṅgā–Alaknandā confluence are rewarded with Vishnu's darśana, naming the highest prayāga _(actors: Nārada, Vishnu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-prayag-alaknanda-confluences.md) — (Wikipedia, Panch Prayag; Kedāra-khaṇḍa tradition)
- **Vishnu Gifts Ambarisha the Sudarshana** — Pleased by the king-devotee Ambarisha's flawless total sensory devotion, Vishnu personally awards him the Sudarshana-cakra as a perpetual guardian _(actors: Vishnu, Ambarisha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ambarisha-durvasa.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 9.4.21-22)
- **Vishnu instructs Garuda on death and the afterlife** — Garuda asks Vishnu about the fate of the dead; Vishnu describes the thumb-sized soul's departure, seizure by Yamaduutas, and the year-long road to Yama-loka — the framing teaching of the Preta-khanda _(actors: Vishnu, Garuda)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/garuda-purana.md) — (Garuda Purana (Preta-khanda))
- **Vishṇumūrti Theyyam avenges Palanthāyi Kaṇṇan** — the Narasiṁha-theyyam wraps the Man-Lion's slaying of Hiraṇyakaśipu around the local martyr Palanthāyi Kaṇṇan, a low-caste Viṣṇu-devotee murdered by the Kuruvāṭṭ landlord, whose house institutes the fire-theyyam to appease Viṣṇu's wrath _(actors: Vishṇumūrti, Narasiṁha, Palanthāyi Kaṇṇan, Kuruvāṭṭ Kurup)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/theyyam-deities-and-thottam-myths.md) — (Vishṇumūrti thoṭṭam) [folk]
- **Viṣṇu accepts the curse for the worlds** — rather than resisting, Viṣṇu willingly takes the curse upon himself, converting punishment into the theological mechanism of his descents _(actors: Viṣṇu, Bhṛgu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigus-curse-on-vishnu.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara 51)
- **Viṣṇu beheads Kāvyamātā with the Sudarśana** — to save the paralyzed Indra, Viṣṇu looses the discus and severs the head of Bhṛgu's wife, though she is a woman and a brāhmaṇa's spouse _(actors: Viṣṇu, Kāvyamātā, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigus-curse-on-vishnu.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa, Uttara 51; Devī-Bhāgavata IV.13)
- **Viṣṇu cursed to become the śālagrāma stone (Tulasī–Jālandhara legend)** — Viṣṇu deceives the chaste Vṛndā to break asura Jālandhara's invincibility; she curses Viṣṇu to dwell as a black stone and becomes the Tulasī plant _(actors: Viṣṇu, Vṛndā/Tulasī, Jālandhara, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/muktinath-gandaki-shalagrama-kshetra.md) — (Padma Purāṇa; Devī-Bhāgavata; Brahmavaivarta Purāṇa)
- **Viṣṇu humbles Garuḍa's pride** — when Garuḍa protests and boasts of bearing the worlds on one feather, Viṣṇu lays a single arm on him and crushes him to the ground, shattering his arrogance _(actors: Garuḍa, Viṣṇu, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/matali-sumukha-naga-bridegroom.md) — (Mahābhārata, Udyoga Parva 5.103)
- **Viṣṇu narrates the fate of the dead to Garuḍa** — the trans-temporal frame-dialogue in which the trembling eagle asks how souls escape Yama's torments and receives the Preta-khaṇḍa's doctrine, chartered by this tale _(actors: Viṣṇu, Garuḍa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/babhruvahana-and-the-preta.md) — (Garuḍa Purāṇa, Preta-khaṇḍa frame; Sāroddhāra 7.1–5)
- **Viṣṇu offers his own eye** — reflecting that his eye is itself a lotus, Viṣṇu plucks it out and offers it as the thousandth flower, the charter of his 'lotus-eyed' epithets (Kamala-nayana, Puṇḍarīkākṣa, Rājīva-locana) _(actors: Viṣṇu, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vishnu-lotus-eye-sudarshana.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṃhitā 34)
- **Viṣṇu's deception and Divodāsa's mokṣa** — Viṣṇu, arriving in heterodox mendicant guise with Lakṣmī and Garuḍa, reveals the king's sole flaw — excluding Śiva — whereupon Divodāsa installs the Divodāseśvara liṅga and attains liberation _(actors: Viṣṇu, Lakṣmī, Garuḍa, Divodāsa)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/kashi-line-divodasa.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kāśī-khaṇḍa, Uttarārdha ~ch. 58)
- **Viṣṇu's discus defeated** — Kṣupa wins Viṣṇu's aid; Viṣṇu, disguised as a brāhmaṇa, deploys the Sudarśana-cakra and his astras, but all are turned back by Dadhīca's Śiva-empowered blades of kuśa-grass _(actors: Viṣṇu, Kṣupa, Dadhīca, Sudarśana-cakra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kshupa-dadhicha-dispute.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ~35–36)
- **Viṣṇu's tapas at Kailāsa** — pressed by invincible daityas, Viṣṇu digs a fire-pit on Kailāsa and undertakes penance to propitiate Śiva for a weapon _(actors: Viṣṇu, the afflicted gods, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vishnu-lotus-eye-sudarshana.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṃhitā 34)
- **Viśpalā's iron leg** — after she loses her leg in the night-battle of Khela, the Aśvins fit the warrior-woman Viśpalā with an iron leg so she can run again at dawn _(actors: Aśvins, Viśpalā, Khela)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ashvins.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.116.15; 10.39.8)
- **Viśvāmitra's penance broken by Menakā; birth of Śakuntalā** — Indra sends Menakā to break Viśvāmitra's tapas; their daughter Śakuntalā is abandoned, sheltered by śakunta birds, and raised by Kaṇva _(actors: Viśvāmitra, Menakā, Indra, Kaṇva, Śakuntalā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shakuntala-bharata.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva, Sambhava)
- **Vṛddhagautama and his aged bride renewed** — The noseless son of Gautama, made handsome and learned by an old woman's banked boons, bathes with her in the Gautamī and she rises young _(actors: Vṛddhagautama, the old woman of Śītagiri, Agastya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautami-mahatmya-tales-catalog.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa, Gautamī-māhātmya, vulgate adh. 107)
