- **Vyasa consoles Yudhishthira with the Sixteen Kings** — After Abhimanyu's death, Vyasa recites the deaths of sixteen mighty kings to console the grieving Yudhishthira and teach that grief is vain _(actors: Vyasa, Yudhishthira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shodasarajakiya-sixteen-kings.md) — (Mahabharata, Drona Parva 55-72)
- **Vyāsa dictates the Mahābhārata to Gaṇeśa as scribe** — Vyāsa dictates the epic and Gaṇeśa agrees to write it on condition he never pause; Vyāsa composes knotty kūṭa-ślokas to buy time, and when Gaṇeśa's stylus breaks he snaps off his own tusk to continue, explaining his one-tusked iconography _(actors: Vyāsa, Gaṇeśa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/README.md) — (Mahabharata 1 (Adi Parva))
- **Vāyu Purāṇa — four-pāda structure and Brahmāṇḍa twinning** — One of the oldest Mahāpurāṇas, narrated by Vāyu, organised in Prakriyā/Anuṣaṅga/Upodghāta/Upasaṃhāra pādas and anciently one corpus with the Brahmāṇḍa _(actors: Vāyu, Vyāsa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vayu-purana.md) — (Vāyu Purāṇa; Purāṇic concordances [scholarly])
- **Vyuṣitāśva dies of excess** — the righteous Puru king, conqueror of all directions by the Aśvamedha, is undone by conjugal excess and dies childless of phthisis, leaving his queen Bhadrā a sonless widow _(actors: Vyuṣitāśva, Bhadrā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyushitashva-bhadra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva; Ganguli §CXXI)
- **Why women love song and dance** — Vāc turns from the reciters to the singers, and thus "women are given to vanity" and favour the man who dances and sings _(actors: Vāc, the devas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vac-and-the-soma-purchase.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 3.2.4.6)
- **Yadugiri as a Kṛta-Yuga abode of Nārāyaṇa** — The kṣetra-māhātmya charters Yādavādri/Yadugiri as an ancient self-chosen seat of Nārāyaṇa worshipped across all four Yugas _(actors: Nārāyaṇa/Vishnu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/melkote-tirunarayanapuram.md) — (Nārāyaṇa-/Yadugiri-kṣetra-māhātmya)
- **Agastya weds Kāverī and holds her in his kamaṇḍalu** — Agastya marries Kāverī and carries her, transformed into water, within his water-pot across the parched Deccan _(actors: Agastya, Kāverī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sacred-rivers-and-descent-myths.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kāverī-māhātmya)
- **Airāvata tramples the garland and Durvāsas curses the devas** — Irritated by the bees, Airāvata flings the garland down and tramples it; the enraged Durvāsas curses Indra and the devas to lose their śrī, becoming subject to decay and defeat _(actors: Airāvata, Durvāsas, Indra)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/airavata-and-celestial-animals.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.9; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8.5)
- **Ajamila Cries 'Narayana' at Death** — Dying at eighty-eight with his mind on his youngest son Narayana, the terrified Ajamila cries out the name, pronouncing the very name of the Supreme Lord _(actors: Ajamila)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ajamila.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6.1.27-30)
- **Alekhya enumerated among the 64 kalas** — Painting (alekhya) is fixed as the 5th of Vatsyayana's sixty-four arts, establishing the classical text-anchor for the whole devotional-painting family _(actors: Vatsyayana, Yashodhara (commentator))_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/chitra-kala-painting-traditions.md) — (Kamasutra 1.3 (Vidya-samuddesa); Jayamangala commentary)
- **Ambarisha's Ekadashi Vow and the Paarana Dilemma** — Ambarisha completes a year-long Ekadashi-Dvadashi vow but, with Durvasa lingering past the Dvadashi window, resolves the dharma-conflict by drinking only water _(actors: Ambarisha, Durvasa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ambarisha-durvasa.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 9.4.23-40)
- **Amrita-varsha — the moon drips nectar onto the kheer** — On this one night the full moon's rays carry amrita; rice-kheer left under the moonbeams becomes cooling, medicinal prasada, linked to the moon's churning-born soma _(actors: Candra/Soma (the moon); devotees; substrate: gods of the Samudra Manthana)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sharad-purnima-kojagari-lakshmi-puja.md) — (Folk amrita-moon / kheer tradition; Samudra Manthana and Chandra/Soma Puranic substrate; Ayurvedic ritu logic)
- **Amrta Drop Falls at Ujjain on the Kshipra** — In the samudra-manthana etiology, a drop of amrta from the kumbha fell at Ujjain, sanctifying the Kshipra river and grounding the Simhastha Kumbh bathing → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ujjain-avantika.md) — (Puranic Kumbh tradition)
- **Anatomical census of the body** — The text enumerates the body's parts — 360 bones, joints, sinews, hairs, fluid-measures, the three doṣas, seven dhātus, six tastes and the vital airs _(actors: Pippalāda; the human body)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/garbha-upanishad.md) — (Garbha Upaniṣad 1; cf. Caraka- and Suśruta-Saṃhitā)
- **Annakūṭa / Govardhana Pūjā instituted** — Cowherds' offering to Govardhana becomes the Annakūṭa festival, the chief annual utsava re-enacted before Śrīnāthajī _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, cowherds; later Puṣṭimārga devotees)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/nathdwara-shrinathji.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.24; Puṣṭimārga calendar)
- **Apālā's Soma offering and triple purification by Indra** — Apālā presses Soma with her teeth for Indra, who draws her through the chariot/cart/yoke holes to purify her skin and grants fertility boons to her father's head, his field, and her body _(actors: Apālā Ātreyī, Indra, Atri (father))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/rigvedic-women-seers-brahmavadinis.md) — (Ṛgveda 8.91)
- **Aśvins give Dadhyañc a horse's head and learn the honey-doctrine** — The Aśvins remove Dadhyañc's head for safekeeping, fix a horse's head on him through which he teaches them the Madhu-vidyā, then restore his own head after Indra strikes off the horse-head _(actors: Aśvins, Dadhyañc, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dadhichi.md) — (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2.5.16–19; Ṛgveda 1.116.12, 1.117.22)
- **Bharata-Bharadvaja (Vitatha) dynastic transfer** — The childless emperor Bharata is given Bharadvaja (called Vitatha) by the gods to continue the Lunar/Bharata line _(actors: Bharata, Bharadvaja/Vitatha, the gods)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharadvaja.md) — (Mahabharata Adi Parva; Bhagavata Purana 9.20)
- **Bhaṇḍāsura's penance and boon** — Tutored by the asura-guru Śukra, Bhaṇḍa wins from Śiva the boon that half of any foe's strength passes to him, making him near-invincible _(actors: Bhaṇḍāsura, Śukra, Śiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna))
- **Birajā Devī and the navel Śakti Pīṭha** — Jajpur is the Śakti Pīṭha where Satī's navel fell, presided over by Birajā/Girijā Devī with Bhairava Jagannātha/Varāha, binding goddess-pīṭha to the pitṛ-river _(actors: Satī; Birajā/Girijā Devī; Bhairava Jagannātha/Varāha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaitarani-jajpur-viraja-kshetra.md) — (Śakti-pīṭha lists; regional sthala tradition)
- **Birth of Durgā from combined tejas** — The gods, ousted by Mahiṣāsura, emit their radiant tejas which coalesces into the eighteen-armed warrior Goddess Durgā, each god arming her with his weapon _(actors: Durgā, Viṣṇu, Śiva, Brahmā, Indra and the gods)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devī Māhātmya ch.2)
- **Birth of Gaṇeśa from Lalitā's glance (Śākta version)** — When Viśukra plants the Vighna-yantra to paralyse Lalitā's army, the goddess glances at Kāmeśvara and Mahā-Gaṇapati is born, shattering the obstruction _(actors: Lalitā, Kāmeśvara, Gaṇeśa, Viśukra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahmanda-purana.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna))
- **Birth of the female Tīrthaṅkara Mallinātha (Mallī)** — Per the Śvetāmbara account, the 19th Tīrthaṅkara Mallinātha is born female (Mallī) owing to a karmic residue of deceit in a past life and attains liberation as a woman; the Digambara tradition denies this, holding the figure male because (on its premises) a woman cannot attain mokṣa _(actors: Mallinātha/Mallī)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Śvetāmbara Jñātā-dharma-kathā; Digambara tradition)
- **Birth of the goddess-daughters and Merakhiyā** — Several divine daughters including Janbāī (future Khodiyār) and a son Merakhiyā are born and recognized as Cāraṇ āīs _(actors: Janbāī/Khodiyār, sister-āīs, Merakhiyā, Mamaḍiyā, Devaḷ)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khodiyar-mata.md) — (Cāraṇ folk tradition)
- **Birth of Urvashī from Nara-Nārāyaṇa's thigh** — Urvashī is born from the thigh of the sage Nara-Nārāyaṇa at Badarī, created to humble the apsarās Indra had sent to disturb his penance _(actors: Urvashī, Nara-Nārāyaṇa)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/apsaras.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa; RV 10.95)
- **Brahmā and the origin-of-painting floor-drawing legend** — Brahmā bids a grieving father draw his dead son's likeness on the ground so He can restore his life, mythologized as the origin of painting and floor-art _(actors: Brahmā, a grieving king/priest)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/rangoli-kolam-floor-art.md) — (Citralakṣaṇa (via Wikipedia, Rangoli) [late text][disputed])
- **Brahmā appears as the swan (haṃsa)** — The creator Brahmā takes the form of a haṃsa (swan/goose) to answer the Sādhyas, embodying the paramahaṃsa ideal of the discriminating sage _(actors: Brahmā in swan-form; the Sādhyas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-gita.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva, Mokṣadharma ~12.299)
- **Brahmā reveals the Name of Nārāyaṇa as the secret of all Vedas** — Brahmā answers that the deliverance from Kali is the secret hidden in all the Vedas: uttering the name of the original Person, Nārāyaṇa _(actors: Brahmā, Nārada)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/kalisantarana-upanishad.md) — (Kālisantaraṇa Upaniṣad)
- **Brahmā's Aśvamedha at Satyavrata-kṣetra** — Brahmā performs the great aśvamedha at Kāñcī; when the river Vegavati rises to wash it away, Viṣṇu lies across the flood to stop it and rises as Varadarāja from the yajña-fire to dwell forever _(actors: Brahmā, Viṣṇu (Varadarāja), Vegavati, Sarasvatī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanchipuram-city-of-temples.md) — (sthala-purana; Wikipedia, Varadharaja Perumal Temple)
- **Bull-form dive at Guptakāśī** — Śiva takes the bull (vṛṣabha) form at Guptakāśī; Bhīma seizes him, and the bull-Śiva dives into the earth refusing capture _(actors: Śiva (vṛṣabha-rūpa), Bhīma)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kedarnath-kedara-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa)
- **Candra favours Rohini and neglects the others** — Candra becomes enamoured of Rohini and lingers with her, neglecting his other twenty-six wives, who appeal to their father Daksha _(actors: Candra, Rohini, the slighted wives)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/nakshatras.md) — (Bhagavata Purana)
- **Chain of divine worshippers of the mūla-mūrti** — The Celuvanārāyaṇa image is said to have been worshipped successively by Brahmā, Sanatkumāra, Rāma (Tretā) and Kṛṣṇa (Dvāpara) before being lost _(actors: Brahmā, Sanatkumāra, Rāma, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/melkote-tirunarayanapuram.md) — (Yadugiri-kṣetra-māhātmya)
- **Collapse of Cosmic Order by Gayāsura's Holiness** — Because everyone who sees Gayāsura attains liberation regardless of karma, Yama's hells empty and the order of reward-and-punishment collapses, prompting the devas under Brahmā to act _(actors: Gayāsura, Yama, Brahmā, devas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/gaya-pitru-tirtha.md) — (Vayu Purana; Agni Purana)
- **Cosmic egg and Adi-Sāṅkhya cosmogony** — From the unmanifest Pradhāna arise mahat, ahaṃkāra, the tanmātras and elements, condensing into the golden brahmāṇḍa within which Brahmā fashions the worlds _(actors: Brahmā, Pradhāna)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vayu-purana.md) — (Vāyu Purāṇa)
- **Curse-origin of the four learned birds** — Four brahmana sons (grandsons of Vipulasva) are cursed by their father to be reborn as birds yet retain Vedic knowledge, becoming enlightened expounders in the Vindhyas _(actors: Sukrsha, the four birds, Drona, the apsaras Vapu, Durvasas)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/markandeya-purana.md) — (Markandeya Purana)
- **Dakṣa's Curse on Chandra (the Moon)** — Chandra marries Daksha's daughters but dotes only on Rohini; the slighted wives complain and Daksha curses the Moon to wane, until Chandra worships Shiva at Thingalur and regains his light _(actors: Chandra, Daksha, Rohini, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/navagraha-temples.md) — (Sthala-purana)
- **Dattātreya's eternal tapas on the Girnar summit** — The deathless avadhūta Dattātreya performs and abides in eternal tapas on the highest peak, leaving his worshipped footprints (Datta Pādukā) _(actors: Dattātreya, Atri, Anasūyā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa Prabhāsa-khaṇḍa (Vastrāpatha-kṣetra-māhātmya); Datta-sampradāya tradition)
- **Declaration of ātman–Brahman identity (Chapter 1)** — The opening chapter pours out the conviction of all-pervading non-duality, framing grace itself as impelling the realization that ātman is Brahman _(actors: Dattātreya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/avadhuta-gita.md) — (Avadhūta Gītā 1)
- **Devas and Asuras contend over the Udgītha** — Gods and demons contend; the gods meditate on each sense-faculty as Udgītha but each is pierced with evil until the vital breath (mukhya prāṇa) shatters the demons like a clod on rock _(actors: devas, asuras, prāṇa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/chandogya-upanishad.md) — (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 1.2)
- **Dev Deepawali — the gods descend to bathe at Kashi** — On Kartik Purnima the gods are said to descend to the Ganga ghats of Varanasi, celebrated as the gods' own Diwali with myriad lamps _(actors: the devas, Ganga)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Puranic/regional tradition; Drik Panchang)
- **Durvāsā's garland and the curse of lost śrī (Samudra-manthana frame)** — Durvāsā gifts Indra a śrī-bearing garland which Airāvata tramples; the slighted sage curses the Devas to lose all fortune and vigor, driving the weakened gods to undertake the churning of the ocean for amṛta _(actors: Durvāsā, Indra, Airāvata, the Devas, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ambarisha-durvasa.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8.5; Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.9 (Santānaka garland variant); absent in Mahābhārata Ādi Parva Amṛta-manthana)
- **Duṣyanta weds Śakuntalā by gāndharva rite** — King Duṣyanta meets Śakuntalā at Kaṇva's hermitage and marries her by gāndharva rite on the condition their son be heir _(actors: Duṣyanta, Śakuntalā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shakuntala-bharata.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva, Sambhava)
- **Fire-birth of Mīnākṣī** — A three-breasted girl, Pārvatī incarnate, rises from the sacrificial fire-pit of childless Pāṇḍya Malayadhvaja and queen Kāñcanamālai _(actors: Malayadhvaja, Kāñcanamālai, Mīnākṣī/Taḍātakai (Pārvatī))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/madurai-meenakshi-sundareshwara-kshetra.md) — (Hālāsya Māhātmya; Tiruviḷaiyāḍal Purāṇam)
- **Ganesha Slays Sindhurasura at Morgaon** — Riding his peacock vahana as Mayureshvara, Ganesha slays the asura Sindhura whose head falls at Morgaon (peacock-village), the chief Ashtavinayaka shrine _(actors: Ganesha, Sindhurasura, Chakrapani, Brahma)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashtavinayaka.md) — (Ganesha Purana)
- **Goddess Ekādaśī born as śakti to destroy Mura** — In the Padma Purāṇa Ekādaśī-māhātmya tradition, the demon Mura arises from Viṣṇu's body during his cosmic sleep; the personified goddess Ekādaśī is born as the śakti who destroys Mura while protecting the sleeping god — the etiological basis of the Ekādaśī fast. A distinct, non-Kṛṣṇa Mura harmonized with the Bhāgavata figure by the shared name _(actors: Goddess Ekādaśī, Mura, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/asura-wars-and-roster.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Ekādaśī-māhātmya)
- **Hara-Sapa-Vimochana Frees Shiva of the Brahma-Slaying Curse** — At Tirukkandiyur, Vishnu as Hara-Sapa-Vimochana Perumal frees Shiva from the curse of Brahma-slaying (brahma-hatya) _(actors: Vishnu, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/108-divya-desams.md) — (Naalayira Divya Prabandham / sthala-purana)
- **Identity of Śiva, Self, and Brahman declared** — Ṛbhu collapses the Śaiva Śiva, Vedāntic Brahman, and innermost Ātman into one undivided awareness, dissolving sectarian Śaivism into pure non-dual jñāna _(actors: Ṛbhu, Nidāgha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ribhu-gita.md) — (Śivarahasya Purāṇa (Ribhu-Gītā))
- **Idumban and the origin of the kavadi** — Sage Agastya sends his disciple Idumban to carry the hills Sivagiri and Saktigiri south on a shoulder-pole; at Palani the boy-Murugan (Dandayudhapani) sits immovable on one hill and slays then revives Idumban, the shoulder-pole becoming the prototype kavadi and Idumban the guardian saluted first _(actors: Agastya, Idumban, Murugan (Dandayudhapani))_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Palani sthala tradition; Wikipedia (Thaipusam))
- **Śilāda's penance for a deathless son** — Childless sage Śilāda performs tapas to Śiva for an un-womb-born, immortal son _(actors: Śilāda, Śiva, Indra)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nandi-and-shaiva-mounts.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa; Liṅga Purāṇa)
- **Indra breaks open the Vala cave** — Aided by Sarama, the Angirasas and Brihaspati, Indra splits the Vala enclosure where the Panis hid the cosmic cows, driving out the cows, dawn and waters _(actors: Indra, the Panis, Sarama, Brihaspati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/indra.md) — (Rigveda 3, 10.108)
- **Indra Splits Open Vala to Free the Cattle and Dawns** — Indra breaks open the Vala cave to release the imprisoned cattle and dawns, a recurring Vedic myth of liberating light and order _(actors: Indra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedic-deities-and-suktas.md) — (Rigveda)
- **Invention and bearing of the Mahatī vīṇā** — Nārada becomes patron of saṅgīta and the seven svaras as wielder of the great vīṇā Mahatī, earning the epithet Mahatī-vīṇā-dhara _(actors: Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada.md) — (saṅgīta-śāstra tradition; Nāradīya Śikṣā; Saṅgīta-makaranda)
- **Śiva and Pārvatī follow their son and manifest as Mallikārjuna** — Grieving the absent Kumāra, Śiva and Pārvatī follow him to Śrīśaila and abide there as the self-manifest liṅga named Mallikā (Pārvatī/jasmine) + Arjuna (Śiva) _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Kārttikeya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srisailam-srisaila-mallikarjuna-bhramaramba.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa (Śrīśaila-khaṇḍa); Śiva Purāṇa; Dvādaśa Jyotirliṅga Stotra)
- **Śiva descends as Mārtāṇḍa Bhairava** — Śiva manifests as a fierce mounted solar-warrior form (Mārtāṇḍa Bhairava) and rides to Jejuri/Mailāra to confront the demons _(actors: Śiva as Mārtāṇḍa Bhairava, the gods as his army)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khandoba-martanda-bhairava.md) — (Mallāri Māhātmya; Marathi folk tradition)
- **Śiva manifests as the youthful south-facing Guru** — Śiva appears beneath a banyan as a radiant sixteen-year-old yogin facing south, the young teacher among aged pupils _(actors: Śiva (as Dakṣiṇāmūrti); the four Kumāras)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dakshinamurti.md) — (Sūta Saṃhitā; Āgamic iconographic tradition)
- **Śiva states the guṇa-classification of Purāṇas to Pārvatī** — Śiva teaches Pārvatī the sāttvic/rājasic/tāmasic grouping of the eighteen Purāṇas, each tied to a deity and destination _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/README.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa 236.18–21)
- **Śiva teaches the guṇa-classification to Pārvatī** — Śiva instructs Pārvatī, grouping the eighteen Mahāpurāṇas into sāttvic, rājasic and tāmasic classes by presiding deity and destination _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/smriti-puranas.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa 236.18–21)
- **Jaya and Vijaya's three demonic births across the yugas** — The cursed pair are born as Hiraṇyākṣa/Hiraṇyakaśipu (Satya), Rāvaṇa/Kumbhakarṇa (Tretā), and Śiśupāla/Dantavakra (Dvāpara), each slain by Viṣṇu's avatāras _(actors: Jaya, Vijaya, Viṣṇu's avatāras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/jaya-vijaya.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 7.1, 10.74, 10.78)
- **Kadrū's curse on the nāgas (ratified by Brahmā)** — Kadrū curses her serpent-sons to burn in Janamejaya's sacrifice; Brahmā ratifies it as a cull of the over-numerous nāgas _(actors: Kadrū, the nāgas, Brahmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/astika-sarpa-satra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva 20)
- **Kahoda Drowned by Bandin** — Ashtavakra's father Kahoda is defeated in debate by Bandin at King Janaka's sacrifice and drowned per the contest's rule _(actors: Kahoda, Bandin, Janaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/ashtavakra.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva CXXXII)
- **Kṛṣṇa-Nārāyaṇa and Arjuna-Nara identity affirmed** — The Mahābhārata identifies Kṛṣṇa as Nārāyaṇa and Arjuna as Nara reborn, anchoring epic theology to the Badarikāśrama hermitage _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna, Nara, Nārāyaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/badrinath-badarikashrama-kshetra.md) — (Mahābhārata (Nārāyaṇīya, Śānti Parva))
- **Kīrtimukha conflated with Rāhu the eclipse-demon** — In variant tellings the self-consuming, jawless devouring-face is identified with or doubled by Rāhu the eclipse-graha who swallows sun and moon _(actors: Kīrtimukha, Rāhu)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/temple-iconographic-motifs.md) — (Coomaraswamy, 'The Face of Glory')
- **Kubera's former-life merit as Gunanidhi** — In a folk variant, the thief Gunanidhi, robbing a Shiva temple, inadvertently keeps a lamp burning before the linga, earning merit that reincarnates him as the wealth-god and friend of Shiva _(actors: Kubera (Gunanidhi), Shiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/kubera.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Lakshmi Departs Vaikuntha for Earth** — Offended that her abode was kicked by Bhrgu, Lakshmi leaves Vaikuntha for earth, triggering Vishnu's descent after her as Shrinivasa _(actors: Lakshmi, Vishnu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Venkatacala-Mahatmya)
- **Lakshmi withdraws into the ocean and asuras rout the devas** — Stripped of fortune, the three worlds wither, Lakshmi re-enters the Kshira-Sagara, and Bali's asuras rout the weakened devas, who flee Svarga _(actors: Lakshmi, Bali, the asuras, the devas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/samudra-manthana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 8.5-8.8; Vishnu Purana 1.9)
- **Mahalakshmi slays Mahishasura** — As the rajasika aspect, the Goddess slays the buffalo-demon Mahishasura in the middle carita _(actors: Mahalakshmi, Mahishasura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 2-4)
- **Maṅkaṇaka's sap-blood and Śiva's humbling at Saptasārasvata** — The sage Maṅkaṇaka cuts his hand and vegetable sap (not blood) flows out; he dances in cosmic exultation until Śiva, cutting his own thumb to make ash flow forth, humbles him, after which the sage settles at Saptasārasvata where the Sarasvatī flows as seven streams (Suprabhā, Kāñcanākṣī, Viśālā, Mānasahradā, Sarasvatī/Oghavatī, Sureṇu, Vimalodakā) _(actors: Maṅkaṇaka, Śiva, the seven Sarasvatīs)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kurukshetra-dharmakshetra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana-parva (Tīrtha-yātrā); Śalya-parva 37–38)
- **Mohini tests Rukmangada and Vishnu bears the family to Vaikuntha** — Brahma fashions the enchantress Mohini to break Rukmangada's vow; she forces him to choose between his fast and beheading his son Dharmangada, but as the sword falls Vishnu intervenes, reveals the test, and bears the family to Vaikuntha _(actors: Mohini, Rukmangada, Dharmangada, Sandhyavali, Vishnu, Brahma)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/narada-purana.md) — (Narada Purana (Rukmangada-carita))
- **Mārkaṇḍeya's First Narmadā Parikramā** — Sage Markandeya, held to be the first parikramavasi, circumambulates the Narmada and each of her tributaries, founding the most extensive form of the river-circuit vow _(actors: Markandeya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/narmada-parikrama.md) — (Narmada Parikrama tradition)
- **Naming of Ekāmra Kṣetra (the one-mango field)** — The temple-city is sacralized as the field of the single primordial mango tree under which Śiva dwelt with Pārvatī, around the Bindusāgara _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lingaraja-bhubaneswar-ekamra-kshetra.md) — (Ekāmra Purāṇa / Ekāmra-kṣetra-māhātmya)
- **Nandin snatches back the manuscript** — Śiva's attendant Nandin tears away the leaf Śaṅkara had copied, leaving him only the first portion, which prompts Śaṅkara to compose the remaining 59 verses _(actors: Nandin (Nandikeśvara), Ādi Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/soundarya-lahari.md) — (Saundarya-laharī; Śrīvidyā hagiography)
- **Narada consoles King Srinjaya (Shodasarajakiya recap)** — Narada and Parvata console King Srinjaya over his son Svarnasthivin's death by reciting the same roll of sixteen great kings who all died _(actors: Narada, Parvata, Srinjaya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shodasarajakiya-sixteen-kings.md) — (Mahabharata, Drona Parva / Shanti Parva 29)
- **Nara-Nārāyaṇa Teach at Badarī** — The twin sages Nara and Nārāyaṇa impart ekāntika devotion to the sage Nārada at their hermitage at Badarī _(actors: Nara, Nārāyaṇa, Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narayaniya.md) — (Mahabharata Shanti Parva (Narayaniya))
- **Nightly flower-gathering from the royal garden** — Moving invisibly, Puṣpadanta plucks choice flowers each night from a royal (often King Citraratha's) garden to offer Śiva _(actors: Puṣpadanta, King Citraratha (garden's owner, variant))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-mahimna-stotra.md) — (Puṣpadanta frame-legend; Citraratha-garden variant (regional))
- **Nightly theft of flowers for Śiva** — Invisible, Puṣpadanta gathers choice flowers from a royal garden each night to offer to Śiva _(actors: Puṣpadanta, King Citraratha (garden's owner, regional variant))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-mahimna-stotra.md) — (Śiva-mahimnaḥ stotra tradition; folk legend)
- **Nārada listed among Viṣṇu's avatāras** — The Bhāgavata's descent-list names Nārada as the avatāra who propounds the Pāñcarātra/Sātvata-tantra path of devotion _(actors: Nārada, Viṣṇu, Sūta, Śaunaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 1.3.8)
- **Nārāyaṇa-sarovar — Viṣṇu's self-manifestation at the sea's edge** — Viṣṇu/Nārāyaṇa manifests at the Kutch lake, sanctifying it as a Vaiṣṇava tīrtha and member of the five lakes _(actors: Viṣṇu/Nārāyaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-sarovar-sacred-lakes.md) — (Kutch sthala-māhātmya; Bhāgavata-tradition lists)
- **Parvati Gives Murugan the Vel** — Parvati/Shakti gives Murugan her crystallised power as the lance Vel, the Shakti-vel that becomes the network's central object of worship _(actors: Parvati, Murugan)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Tirumurukarruppatai; Kanda Puranam)
- **Pārvatī asks the secret of immortality** — Pārvatī asks Śiva why he is deathless and she is reborn, prompting him to reveal the Amar Kathā _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/amarnath-ice-lingam-and-amar-katha.md) — (Amareśvara māhātmya; Kashmiri Śaiva oral tradition)
- **Rati's penance wins Kama's rebirth** — Rati performs penance and the gods intercede; Shiva grants that Kama will be reborn, visible to Rati alone or bodily in the next age _(actors: Rati, Shiva, Kama)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/kamadeva-and-minor-deities.md) — (Shiva Purana; Matsya Purana)
- **Rāma defers union to end of Kali Yuga** — Rāma, met during exile and bound to Sītā, tells Vaiṣṇavī to do tapas in the Trikūṭa hills and await union with him as Kalki at the close of Kali Yuga _(actors: Vaiṣṇavī, Rāma (future Kalki))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaishno-devi-trikuta-katra.md) — (Vaiṣṇo Devī legend; Trikūṭa-māhātmya)
- **Sandhyā Tāṇḍava in the Rajata/Veḷḷi Sabhai (Madurai)** — As Sundareśvara, consort of Mīnākṣī, Śiva dances the twilight dance with the right foot raised (mirror of Chidambaram) at the Pāṇḍya king's request _(actors: Śiva-Sundareśvara, Mīnākṣī, Pāṇḍya king)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancha-sabhai-nataraja-dance-halls.md) — (Tiruviḷaiyāḍal Purāṇam; Madurai temple tradition)
- **Sarasvati curses Ganga to flow on earth** — In the Shakta account, after a jealous quarrel among Vishnu's three consorts, Sarasvati curses Ganga to flow on earth as a river _(actors: Sarasvati, Ganga, Vishnu, Lakshmi)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganga.md) — (Devi Bhagavata Purana 9)
- **Savitri chooses Satyavan and Narada's death-prophecy** — Savitri chooses the forest-prince Satyavan, son of the blind dethroned king Dyumatsena; the sage Narada declares Satyavan destined to die in exactly one year, but Savitri refuses to recant _(actors: Savitri, Satyavan, Dyumatsena, Narada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/savitri-satyavan.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva 292-293)
- **Self-manifestation of Ranganatha at Srirangam** — Vishnu's reclining Ranganatha murti is disclosed (given to Ikshvaku) on the Kaveri island, uncarved by mortal hand, as Bhuloka Vaikuntha _(actors: Vishnu as Ranganatha; Ikshvaku)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-svayamvyakta-vishnu-kshetras.md) — (Padma Purana; Srimaranga-mahatmya)
- **Shiva beheads the boy guardian** — The boy posted at the door bars Shiva from entering Parvati's chamber, fights the ganas, and is decapitated by Shiva's trident _(actors: Shiva, Ganesha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Six candidate definitions of brāhmaṇa enumerated** — Five-to-six possible definitions of brāhmaṇa-hood (jīva, body, birth, knowledge, karma, dharma) are set up as positions to be refuted _(actors: Upaniṣadic teacher)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/vajrasuchika-upanishad.md) — (Vajrasūcikā Upaniṣad 2)
- **Six-fold birth of Skanda (Shanmukha)** — Shiva's too-potent seed, relayed through Agni, Ganga and a reed-thicket, becomes six infants nursed by the six Krittikas and fused by Parvati into the six-faced Kartikeya _(actors: Shiva, Agni, Ganga, Krittikas, Parvati, Kartikeya)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/skanda-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Skanda Purana (Maheshvara/Kedara-khanda); cf. Mahabharata, Kumarasambhava)
- **Skanda shatters the liṅga into five fragments** — Kumāra/Skanda slays Tārakāsura, and the self-rejoining Śivaliṅga is held apart by the gods and splinters into five pieces that fly to the five Andhra sites _(actors: Kumāra/Skanda (Kārtikeya), Viṣṇu, Indra, Soma/Candra, the devas, Tārakāsura)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancharama-kshetras-andhra.md) — (Pañcārāma sthala-purāṇa (regional); cf. Skanda & Śiva Purāṇas)
- **Soma's penance and the tempering of the curse at Prabhasa** — Soma worships a Shiva-linga at the Sarasvati-sea confluence; Shiva halves the curse into the waxing/waning lunar cycle, restoring the moon's radiance and naming the place Prabhasa and the linga Somanatha _(actors: Soma/Candra, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/prabhasa-somnath-tirtha.md) — (Skanda Purana Prabhasa-khanda; Shiva Purana)
- **Sāvitrī chooses Satyavān despite Nārada's death-prophecy** — Sāvitrī chooses the forest-prince Satyavān; Nārada foretells he will die in one year, but she refuses to recant _(actors: Sāvitrī, Satyavān, Nārada, Dyumatsena)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/savitri-satyavan.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva 292–293)
- **The aṅga-rakṣā limb-armouring** — The hymn sets Rāma and his Rāmāyaṇa epithets upon each limb of the reciter's body in sequence, enclosing it in a protective kavaca _(actors: Rāma (invoked), the reciter)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/ramaraksha-stotra.md) — (Rāma-rakṣā Stotra, vv. 4-11)
- **The boar and the swan fail to find the ends** — Viṣṇu as the boar Varāha digs downward and Brahmā as a swan flies upward, but neither reaches an end of the fiery pillar, revealing Śiva as the unbounded source _(actors: Viṣṇu (Varāha), Brahmā (haṃsa), Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arunachala-tiruvannamalai-fire-hill.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa; Aruṇācala-māhātmya)
- **The boon of a son equal to Viṣṇu, granted as three births** — Unable to wish for anything higher than a son like Viṣṇu, the couple receive the only possible fulfilment: the Lord himself will be born to them as their son three times _(actors: Sutapas, Pṛśni, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/prishnigarbha-three-births.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.3.37-40)
- **The cast-out / ash-born child** — Variant: the boy is rejected, cursed or cast into the cremation fire by his divine parents and survives to rule the burning-ground, explaining his fierceness _(actors: Sudalaimāḍaṉ, Pārvatī, Śiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/sudalaimadan.md) — (Sudalaimāḍaṉ kodai oral tradition [disputed])
- **The Devī's self-disclosure as Brahman** — The Goddess declares she alone existed before creation as the one non-dual Brahman/Cit-śakti, from whom māyā and the manifest world arose _(actors: Bhuvaneśvarī (Goddess as Brahman), Himavat)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/devi-gita.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.32)
- **The divine medical relay (Brahmā to Indra)** — Āyurveda passes from Brahmā to Dakṣa Prajāpati, to the Aśvins, to Indra, from whom it reaches the human sage-world _(actors: Brahmā, Dakṣa Prajāpati, the Aśvins, Indra)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/ayurveda-and-the-science-of-life.md) — (Caraka Saṃhitā Sū. 1; Suśruta Saṃhitā Sū. 1)
- **The Goddess manifests as Kanyākumārī and performs tapas** — Ādi Parāśakti / Pārvatī abides as the virgin Kanyākumārī at the southern cape and undertakes severe penance to win Śiva of Suchīndram as husband _(actors: Kanyākumārī (Pārvatī/Bhagavathy); Śiva (Sthāṇunātha of Suchīndram))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-kanyakumari.md) — (Sthala-purāṇa of Kanyākumārī)
- **The gods pin the being face-down** — The assembled devas overthrow the giant and hold it permanently face-down (adhomukha), head to the northeast, each god gripping a limb _(actors: 45 presiding devatas (Brahma, the 8 Dikpalas, et al.), the cosmic being)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/vastu-shastra-and-the-vastu-purusha-mandala.md) — (Matsya Purana; Brihat Samhita 52)
- **The gods seize the deer and the antler breaks into the Paśupati liṅga** — In the struggle to fetch Śiva back, his horn/antler breaks; the fragment becomes the worshipped liṅga and he consents to dwell as Paśupati _(actors: Śiva (deer-form), the devas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pashupatinath-kathmandu-nepal-kshetra.md) — (Nepāla-māhātmya; Paśupati Purāṇa)
- **The guru's question 'Who are you?' (Narmada cave frame)** — In the hagiographic frame, Govinda Bhagavatpada asks the young Shankara 'ko'si tvam' and the six verses are his answer, negating every false identity down to Shiva-Consciousness _(actors: Adi Shankara, Govinda Bhagavatpada (variant: Gaudapada / unnamed sage))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/nirvana-shatkam.md) — (Shankara-vijaya hagiographies (Madhava-Vidyaranya Shankara-dig-vijaya); traditional frame of the Nirvana Shatkam)
- **The hill turns blue (Nīlācala)** — The mountain turns blue where the yoni struck it, giving Nīlācala/Nīlaśaila its name _(actors: the Goddess (Satī's yoni), Nīlācala hill)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kamakhya-nilachala-kamarupa.md) — (Kālikā Purāṇa; Yoginī Tantra)
- **The slaying of Mahiṣāsura** — Durga beheads the shape-shifting buffalo-demon Mahiṣa, the central victory the hymn exults in _(actors: Durgā/Mahiṣāsura-mardinī, her lion mount, Mahiṣa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/mahishasura-mardini-stotra.md) — (Devī Māhātmya; Mahiṣāsura-mardinī stotra)
- **The soul's crossing of the Vaitarani river** — The preta crosses the dreadful river of blood and pus only by merit and a cow gifted (vaitarani-dana) by the family, which becomes its boat _(actors: the preta (soul), family of the deceased)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/garuda-purana.md) — (Garuda Purana (Preta-khanda))
- **The Tārakāmaya War** — The heavens split into war over Tārā, with devas demanding her return against Soma, Śukra, and Śiva, until Brahmā intervenes and forces Tārā's return _(actors: Bṛhaspati, Indra, Soma, Śukra, Śiva, Brahmā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/the-gurus-brihaspati-and-shukra.md) — (Padma Purāṇa; Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa)
- **Threefold birth of the self** — Aitareya teaches the self's three births — conception, gestation, and rebirth in one's offspring — with the ātman entering the body through the crown _(actors: Ātman)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/principal-upanishads-detail.md) — (Aitareya Upaniṣad 2.1)
- **Threefold reading of the bilva leaf** — The opening verse glosses the leaf's three leaflets as the three gunas, Shiva's three eyes, and the three-pointed trishula (with a parallel trimurti reading) _(actors: Shiva; the leaf-symbol)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bilvashtaka.md) — (Bilvashtaka v.1; sacred-flora tradition)
- **Tvaṣṭṛ creates Vṛtra in revenge** — Enraged at his son's murder, Tvaṣṭṛ bars Indra from the soma sacrifice and performs a fire-rite to generate the avenger Vṛtra, but a mispronunciation dooms Vṛtra to die by Indra's hand _(actors: Tvaṣṭṛ, Vṛtra, Indra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vishvakarma-and-tvashtar.md) — (Taittirīya Saṃhitā 2.4.12; Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 6.9)
- **Vasiṣṭha's hermitage and the Gaumukh spring** — Vasiṣṭha settles his āśrama on Arbuda; the Gaumukh spring issues from a stone cow's-mouth and is linked to his wish-cow Nandinī/Kāmadhenu _(actors: Vasiṣṭha, Nandinī / Kāmadhenu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mount-abu-arbuda-achaleshwar.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Arbuda-khaṇḍa; Gaumukh sthala tradition; cf. Rāmāyaṇa Bāla-kāṇḍa)
- **Vishnu's footprint enshrined at Vishnupada Temple** — The Vishnupada Temple at Gaya enshrines the basalt footprint of Vishnu, the foot that subdued Gayasura, marking the principal site of Gaya pinda-dana _(actors: Vishnu)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/shraddha-and-pitr-rites.md) — (Vishnu Purana; Vayu Purana)
- **Viṣṇu's decree to reside as śālagrāma in the Gaṇḍakī beside Tulasī** — Accepting the curse, Viṣṇu ordains he will forever manifest as the self-born śālagrāma-śilā in the Kālī-Gandakī, worshipped only with Tulasī _(actors: Viṣṇu, Tulasī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/muktinath-gandaki-shalagrama-kshetra.md) — (Padma Purāṇa; Skanda Purāṇa, śālagrāma-māhātmya)
- **Vrika Questions Narada** — The asura Vrika, son of Shakuni, meets the wandering Narada and asks which of the three chief gods is most quickly pleased _(actors: Vrika, Narada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhasmasura-and-vrikasura.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 10.88.13-16)
- **Yama's whisper to his noose-bearer** — watching his own dūta set out pāśa in hand, the death-god leans to his ear and orders him to keep clear of the worshippers of Madhusūdana _(actors: Yama, the Yamadūta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yama-gita.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa III.7)
- **Yayāti rescues Devayānī and weds her** — Śarmiṣṭhā throws Devayānī into a well; King Yayāti pulls her out, weds her, with Śarmiṣṭhā given as her serving-maid _(actors: Yayāti, Devayānī, Śarmiṣṭhā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yayati.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva 76–82; Bhāgavata 9.18)
- **Aṅgāraka (Mars) Healed of Leprosy** — Mars, afflicted with leprosy, worships Vaidyanatha-Shiva at Vaitheeswaran Koil and is healed, fixing the temple as the Mars node and divine-physician shrine _(actors: Angaraka, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/navagraha-temples.md) — (Sthala-purana)
- **Ambikā sanctifies the Vastrāpatha-kṣetra** — The Goddess as Ambikā/Ambā becomes the presiding kṣetra-devī of the hill, paired with Dattātreya in the Purāṇic charter of Vastrāpatha _(actors: Ambikā (Ambā Mātā), Dattātreya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa Prabhāsa-khaṇḍa (Vastrāpatha-māhātmya); regional tradition)
- **Ashtavakra Defeats Bandin in Debate** — The boy Ashtavakra out-argues the unbeaten Bandin in a counting debate, completing the verse on thirteen; Bandin reveals he is Varuna's son and the drowned brahmanas rise alive _(actors: Ashtavakra, Bandin, Janaka, Kahoda)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/ashtavakra.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva CXXXIV)
- **Betrothal to Śiva of Suchīndram** — Pleased by her tapas, Śiva of Suchīndram agrees to wed Kanyākumārī and a pre-dawn wedding muhūrta is fixed _(actors: Kanyākumārī; Śiva (Sthāṇunātha))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-kanyakumari.md) — (Sthala-purāṇa of Kanyākumārī)
- **Bhārgava line founded (Śukra, Cyavana, Paraśurāma)** — Bhṛgu's descendants — Śukra the asura-guru, Cyavana, and through Ṛcīka–Jamadagni the avatāra Paraśurāma — form the Bhārgava clan _(actors: Bhṛgu, Śukra, Cyavana, Ṛcīka, Jamadagni, Paraśurāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigu.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi/Anuśāsana Parva; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9.15–16)
- **Birth of Budha (Mercury)** — Tārā returns pregnant and, pressed by Brahmā, confesses Soma is the father; the radiant child is named Budha, the planet Mercury _(actors: Tārā, Soma, Budha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/the-gurus-brihaspati-and-shukra.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa; Bhāgavata 9; Devī Bhāgavata)
- **Birth of Nandi from the sacrificial ground** — Nandi arises in Śilāda's yajña-field as a portion (aṃśa) of Śiva himself _(actors: Nandi, Śilāda, Śiva, sages Mitra and Varuṇa)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nandi-and-shaiva-mounts.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa (Śatarudra-saṃhitā); Liṅga Purāṇa)
- **Brahma and the origin of painting** — Grieving a brahmana's dead son, Brahma bids the father draw the boy on the ground and breathes life into the image, the legendary first painting _(actors: Brahma, a grieving brahmana, his son)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/chitra-kala-painting-traditions.md) — (Citralakshana)
- **Brahma's boon — the Vastu-purusha** — The pinned being begs not to be forgotten; Brahma makes him lord of every building-site, to be honored in construction on pain of vastu-dosha _(actors: Brahma, the Vastu-purusha)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/vastu-shastra-and-the-vastu-purusha-mandala.md) — (Matsya Purana; Brihat Samhita)
- **Brahmā's false witness and curse** — Brahmā falsely claims to have reached the summit, using the ketakī flower and a cow as witnesses, and is cursed to receive no worship while the ketakī is barred from Śiva's pūjā _(actors: Brahmā, Śiva, ketakī flower, cow)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arunachala-tiruvannamalai-fire-hill.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa)
- **Candravatī discovers the self-manifest liṅga (variant)** — A princess Candravatī sees a kapila cow emptying its milk over an anthill, digs, and finds the svayambhu liṅga, worshipping it under jasmine creepers _(actors: Candravatī, the kapila cow)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srisailam-srisaila-mallikarjuna-bhramaramba.md) — (Śrīśaila sthala-purāṇa (folk/regional variant))
- **Conception from blood and seed; sex-determination** — Conception arises from the union of mother's blood and father's seed; predominance of one yields son or daughter, equality an intersex child, division twins _(actors: mother, father, the conceptus)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/garbha-upanishad.md) — (Garbha Upaniṣad 2–3)
- **Cow-and-milk rediscovery of the buried four-faced horn (variant)** — A cow spontaneously sprinkles milk over a spot; digging reveals the radiant chaturmukha liṅga of the lost horn _(actors: Śiva (liṅga), a milk-giving cow)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pashupatinath-kathmandu-nepal-kshetra.md) — (local māhātmya (folk))
- **Dakṣa-yajña, Satī's self-immolation, and Vīrabhadra's destruction of the rite** — Dakṣa Prajāpati holds a great sacrifice without inviting Śiva; Satī self-immolates and Śiva's emanation Vīrabhadra destroys the sacrifice — the archetypal sacrifice gone wrong _(actors: Daksha, Sati, Shiva, Virabhadra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/yajnas-and-vedic-rituals.md) — (Puranas; Mahabharata)
- **Dakṣiṇāmūrti teaches in silence** — Śiva as the south-facing supreme guru expounds jñāna and yoga wordlessly through cinmudrā to the four aged Kumāras _(actors: Śiva (Dakṣiṇāmūrti), the four Kumāras)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Dakṣiṇāmūrti Stotra)
- **Daksha curses Candra with wasting disease** — Daksha curses Candra with kshaya-roga (wasting disease) so his brilliance dwindles away, in response to the wives' complaint _(actors: Daksha, Candra)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/nakshatras.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6.6)
- **Dattātreya's eternal tapas on the summit (Datta-tīrtha)** — The avadhūta Dattātreya performs and still performs deathless tapas on Girnar's highest peak, sanctifying it as Vastrāpatha _(actors: Dattātreya, Atri, Anasūyā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa Prabhāsa-khaṇḍa (Vastrāpatha-kṣetra-māhātmya); Datta-sampradāya tradition)
- **Defeat of Śumbha and Niśumbha** — The hymn extends praise to the Goddess's destruction of the asuras Śumbha and Niśumbha and their armies _(actors: Durgā/Caṇḍikā, Śumbha, Niśumbha, asura host)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/mahishasura-mardini-stotra.md) — (Devī Māhātmya; Mahiṣāsura-mardinī stotra)
- **Disclosure of the sixteen-word mahāmantra** — Brahmā discloses the sixteen-word, thirty-two-syllable Hare Rāma / Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra as the audible form of the Name and the supreme Kali-crossing formula _(actors: Brahmā, Nārada)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/kalisantarana-upanishad.md) — (Kālisantaraṇa Upaniṣad; Gauḍīya/ISKCON usage (received order))
- **Drying and Disappearance of the Sarasvatī** — The once-mighty Sarasvati dries and goes underground (the vinashana), re-emerging unseen at sacred confluences such as the Triveni Sangam _(actors: Sarasvati)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/prayagraj-triveni-sangam.md) — (Mahabharata)
- **Durvasa Hurls the Kritya Fire-Demon** — Enraged that the king 'ate' before serving him, Durvasa tears a lock from his hair and creates a blazing female fire-demon, the Kritya, to attack Ambarisha _(actors: Durvasa, the Kritya, Ambarisha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ambarisha-durvasa.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 9.4.45-48)
- **Duṣyanta denies Śakuntalā; the celestial voice names Bharata** — Duṣyanta publicly disowns Śakuntalā and son until a sky-voice affirms the child, who is named Bharata and crowned heir _(actors: Duṣyanta, Śakuntalā, Bharata)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shakuntala-bharata.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva, Sambhava)
- **Emanation of the Daśa Mahāvidyā from Satī** — The ten Mahāvidyā goddesses emanate from Satī (or Pārvatī) when she blocks Śiva's exit in a quarrel, surrounding him with ten terrifying forms _(actors: Satī, Śiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shakta-and-tantra-lineages.md) — (Wikipedia, Mahavidya)
- **Enrolment in Śiva's bhūta-gaṇa** — Sudalaimāḍaṉ is folded into Śiva's host of fierce cremation-ground attendant spirits as a localized Tamil bhūta-guardian _(actors: Sudalaimāḍaṉ, Śiva, the bhūta-gaṇa)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/sudalaimadan.md) — (Tamil Śaiva folk tradition)
- **First birth as Pṛśnigarbha** — In the first age the Lord is born to Sutapas and Pṛśni and becomes renowned by the name Pṛśnigarbha, 'he of Pṛśni's womb' _(actors: Viṣṇu (as Pṛśnigarbha), Sutapas, Pṛśni)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/prishnigarbha-three-births.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.3.41)
- **Founding of Pushkara as Brahma's tirtha** — Brahma's sacrifice founds Pushkara (Ajmer) as his chief shrine, with the Savitri/Gayatri episode explaining Brahma's near-absence of temples _(actors: Brahma, Savitri, Gayatri)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/padma-purana.md) — (Padma Purana (Srishti Khanda))
- **Gaṇḍakī enumerated as a tīrtha in the Tīrtha-yātrā Parva** — Sages Pulastya/Dhaumya list the Gaṇḍakī and its śālagrāma-tīrtha among the great sin-cleansing fords during the Pāṇḍavas' pilgrimage _(actors: Pulastya, Dhaumya, the Pāṇḍavas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/muktinath-gandaki-shalagrama-kshetra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva (Tīrtha-yātrā Parva))
- **Gaṇeśa-as-crow tips the kamaṇḍalu — Talakāverī descent** — During a famine Gaṇeśa in crow-form tips Agastya's pot, releasing Kāverī to well up at Talakāverī and flow east _(actors: Gaṇeśa (as crow), Agastya, Kāverī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sacred-rivers-and-descent-myths.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Agastya-saṃhitā)
- **Gajendra-Moksha at Tirukkavithalam** — Vishnu as Gajendra Varadan saves the elephant-king Gajendra from the crocodile, the Gajendra-moksha legend localized at Tirukkavithalam _(actors: Vishnu, Gajendra)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/108-divya-desams.md) — (Bhagavata Purana tradition / sthala-purana)
- **Ganesha restored with an elephant head** — To console the grieving Parvati, Shiva fixes an elephant's head onto the boy's body, reviving him as Gajanana and granting him first worship among gods _(actors: Shiva, Parvati, Ganesha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Garuḍa fetches the amṛta; serpents get forked tongues** — Garuḍa frees Vinatā by fetching the gods' nectar as nāga-ransom; Indra reclaims it and the snakes split their tongues licking the kuśa-grass _(actors: Garuḍa, Vinatā, the nāgas, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/astika-sarpa-satra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva 23–34)
- **Ghṛtācī as consort of Viśvakarmā** — In the Vāmana Purāṇa tradition Ghṛtācī is named the wife of the architect-god Viśvakarmā, mother of his daughters (including Saṃjñā/Saraṇyū) and artisan progeny, linking her by adjacency to the Saṃvaraṇa–Sūrya material _(actors: Ghṛtācī, Viśvakarmā)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/apsaras.md) — (Vāmana Purāṇa)
- **Indra slays Namuci by a foam-trick** — Indra kills the demon Namuci, who could be slain by neither wet nor dry nor day nor night, using a trick of foam _(actors: Indra, Namuci)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/indra.md) — (Rigveda)
- **Indra slays the Vṛtras with Dadhyañc's bones** — Dadhyañc's horse-head is recovered from the lake Śaryaṇāvat near Kurukṣetra, and Indra, using the bones of that head, slays ninety-nine Vṛtras — the Vedic germ of the spine-vajra story _(actors: Indra, Dadhyañc)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dadhichi.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.84.13; Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 14.1.1)
- **Ishvara Gita spoken by Shiva** — After Vishnu and Shiva embrace, Shiva expounds Atman, Brahman, Prakriti, Maya, yoga and moksha through a Shaiva non-dualist lens — a Bhagavad Gita with Shiva as spokesman _(actors: Shiva, Vishnu, the rishis)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/kurma-purana.md) — (Kurma Purana (Ishvara Gita, Uttara-vibhaga))
- **Śiva Lifts Kāśī on His Trident at Pralaya** — At each universal dissolution Śiva lifts Kashi up on his trident to keep it safe, setting it back when creation resumes, so the city alone survives every pralaya _(actors: Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kashi-varanasi.md) — (Skanda Purana, Kashi-khanda)
- **Judgment of the soul by Yama and Citragupta** — At Yama's city the scribe Citragupta reads the ledger of deeds and Yama assigns heaven, a hell, or rebirth _(actors: Yama, Citragupta, the soul)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/garuda-purana.md) — (Garuda Purana (Preta-khanda))
- **Kakudmi and Revati visit Brahmaloka** — King Kakudmi (Raivata), finding no suitor worthy of his daughter Revati, travels with her to Brahma's realm to ask Brahma directly for a husband, arriving while Brahma is absorbed in a Gandharva performance _(actors: Kakudmi/Raivata, Revati, Brahma, Gandharvas Haha and Huhu)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-05-raivata.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 9.3.27-36; Vishnu Purana 4.1)
- **Kalpa, manvantara and yuga arithmetic** — Exposition of the fourteen Manus, the mahāyuga/manvantara/kalpa numbers and the four yugas — a base witness for the timeline's chronology _(actors: the fourteen Manus)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vayu-purana.md) — (Vāyu Purāṇa)
- **Kāmadeva regains his form in Kāmarūpa** — The love-god Kāma, burnt by Śiva, regains his form and desire in this land, glossing the name Kāma-rūpa _(actors: Kāmadeva, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kamakhya-nilachala-kamarupa.md) — (Kālikā Purāṇa; Yoginī Tantra)
- **Kshipra Breaks Mahadev into 84 Pieces** — In one legend the river Shipra breaks Mahadev into 84 pieces to slay the demon Dushana, each piece becoming one of the Chaurasi Mahadev shrines _(actors: Shiva-Mahadev, Kshipra, Dushana)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ujjain-avantika.md) — (Avantya-khanda mahatmya)
- **Madalasa's Atma-jnana lullaby and her sons' renunciation** — Queen Madalasa, a jivanmukta, sings non-dual Vedanta to each infant son, and her first three sons renounce the world straightaway _(actors: Madalasa, King Ritadhvaja, Vikranta, Subahu, Shatrumardana)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/markandeya-purana.md) — (Markandeya Purana (chs. 16-44))
- **Mahasarasvati slays Shumbha-Nishumbha** — As the sattvika aspect, the Goddess slays Shumbha and Nishumbha and emanates Kali/Chandika in the third carita _(actors: Mahasarasvati, Shumbha, Nishumbha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 5-13)
- **Marriage of Savitri and Satyavan in the forest** — Ashvapati gives Savitri to Satyavan in the hermitage; she dons bark-cloth and serves her blind father-in-law Dyumatsena as a devoted ascetic-wife _(actors: Savitri, Satyavan, Ashvapati, Dyumatsena)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/savitri-satyavan.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva 293)
- **Meenakshi Tirukalyanam — the celestial wedding at Madurai** — Born from the Pandya king's yajna with a third breast that vanishes on meeting Shiva, the fish-eyed goddess Meenakshi conquers the directions, is crowned Pandya sovereign, and weds Shiva as Sundareswarar; enacted as the Chithirai Tirukalyanam, one of Shiva's 64 tiruvilaiyadal _(actors: Meenakshi (Devi), Shiva/Sundareswarar, the Pandya king)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Tiruvilaiyadal Puranam / Halasya Mahatmya)
- **Mīnākṣī's conquests and meeting with Śiva** — Crowned as warrior-queen Taḍātakai, she conquers the directions and besieges Kailāsa, where her third breast vanishes on meeting Śiva, revealing him as her destined consort _(actors: Mīnākṣī (Taḍātakai), Śiva (Sundareśvara))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/madurai-meenakshi-sundareshwara-kshetra.md) — (Tiruviḷaiyāḍal Purāṇam)
- **Mohinī beguiles Śiva, begetting Ayyappa** — Mohinī later beguiles Śiva, their union begetting Ayyappa (Hari-Hara-putra), and also deludes the demon Bhasmāsura _(actors: Mohinī (Viṣṇu), Śiva, Ayyappa)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vishnu-forms-and-vyuhas.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa)
- **Murugan Slays Surapadman and Weds Deivanai** — At the Arupadai Veedu abodes Murugan slays the demon Surapadman (who falls at Tiruchendur) and marries Deivanai at Thiruparankundram _(actors: Murugan, Surapadman, Deivanai)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/temple-networks-and-circuits.md) — (Thirumurugatrupadai)
- **Namuci-slaying as a source of Indra's brahmahatyā stain** — The treacherous twilight beheading of Namuci, an oath-breaking, is counted (with the Vṛtra- and Triśiras-slayings) among the deeds that incur Indra's brahmahatyā / sin-stain; some retellings add Namuci's severed head rolling after Indra reproaching him for breaking the truce _(actors: Indra, Namuci)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/asura-wars-and-roster.md) — (Mahābhārata; Purāṇic Indra-guilt cycles)
- **Negation of ritual, caste, and observance (Chapters 2, 7)** — The avadhūta is declared beyond varṇāśrama, prescribed conduct, purity-rules, praise and blame — his ruleless wandering body itself the teaching _(actors: Dattātreya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/avadhuta-gita.md) — (Avadhūta Gītā 2, 7)
- **Nāga-jewel rescue from Pātāla** — When Merakhiyā is snake-bitten/dying, Janbāī plunges into the netherworld waters to fetch the life-restoring nāga-maṇi _(actors: Janbāī/Khodiyār, Merakhiyā, Pātāla nāgas)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khodiyar-mata.md) — (Cāraṇ folk tradition)
- **Nārada's Journey to Śvetadvīpa** — Nārada journeys north past Mount Meru to the White Island to behold the universal form of the Lord and the perfected single-minded white devotees _(actors: Nārada, the white ekāntins)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narayaniya.md) — (Mahabharata Shanti Parva (Narayaniya))
- **Nārada's previous life as the maidservant's son** — In a former kalpa Nārada was a poor maidservant's boy purified by serving travelling bhakti-yogins, granted a fleeting vision of the Lord, who at death merged into Nārāyaṇa and was reborn the immortal sage _(actors: Nārada (former birth), bhakti-yogins, his mother, Nārāyaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 1.6)
- **Nārāyaṇa creates Urvaśī from his thigh** — When Indra sends apsarases to break the sages' tapas, Nārāyaṇa generates the beautiful Urvaśī from his thigh, shaming the nymphs and proving his serenity _(actors: Indra, Nārāyaṇa, the apsarases, Urvaśī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/badrinath-badarikashrama-kshetra.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa; Devī-Bhāgavata)
- **Prajapati-to-Vishnu attribution shift** — Across the Brahmana-to-Purana transition the creator-tortoise is re-identified as a form of Vishnu/Narayana, paralleling Matsya and Varaha _(actors: Prajapati, Vishnu/Narayana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kurma-avatara.md) — (Shatapatha Brahmana 7.5.1; Puranic tradition)
- **Prāṇa overcomes the demons (Pavamāna prayer)** — The vital breath (prāṇa) overcomes the demons' evil, the context of the 'asato mā sadgamaya' prayer leading from untruth to truth, darkness to light, death to immortality _(actors: prāṇa, the demons)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/brihadaranyaka-upanishad.md) — (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 1.3.28)
- **Pratardana dialogue with Indra** — Indra teaches Pratardana that he is prāṇa, the conscious self, life and immortality, declaring life-breath and cognition inseparable _(actors: Indra, Pratardana)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/principal-upanishads-detail.md) — (Kauṣītaki Upaniṣad 3)
- **Pārvatī Creates Gaṇeśa at Gaurī Kuṇḍa** — At Gaurī Kuṇḍa below Dolma La, Pārvatī fashions a son from the paste of her body to guard her bath; Śiva strikes off his head and replaces it, the boy becoming Gaṇeśa _(actors: Pārvatī, Śiva, Gaṇeśa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/manasarovar-kailash-yatra.md) — (sthala-purana; Ganesha birth-myth)
- **Pārvatī slays the demons Kṛti and Vāsa** — Pārvatī (Gaurī) slays the asuras Kṛti and Vāsa in the field, the episode etymologizing the Kṛttivāsa epithet of the liṅga _(actors: Pārvatī/Gaurī, the asuras Kṛti and Vāsa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lingaraja-bhubaneswar-ekamra-kshetra.md) — (Ekāmra Purāṇa)
- **Ūrdhva Tāṇḍava and the Kālī dance-contest (Tiruvalangadu)** — In the banyan cremation-forest Śiva defeats the goddess Kālī in a dance-duel by raising one leg vertically, a posture she would not copy out of modesty, pacifying her _(actors: Śiva-Vaṭāraṇyeśvara, Kālī, Kāraikkāl Ammaiyār)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancha-sabhai-nataraja-dance-halls.md) — (Tiruvalangadu sthala-purāṇa; Tēvāram; Kāraikkāl Ammaiyār's Aṟputat Tiruvantāti; Periya Purāṇa)
- **Refutation of jīva and body** — The brāhmaṇa is shown to be neither the soul (common to all beings) nor the body (made of the same five elements, no colour-rule of caste) _(actors: Upaniṣadic teacher)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/vajrasuchika-upanishad.md) — (Vajrasūcikā Upaniṣad 3-4)
- **Roll-call of the sixteen chakravartins** — Marutta, Suhotra, Brihadratha/Paurava, Shibi, Rama Dasarathi, Bhagiratha, Dilipa, Mandhatr, Yayati, Ambarisha, Shashabindu, Gaya, Rantideva, Bharata, Prithu, and Sagara are each praised as supreme sacrificer-conqueror-givers and shown to have died _(actors: Marutta, Suhotra, Brihadratha, Shibi, Rama, Bhagiratha, Dilipa, Mandhatr, Yayati, Ambarisha, Shashabindu, Gaya, Rantideva, Bharata, Prithu, Sagara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shodasarajakiya-sixteen-kings.md) — (Mahabharata, Drona Parva 55-72)
- **Romaśā's bridal dialogue with King Svanaya/Bhāvayavya** — Queen Romaśā answers her royal husband's touch in a frank conjugal verse appended to Kakṣīvat's gift-praise hymn _(actors: Romaśā, King Bhāvayavya/Svanaya, Kakṣīvat)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/rigvedic-women-seers-brahmavadinis.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.126.7)
- **Rudra Creates and Rolls Up the Worlds** — The one Rudra, allowing no second, holds all worlds with his ruling powers, creates beings, and at the end of time rolls them up (samcukoca) into himself _(actors: Rudra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/svetashvatara-upanishad.md) — (Shvetashvatara Upanishad 3.2)
- **Sarayū saṅgama tīrthas with Gomatī and Gaṅgā** — The Sarayū's confluences with the Gomatī and (via the Ghāghrā) the Gaṅgā are revered bathing-tīrthas, read as a reunion of sister-rivers _(actors: Sarayū, Gomatī, Gaṅgā (rivers))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sarayu-ayodhya-river.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa 23–24; Skanda Purāṇa Ayodhyā-māhātmya; sthala-tradition)
- **Sati immolates herself at the Daksha-yajna** — Sati, married to Shiva against her father Daksha's contempt and humiliated at his sacrifice, immolates herself in yogic fire _(actors: Sati, Daksha, Shiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Rudra-samhita, Sati-khanda))
- **Satī's Navel Falls at Kāñcī** — In the Satī dismemberment myth the goddess's navel falls at Kāñcī, anchoring Kāmākṣī as a Śakti Pīṭha and the 'navel of the goddess' _(actors: Satī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanchipuram-city-of-temples.md) — (Devi-Bhagavata / Shakti-pitha lists)
- **Satī's self-immolation and the Dakṣa-yajña** — Dakṣa stages an insulting sacrifice; Satī immolates herself, rupturing Dakṣa and Śiva, after which Pārvatī later performs austerity to win Śiva _(actors: Satī, Dakṣa, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahma-purana.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa)
- **Satyavān dies and Sāvitrī follows Yama** — On the foretold day Satyavān dies; Yama comes in person to take his soul and Sāvitrī follows Death southward _(actors: Satyavān, Yama, Sāvitrī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/savitri-satyavan.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva 294–295)
- **Seer of Rigveda Mandala VI** — Bharadvaja is the apical rishi to whom the sixth family-book of the Rigveda (75 hymns, chiefly to Agni and Indra) is ascribed _(actors: Bharadvaja, the Bharadvaja seer-family)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharadvaja.md) — (Rigveda Mandala VI)
- **Self-manifestation of Bhu-Varaha at Srimushnam** — The Sveta-Varaha (white-boar) murti lifting Bhu-devi arises on its own at the white-boar tank, the chief southern Varaha-kshetra _(actors: Vishnu as Bhu-Varaha; Bhu-devi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-svayamvyakta-vishnu-kshetras.md) — (Varaha Purana; Srimushna-mahatmya)
- **Sivah saktya yuktah — the opening declaration** — Verse 1 declares that only when joined with Shakti is Shiva able to act, the charter statement of Shakta theology _(actors: Shiva, Shakti (Tripurasundari))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/soundarya-lahari.md) — (Saundarya-lahari 1)
- **Slaughter of the Exposed Kalakeyas** — With the ocean drained, the gods slaughter the exposed Kalakeya asuras, already weakened by the wrath of the brahmins _(actors: the Devas, the Kalakeyas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/agastya.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva)
- **Slaying of Mahiṣāsura** — After a shape-shifting duel the Goddess pins the buffalo-demon Mahiṣāsura and beheads him as the true demon emerges from the buffalo's mouth, earning the name Mahiṣāsura-mardinī _(actors: Durgā (Mahiṣāsura-mardinī), Mahiṣāsura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devī Māhātmya ch.2-4)
- **Slaying of Malla and Maṇi** — The god slays Malla (hence Mallāri); the dying Maṇi gifts his white horse and wins the boon of joined worship and animal offerings _(actors: Mārtāṇḍa Bhairava, Maṇi, Malla)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khandoba-martanda-bhairava.md) — (Mallāri Māhātmya; Jejuri/Kannada Mailāra folk variants)
- **Slaying of Surapadma at Tiruchendur** — After a six-day fast Murugan fells the asura Surapadma in the seaside battle at Tiruchendur, the climactic victory of the Skanda war-cycle _(actors: Murugan, Surapadma)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Kanda Puranam; Surasamharam legend)
- **Taraka-vadha by Skanda** — Anointed Devasenapati, Skanda leads the devas and slays Tarakasura (and in expanded cycles Surapadma, Simhamukha and Pralamba), restoring cosmic order _(actors: Skanda, Tarakasura, Surapadma)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/skanda-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Skanda Purana (Maheshvara-khanda); Tamil Kandapuranam)
- **Teaching by silence (mauna-vyākhyāna)** — Dakṣiṇāmūrti raises the cin-mudrā and the disciples' doubts dissolve through wordless transmission of para-brahman _(actors: Dakṣiṇāmūrti (Śiva); the four Kumāras)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dakshinamurti.md) — (Dakṣiṇāmūrti Stotra; Sūta Saṃhitā)
- **Teaching of ajāta-vāda (no world, no bondage, no liberation)** — Through sustained refrain Ṛbhu affirms that body, mind, guru, disciple, bondage, path, and liberation do not exist as separate realities — only the one Self is _(actors: Ṛbhu, Nidāgha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ribhu-gita.md) — (Śivarahasya Purāṇa (Ribhu-Gītā))
- **Teaching of māyā and the unfolding world** — The Goddess expounds māyā as her own power by which the one appears as many, blending Sāṃkhya prakṛti-categories with Vedāntic non-duality _(actors: Bhuvaneśvarī, Himavat)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/devi-gita.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.32–33)
- **The ahaituki bhakti prayer** — The poet prays for nothing but motiveless devotion birth after birth, disclaiming wealth, followers, beauty, and fame _(actors: Kulasekhara; Jagad-isha (Vishnu))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/mukunda-mala.md) — (Mukunda-mala Stotra (na dhanam na janam verse))
- **The avatāra-and-dharma doctrine** — Gaṇeśa reveals himself as the unborn Lord who takes form age after age to protect dharma, adapting Bhagavad Gītā 4 into a Gāṇapatya frame _(actors: Gajānana (Gaṇeśa), King Vareṇya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ganesha-gita.md) — (Gaṇeśa Purāṇa, Krīḍā-khaṇḍa ch. 140)
- **The eight-case Rāma-nāma benediction** — The famed verse declines the name 'Rāma' through all eight Sanskrit grammatical cases, beginning 'Rāmo rāja-maṇiḥ sadā vijayate.' _(actors: Rāma (the name), the reciter)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/ramaraksha-stotra.md) — (Rāma-rakṣā Stotra, v. 14)
- **The gods install their fragments at the five ārāmas** — Indra, Soma/Candra, Viṣṇu and Kumāra each install a liṅga-fragment, giving the sites their names (Amarārāma, Somārāma, Kumārārāma, etc.) _(actors: Indra, Soma/Candra, Viṣṇu, Kumāra/Skanda)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancharama-kshetras-andhra.md) — (Pañcārāma sthala-purāṇa variants (folk/regional))
- **The hump surfaces at Kedarnath** — Re-emerging piecemeal across Garhwal, the bull's hump rises at Kedarnath as a conical svayambhu rock; the Pāṇḍavas raise the temple and are absolved _(actors: Śiva, Pāṇḍavas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kedarnath-kedara-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa; Mahābhārata-derived tradition)
- **The ladder of negation across six verses** — The hymn negates inner organ and senses, vital airs and sheaths, passions and purusharthas, merit/sin and ritual, death/caste/kin, then declares the formless all-pervading Self _(actors: the meditating Self / aspirant; Adi Shankara as author)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/nirvana-shatkam.md) — (Nirvana Shatkam verses 1-6)
- **The Mahābhārata Vaitaraṇī ford** — The Vaitaraṇī is named as a Kaliṅga river-ford in the Tīrtha-yātrā Parva (Pulastya's roster and Lomaśa's walked route), bathing there yielding cleansing and cow-gift merit _(actors: Yudhiṣṭhira; Nārada/Pulastya; Lomaśa; the Pāṇḍavas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaitarani-jajpur-viraja-kshetra.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva, Tīrtha-yātrā Parva)
- **The phala-shruti and forgiveness prayer** — The closing verses declare the fruit of bilva-offering and end on a ksama-prarthana, the single offered leaf standing in for ritual faults _(actors: The worshipper; Shiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bilvashtaka.md) — (Bilvashtaka vv.7-8)
- **The race around the parents** — In a race against Kārtikeya for a divine fruit, Gaṇeśa circumambulates his parents declaring them his whole world and wins, sending the slighted Kārtikeya south _(actors: Gaṇeśa, Kārtikeya, Śiva, Pārvatī, Nārada)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Skanda Purāṇa)
- **The road as shedding of attachments** — Śiva leaves Nandi at Pahalgām, the moon at Chandanwāḍī, the serpents at Śeṣanāg, Gaṇeśa and the five elements at Mahāgaṇeś/Pañchtaraṇī, so none can overhear _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Nandi, Candra, the nāgas, Gaṇeśa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/amarnath-ice-lingam-and-amar-katha.md) — (Amarnāth Yātrā sthala tradition)
- **The Sarasvati resurfaces at Prabhasa** — The vanished Sarasvati river flows unseen and re-emerges at Prabhasa to pour into the ocean, forming the Triveni river-sea confluence _(actors: Sarasvati (river), Hiranya, Kapila)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/prabhasa-somnath-tirtha.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva 80-82; Skanda Purana Prabhasa-khanda)
- **The Sarasvatī merges into the sea at Nārāyaṇa-sarovar** — The lost Sarasvatī river is held to complete its course and join the Arabian Sea at the Kutch coast, an oceanic confluence-sanctity _(actors: River Sarasvatī (personified); Kutch tradition)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-sarovar-sacred-lakes.md) — (Kutch sthala-tradition)
- **The Spider and the Elephant at Thiruvanaikaval** — Two cursed Shiva-ganas reborn as a spider and an elephant worship the jambu-linga; their rivalry kills both, and Shiva frees them from the curse, the spider reborn as the Chola king Koccengat _(actors: Malyavan, Pushpadanta (spider and elephant), Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancha-bhuta-sthalams.md) — (Jambukeswarar mahatmya)
- **The swan teaches forbearance and patient silence** — The swan-Brahmā teaches that the highest good is self-conquest: repay abuse not with retaliation but with patient silence and equanimity, conquering both the abuser and oneself _(actors: Brahmā in swan-form; the Sādhyas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-gita.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva, Mokṣadharma ~12.299)
- **The Vedavyāsa office and the 28th Vyāsa** — A Veda-Vyāsa is appointed each Dvāpara to re-arrange the Veda; Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana is the 28th of the cycle, with Aśvatthāmā named as the coming 29th _(actors: Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana Vyāsa, the twenty-eight Vyāsas, Aśvatthāmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyasa.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 3.3; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 1.4)
- **The Vritra-gita: Vritra accepts his destiny** — The defeated asura-king Vritra, taught by Shukracharya, expounds calm renunciation and devotion to Vishnu _(actors: Vritra, Shukracharya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/lesser-gitas-catalog.md) — (Mahabharata Santi Parva ~12.279-281)
- **Trampling of Śiva's consecrated offering** — Not seeing them, Puṣpadanta treads on bilva leaves/flowers (or nirmālya) already consecrated to Śiva, and the sacrilege instantly strips him of his invisibility _(actors: Puṣpadanta, Śiva (offended deity))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-mahimna-stotra.md) — (Puṣpadanta frame-legend (folk; trampled-object variants regional))
- **Trampling of Śiva's sacred offering** — Unaware, Puṣpadanta treads on bilva leaves/flowers already consecrated to Śiva and instantly loses his invisibility _(actors: Puṣpadanta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-mahimna-stotra.md) — (Śiva-mahimnaḥ stotra frame; folk legend)
- **Tumburu–Nārada musical rivalry** — Nārada and the gandharva Tumburu are paired as rival celestial virtuosi, with Tumburu's superior vīṇā-craft repeatedly humbling Nārada's pride before Viṣṇu, Śiva and Indra _(actors: Nārada, Tumburu, Viṣṇu, Śiva, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada.md) — (Mahābhārata; Purāṇas)
- **Tyranny from Śūnyaka and desolation of the worlds** — Bhaṇḍāsura rules from the City of the Void with brothers Viṣukra and Viṣaṅga, draining the cosmos of love and fertility; Nārada counsels the devas to invoke Parāśakti _(actors: Bhaṇḍāsura, Viṣukra, Viṣaṅga, the devas, Nārada)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna))
- **Uddālaka teaches Śvetaketu 'Tat tvam asi'** — To humble his conceited son Śvetaketu, Uddālaka Āruṇi teaches the Sad-vidyā through nine parables, each closing with 'Tat tvam asi' — that thou art _(actors: Uddālaka Āruṇi, Śvetaketu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/chandogya-upanishad.md) — (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.1–6.16)
- **Śukra curses Yayāti with premature old age** — Discovering the broken oath, Śukra curses Yayāti to instant decrepitude, granting the loophole that he may trade his age for another's youth _(actors: Yayāti, Śukrācārya, Devayānī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yayati.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva 85; Bhāgavata 9.18)
- **Varāha lifts Bhū-devī and slays Hiraṇyākṣa at Śrīmuṣṇam** — The Boar avatāra rescues the Earth-goddess from the pralaya-waters and kills the demon Hiraṇyākṣa, then remains present at Śrīmuṣṇam as Bhū-Varāha _(actors: Vishnu (Bhū-Varāha), Bhū-devī, Hiraṇyākṣa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/avatara-kshetras-srikurmam-srimushnam.md) — (Varāha Purāṇa; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 3; Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa Śrīmuṣṇa-māhātmya)
- **Vasiṣṭha's agni-kuṇḍa fire-sacrifice** — Robbed of his wish-cow or beset by asuras, Vasiṣṭha kindles a great fire-pit atop Arbuda and performs a homa to bring forth defenders of dharma _(actors: Vasiṣṭha, Viśvāmitra (rival), asuras)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mount-abu-arbuda-achaleshwar.md) — (Pṛthvīrāj Rāso; Agnikula bardic tradition [late text][disputed])
- **Vāg-Devatās recite the Lalitā-Sahasranāma** — The Vāg-Devatās recite the thousand-name hymn to Lalitā to Hayagrīva, encoding the Śrī-Cakra and Śrī-Vidyā mantra doctrine _(actors: Vāg-Devatās, Hayagrīva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahmanda-purana.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna))
- **Vishnu counsels the churning truce** — The devas approach Vishnu at the Kshira-Sagara, who counsels a temporary truce with the asuras to churn the ocean for amrita, promising the devas will ultimately keep it _(actors: Vishnu, Brahma, the devas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/samudra-manthana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 8.6; Mahabharata, Adi Parva)
- **Vishnudutas Rescue Ajamila from the Yamadutas** — Hearing the holy name, four Vishnudutas descend and forcibly stop the three Yamadutas from binding and removing Ajamila's soul, then debate the jurisdiction of Yama _(actors: Vishnudutas, Yamadutas, Ajamila)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ajamila.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6.1.34-6.2.49)
- **Vishnu's Three Strides (Trivikrama)** — Vishnu measures out the three worlds — earth, mid-air, and heaven — in three strides, establishing the cosmos as living-space for gods and men and setting his 'highest step' as the abode of bliss _(actors: Vishnu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedic-deities-and-suktas.md) — (Rigveda 1.22; 1.154)
- **Vishnu Worships Ganesha to Subdue Madhu and Kaitabha at Siddhatek** — At creation's edge Vishnu cannot subdue the asuras Madhu and Kaitabha until he worships Ganesha at Siddhatek and obtains siddhi, then prevails _(actors: Vishnu, Madhu, Kaitabha, Ganesha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashtavinayaka.md) — (Ganesha Purana; cf. Devi-Mahatmya)
- **Visnu accepts the curse and offers two roads** — Visnu bows to the Kumaras, accepts the irrevocable curse, and offers his servants seven devotee-births or three enemy-births back to him _(actors: Visnu, Jaya, Vijaya, Sanaka-kumaras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/jaya-vijaya.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 3.16; 7.1)
- **Viṣṇu Leaves His Footprint at Har-ki-Pauri** — Viṣṇu appears at Har-ki-Pauri in the Vedic age and leaves his footprints in the stone of the ghāṭ, kept perpetually touched by the Gaṅgā _(actors: Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/haridwar-rishikesh-ganga-dwara.md) — (Wikipedia, Har Ki Pauri)
- **Vrika's Flesh-Offering Penance at Kedara** — At Kedaranatha Vrika feeds the sacrificial fire with pieces of his own flesh for seven days until Shiva emerges from the fire to stay his self-beheading _(actors: Vrika, Shiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhasmasura-and-vrikasura.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 10.88.17-19)
- **Yajña on Gayāsura's Body** — Asked for a boon, Gayāsura offers his own body as the altar; he lies head-to-north and Brahmā and the devas perform the great sacrifice upon him, making his body the holiest ground _(actors: Gayāsura, Brahmā, devas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/gaya-pitru-tirtha.md) — (Vayu Purana, Gaya-mahatmya)
- **Ajamila's Repentance and Liberation at Haridvara** — Restored and transformed by faith, Ajamila renounces his home, performs bhakti-yoga at Haridvara, and at his second death is borne by the Vishnudutas to Vaikuntha _(actors: Ajamila, Vishnudutas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ajamila.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6.2.25-46)
- **Ajāta-vāda: no creation, no bondage, no seeker** — Core teaching that nothing was ever created or bound, there is no aspirant and no path, and liberation is only the falling-away of an illusion _(actors: Dattātreya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/avadhuta-gita.md) — (Avadhūta Gītā 1, 3, 4, 6)
- **Ashtavakra Made Straight in the River** — The restored Kahoda bids Ashtavakra bathe in the river Samanga, and his eight bends are made straight _(actors: Ashtavakra, Kahoda)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/ashtavakra.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva CXXXIV)
- **Aṣṭāvakra debates the celestial woman on women's independence** — The sage Aṣṭāvakra, sent to test marriage, debates a celestial woman (Diśā) on whether a woman is independent or always under guardianship, expounding strī-dharma _(actors: Aṣṭāvakra, Diśā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/13-anushasana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 13.19-21)
- **Bharata becomes Cakravartin and eponym of Bhāratavarṣa** — Bharata grows into a universal sovereign whose name is given to the dynasty and to the subcontinent Bhāratavarṣa _(actors: Bharata)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shakuntala-bharata.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva, Sambhava)
- **Bilva-offering on Mahashivaratri and Pradosha** — The hymn accompanies bilva-arpana held especially efficacious on Mahashivaratri and at each fortnightly Pradosha dusk _(actors: Devotees; Shiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bilvashtaka.md) — (Bilvashtaka; living liturgical tradition)
