- **Birth of Kauśikī and Kālikā** — From Pārvatī's bodily sheath emerges the radiant Ambikā/Kauśikī to fight Śumbha-Niśumbha, while her darkened remainder becomes Kālikā _(actors: Pārvatī, Kauśikī/Ambikā, Kālikā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devī Māhātmya ch.5)
- **Birth of the Agnikula Rajput clans** — Armed heroes spring from the fire-pit as ancestors of the Agnivaṃśa Rajputs — Pratīhāra, Cāhamāna (Chauhan), Caulukya (Solanki) and Paramāra; variants give a single Paramāra hero _(actors: Vasiṣṭha, the fire-born Rajput ancestors (Pratihara, Chauhan, Solanki, Paramara))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mount-abu-arbuda-achaleshwar.md) — (Pṛthvīrāj Rāso; bardic genealogies; Paramāra inscriptional variant [late text][disputed])
- **Body-distribution: head at Paśupatinātha** — Trans-Himalayan tradition holds the bull's head/face surfaced at Paśupatinātha in Nepal while the hump remains at Kedarnath, linking the two kṣetras _(actors: Śiva (bull-form))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kedarnath-kedara-kshetra.md) — (Folk/regional tradition; cf. Skanda Purāṇa Kedāra-khaṇḍa Pañcha Kedār variant)
- **Boy-Sage Śveta Saved from Yama** — The boy-sage Sveta, fated to die at sixteen, is saved from Yama by Shiva at Thiruvenkadu, where Shiva subdues death itself _(actors: Sveta, Shiva, Yama)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/navagraha-temples.md) — (Sthala-purana)
- **Brahma's revelation of elapsed time** — Brahma laughs and explains that twenty-seven chaturyugas (roughly 116 million human years) have passed on Earth during their brief wait, so the proposed bridegrooms and their dynasties are long dead — an early literary depiction of time dilation _(actors: Brahma, Kakudmi, Revati)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-05-raivata.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 9.3)
- **Bull/buffalo-form Śiva flees the Pāṇḍavas and dives at Guptakāśī** — Pursued by the Pāṇḍavas seeking absolution, the bull-form Śiva dives into the earth at Guptakāśī to re-emerge across the Himalaya _(actors: Śiva (bull/buffalo-form), the Pāṇḍavas, Bhīma)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pashupatinath-kathmandu-nepal-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa)
- **Capture and punishment** — Made visible, Puṣpadanta is seized by the garden guards and imprisoned, bereft of his power _(actors: Puṣpadanta, royal guards, the king)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-mahimna-stotra.md) — (Śiva-mahimnaḥ stotra frame; folk legend)
- **Caraka lineage descends to Bharadvāja and Ātreya** — Indra transmits medicine to Bharadvāja (seeking relief of human suffering), then to Ātreya Punarvasu and Agniveśa, founding the internal-medicine school _(actors: Indra, Bharadvāja, Ātreya Punarvasu, Agniveśa)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/ayurveda-and-the-science-of-life.md) — (Caraka Saṃhitā Sū. 1)
- **Chandra's Curse Tempered at Somnath** — The Moon-god Chandra, cursed by Daksha to wane for neglecting his wives, worships Shiva at Prabhas Patan, and Shiva tempers the curse into periodic waxing and waning, founding the Somnath jyotirlinga _(actors: Chandra (Soma), Daksha, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/12-jyotirlingas.md) — (Shiva Purana; Skanda Purana (Prabhasa Khanda))
- **Child Murugan Teaches Om to Shiva at Swamimalai** — At Swamimalai the child Murugan teaches the meaning of the Pranava (Om) to his father Shiva, becoming Swaminatha _(actors: Murugan, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/temple-networks-and-circuits.md) — (Arupadai Veedu tradition)
- **Dadhīci drinks the gods' weapons** — Tired of guarding the divine weapons deposited with him, Dadhīci dissolves them in water and drinks them, absorbing their power into his bones so his skeleton alone can yield the supreme weapon _(actors: Dadhīci, the gods)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dadhichi.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 6.10)
- **Dadhichi sacrifices his bones for the vajra** — When a Vritra can only be killed by a sage's bones, the ascetic Dadhichi yields his body, and Vishvakarma forges the Vajrayudha from his spine for Indra _(actors: Dadhichi, Vishvakarma, Indra, Vritra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/indra.md) — (Bhagavata Purana; Mahabharata)
- **Declaration of rule-free chanting and phala-śruti** — Brahmā declares the mantra destroys Kali's covering, may be chanted without restriction of time or purity, and grants purification and liberation to the chanter _(actors: Brahmā, Nārada)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/kalisantarana-upanishad.md) — (Kālisantaraṇa Upaniṣad)
- **Durvāsā's curse of forgetting on Śakuntalā** — Daydreaming of her husband Duṣyanta, Śakuntalā fails to honor the visiting Durvāsā, who curses that the one she thinks of will forget her; softened, the curse lifts when the king sees his signet ring — driving the second half of Kālidāsa's drama _(actors: Durvāsā, Śakuntalā, Duṣyanta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ambarisha-durvasa.md) — (Kālidāsa, Abhijñānaśākuntalam Act 4 (Kālidāsa's invention, absent in Mahābhārata))
- **Fall of Satī's body-part establishes the Bhramarāmbā Śakti Pīṭha** — During Śiva's grief-wandering with Satī's corpse, a fragment (right anklet per Pīṭhanirṇaya; neck/upper lip per folk variant) falls at Śrīśaila, fixing the Goddess Bhramarāmbā and Bhairava Sundarānanda _(actors: Satī, Śiva, Viṣṇu, Bhramarāmbā, Bhairava Sundarānanda)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srisailam-srisaila-mallikarjuna-bhramaramba.md) — (Aṣṭādaśa Pīṭha Stotram; Tantra Cūḍāmaṇi/Pīṭhanirṇaya; Skanda Purāṇa (Śrīśaila-khaṇḍa))
- **Four-armed birth and self-revelation to Devakī** — Viṣṇu is born to Devakī in full four-armed majesty in the Mathurā prison and, before shrinking to infant form, reveals that this is his third birth from her and Vasudeva _(actors: Viṣṇu/Kṛṣṇa, Devakī, Vasudeva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/prishnigarbha-three-births.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.3.9-13, 10.3.32-43)
- **Ghora Āṅgirasa instructs Kṛṣṇa Devakīputra** — The sage Ghora Āṅgirasa instructs Kṛṣṇa, son of Devakī — one of the earliest textual mentions of Kṛṣṇa — figuring life as a soma-sacrifice _(actors: Ghora Āṅgirasa, Kṛṣṇa Devakīputra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/chandogya-upanishad.md) — (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 3.17)
- **Hariṇī's curse (apsarā backstory)** — In a former life Indumatī was the apsarā Hariṇī, sent by Indra to break Tṛṇabindu's tapas; the sage cursed her to mortal birth, releasable when a celestial object touched her _(actors: Hariṇī (Indumatī), Indra, sage Tṛṇabindu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/aja-indumati.md) — (Kālidāsa, Raghuvaṃśa canto 8)
- **Hiranyakashipu, Prahlada, and Narasimha (Vishnu P.)** — The asura king Hiranyakashipu persecutes his Vishnu-devoted son Prahlada, prompting Vishnu to erupt as the man-lion Narasimha and slay the boon-protected tyrant _(actors: Hiranyakashipu, Prahlada, Narasimha)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vishnu-purana.md) — (Vishnu Purana (Amsha 1); cf. Bhagavata 7)
- **Import of the Maga (Śākadvīpīya) priests** — Sāmba brings sun-worshipping Maga priests from Śākadvīpa to serve the Sun-image, the Purāṇic memory of the Indo-Iranian solar cult entering India _(actors: Sāmba, Maga/Bhojaka priests)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/konark-sun-temple-arka-kshetra.md) — (Sāmba Purāṇa; Bhaviṣya Purāṇa)
- **Indra eclipses Varuṇa as king of the gods** — Across the later Vedic layers the warrior storm-god Indra rises and displaces Varuṇa as effective king of the gods, his slaying of Vṛtra overtaking Varuṇa's law-sovereignty _(actors: Indra, Varuṇa, Vṛtra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/varuna.md) — (Ṛgveda 7.82-85)
- **Indra's sabotage of the hundredth Aśvamedha** — Because a hundred horse sacrifices entitle the performer to Indra's throne, Indra habitually sabotages the hundredth Aśvamedha to protect his sovereignty _(actors: Indra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/yajnas-and-vedic-rituals.md) — (Shatapatha Brahmana 13; Mahabharata)
- **Instruction of King Alarka by Dattatreya** — Alarka, his kingdom threatened to provoke his awakening, follows his mother's ring-counsel to the avadhuta Dattatreya and receives the realization of the Self, finally renouncing _(actors: Alarka, Madalasa, Dattatreya, Subahu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/markandeya-purana.md) — (Markandeya Purana (chs. 39-43))
- **Śiva narrates the Amar Kathā in the empty cave** — Stripped of every witness, Śiva spreads a deer-skin / ring of fire and tells Pārvatī the secret of amaratva _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/amarnath-ice-lingam-and-amar-katha.md) — (Amar Kathā / Amareśvara māhātmya)
- **Jaya and Vijaya choose three demonic births** — Unable to bear long separation, the two choose to be born three times as Visnu's enemies, each life ended by his hand, to return home swiftly _(actors: Jaya, Vijaya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/jaya-vijaya.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 7.1)
- **Kallazhagar's late arrival and Vaigai crossing** — Kallazhagar (Vishnu, the bride Meenakshi's brother) sets out from Alagar Koil to give away the bride but arrives late, finds the wedding done, and turns back at the river Vaigai; his entry into the river is the Vaishnava climax of the Madurai Chithirai festival, a rare Shaiva-Vaishnava civic confluence _(actors: Kallazhagar/Alagar (Vishnu), Meenakshi, Sundareswarar)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Madurai Chithirai festival tradition; Wikipedia (Chithirai Thiruvizha))
- **Koṭeśvar and the crore of liṅgas (Rāvaṇa's ātma-liṅga)** — Rāvaṇa receives a powerful liṅga from Śiva and, through trickery, leaves a crore of replica liṅgas at Koṭeśvar beside Nārāyaṇa-sarovar _(actors: Rāvaṇa; Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-sarovar-sacred-lakes.md) — (Koṭeśvar sthala-purāṇa)
- **Kumāra consecrates Kumārārāma at Samalkota** — Skanda himself installs and worships his fragment as Kumāra Bhīmeśvara, the site most directly tied to the shattering-myth _(actors: Kumāra/Skanda)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancharama-kshetras-andhra.md) — (Kumārārāma sthala-purāṇa (regional))
- **Lakṣmī becomes the badarī tree to shelter Viṣṇu** — As Viṣṇu does open-air austerities in Himalayan cold, Lakṣmī transforms into a jujube-berry (badarī) tree to shade him, naming the place Badari-kā-āśrama _(actors: Viṣṇu, Lakṣmī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/badrinath-badarikashrama-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Badarī-māhātmya; Wikipedia (Sapta Badri))
- **Mahiṣāsura-vadha (Śākta variant)** — The gods pool their tejas into the lion-riding Durgā/Caṇḍikā, who is armed by each deity and slays the buffalo-demon Mahiṣa _(actors: Durgā/Caṇḍikā, Mahiṣāsura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/devi-bhagavata-upapurana.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata, skandha V)
- **Maya the asura-architect and the burning of Tripura** — The asura craftsman Maya builds the three flying cities and a life-restoring nectar step-well; Vishnu drains the well to stop the demons' resurrection and Shiva destroys Tripura with a single fiery arrow _(actors: Maya, Vishnu (Janardana), Shiva, the Pramatha hosts, the asuras)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/matsya-purana.md) — (Matsya Purana)
- **Meditation on Rāma and Lakṣmaṇa as lions** — Closing verses contemplate Rāma and Lakṣmaṇa striding forth like two bow-bearing lions, with the 'āpadām apahartāram' benediction _(actors: Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/ramaraksha-stotra.md) — (Rāma-rakṣā Stotra, vv. 30-33)
- **Menakā breaks Viśvāmitra's penance** — Indra sends Menakā against the rishi Viśvāmitra; aided by Vāyu and Kāmadeva she seduces him, and from their union is born Śakuntalā _(actors: Menakā, Viśvāmitra, Indra)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/apsaras.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva; Rāmāyaṇa Bāla Kāṇḍa)
- **Month-by-month development of the fetus** — The conceptus passes from kalala (drop) through bubble and lump to a fetus whose head, limbs, spine, face and sense-organs form across eight months _(actors: the fetus in the womb)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/garbha-upanishad.md) — (Garbha Upaniṣad 3–4)
- **Mārkaṇḍeya's vision of the pralaya** — The deathless sage Mārkaṇḍeya survives the dissolution, beholds the infant Viṣṇu on a banyan leaf upon the cosmic waters, is swallowed, and sees the universe inside the Lord's body _(actors: Mārkaṇḍeya, Viṣṇu/Nārāyaṇa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahma-purana.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa)
- **Muktī/Tāmira Tāṇḍava in the copper hall (Tirunelveli)** — Śiva as Nellaiappar dances the liberation-dance in the copper-roofed sabhā of the Kāntimati–Nellaiappar temple, famed for its musical stone pillars _(actors: Śiva-Nellaiappar, Kāntimati)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancha-sabhai-nataraja-dance-halls.md) — (Nellaiappar (Tirunelveli) sthala-purāṇa)
- **Naṭarāja's Ānanda Tāṇḍava** — Śiva as Lord of Dance performs the cosmic dance of bliss at Cidambaram, enacting the five acts (creation, preservation, dissolution, concealment, grace) and crushing the dwarf Apasmāra _(actors: Śiva (Naṭarāja), Apasmāra, Kālī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Cidambaram Naṭarāja tradition, Tirumantiram, Śaiva Siddhānta)
- **Naṭarāja's Ānanda Tāṇḍava — the cosmic dance of bliss** — Śiva as Lord of Dance performs the dance of bliss at Cidambaram, enacting the five cosmic acts (creation, preservation, dissolution, concealment, grace) and crushing the dwarf of ignorance Apasmāra _(actors: Śiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Cidambaram Naṭarāja tradition; Śaiva Siddhānta; Tirumular's Tirumantiram)
- **Naciketas's three boons and Yama's teaching** — The boy Naciketas, sent to Yama's realm, wins three boons and Yama reluctantly teaches him the immortal Self that is never born and never dies _(actors: Naciketas, Yama)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/principal-upanishads-detail.md) — (Kaṭha Upaniṣad)
- **Nandi granted deathlessness and union with Śiva** — Śiva bathes Nandi in the Gaṅgā, grants a third eye and bull-crest, and declares him ageless and never to be parted from himself; weds him to Suyaśā/Marudvatī _(actors: Śiva, Nandi, Suyaśā/Marudvatī)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nandi-and-shaiva-mounts.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa; Liṅga Purāṇa)
- **Naraka and the Kāmarūpa dynasty** — King Naraka of Prāgjyotiṣa-Kāmarūpa worships then transgresses the Goddess, who withdraws favour, fixing the royal line to her shrine _(actors: Narakāsura, Kāmākhyā Devī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kamakhya-nilachala-kamarupa.md) — (Kālikā Purāṇa)
- **Nemīnātha's renunciation, omniscience and nirvāṇa on Ujjayanta** — The 22nd Jain Tīrthaṅkara Nemīnātha (Ariṣṭanemi), Kṛṣṇa's cousin, renounces on his wedding day and attains dīkṣā, kevala-jñāna and nirvāṇa on Girnar _(actors: Nemīnātha (Ariṣṭanemi), Kṛṣṇa (as kinsman))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Jain Uttarapurāṇa; Hari-vaṃśa-purāṇa; Kalpa-sūtra)
- **Nidāgha's awakening** — The disciple Nidāgha recognizes his own identity with the formless Self as the illusion of separateness falls away _(actors: Nidāgha, Ṛbhu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ribhu-gita.md) — (Śivarahasya Purāṇa (Ribhu-Gītā))
- **Nārada's cock-crow thwarts the wedding** — To preserve the celibate Śakti needed to kill Bāṇāsura, Nārada takes a cock's form and crows; Śiva, thinking dawn had passed, turns back and the marriage hour lapses _(actors: Nārada; Śiva; Kanyākumārī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-kanyakumari.md) — (Sthala-purāṇa of Kanyākumārī (folk))
- **Original shrine decays and icon buried** — Prahlāda's original temple decays across the Yugas and the self-manifest icon lies buried in an anthill until refound _(actors: Varāha-Lakṣmī-Narasiṃha (self-manifest icon))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/simhachalam-varaha-narasimha.md) — (Siṃhācala-kṣetra-māhātmya (folk/regional))
- **Origin of bhaṇḍāra (turmeric) worship** — In victory the god and army are showered in turmeric, founding the defining bhaṇḍāra offering and the cry 'Yelkoṭ! Jai Mālhār!' _(actors: Mārtāṇḍa Bhairava, devotees)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khandoba-martanda-bhairava.md) — (Marathi folk tradition [folk/regional])
- **Pairing with the second Māḍaṉ** — Variant: Sudalaimāḍaṉ is paired with a second Māḍaṉ (e.g. Vellai-māḍaṉ) as brothers or two faces of the guardian _(actors: Sudalaimāḍaṉ, Vellai-māḍaṉ)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/sudalaimadan.md) — (Sudalaimāḍaṉ kodai tradition [regional])
- **Pattachitra and the Jagannatha cult of Puri** — Odisha cloth-scroll painters depict the Jagannatha-Balabhadra-Subhadra triad and supply the Anasara-patti substitute icons worshipped while the wooden murtis are repainted _(actors: Patua/chitrakara painters, Jagannatha triad)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/chitra-kala-painting-traditions.md) — (Wikipedia, Pattachitra; Jagannatha sthala tradition)
- **Pūru gives his youth; sons cursed and the dynasty splits** — The four elder sons refuse and are cursed; youngest Pūru gives his youth and is crowned heir, forking the Lunar line into Yādava and Paurava branches _(actors: Yayāti, Yadu, Pūru)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yayati.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva 85–88; Bhāgavata 9.18–19)
- **Puṣpadanta caught and bereft of his power** — Made suddenly visible, Puṣpadanta is exposed and seized by the king's guards, robbed of the gift that defined him _(actors: Puṣpadanta, the king's guards)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-mahimna-stotra.md) — (Puṣpadanta frame-legend (folk))
- **Purāṇic pitṛ/śrāddha-māhātmya of the river** — Purāṇic māhātmyas rank the Vaitaraṇī a first-tier ancestor-rite river where bathing, tarpaṇa and piṇḍa-offering discharge the debt to the dead _(actors: pilgrims; the ancestors (pitṛs); officiating priests)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaitarani-jajpur-viraja-kshetra.md) — (Padma & Skanda Purāṇa tīrtha-māhātmyas)
- **Refutation of birth via the roll-call of low-born sages** — Birth cannot define brāhmaṇa-hood: revered ṛṣis (Ṛṣyaśṛnga, Vyāsa, Vālmīki, Vasiṣṭha, Agastya, Gautama, Kauśika, Jāmbuka) had irregular or non-brahmin origins yet are supreme brāhmaṇas _(actors: Upaniṣadic teacher; the cited sages Ṛṣyaśṛnga, Vyāsa, Vālmīki, Vasiṣṭha, Agastya, Gautama, Kauśika, Jāmbuka)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/vajrasuchika-upanishad.md) — (Vajrasūcikā Upaniṣad 5)
- **Rehearsal of Hanuman's Ramayana feats** — The hymn's caupais retell Hanuman's Treta-yuga deeds (Sita's message, burning of Lanka, carrying the Sanjivani mountain) as eternally present devotional content _(actors: Hanuman, Rama, Sita, Lakshmana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/hanuman-chalisa.md) — (Hanuman Chalisa cpr. 11-18; Valmiki Ramayana Sundara-kanda/Yuddha-kanda)
- **Sapindikarana (preta becomes pitr)** — The recurring decisive death-rite in which, on the 12th day, the single preta-pinda is merged into the three ancestral pindas, converting the hungry ghost into a settled ancestor and bumping the great-great-grandfather into the remote ancestors _(actors: the deceased (preta), descendants, three pitrs)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/shraddha-and-pitr-rites.md) — (Garuda Purana, Pretakhanda; grhya-sutras)
- **Sarasvatī saves Vasiṣṭha and is cursed at Vasiṣṭhāpavāha** — Commanded by Viśvāmitra to sweep Vasiṣṭha to him for slaughter, the Sarasvatī instead carries Vasiṣṭha away to safety; Viśvāmitra curses her to flow with blood until rākṣasas drink there and are freed and the sages restore her purity (the Aruṇā confluence). The ford Viśāla/Vasiṣṭha-tīrtha commemorates her loyalty _(actors: Sarasvatī, Vasiṣṭha, Viśvāmitra, rākṣasas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kurukshetra-dharmakshetra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śalya-parva 41–42; Vana-parva (Tīrtha-yātrā))
- **Savitri's Triratra vow and Satyavan's death** — Savitri undertakes a three-night standing fast, accompanies Satyavan into the forest on the fatal day, and he is stricken and dies with his head in her lap as Yama comes in person to take his soul _(actors: Savitri, Satyavan, Yama)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/savitri-satyavan.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva 293-295)
- **Self-manifestation of Venkateshwara on Tirumala** — Srinivasa-Venkateshwara stands self-manifest on the Seven Hills as the Kali-yuga refuge where Vishnu stays bodily present through the dark age _(actors: Vishnu as Srinivasa/Venkateshwara)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-svayamvyakta-vishnu-kshetras.md) — (Varaha/Bhavishyottara Purana; Venkatacala Mahatmya)
- **Shani's glance burns off Ganesha's head** — In a variant, Shani's destructive glance burns off the infant Ganesha's head and Vishnu brings a young elephant's head to replace it _(actors: Shani, Ganesha, Vishnu, Parvati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Brahmavaivarta Purana)
- **Shanti Parva recapitulation by Bhishma** — Bhishma re-states the Sixteen-Kings consolation within the Raja-/Moksha-dharma instruction, the verses recurring on the deaths of the great emperors _(actors: Bhishma, Yudhishthira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shodasarajakiya-sixteen-kings.md) — (Mahabharata, Shanti Parva ~29)
- **Shibi gives his eyes to a blind beggar (Sibi Jataka)** — In the Buddhist Sibi Jataka, Sakka disguised as a blind brahmana begs an eye and Shibi gives both, then has them restored by an Act of Truth _(actors: Shibi, Sakka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shibi.md) — (Sibi Jataka no. 499; Jatakamala 2)
- **Shiva Grants the Death-Touch Boon** — Shiva, bound by his word, grants Vrika the boon that whomever he touches on the head with his hand will die _(actors: Shiva, Vrika)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhasmasura-and-vrikasura.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 10.88.20-22)
- **Shiva manifests as the fire-column at Arunachala** — At Arunachala/Tiruvannamalai Shiva manifests as the infinite column of fire (teja-linga), the etiology of the Karttikai-Dipam festival _(actors: Shiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/skanda-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Skanda Purana (Arunachala-khanda))
- **Shiva Teaches Akhilāṇḍeśvarī Shiva-jñāna** — Shiva imparts Shiva-jnana to the fierce goddess Akhilandeshvari at Thiruvanaikaval, where she stands facing east as disciple and the linga faces west as guru _(actors: Shiva, Akhilandeshvari)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancha-bhuta-sthalams.md) — (Temple tradition)
- **Skanda slays Surapadman (Tamil recension)** — In the Tamil Kandapuranam, Skanda destroys the demon Surapadman across six war-camps and marries Valli _(actors: Skanda, Surapadman, Valli)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/skanda-purana.md) — (Tamil Kandapuranam (ascribed to Shankari-samhita))
- **Sūrya reveals the Sūrya Siddhānta to Maya** — At a yuga-junction the Sun sends an emissary to teach Maya the astronomy of planetary motions, sphericity and the Mahāyuga _(actors: Maya, Sūrya, the solar emissary (puruṣa))_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/maya-danava.md) — (Sūrya Siddhānta opening frame)
- **Surapadma Split into Peacock and Cock** — The dying Surapadma turns into a mango tree which the Vel splits in two; one half becomes Murugan's peacock vahana and the other the cock of his banner, redeeming the asura into service _(actors: Murugan, Surapadma)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Kanda Puranam; Surasamharam legend)
- **Sāvitrī wins five boons and Satyavān's life from Yama** — By her dharma-wisdom Sāvitrī extracts five boons, trapping Yama with the boon of sons by Satyavān, forcing him to restore Satyavān to life _(actors: Sāvitrī, Yama, Satyavān)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/savitri-satyavan.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva 296–297)
- **Teaching of Nārada at Badarī (Naranārāyaṇīya frame)** — The twin sages impart the ekāntika dharma to Nārada at Badarī, the inner narrative frame of the Nārāyaṇīya that transmits to Bhīṣma and Yudhiṣṭhira _(actors: Nara, Nārāyaṇa, Nārada, Bhīṣma, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nara-narayana.md) — (Mahābhārata, Nārāyaṇīya / Śānti Parva)
- **Teaching on control of speech and truthfulness** — The swan exalts restraint of speech (vāk-saṃyama), measured gentle true words or silence, and truthfulness (satya) paired with non-injury and self-restraint as the path to the supreme good _(actors: Brahmā in swan-form; the Sādhyas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-gita.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva, Mokṣadharma ~12.299)
- **The Bindusāgara gathers a drop of every tīrtha** — The temple-tank is said to collect a drop of every sacred tīrtha of India, so a bath there equals all pilgrimages _(actors: the Bindusāgara tank, pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lingaraja-bhubaneswar-ekamra-kshetra.md) — (Ekāmra-kṣetra-māhātmya)
- **The body becomes the cosmogram grid** — The prone body is mapped onto the squared site as the vastu-purusha-mandala (64- and 81-pada grids) with the 45 devatas and the central Brahmasthana _(actors: Vastu-purusha, Brahma at the Brahmasthana, the 45 devatas)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/vastu-shastra-and-the-vastu-purusha-mandala.md) — (Mayamata; Manasara; Brihat Samhita 52)
- **The Datta-pādukā footprint shrine** — The summit enshrines Dattātreya's footprints as his abiding bodily presence, the focal darśana of the climb _(actors: Dattātreya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Datta-sampradāya tradition; Wikipedia 'Girnar')
- **The death-bed prayer for remembrance of Hari** — The hymn begs that the Name remain on the lips at the hour of death when the throat is choked _(actors: Kulasekhara; Krishna/Hari)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/mukunda-mala.md) — (Mukunda-mala Stotra (prana-prayana verse))
- **The deformed rāgas humble Nārada** — Vain of his music, Nārada is shown the rāgas and rāgiṇīs personified as maimed beings he mangled by singing out of tune, and undertakes tapas to master music properly _(actors: Nārada, Viṣṇu (var. Śiva), the rāgiṇīs)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada.md) — (later Purāṇic / musicological lore; rāga-mālā tradition)
- **The fire-pillar cools into Aruṇācala hill** — At the gods' plea the unbearable fiery column cools and condenses into the Aruṇācala mountain, with a worshippable liṅga (Aṇṇāmalaiyār) installed at its base _(actors: Śiva, Brahmā, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arunachala-tiruvannamalai-fire-hill.md) — (Aruṇācala-māhātmya (Skanda Purāṇa))
- **The foot-dust cosmogony** — Verse 2 holds that Brahma creates, Vishnu sustains, and Rudra destroys the worlds from the dust of the Goddess's feet _(actors: Devi (Tripurasundari), Brahma, Vishnu, Rudra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/soundarya-lahari.md) — (Saundarya-lahari 2)
- **The Hamsa-gita: Brahma as swan instructs the Sadhyas** — Brahma in the form of a swan answers the Sadhyas on the highest good, teaching forbearance and self-mastery _(actors: Brahma, the Sadhyas, Vyasa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/lesser-gitas-catalog.md) — (Mahabharata Santi Parva ~12.288-299)
- **The limp and the name Khoḍiyār** — Hurrying back before the pyre, Janbāī injures her foot and walks with a limp (khoḍa), revives Merakhiyā, and is named Khoḍiyār _(actors: Janbāī/Khodiyār, Merakhiyā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khodiyar-mata.md) — (Cāraṇ folk tradition; variant of the agreed funeral signal)
- **The Mahāyāga and the rise of Lalitā Mahātripurasundarī** — From the fire-pit (cit-kuṇḍa) of the devas' great sacrifice arises Lalitā Tripurasundarī, four-armed with noose, goad, sugarcane bow and five flower-arrows _(actors: the devas, Lalitā Tripurasundarī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna); Lalitā Sahasranāma)
- **The moon's curse on Gaṇeśa Caturthī** — Mocked by Candra after his belly bursts from modakas, Gaṇeśa decrees that whoever views the moon on his festival day will be falsely accused _(actors: Gaṇeśa, Candra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Gaṇeśa Purāṇa)
- **The Sarasvatī-sacrifice dispute and Bhṛgu's commission** — Sages disputing Trimūrti supremacy depute Bhṛgu to test Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva for worthiness of the chief offerings _(actors: the ṛṣis, Bhṛgu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigu.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.89; Padma Purāṇa)
- **The Sudarshana Destroys the Kritya and Pursues Durvasa** — The Sudarshana-cakra instantly incinerates the Kritya, then turns on its sender Durvasa, chasing him across the three worlds as Brahma and Shiva confess they cannot save him _(actors: Sudarshana-cakra, Durvasa, Brahma, Shiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ambarisha-durvasa.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 9.4.50-61)
- **The Virāṭ (cosmic) theophany** — At Himavat's request the Goddess grants divine sight and reveals her all-pervading universal form containing all worlds and beings — the Śākta counterpart to Bhagavad Gītā ch. 11 _(actors: Bhuvaneśvarī (Virāṭ-rūpa), Himavat, the gods)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/devi-gita.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.33–34)
- **The waxing-and-waning compromise** — Unable to revoke his word, Daksha modulates the curse so the Moon wanes for half the month and waxes back for the other half, originating the dark and bright fortnights _(actors: Daksha, Candra, the gods)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/nakshatras.md) — (Bhagavata Purana)
- **Three lifetimes of Veda study** — Indra grants Bharadvaja three full lifetimes of study, then shows his learning is a handful of dust against the mountains of the Veda, teaching him the Savitra Agni rite as essence _(actors: Bharadvaja, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharadvaja.md) — (Taittiriya Brahmana 3.10.11; Kathaka tradition)
- **Tirukkalyāṇam — the celestial marriage** — The wedding of Mīnākṣī and Sundareśvara at Madurai, with Viṣṇu giving away the bride as her brother, celebrated as the Chithirai Tiruviḻā _(actors: Mīnākṣī, Sundareśvara, Viṣṇu (Aḻagar))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/madurai-meenakshi-sundareshwara-kshetra.md) — (Tiruviḷaiyāḍal Purāṇam; temple tradition)
- **Treading down Apasmāra (ignorance)** — The icon's pendant foot rests on the dwarf-demon Apasmāra/Muyalaka, knowledge treading down nescience _(actors: Dakṣiṇāmūrti; Apasmāra-puruṣa)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dakshinamurti.md) — (Aṃśumadbhedāgama; Kāmikāgama; iconographic tradition)
- **Tuṅgā and Bhadrā spring from the Varāha's tusks** — The two streams Tuṅgā and Bhadrā issue from the tusks of Viṣṇu's Varāha avatāra on Varāha-parvata and unite as the Tuṅgabhadrā _(actors: Viṣṇu (Varāha), Tuṅgā, Bhadrā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sacred-rivers-and-descent-myths.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa; sthala-tradition)
- **Tulasī deceived; the śālagrāma curse** — To enable Śaṅkhacūḍa's slaying, Viṣṇu deceives the chaste Tulasī by taking her husband's form; the enraged Tulasī curses Viṣṇu into the śālagrāma stone and is herself transformed into the tulasī plant and the Gaṇḍakī river _(actors: Tulasī, Viṣṇu, Śaṅkhacūḍa, Śiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahmavaivarta-purana.md) — (Brahmavaivarta Purāṇa (Prakṛti-khaṇḍa))
- **Vibhīṣaṇa sets the vimāna on the Kāverī island** — Carrying the vimāna under the rule that it roots wherever grounded, Vibhīṣaṇa halts at Tiruvaraṅgam and Raṅganātha refuses to rise _(actors: Vibhīṣaṇa, Raṅganātha, (variant: Gaṇeśa as cowherd))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srirangam-ranganatha.md) — (Śrīraṅga-māhātmya; folk Gaṇeśa/Ucchi Piḷḷaiyār variant)
- **Vishnu incarnates as Kurma to bear Mount Mandara** — Mount Mandara is uprooted as churning-rod and Vasuki wound as the rope; when the mountain sinks Vishnu incarnates as the tortoise Kurma and supports it on his shell _(actors: Vishnu/Kurma, Vasuki, devas, asuras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/samudra-manthana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 8.6-8.7; Mahabharata, Adi Parva)
- **Vishvakarma trims the Sun** — To relieve Sanjna who could not bear Surya's blaze, Vishvakarma shaves off an eighth of the Sun's radiance, the shavings forged into Vishnu's chakra, Shiva's trident, and other divine weapons _(actors: Vishvakarma, Surya, Sanjna)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vishvakarma-and-tvashtar.md) — (Markandeya Purana; Matsya Purana)
- **Viṣṇu Pins Gayāsura with the Dharmaśilā** — When Gayāsura's body keeps quaking, Viṣṇu places a great stone slab on him and presses it with his foot, leaving the Viṣṇupada footprint _(actors: Viṣṇu, Gayāsura, devas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/gaya-pitru-tirtha.md) — (Vayu Purana; Agni Purana)
- **Viśvavārā tends the dawn fire-sacrifice** — The Atri-clan seer Viśvavārā herself kindles and offers at the Agni fire-altar at dawn, praying for household concord and prosperity _(actors: Viśvavārā Ātreyī, Agni)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/rigvedic-women-seers-brahmavadinis.md) — (Ṛgveda 5.28)
- **Vyasa Gita taught by Vyasa** — Vyasa teaches knowledge of Shiva through combined devotion and prescribed works, the duties of the ashramas, and the path to liberation _(actors: Vyasa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/kurma-purana.md) — (Kurma Purana (Vyasa Gita, Uttara-vibhaga chs. 12-33))
- **Yama visits his sister Yamuna (Bhai Dooj charter)** — Yama, lord of death, visits his sister Yamuna who tilaks and feasts him; moved, he decrees that brothers so honored by sisters on Kartik Shukla Dvitiya escape Naraka _(actors: Yama, Yamuna)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Puranic tradition; Drik Panchang)
- **Yājñavalkya cursed and the Yajurveda split** — Cursed by his teacher Vaiśampāyana, Yājñavalkya vomits the old Kṛṣṇa Yajus (taken up as partridges), then the Sun-god as a horse teaches him the fresh Śukla Yajurveda — the origin myth of the White/Black split _(actors: Yājñavalkya, Vaiśampāyana, Sūrya)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/shruti-vedas-and-upanishads.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 3.5)
- **Yājñavalkya–Maitreyī dialogue** — About to renounce, Yājñavalkya teaches his wife Maitreyī that immortality comes not from wealth but from knowing the Ātman, for whose sake all is dear _(actors: Yājñavalkya, Maitreyī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/brihadaranyaka-upanishad.md) — (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2.4 (repeated 4.5))
- **Yuga-dharma and the decline of the ages** — Detailed account of how dharma contracts from Satya to Kali, with the lengths and degeneration of varṇa, lifespan and virtue across the four yugas _(actors: —)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vayu-purana.md) — (Vāyu Purāṇa; Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa)
- **Achaleśvar — Śiva's earth-steadying toe-print** — Śiva is worshipped at Achaleśvar as a natural toe-print holding up the earth — the deity Achala, 'the immovable,' fixing the hill and world in place _(actors: Śiva (Achala), Nandin)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mount-abu-arbuda-achaleshwar.md) — (Achaleśvar / Achalgarh sthala tradition)
- **Aditi's Payo-vrata and the second birth as Vāmana** — The same souls reborn as Kaśyapa and Aditi; Aditi's milk-vow penance earns Viṣṇu's descent as the dwarf Vāmana/Upendra, the couple's second birth _(actors: Kaśyapa, Aditi, Viṣṇu (as Vāmana/Upendra), Bali)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/prishnigarbha-three-births.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 6.18, 8.16-8.17, 10.3.41-42)
- **Agastya Digests the Asura Vatapi** — Agastya eats the goat-disguised cannibal asura Vatapi served by Ilvala and says 'Vatapi jiryatam,' digesting and dissolving the demon before he can be summoned back _(actors: Agastya, Vatapi, Ilvala)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/agastya.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva)
- **Ambikā as kṣetra-devī of Vastrāpatha** — The Goddess Ambā/Ambikā presides over the hill as kuladevī, paired with Dattātreya as the two powers sanctifying the field _(actors: Ambikā / Ambā-Mātā, Dattātreya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa Prabhāsa-khaṇḍa; regional tradition)
- **Andhaka-vadha — impalement of the blind demon** — Andhaka, monstrous son of Hiranyaksha, lusts after Parvati and is impaled on Shiva's trident until his pride burns away, then converted into a devotee and gana-chief _(actors: Shiva, Andhaka, Parvati, Hiranyaksha)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/linga-purana.md) — (Linga Purana)
- **Appointment as gaṇa-chief and gatekeeper of Kailāsa** — Śiva makes Nandi commander of his gaṇa-host (Nandikeśvara/Nandīśvara) and dvārapāla controlling access to the god _(actors: Śiva, Nandi, the gaṇas/bhūtas/pramathas)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nandi-and-shaiva-mounts.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa; Śiva Purāṇa; Vāyu Purāṇa; Skanda Purāṇa)
- **Ashtavakra's Marriage Test for Suprabha** — To wed Suprabha, the grown Ashtavakra passes the northern temptation set by the rishi Vadanya, refusing a queen's seduction _(actors: Ashtavakra, Suprabha, Vadanya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/ashtavakra.md) — (Mahabharata, Anushasana Parva)
- **Bharata, the deer, and Jada-Bharata** — King Bharata renounces his kingdom but becomes attached to a fawn, is reborn a deer and then as the idiot-seeming sage Jada-Bharata, who instructs the king of Sauvira in non-dualist wisdom _(actors: Bharata, Jada-Bharata, king of Sauvira)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vishnu-purana.md) — (Vishnu Purana (Amsha 2); cf. Bhagavata 5)
- **Bhṛgu–Varuṇa inquiry into Brahman** — Varuṇa guides his son Bhṛgu through successive realizations deriving Brahman as food ascending to bliss in the Taittirīya teaching _(actors: Bhṛgu, Varuṇa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/principal-upanishads-detail.md) — (Taittirīya Upaniṣad (Bhṛgu-vallī))
- **Bilva-mahatmya in the Skanda Purana** — The Skanda Purana carries extensive bilva-mahatmya praising the tree and the merit of leaf-offering to Shiva, a reservoir the hymn echoes _(actors: Shiva; Skanda Purana tradition)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bilvashtaka.md) — (Skanda Purana, bilva-mahatmya strands)
- **Birth of Hariharaputra (Śāstā / Ayyappa)** — In regional Śaiva tradition the Śiva–Mohinī union produces Hariharaputra, Śāstā/Ayyappan of Śabarimala, son of Hari and Hara (implicit/appended, not in the Bhāgavata text proper) _(actors: Śiva, Mohinī (Viṣṇu), Ayyappa/Śāstā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mohini-avatara.md) — (South Indian / Śaiva Ayyappa tradition (cf. Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8.12))
- **Body-distribution: head at Paśupatinātha, hump at Kedarnath** — The re-emerging bull-form Śiva leaves his head/face at Paśupatinātha in Nepal and his hump at Kedarnath, joining the two as one dismembered bull across the range _(actors: Śiva (bull-form), the Pāṇḍavas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pashupatinath-kathmandu-nepal-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa; Nepāla-māhātmya; folk tradition)
- **Boon of Gayā the Eternal Pitṛ-Tīrtha** — Viṣṇu and the gods grant that the demon's name mark the spot forever and that any ancestral rite performed there liberate the dead, turning the demon's self-sacrifice into sacred geography _(actors: Viṣṇu, Gayāsura, devas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/gaya-pitru-tirtha.md) — (Vayu Purana, Gaya-mahatmya)
- **Brahmā and Śiva fail the test** — Bhṛgu provokes Brahmā (anger) and Śiva (trident raised, restrained by Pārvatī), disqualifying both as rajas/tamas-stained _(actors: Bhṛgu, Brahmā, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigu.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.89; Padma Purāṇa)
- **Candra's penance to Shiva at Prabhasa** — Candra worships Shiva at Somnath/Prabhasa to be cured, and Shiva wears the saved crescent as Candrashekhara _(actors: Candra, Shiva)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/nakshatras.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Citra/Tripura Tāṇḍava in the picture hall (Kutralam)** — At Courtallam Śiva as Kuṟṟālanāthar dances the picture-dance worshipped through a mural rather than a metal icon; Agastya is said to have founded the Śaiva shrine _(actors: Śiva-Kuṟṟālanāthar, Kuḻalvāymoḻi, Agastya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancha-sabhai-nataraja-dance-halls.md) — (Kutralam (Courtallam) sthala-purāṇa)
- **Codification by the divine architects** — Vishvakarma (architect of devas) and Maya (architect of asuras) are made the mythic authors of the vastu/shilpa craft and its two textual streams _(actors: Vishvakarma, Maya Danava)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/vastu-shastra-and-the-vastu-purusha-mandala.md) — (Mahabharata Sabha Parva; Mayamata; Shilpa Shastras)
- **Composition of the Śiva Mahimna Stotra as atonement** — Realizing the offense was against Śiva, Puṣpadanta composes the Śiva-mahimnaḥ stotra praising the god's immeasurable greatness to win back grace; the hymn pleases Śiva and his powers are restored _(actors: Puṣpadanta, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-mahimna-stotra.md) — (Śiva-mahimnaḥ stotra (Puṣpadanta frame; signature/colophon verses naming the author))
- **Conception of Skanda/Kārtikeya** — Śiva's too-fiery seed is carried by Agni and Gaṅgā and deposited in the Saravaṇa reeds, conceived to produce a son able to slay Tārakāsura _(actors: Śiva, Agni, Gaṅgā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva 224-232, Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Dadhīci gives his bones for the vajra** — When Viṣṇu reveals that only a weapon forged from Dadhīci's thunder-laden bones can slay Vṛtra, the sage consents to give his life and quits his body in samādhi so his spine can be taken _(actors: Dadhīci, Indra, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dadhichi.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 6.9–10)
- **Dakṣa's yajña at Drākṣārāma and Manikyāmbā Śakti Pīṭha** — Drākṣārāma is identified as Dakṣa's garden / yajña-site, and consort Manikyāmbā is counted as the pīṭha where Sati's left cheek fell _(actors: Dakṣa, Sati, Vīrabhadra, Manikyāmbā/Devī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancharama-kshetras-andhra.md) — (Bhīmeśvara Purāṇamu (Śrīnātha); Aṣṭādaśa Śakti Pīṭha Stotra)
- **Dakshas curse causes the moon's waning** — Candra favors Rohini and neglects his other 26 nakshatra-wives; Daksha curses him to waste away, and Shiva redeems him to wax and wane eternally _(actors: Candra, Daksha, Rohini, Shiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/chandra-soma.md) — (Skanda/Padma Purana)
- **Dhūmralocana burnt to ash** — The Goddess reduces Śumbha's general Dhūmralocana to ashes with a single huṃkāra and her lion mauls his army _(actors: Devī, Dhūmralocana, Śumbha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devī Māhātmya ch.6)
- **Dismemberment of the asura Bala into the nine gems** — The great asura Bala (Vala) is sacrificed by the gods and his scattered body-parts become the nine precious gems (blood→ruby, teeth→pearl, etc.), the origin myth of the navaratna _(actors: the gods, the asura Bala/Vala)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/garuda-purana.md) — (Garuda Purana (gem section); Agni Purana; Buddha-bhatta's Ratna-pariksha)
- **Ensoulment of the fetus** — In the seventh month the fetus is endowed with the jīva (individual soul) and becomes animate _(actors: the jīva (soul); the fetus)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/garbha-upanishad.md) — (Garbha Upaniṣad 3)
- **Establishment of Muktinath as a jwālā-mukhī mokṣa-kṣetra shared with Buddhism** — The Mukti-kṣetra with its 108 dhārās and natural rock-flame becomes a liberation-granting shrine revered jointly as the Buddhist Chumig Gyatsa _(actors: Mukti-Nārāyaṇa/Śrī Mūrti, Jwālā-Māī, Padmasambhava, Buddhist nuns)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/muktinath-gandaki-shalagrama-kshetra.md) — (Vaiṣṇava sthala-māhātmya; Varāha Purāṇa tradition; temple/Tibetan tradition)
- **Establishment of the seat and disappearance** — Khodiyār establishes her sthāna and finally withdraws/merges into the divine, leaving the Galdharā springs and river-shrines as tīrthas _(actors: Khodiyār Mātā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khodiyar-mata.md) — (Saurāṣṭra sthala-tradition)
- **Exposition of the three yogas (jñāna, karma, bhakti)** — The Goddess lays out the converging paths of knowledge, desireless action, and devotion, distinguishing supreme (parā) from preparatory (gauṇī) bhakti _(actors: Bhuvaneśvarī, Himavat)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/devi-gita.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.35–37)
- **Forbearance declared the higher strength** — The swan answers that endurance is not weakness: the forbearing man who is unmoved by praise or blame is the stronger, having conquered his own mind, the harder enemy _(actors: Brahmā in swan-form; the Sādhyas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-gita.md) — (Mahābhārata, Śānti Parva, Mokṣadharma ~12.299)
- **Founding of the Pañcha Kedār circuit** — Kedarnath becomes head of the five Garhwal body-part shrines (hump→Kedarnath … hair→Kalpeśvara), the only Pañcha Kedār shrine that is also a Jyotirliṅga _(actors: Pāṇḍavas, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kedarnath-kedara-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa; Wikipedia, Panch Kedar)
- **Ganesha Grants Boons to Sage Gritsamada at Mahad** — Sage Gritsamada worships Ganesha at Mahad after a curse and is granted boons, enshrining the deity as Varada-Vinayaka, the boon-giver _(actors: Gritsamada, Ganesha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashtavinayaka.md) — (Ganesha Purana tradition)
- **Gautama brings the Godavari (Gautamī Gaṅgā) to the Deccan** — After a contrived cow-killing sin engineered by jealous ṛṣis, the sage Gautama brings the Gaṅgā down to the Deccan as the Godavari to expiate it _(actors: Gautama, Gaṅgā)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahma-purana.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa (Gautamī-māhātmya))
- **Gayāsura and the Gayā-māhātmya** — The pious asura Gayāsura is pinned to the earth so his sanctified ground becomes the supreme pitṛ-tīrtha where a single piṇḍa-offering liberates one's forebears _(actors: Gayāsura, Viṣṇu, the gods)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vayu-purana.md) — (Vāyu Purāṇa (Gayā-māhātmya); cf. Agni/Garuḍa/Brahma Purāṇas)
- **Hālāhala / Nīlakaṇṭha** — During the ocean-churning Śiva drinks the all-destroying Hālāhala poison to save creation; Pārvatī holds his throat, lodging it there and turning his throat blue _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Vāsuki, devas, asuras)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8.7, Viṣṇu Purāṇa, Mahābhārata Ādi Parva, Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Indra slays Vṛtra mounted on Airāvata** — Airāvata is the mount on which Indra slays the dragon Vṛtra, cementing the elephant's rain-and-war role _(actors: Indra, Airāvata, Vṛtra)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/airavata-and-celestial-animals.md) — (Mahābhārata; Ṛgvedic Vṛtra cycle)
- **Inversion of the Trimūrti hierarchy** — Within the discourse Gaṇeśa is declared the source from whom Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and Śiva derive their powers, and is identified with OṂ and the bindu as the first principle of reality _(actors: Gajānana (Gaṇeśa))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ganesha-gita.md) — (Gaṇeśa Purāṇa, Krīḍā-khaṇḍa chs. 138–148)
- **Invocation of the rescued devotees** — The hymn invokes past acts of grace across the yugas as warrant for refuge: Gajendra freed, Draupadi clothed, Prahlada saved, Ahalya lifted _(actors: Vishnu/Krishna; Gajendra; Draupadi; Prahlada; Ahalya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/mukunda-mala.md) — (Mukunda-mala Stotra)
- **Jānaśruti seeks the sage Raikva** — The proud king Jānaśruti, hearing flamingoes praise the cart-man sage Raikva, seeks him out and is taught the saṃvarga-vidyā of air and breath as the absorbers _(actors: Jānaśruti Pautrāyaṇa, Raikva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/chandogya-upanishad.md) — (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 4.1–4.3)
- **Kacha infiltrates Śukra to steal the Sañjīvanī** — The devas send Bṛhaspati's son Kacha to become Śukra's disciple and secretly learn the life-restoring mṛtasañjīvanī vidyā that the asuras alone possess _(actors: Kacha, Śukra, Devayānī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/the-gurus-brihaspati-and-shukra.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva; Matsya Purāṇa; Agni Purāṇa)
- **Kallaḻagar Vaigai river-crossing** — Viṣṇu as Kallaḻagar rides late to give away his sister, finds the wedding done, and steps into the Vaigai river instead of entering the city _(actors: Viṣṇu (Kallaḻagar) of Aḻagar Koyil)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/madurai-meenakshi-sundareshwara-kshetra.md) — (temple tradition; Chithirai festival lore)
- **Kardama's tapas and the naming of Bindu-sarovara** — Viṣṇu's tears (bindu) of compassion fall at the Sarasvatī-bank lake where the Prajāpati Kardama performs austerity, naming Bindu-sarovara _(actors: Kardama; Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-sarovar-sacred-lakes.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 3.21)
- **Katyayani born to slay Mahishasura** — The Goddess is born in sage Katyayana's hermitage specifically as the fierce warrior-form Katyayani to slay Mahishasura _(actors: Katyayani, Katyayana, Mahishasura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Devi Mahatmya; Devi Kavaca; Bhagavata Purana 10)
- **Kundalini ascent and the Sri Cakra** — Verses 8–11 locate the Goddess as Kundalini ascending the cakras to union with Shiva and describe the Sri Cakra of intersecting Shiva and Shakti triangles _(actors: Devi as Kundalini, Shiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/soundarya-lahari.md) — (Saundarya-lahari 8–11)
- **Lingodbhava — Shiva's infinite pillar of fire at Arunachala** — When Brahma and Vishnu dispute supremacy, Shiva appears as an endless column of fire whose top and base neither can find; Arunachala hill is that fiery linga, re-enacted annually by the Maha/Bharani Deepam ghee-beacon atop Tiruvannamalai during Karthigai Deepam on the Krittika asterism _(actors: Shiva, Brahma, Vishnu)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Lingodbhava / Arunachala sthala purana; Wikipedia (Karthikai Deepam))
- **Lopāmudrā urges Agastya to householder dharma** — In a dialogue-hymn Lopāmudrā presses the long-austere Agastya to balance tapas with procreation _(actors: Lopāmudrā, Agastya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/rigvedic-women-seers-brahmavadinis.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.179)
- **Mahishasura conquers heaven** — After a hundred-year war the buffalo-demon Mahishasura defeats Indra, usurps the three worlds, and expels the gods from svarga _(actors: Mahishasura, Indra, the devas)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 2)
- **Marriage of Lalitā to Kāmeśvara and enthronement in Śrīpura** — Śiva as Kāmeśvara weds Lalitā; the pair are enthroned on the Pañca-brahmāsana in the jeweled city Śrīpura, cosmographic form of the Śrīcakra _(actors: Lalitā, Kāmeśvara (Śiva), Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Rudra, Īśāna, Sadāśiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa; Lalitā Sahasranāma)
- **Marriage to Mhāḷsā** — Khaṇḍobā weds Mhāḷsā, a Brahmin merchant's daughter identified with Pārvatī, representing the settled agrarian-Brahmin pole of the cult _(actors: Khaṇḍobā, Mhāḷsā (Pārvatī))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khandoba-martanda-bhairava.md) — (Mallāri Māhātmya; Marathi tradition)
- **Murugan's Marriage to Deivanai at Tirupparankundram** — After his victory Murugan weds Deivanai, Indra's foster-daughter, at Tirupparankundram, taking the dharmic deva-bride _(actors: Murugan, Deivanai)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Tirumurukarruppatai; Kanda Puranam)
- **Named among the seven rishis in the Brihadaranyaka** — The vamsha-style enumeration of Brihadaranyaka Upanishad 2.2.4 names Bharadvaja among the seven rishis, paired with his father Brihaspati _(actors: Bharadvaja, Brihaspati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharadvaja.md) — (Brihadaranyaka Upanishad 2.2.4)
- **Nārada attains Viṣṇu's vision at the Nārada-kuṇḍa** — Sage Nārada attains the vision of Viṣṇu at Badarī; the Nārada-kuṇḍa pool in the Alaknandā is named for him and yields the self-manifest mūrti _(actors: Nārada, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/badrinath-badarikashrama-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Badarī-māhātmya)
- **Pabuji ki phad sung before the painted shrine** — The Bhopa bard unrolls the Pabuji phad scroll as a portable shrine and sings the epic of the Rathod folk-deity Pabuji, held an amsa of Lakshmana _(actors: Bhopa and Bhopi bards, Pabuji)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/chitra-kala-painting-traditions.md) — (Wikipedia, Phad painting; J. D. Smith, The Epic of Pabuji)
- **Pampā the river-goddess weds Śiva as Virūpākṣa** — Pampā/Pampāvatī, daughter of Brahmā, wins Śiva as Virūpākṣa through tapas on Hemakūṭa, naming Pampā-kṣetra (Hampi) _(actors: Pampā/Pampāvatī, Śiva (Virūpākṣa), Brahmā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sacred-rivers-and-descent-myths.md) — (Pampā-māhātmya; sthala-purāṇa)
- **Piṅgalā retold among the Avadhūta's 24 gurus** — In the Bhāgavata's Uddhava-Gītā the young avadhūta tells King Yadu that Piṅgalā the courtesan of Videha was one of his twenty-four gurus, quoting the hope-maxim almost verbatim _(actors: the avadhūta brāhmaṇa; King Yadu; Piṅgalā (cited); (frame: Kṛṣṇa to Uddhava))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pingala-gita.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 11.8.22–44)
- **Prajāpati's da-da-da teaching** — Prajāpati's single syllable 'da' is heard by gods, humans and demons as the threefold instruction to control oneself, give, and be compassionate _(actors: Prajāpati, gods, humans, demons)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/brihadaranyaka-upanishad.md) — (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 5.2)
- **Pride after Kāma's failure against Śiva** — Nārada grows proud of conquering desire where the burned Kāmadeva failed against Śiva, prompting Viṣṇu to humble him _(actors: Nārada, Kāmadeva, Śiva, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa; Bhāgavata-derived retellings)
- **Pārvatī's penance and Ardhanārīśvara merger** — Pārvatī covers Śiva's eyes plunging the cosmos into darkness, does penance at Tiruvaṇṇāmalai (slaying the demon Mahiṣa), and is re-merged into Śiva's left half as Ardhanārīśvara; she becomes the goddess Apītakucāmbāḷ/Uṇṇāmulai _(actors: Pārvatī, Śiva, demon Mahiṣa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arunachala-tiruvannamalai-fire-hill.md) — (Aruṇācala-māhātmya (Skanda Purāṇa))
- **Raṅganātha reclines facing south toward Laṅkā** — To console Vibhīṣaṇa, Raṅganātha stays but reclines facing south so his blessing perpetually reaches Laṅkā _(actors: Raṅganātha, Vibhīṣaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srirangam-ranganatha.md) — (Śrīraṅga-māhātmya)
- **Rambhā cursed to stone by Viśvāmitra** — Sent against Viśvāmitra, Rambhā fails and is cursed to become stone for ten thousand years _(actors: Rambhā, Viśvāmitra)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/apsaras.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa Bāla Kāṇḍa)
- **Refuge-invocation of Hanumān** — The hymn closes by taking refuge in Hanumān, swift as thought and chief of the monkey host, as Rāma's messenger and guardian _(actors: Hanumān, the reciter)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/ramaraksha-stotra.md) — (Rāma-rakṣā Stotra, v. 38)
- **Refutation of knowledge, karma and dharma** — Scholarship, ritual action and piety are each rejected as the definition of brāhmaṇa because they are shared with other varṇas or are external _(actors: Upaniṣadic teacher)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/vajrasuchika-upanishad.md) — (Vajrasūcikā Upaniṣad 6-8)
- **Sati's body becomes the Shakti Pithas** — In the variant where Shiva carries Sati's corpse and Vishnu's discus dismembers it, her scattered body-parts fall to earth and become the Shakti Pithas _(actors: Shiva, Sati, Vishnu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Sati-khanda))
- **Satī's Heart and Navel Fall at Māyāpurī** — When Viṣṇu dismembers Satī's corpse to end Śiva's grief-dance, her heart and navel fall at Māyāpurī (Haridwar), anchoring it among the Śakti Pīṭhas _(actors: Satī, Viṣṇu, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/haridwar-rishikesh-ganga-dwara.md) — (Wikipedia, Maya Devi Temple; Shakti Pitha tradition)
- **Savitri's dialectical duel and five boons from Yama** — Following Yama southward and reciting dharma aphorisms, Savitri wins boons restoring Dyumatsena's sight and kingdom, a hundred sons for Ashvapati, and a hundred sons by Satyavan, trapping Yama into restoring Satyavan's life _(actors: Savitri, Yama, Satyavan, Dyumatsena, Ashvapati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/savitri-satyavan.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva 296-297)
- **Self-formation of the shaligrama at Salagrama/Muktinath** — On the Kali-Gandaki in Mustang the aniconic self-formed shaligrama stones are worshipped as Vishnu without consecration, the purest self-manifest form _(actors: Vishnu (Sri Murti); Gandaki river)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-svayamvyakta-vishnu-kshetras.md) — (Garuda Purana; Devi Bhagavata; Gandaki-mahatmya)
- **Shiva and Parvati Follow Skanda to Srisailam** — After Kartikeya leaves in pique over Ganesha's marriage, Shiva and Parvati follow him to the hill and reside there as Mallikarjuna, establishing the Mallikarjuna jyotirlinga _(actors: Shiva, Parvati, Kartikeya, Ganesha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/12-jyotirlingas.md) — (Skanda Purana; Shiva Purana)
- **Shiva drinks the Halahala poison and becomes Nilakantha** — The world-poison Halahala/Kalakuta surfaces first, threatening creation; Shiva drinks it and Parvati stays it in his throat, which turns blue, giving him the name Nilakantha _(actors: Shiva, Parvati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/samudra-manthana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 8.7; Vishnu Purana; Shiva Purana)
- **Shiva Flees to Vaikuntha** — Vrika turns to test the boon on Shiva's own head, and the terrified god flees across the universe to Vishnu's Vaikuntha _(actors: Vrika, Shiva, Vishnu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhasmasura-and-vrikasura.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 10.88.23-25)
- **Slaying of Andhakasura by Bhairava** — The blind demon Andhaka is defeated, the slaying associated with Bhairava, culminating in Andhaka's redemption as a gana _(actors: Bhairava (Shiva), Andhakasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/kurma-purana.md) — (Kurma Purana)
- **Slaying of Śumbha, Niśumbha, and Raktabīja** — The Goddess emanates the fair Kauśikī, leaving dark Kālikā behind, and destroys Śumbha and Niśumbha with their generals Caṇḍa, Muṇḍa (→Cāmuṇḍā) and Raktabīja, whose blood-drops Kālī drinks _(actors: Kauśikī, Kālikā, Śumbha, Niśumbha, Raktabīja, Caṇḍa, Muṇḍa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/devi-bhagavata-upapurana.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata, skandha V)
- **Soma cured of Dakṣa's wasting-curse at Soma-tīrtha** — Soma (the Moon), afflicted with the kṣaya (consumption) curse of Dakṣa for neglecting his other daughter-wives, bathes in the Sarasvatī at Soma-tīrtha and is released, his waxing and waning fixed thereafter; the ford thereby gains power to cure illness, with special merit on Somavatī Amāvasyā _(actors: Soma (Candra), Dakṣa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kurukshetra-dharmakshetra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana-parva (Tīrtha-yātrā); Śalya-parva; Soma–Dakṣa curse cycle)
- **Suśruta lineage descends through Divodāsa Dhanvantari** — Dhanvantari incarnate as Divodāsa, king of Kāśī, teaches the surgical science to Suśruta and fellow pupils _(actors: Dhanvantari/Divodāsa, Suśruta)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/ayurveda-and-the-science-of-life.md) — (Suśruta Saṃhitā Sū. 1)
- **Sāvitrī wins Satyavān back from Yama** — Sāvitrī follows Yama as he carries off her husband Satyavān's soul and wins him back by her wit and virtue, in an expanded Sāvitrī–Yama discourse on dharma and the soul _(actors: Sāvitrī, Satyavān, Yama)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahmavaivarta-purana.md) — (Brahmavaivarta Purāṇa (Prakṛti-khaṇḍa))
- **The avadhūta-lakṣaṇa and the four syllables (Chapter 8)** — The marks of the true avadhūta are given and the word a-va-dhū-ta is read as a map of liberation; this chapter is often judged a later addition _(actors: Dattātreya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/avadhuta-gita.md) — (Avadhūta Gītā 8)
- **The Bull of Dharma loses a leg each yuga** — Dharma personified as a bull loses one leg each yuga, virtue diminishing from four quarters to one across Satya, Treta, Dvapara, and Kali, restored to four at the next Satya _(actors: Dharma (the bull))_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/yugas-and-mahayuga.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 1.16-17; Mahabharata Vana Parva 149)
- **The dance-contest against Kālī** — Śiva wins a dance-duel against Kālī by raising his leg in the ūrdhva-tāṇḍava pose, which Kālī, bound by modesty, cannot match and concedes _(actors: Śiva, Kālī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Tamil Śaiva sthala-purāṇa; Cidambaram māhātmya)
- **The Goddess slays the bee-demon Aruṇāsura as a swarm of bees** — To destroy a demon immune to weapons, the Goddess emits black bees from her body that kill him, earning the name Bhramarāmbā, she-who-fought-as-bees _(actors: Bhramarāmbā Devī, Aruṇāsura (asura))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srisailam-srisaila-mallikarjuna-bhramaramba.md) — (Devī-māhātmya / Śākta lore; Śrīśaila sthala tradition)
- **The mythic Vaitaraṇī crossing and vaitaraṇī-dāna** — The preta crosses the foul underworld Vaitaraṇī by holding the tail of a cow gifted at death (vaitaraṇī-dāna); the earthly river inherits this saving logic _(actors: the preta (soul); Yama; the family; the Vaitaraṇī cow)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaitarani-jajpur-viraja-kshetra.md) — (Garuḍa Purāṇa, Pretakhaṇḍa/Pretakalpa; Bhāgavata Purāṇa 5.26)
- **The pair of pigeons overhear and become deathless** — A pair of doves (kapota) unnoticed overhear the entire Amar Kathā and become immortal, said to still inhabit the cave _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, the kapota (immortal dove-pair))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/amarnath-ice-lingam-and-amar-katha.md) — (Amar Kathā tradition; living pilgrim lore)
- **The swayambhū Kṛttivāsa liṅga and Harihara compact** — The self-manifest Śiva-liṅga is declared Tribhuvaneśvara, lord of all three worlds and both sects, so Hari shares Hara's seat _(actors: Śiva (Kṛttivāsa/Tribhuvaneśvara), Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lingaraja-bhubaneswar-ekamra-kshetra.md) — (Ekāmra Purāṇa)
- **The three-wives quarrel and Vishnu's redistribution of consorts** — Jealous Sarasvati curses Lakshmi to become the Tulasi plant and a river; Ganga curses Sarasvati to become a river bearing people's sins; Vishnu ends the strife by keeping Lakshmi, sending Ganga to Shiva and Sarasvati to Brahma _(actors: Vishnu, Lakshmi, Sarasvati, Ganga)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/saraswati.md) — (Devi Bhagavata Purana; Brahmavaivarta Purana)
- **Umā–Maheśvara dialogue on women's duties** — Śiva, questioned by Umā (Pārvatī), expounds the ideal conduct of a wife: devotion, sweet speech, and treating the husband as god _(actors: Śiva, Umā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/13-anushasana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 13.146)
- **Upamanyu's childhood vow for Śiva's grace** — The boy Upamanyu, craving milk, is told it is Śiva's gift alone and resolves to win it by austerity to Śiva _(actors: Upamanyu, his mother)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-sahasranama.md) — (Mahābhārata, Anuśāsana Parva XIII (Upamanyu legend))
- **Vishnu Declares Himself Subject to His Devotees** — Fleeing to Vaikuntha, Durvasa is told by Vishnu that He cannot recall the discus or act against His devotee, uttering 'aham bhakta-paradhino,' and is sent to beg Ambarisha's pardon _(actors: Vishnu, Durvasa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ambarisha-durvasa.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 9.4.62-68)
- **Vow of perpetual virginity** — Left unwed at the missed muhūrta, the Goddess resolves to remain forever Kumārī, retaining the undiminished celibate power that enables her victory _(actors: Kanyākumārī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-kanyakumari.md) — (Sthala-purāṇa of Kanyākumārī)
- **Yama Confesses Vishnu's Supremacy** — When the baffled Yamadutas report their defeat, Yamaraja reveals there is a Supreme Lord above him, names the twelve mahajanas, and commands his servants never to approach devotees of the Name _(actors: Yamaraja, the Yamadutas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ajamila.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6.3)
- **Yayāti's renunciation after a thousand years of indulgence** — After a millennium of pleasure Yayāti realizes desire only grows, returns Pūru's youth, and retires to the forest attaining heaven _(actors: Yayāti, Pūru)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yayati.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva 88; Bhāgavata 9.19)
- **Yogis See the Self-Power of the Deva** — Practitioners of dhyana-yoga, through meditation, perceive the hidden self-power (devatma-shakti) of God veiled by its own qualities, identifying the personal Lord as the first cause _(actors: meditating sages, the Deva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/svetashvatara-upanishad.md) — (Shvetashvatara Upanishad 1.3)
- **Agastya Destroys Ilvala** — Enraged that his brother is digested, Ilvala is reduced to ashes by the fire from Agastya's glance, and the sage departs with the riches he sought _(actors: Agastya, Ilvala)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/agastya.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva)
- **Agni Purified by Worshipping Shiva at Kanjanur** — The fire-god Agni worships Shiva at Kanjanur to be purified, giving the temple its name Agnishvarar _(actors: Agni, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/navagraha-temples.md) — (Sthala-purana)
- **Ambarisha's Prayer Spares Durvasa** — After a year of pursuit Durvasa falls at the king's feet; Ambarisha prays the Sudarshana-stuti, the discus relents, and the king feeds Durvasa before breaking his own fast _(actors: Ambarisha, Durvasa, Sudarshana-cakra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ambarisha-durvasa.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 9.5.1-20)
- **Bhṛgu kicks Viṣṇu's chest; the Śrīvatsa mark** — Bhṛgu kicks the sleeping Viṣṇu's chest; Viṣṇu calmly massages the sage's foot, the print remaining as the Śrīvatsa, winning the verdict of supremacy _(actors: Bhṛgu, Viṣṇu, Lakṣmī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigu.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.89.11–14; Padma Purāṇa)
- **Bhrāmarī Devī slays Aruṇāsura** — When the demon Aruṇa, immune to harm from any two-footed creature, threatens the gods, the Goddess takes the form of Bhrāmarī and swarms of six-legged bees and wasps tear his army apart _(actors: Bhrāmarī Devī, Aruṇāsura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/devi-bhagavata-upapurana.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata, skandha X)
- **Bilva-mahatmya in the Padma Purana** — The Padma Purana preserves bilva-mahatmya glorifying the leaf-offering and the tree's link to the goddess Lakshmi _(actors: Shiva; Lakshmi; Padma Purana tradition)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bilvashtaka.md) — (Padma Purana, bilva-mahatmya strands)
- **Birth of Durga from combined tejas** — The enraged gods emit tejas that coalesces into the eighteen-armed Goddess Durga/Chandika, each body-part formed from a specific god's light _(actors: Durga, Vishnu, Shiva, Brahma, Indra, the devas)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 2)
- **Birth of Kapila and the Kapilopadeśa at Bindu-sarovar** — Kapila, Viṣṇu incarnate, is born to Kardama and Devahūti and expounds the Sāṅkhya tattva-system to his mother, liberating her _(actors: Kapila; Devahūti; Kardama)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-sarovar-sacred-lakes.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 3.24–33)
- **Bāṇāsura-vadha** — Because she remains a virgin, the boon's loophole holds and Kanyākumārī battles and slays the demon Bāṇāsura at Land's End, freeing the devas _(actors: Kanyākumārī; Bāṇāsura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-kanyakumari.md) — (Sthala-purāṇa of Kanyākumārī (folk))
- **Candra abducts Tara and the Tarakamaya war** — Candra abducts Tara, wife of Brihaspati, triggering the Tarakamaya war until Brahma intervenes; from the union Budha is born, founding the Lunar line _(actors: Candra, Tara, Brihaspati, Budha, Brahma)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/chandra-soma.md) — (Vishnu Purana)
- **Chandra cursed by Ganesha** — Chandra mocks Ganesha's broken-tusk form and is cursed so that whoever sees the Moon on Ganesha Chaturthi is falsely accused _(actors: Chandra, Ganesha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/navagraha.md) — (Puranic/folk)
- **Daksha's sacrifice destroyed; Shiva weds Uma** — The Vamana Purana narrates Sati's self-immolation at Daksha's sacrifice, Shiva's wrecking of the yajna, and his later marriage to Parvati/Uma _(actors: Sati, Daksha, Shiva, Parvati)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vamana-purana.md) — (Vamana Purana)
- **Daksha-yajna wrecked by Virabhadra** — Daksha's sacrifice slights Shiva and is wrecked by Virabhadra and Bhadrakali — Bhaga's eye and Pushan's teeth are struck — until Shiva is honored and order restored _(actors: Daksha, Virabhadra, Bhadrakali, Shiva, Bhaga, Pushan)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/linga-purana.md) — (Linga Purana)
- **Devnarayan ki phad** — The longest phad scroll narrates the epic of the Gurjar deity Devnarayan, regarded an avatara of Vishnu, recited before the cloth-shrine _(actors: Bhopa bards, Devnarayan)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/chitra-kala-painting-traditions.md) — (Wikipedia, Phad painting; Dev Narayan)
- **Devāpi enters yogic suspension at Kalāpa-grāma** — Rather than dying, Devāpi enters deep yogic samādhi at the hidden village Kalāpa and survives in concealment through the entirety of Kali Yuga _(actors: Devāpi)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/devapi.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa IV.24; Bhāgavata Purāṇa XII.2.37-38)
- **Division of the Veda by the 28 Vyasas** — The Veda is recurringly redacted by a Vyasa in each Dvapara, situating Krishna-Dvaipayana as the 28th, one per mahayuga across the manvantara _(actors: the 28 Vyasas, Krishna-Dvaipayana)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vishnu-purana.md) — (Vishnu Purana (Amsha 3))
- **Fixing of the Vishnu-kshetra at Naimisharanya** — Hari's Manomaya Cakra strikes earth at the Gomati forest, opening the Cakra-tirtha and fixing the self-manifest abode amid the Purana frame-story sattra _(actors: Brahma/Vishnu (Manomaya Cakra); Suta; the 88,000 rishis)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-svayamvyakta-vishnu-kshetras.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 1.1; Skanda Purana Naimisa-khanda)
- **Ganesha Recovers the Chintamani Jewel at Theur** — After the greedy prince Guna steals sage Kapila's wish-fulfilling Chintamani jewel, Ganesha slays Guna and recovers it, becoming Chintamani Vinayaka _(actors: Guna, Kapila, Ganesha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashtavinayaka.md) — (Ganesha Purana tradition)
- **Ganesha Slays Sindhu at Morgaon** — Ganesha as the peacock-rider Mayureshwar slays the demon Sindhu, founding the adi node of the Ashtavinayaka at Morgaon _(actors: Ganesha, Sindhu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/temple-networks-and-circuits.md) — (Ashtavinayaka sthala-purana)
- **Guru Śikhar — Dattātreya's summit** — The highest Aravalli peak is crowned by a shrine to Dattātreya (and Anasūyā), echoing the Datta summit-cult of nearby Girnar _(actors: Dattātreya, Anasūyā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mount-abu-arbuda-achaleshwar.md) — (Guru Śikhar sthala tradition)
- **Head-to-foot praise (anga-varnana)** — Verses 42–100 describe the Goddess limb by limb from crown to lotus-feet, closing with self-surrender offering her own radiance back to her _(actors: Devi (Lalita Tripurasundari))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/soundarya-lahari.md) — (Saundarya-lahari 42–100)
- **Identification with Kauśikī** — The Vindhya-dwelling goddess is equated with Kauśikī/Ambikā, who emerged from Pārvatī's sheath to fight Śumbha-Niśumbha _(actors: Vindhyavāsinī, Kauśikī/Ambikā, Pārvatī, Kālikā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vindhyavasini.md) — (Devī Māhātmya 5; Skandapurāṇa Vindhyavāsinī-cycle)
- **Indra incurs Brahmahatya and hides** — Because the Puranic Vritra/Vishvarupa is a brahmana, killing him incurs Brahmahatya; crushed by the sin, Indra shrinks into a lotus stalk and vanishes, leaving Svarga kingless _(actors: Indra, Vishvarupa)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/indra.md) — (Bhagavata; Mahabharata, Udyoga/Shanti Parva)
- **Śiva slays Tripura and Andhaka** — The Śiva Purāṇa records Śiva's slaying of the Tripura demons and of Andhaka _(actors: Śiva, Andhaka)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/smriti-puranas.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Śiva's Ānanda-tāṇḍava as Naṭarāja at Cidambaram** — Śiva dances the bliss-dance enacting the five cosmic acts of creation, preservation, destruction, concealment and grace _(actors: Śiva (Naṭarāja), Apasmāra)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/natya-shastra-and-the-classical-arts.md) — (Nāṭya Śāstra ch. 4; Śaiva Āgamas; Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Śiva Whispers the Tāraka-Mantra at Death** — At the moment of death within Kashi, Śiva whispers the ferry-mantra into the dying's ear, cutting the knot of karma and granting immediate liberation regardless of prior deeds _(actors: Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kashi-varanasi.md) — (Skanda Purana, Kashi-khanda)
- **Jihve kirtaya — command to the faculties** — The poet commands his own tongue, mind, hands, ears, and eyes to worship Keshava, turning the body into an instrument of kirtana _(actors: Kulasekhara; Keshava (Vishnu))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/mukunda-mala.md) — (Mukunda-mala Stotra (jihve kirtaya keshavam))
- **Kacha killed thrice and revived; the belly reversal** — The asuras murder Kacha three times, finally feeding his ashes to Śukra in wine; Śukra teaches the Sañjīvanī from inside his own belly, Kacha bursts out killing the guru and then revives him _(actors: Kacha, Śukra, Devayānī, the asuras)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/the-gurus-brihaspati-and-shukra.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva)
- **Kadrū's curse fulfilled** — The burning fulfills Kadrū's ancient curse that her serpent-sons perish in Janamejaya's fire, tying the rite to the Garuḍa/churning backstory _(actors: Kadrū, Vinatā, the nāgas, Brahmā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/astika-sarpa-satra.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi (Āstīka Parva))
- **Karthigai Deepam — the summit beacon** — At the Kārttikai full moon a colossal ghee lamp (Mahā-Dīpa) is kindled atop Aruṇācala, re-enacting the original pillar of fire as Śiva again shows his endless fiery form _(actors: Śiva, devotees/temple priests)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arunachala-tiruvannamalai-fire-hill.md) — (Aruṇācala-māhātmya; temple festival tradition)
- **King Bhaṅgāśvana becomes a woman** — A king transformed into a woman bears children as both sexes and, asked to choose, keeps the female form, declaring a mother's love and a woman's pleasure greater _(actors: Bhaṅgāśvana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/13-anushasana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 13.12)
- **Kālī slays Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa** — Kālī springs from Caṇḍikā's brow and beheads the generals Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, earning the name Cāmuṇḍā _(actors: Kālī (Cāmuṇḍā), Caṇḍikā, Caṇḍa, Muṇḍa)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devī Māhātmya ch.7)
- **Krittika nurses suckle Kartikeya/Skanda** — The six Pleiad-nurses of the Krittika nakshatra suckle the war-god Karttikeya/Skanda, the origin of his name _(actors: The six Krittikas, Skanda/Kartikeya)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/nakshatras.md) — (Puranic)
- **Manifestation as the dual Tiruvappana and Vellatam theyyam** — Muthappan is venerated as a Viṣṇu-Śiva (Harihara) fusion performed as two paired theyyam figures — the Vaiṣṇava fisher Tiruvappana and the Śaiva hunter Vellatam _(actors: Muthappan (Tiruvappana, Vellatam), theyyam performers)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/muthappan.md) — (Theyyam / Kaliyāṭṭam tradition; North Malabar folk Śaiva-Vaiṣṇava tradition)
- **Murugan's Marriage to Valli at Tiruttani** — At Tiruttani Murugan woos and weds Valli, the hunter-girl of the kurinci hills, the indigenous Tamil love-marriage counterpart to Deivanai _(actors: Murugan, Valli)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Tirumurukarruppatai; Kanda Puranam; Paripadal)
- **Mustering of the Goddess's army of śaktis** — Lalitā fields Vārāhī/Daṇḍanāthā as commander, Śyāmalā/Mantriṇī (Mātaṅgī) as prime minister, child-goddess Bālā, the Nityās, yoginīs and śaktis born of her own body _(actors: Lalitā, Vārāhī (Daṇḍanāthā), Śyāmalā (Mantriṇī/Mātaṅgī), Bālā, the Nityās and yoginīs)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna); Lalitā Sahasranāma)
- **Nandi as bearer of whispered prayers** — Devotees whisper petitions into Nandi's ear at the temple for conveyance to Śiva — the living custom before the liṅga's darśana _(actors: Nandi, devotees, Śiva)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nandi-and-shaiva-mounts.md) — (temple tradition; Śaiva Āgamas)
- **Nara–Nārāyaṇa in the Bhāgavata avatāra rosters** — The Bhāgavata Purāṇa lists Nara-Nārāyaṇa among Viṣṇu's līlā-avatāras as the tapas-and-dharma exemplifying descent, outside the fixed canonical Daśāvatāra _(actors: Viṣṇu/Nārāyaṇa, Nara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nara-narayana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 11.4; SB 1.3)
- **Oppiliappan Served Food Without Salt** — The founding legend of Tiruvinnagar where Vishnu as Oppiliappan vows to accept food served without salt, marking the marriage to Bhumi Devi _(actors: Vishnu (Oppiliappan), Bhumi Devi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/108-divya-desams.md) — (sthala-purana)
- **Origin of the four Shaktis from Shiva** — Four Goddess-energies (Shaktis) arise from Shiva, a Shakta theophany folded into the Shaiva cosmology _(actors: Shiva, the four Shaktis)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/kurma-purana.md) — (Kurma Purana)
- **Parvati's rebirth and tapas** — Sati is reborn as Parvati, daughter of Himavan, and wins the ascetic Shiva through severe penance _(actors: Parvati, Himavan, Shiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Rudra-samhita, Parvati-khanda))
- **Pāṇḍava Mahāprasthāna terminus** — After absolution the Pāṇḍavas begin their Mahāprasthāna, the northward renunciatory ascent into the Himālaya read as passing through the high Kedār country _(actors: Pāṇḍavas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kedarnath-kedara-kshetra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Mahāprasthānika Parva tradition; Skanda Purāṇa, Kedāra-khaṇḍa)
- **Phala-śruti coda — reciting the ford-list itself confers merit** — The parva closes its enumeration with the promise that even hearing or reciting the catalogue of fords frees one from sin, making the list itself a tīrtha _(actors: Pulastya, Bhīṣma (frame narrators))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mahabharata-tirtha-yatra-fords.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva 84–85, 156)
- **Purāṇic geography — seven dvīpas and Meru** — Systematic geography of Jambūdvīpa, the concentric continents and oceans, Mount Meru as world-axis, the varṣas of Bhārata and the motion of the heavenly bodies _(actors: —)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vayu-purana.md) — (Vāyu Purāṇa)
- **Pushkara and Brihaspati charter (River Pushkaram)** — The deva Pushkara, granted by Shiva (var.
