Brahma) the power to sanctify waters, agrees with Brihaspati (Jupiter) that he will enter a river to charge it with full tirtha-power for twelve days whenever Jupiter transits into that river's corresponding zodiacal sign — establishing the 12-yearly Pushkaram bathing-fair cycle across twelve sacred rivers (notably Godavari in Simha and Krishna in Kanya), with Adi and Antya Pushkaram book-ending each transit _(actors: Pushkara (deva), Brihaspati (Jupiter), Shiva/Brahma)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Wikipedia (Pushkaram); Andhra/Telangana endowment traditions [traditional])
- **Recognition as Ādi-Śakti / kuladevī** — Khodiyār is read as a manifestation of Ādi-Śakti/Durgā and becomes kuladevī of many Gujarati communities with major pilgrimage temples and the crocodile vāhana _(actors: Khodiyār Mātā, Leuvā Pāṭīdār, Rajput and Cāraṇ communities)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khodiyar-mata.md) — (Gujarati kuladevī tradition; Cāraṇ āī theology)
- **Redaction of the Caraka Saṃhitā** — Agniveśa's tantra is redacted by Caraka and completed by Dṛḍhabala into the foundational text of internal medicine and the tridoṣa doctrine _(actors: Agniveśa, Caraka, Dṛḍhabala)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/ayurveda-and-the-science-of-life.md) — (Caraka Saṃhitā)
- **Restoration of grace and powers** — Pleased by the hymn, Śiva restores Puṣpadanta's invisibility and grace, establishing the stotra as a model expiatory prayer _(actors: Śiva, Puṣpadanta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-mahimna-stotra.md) — (Śiva-mahimnaḥ stotra (Puṣpadanta frame); devotional tradition)
- **Śrīparvata named as a tīrtha in the Mahābhārata pilgrimage catalogue** — The Tīrtha-yātrā-parvan lists Śrīparvata among the southern Śaiva fords of Bhārata, an early pre-temple witness to the hill's sanctity _(actors: the Pāṇḍavas (pilgrimage frame), narrating sages)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srisailam-srisaila-mallikarjuna-bhramaramba.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana-parvan (Tīrtha-yātrā-parvan))
- **Sanskritizing graft of the Hariharaputra genealogy** — The older Dravidian guardian Ayyaṉār-Śāstā is given the son-of-Śiva-and-Mohinī (Hariharaputra/Bhūtanātha) parentage, folding the village god into the Purāṇic Śāstā genealogy _(actors: Śāstā/Ayyaṉār; Śiva; Vishnu-as-Mohinī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ayyanar-sastha.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa Śāstā stratum; harmonized tradition)
- **Saptarshi of the Vaivasvata Manvantara and gotra-father** — Bharadvaja is counted among the seven sages of the current Manvantara and is the eponymous founder of the Bharadvaja gotra (Angirasa-Barhaspatya-Bharadvaja pravara) _(actors: Bharadvaja, the Saptarshi (Kashyapa, Atri, Vasishtha, Gautama, Vishvamitra, Jamadagni))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharadvaja.md) — (Vishnu Purana 3.1; gotra-pravara tradition)
- **Satī's Self-Immolation at Māyāpurī** — In a local telling Satī performs her yogic self-immolation at Māyāpurī, sitting in dhyāna and consuming her body by inner fire (linked to Daksha's spoiled sacrifice at Kankhal) _(actors: Satī, Daksha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/haridwar-rishikesh-ganga-dwara.md) — (Wikipedia / temple tradition)
- **Satyakāma Jābāla's truthful lineage** — The boy Satyakāma honestly reports his unknown parentage; teacher Gautama accepts him as a true brāhmaṇa, and the elements and birds teach him the quarters of Brahman _(actors: Satyakāma Jābāla, Jabālā, Gautama Hāridrumata)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/chandogya-upanishad.md) — (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 4.4–4.9)
- **Satyavan revived and every boon fulfilled** — Yama looses the noose granting Satyavan four hundred years; Satyavan revives, Dyumatsena's sight and throne are restored as the usurper dies, and both Savitri and Ashvapati gain a hundred sons _(actors: Satyavan, Savitri, Dyumatsena, Ashvapati, Yama)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/savitri-satyavan.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva 297)
- **Self-manifest śālagrāma Badrī-Nārāyaṇa mūrti enshrined** — A black śālagrāma image of Viṣṇu in meditation arises self-manifest (svayam-vyakta) and is worshipped as Badrī Viśāl, flanked by Nara–Nārāyaṇa, Kubera, Nārada and Uddhava _(actors: Viṣṇu (Badrī-Nārāyaṇa))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/badrinath-badarikashrama-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Badarī-māhātmya; Garuḍa Purāṇa; Padma Purāṇa)
- **Shiva Slays Dushana as Mahakala at Ujjain** — Shiva emerges as Mahakala to slay the demon Dushana who tormented the devout brahmin Vridhi and the pious king, founding the Mahakaleshwar jyotirlinga at Ujjain _(actors: Shiva (Mahakala), Dushana, Vridhi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/12-jyotirlingas.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Siddhidatri grants Shiva his powers** — The Navadurga form Siddhidatri is said to have given Shiva his occult powers, making him Ardhanarishvara _(actors: Siddhidatri, Shiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Devi Kavaca; Devi-Bhagavata Purana)
- **Six-fold birth of Kārtikeya** — The seed becomes six infants in the reeds suckled by the six Kṛttikās; Pārvatī's embrace fuses them into the six-faced, twelve-armed Ṣaṇmukha _(actors: Kārtikeya (Skanda), the Kṛttikās, Pārvatī, Agni, Svāhā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva, Skanda Purāṇa, Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Soma worships at Somārāma for curse-relief** — Candra/Soma, afflicted by Dakṣa's wasting-curse, worships the moon-coloured liṅga at Gunupūdi-Bhimavaram to regain his light _(actors: Candra/Soma, Dakṣa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancharama-kshetras-andhra.md) — (Somārāma sthala-purāṇa (folk))
- **Tat tvam asi — Uddālaka teaches Śvetaketu** — Uddālaka Āruṇi instructs his son Śvetaketu nine times that 'That thou art,' teaching the identity of the self with universal Being _(actors: Uddālaka Āruṇi, Śvetaketu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/principal-upanishads-detail.md) — (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.8.7)
- **Teaching of aṣṭāṅga-yoga and kuṇḍalinī** — The Goddess teaches the eight limbs of yoga together with nāḍī/cakra physiology and the rousing of kuṇḍalinī, the text's Tantric layer _(actors: Bhuvaneśvarī, Himavat)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/devi-gita.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.35)
- **The Ashtavakra Gita Spoken to Janaka** — Ashtavakra instructs King Janaka in radical Advaita non-dualism, with Janaka voicing his own awakening mid-dialogue _(actors: Ashtavakra, Janaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/ashtavakra.md) — (Ashtavakra Gita)
- **The co-wife quarrel of Gaṅgā, Sarasvatī, Lakṣmī** — Viṣṇu's three consorts quarrel and curse one another, explaining why Gaṅgā descends to earth and Sarasvatī becomes a river _(actors: Gaṅgā, Sarasvatī, Lakṣmī, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahmavaivarta-purana.md) — (Brahmavaivarta Purāṇa (Prakṛti-khaṇḍa))
- **The golden-lotus tank & Sangam judgment-pool** — Śiva blooms a golden lotus in the Poṟṟāmarai Kuḷam, tradition holding it the pool that judged Sangam verses by floating or sinking _(actors: Śiva, the Tamil Sangam poets)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/madurai-meenakshi-sundareshwara-kshetra.md) — (Tiruviḷaiyāḍal Purāṇam; temple tradition)
- **Theology of the pre-paid crossing (name-as-soteriology)** — Bathing and offering at the hell-named earthly river is held to pre-cross the death-river, so the dead need never face the infernal Vaitaraṇī — the distinctive inversion of Jajpur _(actors: pilgrims; the ancestors; the two Vaitaraṇīs (earthly and infernal))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaitarani-jajpur-viraja-kshetra.md) — (Padma/Skanda māhātmyas; Garuḍa Purāṇa; tīrtha etymology)
- **The Pārvatī–Śiva framing dialogue (appended)** — An appended frame has Pārvatī ask Śiva the easy recitation, and Śiva answer that the single name 'Rāma' equals the whole thousand _(actors: Pārvatī, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/vishnu-sahasranama.md) — (Appended liturgical frame-verses of the Viṣṇu Sahasranāma)
- **The siddhānta: the brāhmaṇa is the knower of Brahman** — The text concludes that the brāhmaṇa is one who has directly realized the non-dual Ātman/Brahman as sat-cit-ānanda, free of egoism and the afflictions, ending as an Advaitic meditation-instruction _(actors: Upaniṣadic teacher)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/vajrasuchika-upanishad.md) — (Vajrasūcikā Upaniṣad 9)
- **The soul recollects past lives in the womb** — The ensouled fetus remembers its previous births and karma, recognizes its bondage to saṃsāra, and suffers in the womb _(actors: the embodied soul (jīva))_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/garbha-upanishad.md) — (Garbha Upaniṣad 4)
- **The Spider, Serpent and Elephant at Srikalahasti** — A spider, a serpent and an elephant each worship the Vayu-linga in their own way until their daily worship clashes; all three attain moksha and their names fuse into Sri-Kala-Hasti _(actors: spider, serpent, elephant, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancha-bhuta-sthalams.md) — (Srikalahasti mahatmya)
- **Third birth as Kṛṣṇa and the instruction to Gokula** — The same souls now Vasudeva and Devakī receive the Lord a third time as Kṛṣṇa, who then bids them treat him as an ordinary child and carry him to Gokula _(actors: Viṣṇu/Kṛṣṇa, Vasudeva, Devakī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/prishnigarbha-three-births.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.3.43-47)
- **Two-clocks placement of ritual body-art** — Body-adornment is fixed scripturally in the timeless 64-kalā curriculum (Kṛṣṇa's 64-day mastery) and historically as a living folk art of the kali-historical period _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Sāndīpani; living practitioners)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/mehndi-and-ritual-body-adornment.md) — (Kāmasūtra 1.3; repo two-clocks convention)
- **Upamanyu wins the ocean of milk from Śiva** — By terrible penance Upamanyu compels Śiva (who first tests him as Indra) to grant an ocean of milk and unwavering devotion _(actors: Upamanyu, Śiva, Indra (Śiva in disguise))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-sahasranama.md) — (Mahābhārata, Anuśāsana Parva XIII (Upamanyu legend))
- **Vishnu Tricks Vrika into Self-Destruction** — Vishnu, as a brahmana boy, plants doubt in Vrika and dares him to test the boon on himself, so Vrika lays his hand on his own head and his skull shatters _(actors: Vishnu, Vrika)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhasmasura-and-vrikasura.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 10.88.26-37)
- **Viṣṇumāyā and the monkey-face curse** — Given a monkey's face at a svayaṃvara instead of Viṣṇu's beauty, the humiliated Nārada curses Viṣṇu to suffer separation from a wife and depend on monkeys, fulfilled in the Rāma avatāra _(actors: Nārada, Viṣṇu, the princess (var. Viśvamohinī))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada.md) — (Bhāgavata-tradition retellings; Devī Bhāgavata; folk Rāmāyaṇa frames)
- **Viśvakarman forges the vajra from Dadhīci's spine** — The divine architect Viśvakarman fashions the adamantine thunderbolt (vajra) and other weapons from Dadhīci's bones, hammering the vajra specifically from his backbone _(actors: Viśvakarman, Dadhīci)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dadhichi.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 6.10)
- **Vāk Āmbhṛṇī proclaims herself the all-pervading power** — In the Devī Sūkta the seer Vāk (Speech), as both ṛṣikā and deity, declares herself the sustaining power over all gods and worlds — a Śākta foundation-text _(actors: Vāk Āmbhṛṇī, Ambhṛṇa (father))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/rigvedic-women-seers-brahmavadinis.md) — (Ṛgveda 10.125)
- **Winning of Bāṇāī the Dhangar wife** — Khaṇḍobā in pastoral guise herds sheep to win Bāṇāī, a Dhangar shepherd woman; the two-wife structure unites agrarian and pastoral communities _(actors: Khaṇḍobā, Bāṇāī, Dhangars, Mhāḷsā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khandoba-martanda-bhairava.md) — (Marathi folk tradition; ovī oral epics [folk/regional])
- **Yama's Boon to His Sister Yamuna at Yamunotri** — Yama grants his sister Yamuna the boon that bathing in or touching her water grants a painless death, the significance of the Yamunotri shrine _(actors: Yama, Yamuna)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/char-dham-and-chota-char-dham.md) — (regional mahatmya)
- **Yayāti falls from heaven and is saved by his grandsons** — Cast out of svarga for pride, Yayāti is restored by the merit of his daughter Mādhavī's four royal-sage sons _(actors: Yayāti, Indra, Mādhavī, Aṣṭaka, Śibi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yayati.md) — (Mahābhārata Udyoga Parva 120–122)
- **Yājñavalkya defeats the sages at Janaka's court** — At King Janaka's great sacrifice Yājñavalkya claims the prize cattle and defeats challenger after challenger (Aśvala, Ārtabhāga, Bhujyu, Uṣasta, Kahola, Gārgī, Uddālaka) on Brahman and the Self _(actors: Yājñavalkya, Janaka, Gārgī, Uddālaka)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/brihadaranyaka-upanishad.md) — (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.1–3.9)
- **Abhijit descends and contests Rohini for seniority** — In the Mahabharata, the star Abhijit slips down from the sky contesting with Rohini for seniority, narrated to Skanda/Indra as why an extra (28th) nakshatra was needed; it becomes the nakshatra of victory _(actors: Abhijit, Rohini, Skanda/Indra)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/nakshatras.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva III)
- **Ambarisha Attains the Lord's Abode** — Having demonstrated bhagavata-dharma perfectly, Ambarisha in old age divides his kingdom among his sons, retires to the forest, and attains the abode of Vishnu _(actors: Ambarisha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ambarisha-durvasa.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 9.5.25-28)
- **Attukal Amma identified with Kannagi** — The Attukal Bhagavathy charter identifies the goddess with Kannagi of the Silappatikaram, who burned Madurai in vengeance, grounding the women-only Attukal Pongala offering _(actors: Attukal Amma, Kannagi)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Silappatikaram; Kerala folklore)
- **Bṛhaddevatā enumerates the women seers** — Śaunaka's Bṛhaddevatā fixes the canonical roll of Ṛgvedic brahmavādinīs (Ghoṣā, Viśvavārā, Apālā, Romaśā, Lopāmudrā, et al.) _(actors: Śaunaka, the brahmavādinīs)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/rigvedic-women-seers-brahmavadinis.md) — (Bṛhaddevatā 2.82–84)
- **Bharadvaja receives Ayurveda from Indra** — Deputed by the sages, Bharadvaja approaches Indra and brings the science of life (Ayurveda) back to the rishis, founding the medical lineage through Atreya and Agnivesha _(actors: Bharadvaja, Indra, Atreya Punarvasu, Agnivesha (later Charaka))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharadvaja.md) — (Charaka Samhita, Sutrasthana 1)
- **Bhasmasura Destroyed by Mohini's Dance** — In the folk version, the ash-demon Bhasmasura wins the incinerate-by-touch boon, turns on Shiva, and Vishnu as Mohini dances him into laying his hand on his own head, reducing him to ashes _(actors: Bhasmasura, Shiva, Mohini)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhasmasura-and-vrikasura.md) — (Regional/folk tradition)
- **Bṛhaspati proclaims ahiṃsā the highest dharma** — Bṛhaspati delivers the celebrated declaration that non-violence is the highest dharma, self-control, gift, and austerity, counselling universal compassion _(actors: Bṛhaspati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/13-anushasana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 13.117)
- **Bhrigu kicks Vishnu's chest (the Bhrigu-test)** — The Prajapati Bhrigu kicks Vishnu's chest to decide the supreme god, earning the foot-mark Shrivatsa _(actors: Bhrigu, Vishnu)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/the-prajapatis.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 10.89; Mahabharata; Padma Purana)
- **Bindu-sarovar established as the mātṛ-tīrtha (Mātṛ-gayā)** — Siddhpur's lake becomes the foremost site of the mother's death-rite, counterpart to Gayā's pitṛ-rite, read back from Kapila liberating Devahūti _(actors: Pilgrims; Siddhpur tradition)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-sarovar-sacred-lakes.md) — (Siddhpur sthala-purāṇa; Skanda Purāṇa)
- **Birth and beheading of Gaṇeśa (Śani-gaze version)** — Pārvatī obtains a son by the Puṇyaka-vrata; Śani's inauspicious gaze severs the infant's head, and Viṣṇu fetches a young elephant's head (Airāvata's calf) to restore him _(actors: Pārvatī, Gaṇeśa, Śani, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahmavaivarta-purana.md) — (Brahmavaivarta Purāṇa (Gaṇeśa-khaṇḍa))
- **Burning of Kama (Kama-dahana)** — Kamadeva, sent to rekindle desire in the ascetic Shiva, is burned to ash by Shiva's third eye and later restored bodiless as Ananga _(actors: Shiva, Kamadeva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Rudra-samhita, Parvati-khanda))
- **Chaṇḍī Slays Śumbha and Niśumbha** — The goddess Chaṇḍī/Chaṇḍikā slays the asuras Śumbha and Niśumbha and their generals Chaṇḍa and Muṇḍa, the deed enshrined at Chaṇḍī Devī on Nīl Parvat _(actors: Chaṇḍī, Śumbha, Niśumbha, Chaṇḍa, Muṇḍa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/haridwar-rishikesh-ganga-dwara.md) — (Devi Mahatmya)
- **Compilation of the Suśruta Saṃhitā** — Suśruta's teaching is preserved as the foundational surgical text, describing instruments, incisions, cataract couching, rhinoplasty, and cadaver anatomy _(actors: Suśruta)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/ayurveda-and-the-science-of-life.md) — (Suśruta Saṃhitā)
- **Creation of Lopamudra** — Fearing his ancestors hang in limbo for want of offspring, Agastya assembles the most beautiful limbs of all creatures and infuses the child into the queen of Vidarbha, born as Lopamudra _(actors: Agastya, Lopamudra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/agastya.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva)
- **Cross-tradition identity as Mailāra/Mallanna/Khaṇḍerāo** — The deity is recognized across the Deccan as a single god under regional names (Mailāralinga, Mallanna, Khaṇḍerāo), with Kannada Mailāra as the older stratum _(actors: Khaṇḍobā/Mailāra/Mallanna, Goṛava and Vāghyā-Muraḷī devotees)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khandoba-martanda-bhairava.md) — (Kannada Mailāra and Telugu Mallanna traditions [folk/regional]; modern scholarship)
- **Devāpi and Maru re-establish the kṣatriya dynasties at Kali's close** — At the end of Kali Yuga, the immortal Devāpi (lunar line) with Maru/Moru (solar Ikṣvāku line) re-emerge from Kalāpa to re-found the kṣatriya dynasties and restore dharma at the dawn of the next Kṛta/Satya Yuga _(actors: Devāpi, Maru (Moru))_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/devapi.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa IV.24; Bhāgavata Purāṇa XII.2.37-38, IX.12)
- **Devī slays Mahiṣāsura and Śumbha–Niśumbha** — The Devī Māhātmya narrates the Goddess Durgā slaying the buffalo-demon Mahiṣāsura and the demons Śumbha and Niśumbha _(actors: Devī/Durgā, Mahiṣāsura, Śumbha, Niśumbha)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/smriti-puranas.md) — (Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa (Devī Māhātmya))
- **Durga assembled from the devas' tejas** — The supreme warrior Durga is assembled from the combined radiant energy of all the devas to slay Mahishasura _(actors: Durga, the devas, Mahishasura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Devi Mahatmya)
- **Gajendra seized by the crocodile** — Gajendra, an elephant-king (cursed former king Indradyumna), is seized by the leg by a crocodile (the cursed gandharva Hūhū) while gathering lotuses on Mount Trikūṭa _(actors: Gajendra, the crocodile/Hūhū)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/airavata-and-celestial-animals.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8.2–8.4)
- **Ganesha wins the race around the parents** — Set a race to circle the world for a divine fruit, Ganesha circumambulates his parents declaring they are his whole world, winning over Kartikeya _(actors: Ganesha, Kartikeya, Shiva, Parvati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Shiva Purana; Skanda Purana)
- **Hayagrīva named in the Nārāyaṇīya avatāra roster** — The Śānti Parva's early avatāra list names Hayaśiras/Hayagrīva as the horse-headed recoverer of the Vedas alongside Haṃsa, Varāha, Narasiṃha, Vāmana _(actors: Viṣṇu's avatāra forms; Bhīṣma, Yudhiṣṭhira (frame))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hayagriva.md) — (Mahābhārata, Nārāyaṇīya / Śānti Parva)
- **Indra slays Triśiras and Vṛtra (Śākta reframing)** — Indra slays the three-headed Triśiras (Viśvarūpa) and the dragon Vṛtra, then purifies the resulting brahmahatyā guilt through Goddess-worship _(actors: Indra, Triśiras, Vṛtra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/devi-bhagavata-upapurana.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata, skandha VI)
- **Indra slays Vṛtra with the Dadhīci-vajra** — Armed with the vajra forged from Dadhīci's spine, Indra slays the drought-serpent Vṛtra, releasing the cosmic waters and restoring world order; in the Bhāgavata Vṛtra dies a willing devotee and attains liberation _(actors: Indra, Vṛtra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dadhichi.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 6.11–13)
- **King Suratha and merchant Samadhi worship the Devi** — The dethroned king Suratha and the merchant Samadhi worship the Goddess under the sage Medhas; Suratha is destined to be reborn as the future 8th Savarni Manu _(actors: Suratha, Samadhi, Medhas)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/markandeya-purana.md) — (Markandeya Purana (Devi Mahatmya frame))
- **Kṛṣṇā issues from the cow-mouth at Mahābaleśvar — Pañcagaṅgā** — The Kṛṣṇā rises from a stone cow-mouth at Mahābaleśvar as one of five rivers (Pañcagaṅgā) born from the same source _(actors: Kṛṣṇā, Veṇī, Kōynā, Gāyatrī, Sāvitrī, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sacred-rivers-and-descent-myths.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Sahyādri-khaṇḍa / Mahābaleśvar-māhātmya)
- **Kālī springs from Durgā's brow and slays Raktabīja** — In the battle with Caṇḍa, Muṇḍa, and Raktabīja, Kālī springs from Durgā's forehead and drinks Raktabīja's cloning blood to win _(actors: Kālī, Durgā, Caṇḍa, Muṇḍa, Raktabīja)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/trimurti-and-tridevi.md) — (Devī Māhātmya ch. 7-8)
- **Lakṣmī's pique and departure; Tirumala/Kolhapur sequels** — Affronted her seat was struck, Lakṣmī leaves Vaikuṇṭha, settling at Kolhapur while Viṣṇu descends as Śrīnivāsa to Tirumala — the separated-couple frame _(actors: Lakṣmī, Viṣṇu, Bhṛgu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigu.md) — (Veṅkaṭācala-Māhātmya; Karavīra-Māhātmya (Padma Purāṇa))
- **Lakshmi re-emerges and weds Vishnu** — The goddess of fortune rises radiant from the ocean and of her own choice garlands and weds Vishnu, restoring sri to the cosmos _(actors: Lakshmi, Vishnu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/samudra-manthana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 8.8; Vishnu Purana 1.9)
- **Madhubani Rama-Sita wedding painting** — Mithila women paint the Rama-Sita wedding in the kohbar nuptial chamber, Mithila being Sita's homeland, alongside Krishna, Durga and Shiva-Parvati _(actors: Mithila women painters, Rama, Sita)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/chitra-kala-painting-traditions.md) — (Wikipedia, Madhubani art)
- **March on Śūnyaka in the Śrīcakra chariots** — Lalitā rides the nine-tiered Cakra-rāja-ratha, with Mantriṇī on Geya-cakra and Vārāhī on Kiri-cakra, advancing on Bhaṇḍāsura's city _(actors: Lalitā, Mantriṇī, Vārāhī, the śakti host)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna))
- **Markandeya witnesses the pralaya** — The deathless Markandeya survives a dissolution, floating on the cosmic waters and seeing the infant Vishnu on the banyan leaf _(actors: Markandeya, Vishnu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/the-chiranjivis.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva; Puranas)
- **Mārkaṇḍeya's Penance Tested by Kāmadeva** — Fearing the sage's austerity, Indra sends Kāmadeva, spring and apsarases to break his penance, but Mārkaṇḍeya remains unmoved _(actors: Indra, Kāmadeva, Vasanta, Apsarās, Mārkaṇḍeya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/markandeya.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 12.8)
- **Muchukunda's service to the devas and boon of sleep** — The Ikshvaku prince Muchukunda, son of Mandhatri, led the devas' armies in a deva-asura war and on release asked Indra only for undisturbed sleep, with the boon that any waker be burnt by his glance _(actors: Muchukunda, Mandhatri, Indra, devas, Skanda/Kartikeya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/muchukunda-and-kalayavana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana X.51.14-22; Harivamsha)
- **Nahusha usurps Svarga and is cursed** — During Indra's absence the mortal king Nahusha is crowned regent, lusts after Shachi, and kicks sage Agastya, who curses him to fall as a serpent; Indra is then restored _(actors: Nahusha, Shachi, Agastya, Indra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/indra.md) — (Mahabharata, Udyoga Parva)
- **Nandi's curse of Rāvaṇa** — Barred at Kailāsa and mocked for his face, Nandi curses Rāvaṇa that monkeys will destroy his race and city; Rāvaṇa then tries to lift Kailāsa and is pinned by Śiva's toe _(actors: Nandi, Rāvaṇa, Śiva)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nandi-and-shaiva-mounts.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa, Uttarakāṇḍa; Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Parvati's Penance and Birth of Girijatmaj at Lenyadri** — Parvati (Girija) performs penance at Lenyadri desiring a son, and Ganesha is born/installed there as Girijatmaja, her own son _(actors: Parvati, Ganesha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashtavinayaka.md) — (Ganesha Purana tradition)
- **Prescription of worship and enumeration of pīṭhas** — The Goddess prescribes external and internal worship (image, mantra, Śrī-cakra, mānasa-pūjā, Navarātri) and enumerates her sacred pīṭhas/kṣetras as the geography of her presence _(actors: Bhuvaneśvarī, Himavat)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/devi-gita.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa VII.38–39)
- **Rāma worships at Kṣīrārāma** — Rāma is said to have worshipped the milk-white liṅga at Palakollu, giving the deity the name Rāmaliṅgeśvara _(actors: Rāma, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancharama-kshetras-andhra.md) — (Kṣīrārāma sthala-purāṇa (regional))
- **Rāvaṇānugraha iconographic tradition** — The legend becomes a canonical sculptural form (Ellora, Elephanta) of Śiva and Pārvatī above the straining demon below _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Rāvaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/shiva-tandava-stotra.md) — (Śaiva Āgama/Śilpa iconography; Ellora and Elephanta reliefs [scholarly])
- **Sanatkumāra teaches Nārada the Infinite** — Sanatkumāra instructs Nārada on bhūman, the Infinite, surpassing all lesser knowledge in the Chāndogya _(actors: Sanatkumāra, Nārada)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/principal-upanishads-detail.md) — (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 7)
- **Sanjna flees Surya leaving Chhaya** — Unable to endure Surya's heat, Sanjna leaves her shadow-double Chhaya in her place and flees as a mare to perform tapas _(actors: Sanjna, Surya, Chhaya)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/surya-and-his-family.md) — (Rigveda 10.17; Markandeya & Matsya Purana)
- **Sarasvati carries the Vadava fire to the ocean** — Sarasvati is dispatched to bear the world-destroying submarine Vadava-agni into the ocean where it can burn harmlessly, splitting into channels and descending as a river to do it _(actors: Sarasvati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/saraswati.md) — (Mahabharata; Skanda Purana)
- **Satī's Earring Falls at Maṇikarṇikā / Viśālākṣī** — A part of Satī (variously an earring or her eyes) falls at Kashi, establishing the Viśālākṣī Śakti-pīṭha and fusing the cremation-ground with goddess-ground _(actors: Satī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kashi-varanasi.md) — (Devi-Bhagavata; Shakti-pitha lists)
- **Satī's yoni falls at Kāmākhyā** — The originating myth of the Kāmākhyā pīṭha: the spot in Kāmrūp, Assam where Satī's yoni fell, becoming the foremost yoginī-pīṭha and a natural yoni-stone cult-image _(actors: Satī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shakta-and-tantra-lineages.md) — (Kālikā Purāṇa; Yoginī Tantra)
- **Self-manifestation at Totadri/Vanamamalai** — Vishnu reclines as Deivanayaga Perumal with Varamangai Nachiyar at the Nanguneri hill-shrine, counted among the eight self-disclosed seats _(actors: Vishnu as Deivanayaga Perumal; Varamangai Nachiyar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-svayamvyakta-vishnu-kshetras.md) — (Vaishnava sthala-purana; Divya Suri Carita)
- **Shiva Splits into Omkara and Amaleshwara at Omkareshwar** — When the Vindhya mountain worships Shiva and the Devas battle the Danavas, Shiva splits into Omkareshwar and Mamleshwar to save the devotees, founding the Omkareshwar jyotirlinga on the Om-shaped Mandhata island _(actors: Shiva, Vindhya, Devas, Danavas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/12-jyotirlingas.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Siddhars enter jiva-samadhi** — Perfected Siddhars are said not to die but to enter conscious living entombment (jiva-samadhi) at shrines such as Palani, Marutamalai and Madurai, remaining present across the Kali age _(actors: Bogar; Agattiyar; Pambatti; Sundaranandar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tamil-siddhars.md) — (Temple tradition; jiva-samadhi shrine lore)
- **Skanda made general and slays Tārakāsura** — Anointed Devasenāpati and armed with the Vel, the boy Skanda leads the devas and kills the otherwise-invincible Tārakāsura, fulfilling his birth-purpose _(actors: Skanda/Kārtikeya, Tārakāsura, Indra, Śiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Matsya Purāṇa, Mahābhārata Vana Parva)
- **Slaying of Raktabīja** — Kālī drinks every drop of the demon Raktabīja's blood mid-air, preventing the clones his blood spawns, so the bloodless original collapses dead _(actors: Kālī/Cāmuṇḍā, Caṇḍikā, Raktabīja, the Mātṛkās)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devī Māhātmya ch.8)
- **Solar and lunar dynasty genealogies** — Sūrya- and Candra-vaṃśa king-lists (vaṃśa/vaṃśānucarita lakṣaṇas) from the Manus through the heroes of the present age _(actors: Sūryavaṃśa and Candravaṃśa kings)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vayu-purana.md) — (Vāyu Purāṇa)
- **The conch passes to Viṣṇu/Kṛṣṇa; etiology of the worship-conch** — The demon's substance becomes the sacred śaṅkha of Viṣṇu-worship and his conch-jewel folds into Kṛṣṇa's regalia, closing the Sudāmā arc _(actors: Viṣṇu/Kṛṣṇa, Śaṅkhacūḍa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shankhachuda-tulasi.md) — (Brahma-Vaivarta Purāṇa, Prakṛti-khaṇḍa)
- **The Fruit-of-Wisdom Race and Murugan's Renunciation at Palani** — In Narada's fruit contest Ganesha wins by circling his parents while Murugan races the world; the slighted Murugan storms off to Palani as the renunciate Dandayudhapani _(actors: Narada, Ganesha, Murugan, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Kanda Puranam; murugan.org)
- **The Goddess remains as guardian-maiden of the cape** — Her task complete, Kanyākumārī stays at the subcontinent's tip as the eternal virgin facing the sea, absorbed in perpetual tapas _(actors: Kanyākumārī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-kanyakumari.md) — (Sthala-purāṇa of Kanyākumārī; temple tradition)
- **The gods arm Durga weapon by weapon** — Each god gives Durga a replica of his own weapon or emblem, and Himavan gives her the lion as mount _(actors: Durga, Shiva, Vishnu, Varuna, Agni, Vayu, Indra, Yama, Himavan)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 2)
- **The gods dig Nakki Lake** — Folk etymology holds the heart-shaped Nakki Lake was scooped out by gods with their fingernails (nakh) in one night to confront the demon Bāskali _(actors: the gods, demon Bāskali / Rakṣa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mount-abu-arbuda-achaleshwar.md) — (Nakki sthala tradition [folk])
- **The Kālī dance-contest and ūrdhva-tāṇḍava** — Śiva subdues the fierce goddess Kālī by performing the raised-leg ūrdhva-tāṇḍava she cannot modestly copy, winning the contest at Tillai/Cidambaram _(actors: Śiva, Kālī (Tilai-Kāḷi))_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/natya-shastra-and-the-classical-arts.md) — (Cidambaram sthala-purāṇa; Kōyil Purāṇam [regional])
- **The sacred fires teach Upakosala** — The neglected pupil Upakosala is taught by the three sacred fires that prāṇa, joy and space are Brahman, and shown the path of the gods (devayāna) _(actors: Upakosala Kāmalāyana, the sacred fires)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/chandogya-upanishad.md) — (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 4.10–4.15)
- **The silver-hall (Sandhyā) dance** — Śiva dances the evening Sandhyā Tāṇḍava in Madurai's Veḷḷi/Rajata Sabhai with his right foot raised, mirror of Chidambaram's left _(actors: Śiva (Sundareśvara), a Pāṇḍya king, Mīnākṣī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/madurai-meenakshi-sundareshwara-kshetra.md) — (Pañcha Sabhai tradition; Tiruviḷaiyāḍal Purāṇam)
- **The Vindhyavāsinī promise in the Nārāyaṇī-stuti** — In Devī Māhātmya ch. 11 the Devī names Vindhyavāsinī among her promised future manifestations alongside Raktadantikā, Śākambharī, Bhīmā, Bhrāmarī _(actors: the Devī (Nārāyaṇī), the gods)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vindhyavasini.md) — (Devī Māhātmya 11; Mūrti Rahasya)
- **The womb-vow of liberation** — In anguish the soul vows that, if delivered from the womb, it will resort to Sāṃkhya-Yoga / Nārāyaṇa-Maheśvara to attain liberation _(actors: the soul in the womb; Nārāyaṇa/Maheśvara invoked)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/garbha-upanishad.md) — (Garbha Upaniṣad 4)
- **Tilottamā destroys Sunda and Upasunda** — Fashioned by Viśvakarmā from the finest particle of every beautiful thing, Tilottamā is created to destroy the invincible asura brothers Sunda and Upasunda, who fight each other to death over her _(actors: Tilottamā, Viśvakarmā, Sunda, Upasunda)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/apsaras.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva)
- **Tripura-dahana, slaying of Jalandhara, and burning of Kama** — Shiva burns the three flying cities of Tripura, slays Jalandhara, and reduces Kama to ash with his third eye _(actors: Shiva, the Tripura asuras, Jalandhara, Kama)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/linga-purana.md) — (Linga Purana)
- **Tripurāntaka — destruction of the three cities** — Śiva incinerates the three flying fortresses of Tāraka's sons (Tārakākṣa, Vidyunmālī, Kamalākṣa) with a single arrow when the cities align, the whole cosmos serving as his weapon _(actors: Śiva, Tārakākṣa, Vidyunmālī, Kamalākṣa, Maya, Brahmā, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa Rudra-saṃhitā, Liṅga Purāṇa, Matsya Purāṇa, Mahābhārata Karṇa Parva)
- **Śukra bans wine for brahmins** — Disgusted at being tricked through liquor, Śukra decrees that no brahmin shall henceforth drink intoxicants, an etiological first prohibition _(actors: Śukra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/the-gurus-brihaspati-and-shukra.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva)
- **Vidagdha Śākalya's head shatters** — In the final debate Yājñavalkya turns examiner; unable to name Yājñavalkya's ultimate support, Vidagdha Śākalya's head shatters and robbers carry off his bones _(actors: Yājñavalkya, Vidagdha Śākalya)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/brihadaranyaka-upanishad.md) — (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.9.26)
- **Vishnu Gains Siddhi at Siddhatek** — Vishnu worships Ganesha at Siddhatek to gain the siddhi (perfection) needed to defeat the demons Madhu and Kaitabha _(actors: Vishnu, Ganesha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/temple-networks-and-circuits.md) — (Ashtavinayaka sthala-purana)
- **Vishnu's maya-moha deludes the asuras** — Vishnu emanates a deceiver (read as a proto-Buddha figure) to seduce the asuras from the Veda so the gods can defeat them _(actors: Vishnu, asuras)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vishnu-purana.md) — (Vishnu Purana (Amsha 3))
- **Vishvamitra Composes the Gayatri Mantra** — The seer Vishvamitra composes the Gayatri/Savitri verse to Savitr in the gayatri metre, which becomes the most revered Vedic mantra and is personified as the goddess Gayatri _(actors: Vishvamitra, Savitr)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedic-deities-and-suktas.md) — (Rigveda 3.62.10)
- **Annapūrṇā Feeds the Begging Śiva** — In the Kashi myth Śiva himself begs alms from the bowl of the goddess Annapūrṇā, the deity of renunciation accepting food from the deity of abundance _(actors: Śiva, Annapūrṇā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kashi-varanasi.md) — (Skanda Purana, Kashi-khanda; folk myth-cycle)
- **Bhṛgu's authorship — Bhṛgu Saṃhitā and Manu Smṛti** — Bhṛgu is the reputed compiler of the astrological Bhṛgu Saṃhitā and the reciter of Manu's dharma in the Manu Smṛti _(actors: Bhṛgu, Manu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhrigu.md) — (Manu Smṛti 1.58–60; Bhṛgu Saṃhitā tradition)
- **Birth of the Ashvins from Surya and Sanjna** — The twin horse-headed Ashvin physicians are conceived when Surya, as a stallion, reunites with Sanjna as a mare _(actors: Surya, Sanjna, the Ashvins)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/surya-and-his-family.md) — (Rigveda)
- **Bālā slays the sons of Bhaṇḍāsura** — The nine-year-old Bālā Tripurasundarī insists on fighting and singlehandedly destroys Bhaṇḍāsura's thirty sons _(actors: Bālā Tripurasundarī, the sons of Bhaṇḍāsura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna))
- **Cāmpāṣaṣṭhī festival commemoration** — The six-day Cāmpāṣaṣṭhī festival at Jejuri annually commemorates the slaying of the demons with turmeric-showering _(actors: Khaṇḍobā, pilgrims at Jejuri)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/khandoba-martanda-bhairava.md) — (Marathi regional tradition [regional])
- **Dakṣa's curse of perpetual wandering** — After Nārada persuades Dakṣa's sons to renounce procreation, the furious Dakṣa curses him to ceaseless roving, an etiology for the wandering-sage archetype _(actors: Nārada, Dakṣa, the Haryaśvas and Śabalāśvas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 6.5)
- **Death of Niśumbha and Śumbha** — The Goddess reabsorbs all the Mātṛkās into herself and slays the asura brothers Niśumbha and Śumbha in single combat, restoring heaven to the gods _(actors: Devī (Caṇḍikā/Kauśikī), Niśumbha, Śumbha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devī Māhātmya ch.9-10)
- **Durga slays Mahishasura's generals** — Durga annihilates Mahishasura's army and slays his generals Chikshura, Chamara, Udagra, Mahahanu and others _(actors: Durga, Chikshura, Chamara, Udagra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 2-3)
- **Durga slays the asura Durgama** — In the Shatakshi/Shakambhari episode, the Goddess slays the asura Durgama, an etymon of her name Durga _(actors: Durga, Durgama)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Devi-Bhagavata Purana)
- **Forgetting at birth by the Vaiṣṇava wind** — At birth the newborn is struck by the vaiṣṇava-vāyu (cosmic illusory wind) and forgets its past lives and its resolve, re-entering worldly life in ignorance _(actors: the newborn; the vaiṣṇava-vāyu (māyā))_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/garbha-upanishad.md) — (Garbha Upaniṣad 4)
- **Gajendra-mokṣa — Viṣṇu rescues the elephant-king** — After a thousand-year struggle Gajendra cries to Viṣṇu, who flies in on Garuḍa, beheads the crocodile with the Sudarśana-cakra, and grants Gajendra sārūpya-mukti _(actors: Gajendra, Viṣṇu, Garuḍa)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/airavata-and-celestial-animals.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8.3–8.4)
- **Ganesha curses the moon** — After Candra mocks his burst belly, the furious Ganesha decrees that whoever looks at the moon on his festival day will be falsely accused _(actors: Ganesha, Candra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Ganesha Purana)
- **Ganesha Defeats Vighnasura at Ozar** — Ganesha defeats the demon Vighnasura (created by Indra to wreck King Abhinandana's sacrifice), sparing him on condition he trouble no worshippers, becoming Vighneshvara _(actors: Vighnasura, Indra, Abhinandana, Ganesha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashtavinayaka.md) — (Ganesha Purana tradition)
- **Ganesha Slays Vighnasura at Ozar** — Ganesha as Vigneshwar slays the demon Vighnasura, founding the gold-domed Ashtavinayaka shrine at Ozar _(actors: Ganesha, Vighnasura)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/temple-networks-and-circuits.md) — (Ashtavinayaka sthala-purana)
- **Hayagrīva in the Bhāgavata 24-avatāra list** — Hayagrīva is added with Haṃsa to extend the Bhāgavata līlā-avatāra enumeration to twenty-four; not part of the fixed Daśāvatāra _(actors: Viṣṇu's avatāras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hayagriva.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 1.3 (with commentary); 2.7)
- **Idumban Carries the Twin Hills (Origin of Kavadi)** — Murugan's disciple Idumban carries twin hills on a kavadi yoke at Palani, the origin of the kavadi-bearing pilgrimage tradition _(actors: Idumban, Murugan)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Kanda Puranam; murugan.org)
- **Jāmbavān recognises Rāma reborn (Rāmāyaṇa bridge)** — Jāmbavān realises his opponent is the Supreme Lord he once served as Rāma and submits, giving the gem and his daughter _(actors: Jāmbavān, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/syamantaka-jewel.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.56)
- **Kanyākumārī fixed as a Śakti Pīṭha** — In the 51 Śakti Pīṭha scheme, Satī's back/spine fell at the cape, enshrining Śakti Śarvāṇī with Bhairava Nimiṣa, layering the Pīṭha stratum onto the virgin-warrior myth _(actors: Satī; Śiva; Śakti Śarvāṇī; Bhairava Nimiṣa)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-kanyakumari.md) — (Tantra Cūḍāmaṇi / Pīṭhanirṇaya; Mahābhāgavata Purāṇa)
- **Kṛṣṇa re-transmits the Haṃsa teaching to Uddhava** — At the Dvāpara–Kali cusp, Kṛṣṇa recounts the ancient swan-dialogue to Uddhava as primordial wisdom, giving the Uddhava Gītā its alternate name Haṃsa Gītā _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Uddhava)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hamsa-avatar.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 11.13.15-17)
- **Kṛṣṇa's boon — worship as Śyām in Kali Yuga** — Kṛṣṇa blesses Barbarīka to be worshipped under Kṛṣṇa's own name Śyām in Kali Yuga, the charter of the later cult _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, Barbarīka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/barbarika-khatu-shyam.md) — (Folk Mahābhārata; Khāṭū Śyām sthala-tradition)
- **Kālī halted upon the supine Śiva** — Kālī's battle-rampage is halted only when Śiva lies in her path; she steps on him and bites her tongue in abashment, expressing Śakti as dynamic and Śiva as the still ground _(actors: Kālī, Śiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/trimurti-and-tridevi.md) — (Devī-Bhāgavata Purāṇa; Tantric tradition)
- **Kāmadahana — burning of Kāma** — Śiva opens his third eye and burns the love-god Kāmadeva to ash for disturbing his meditation; later restored bodiless (Ananga) at Rati's plea _(actors: Śiva, Kāmadeva, Rati, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa Rudra-saṃhitā, Matsya Purāṇa, Kumārasaṃbhava)
- **Marriage of Agastya and Lopamudra** — Grown Lopamudra consents to wed the poverty-sworn ascetic Agastya, sheds her royal silks for bark, and follows him into the forest _(actors: Agastya, Lopamudra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/agastya.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva)
- **Marriage of Shiva and Parvati** — After Parvati's penance, the divine wedding of Shiva and Parvati follows, prerequisite to the births of their sons _(actors: Shiva, Parvati)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Rudra-samhita, Parvati-khanda))
- **Maya's cross-yuga persistence** — Never slain by an avatāra, Maya recurs as the asura world's permanent engineer across Satya, Treta and Dvāpara — effectively chiranjivi-like _(actors: Maya, Viśvakarman)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/maya-danava.md) — (Purāṇic & epic strata; asura roster §VI)
- **Mārkaṇḍeya's Boon to Behold Māyā** — After ages of austerity, Mārkaṇḍeya asks Nārāyaṇa not for a gift but to behold the Lord's illusory potency that creates and dissolves the universe _(actors: Mārkaṇḍeya, Nārāyaṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/markandeya.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 12.8)
- **Murugan slays Sūrapadman** — In the Tamil cycle Murugan hurls the Vel and splits the demon Sūrapadman in two, whose halves become his peacock mount and rooster banner _(actors: Murugan (Skanda), Sūrapadman)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Kanda Purāṇam)
- **Mutual curses of Kacha and Devayānī** — Kacha refuses to marry Devayānī, declaring her his sister; she curses that the stolen vidyā will fail in his hands and he counter-curses that no sage's son will marry her, driving her to wed King Yayāti _(actors: Kacha, Devayānī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/the-gurus-brihaspati-and-shukra.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva)
- **Nachiketā visits Yama's realm and returns** — The boy Nāciketa visits the realm of Yama and returns with knowledge of the afterlife and of right gifting, a Mahābhārata recension of the Kaṭha Upaniṣad tale _(actors: Nachiketā, Yama)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/13-anushasana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 13; Katha Upanishad)
- **Nandikeśvara as lord and transmitter of music and dance** — Nandi keeps time for Śiva's Tāṇḍava and is venerated as master of saṅgīta and nṛtya, presiding over the heavenly arts _(actors: Nandikeśvara, Śiva (Naṭarāja), gandharvas)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nandi-and-shaiva-mounts.md) — (Śaiva tradition; Naṭarāja iconography)
- **Parable of the yakṣa and Umā Haimavatī** — The gods Agni, Vāyu, and Indra boast of victory until a mysterious yakṣa (Brahman), revealed by the goddess Umā Haimavatī, humbles them, showing their power is borrowed _(actors: Agni, Vāyu, Indra, Umā Haimavatī, Brahman)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/principal-upanishads-detail.md) — (Kena Upaniṣad 3-4)
- **Paraśurāma breaks Gaṇeśa's tusk (Ekadanta)** — Barred at Śiva's gate by Gaṇeśa, Paraśurāma hurls Śiva's own axe; Gaṇeśa refuses to nullify his father's weapon and loses his left tusk, becoming Ekadanta _(actors: Paraśurāma, Gaṇeśa, Śiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahmavaivarta-purana.md) — (Brahmavaivarta Purāṇa (Gaṇeśa-khaṇḍa))
- **Pravāhaṇa Jaivali teaches the Five Fires** — The Pāñcāla king Pravāhaṇa Jaivali poses five unanswerable questions to Śvetaketu and Uddālaka, then teaches the pañcāgni-vidyā and the two paths (devayāna/pitṛyāna) of rebirth _(actors: Pravāhaṇa Jaivali, Śvetaketu, Uddālaka)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/chandogya-upanishad.md) — (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 5.3–5.10)
- **Pārvatī transmits the Lāsya to women** — Pārvatī teaches the tender feminine Lāsya to women (Uṣā, thence the women of Dvārakā), founding the feminine solo-dance lineage _(actors: Pārvatī, Uṣā)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/natya-shastra-and-the-classical-arts.md) — (Nāṭya Śāstra ch. 4; Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Ramopakhyana retold in the Pandava forest** — The Mahabharata's Rama-narrative compresses the Dandaka exile and abduction, told to Yudhishthira within the Pandava exile-forest, linking the two great vanavasas _(actors: Markandeya/narrator, Yudhishthira, Rama (in tale))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kamyaka-dvaita-pandava-exile-forests.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana-parva 262-263)
- **Reṇukā beheading etiology of the Māri head** — Paraśurāma beheads and restores Reṇukā with a head-and-body swap, the Treta-yuga myth used to explain the fierce severed-head Māri/Yellammā icon of the kali-historical cult _(actors: Reṇukā; Jamadagni; Paraśurāma; Māri/Yellammā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/mariamman.md) — (Mahābhārata/Purāṇa Reṇukā tale; South Indian folk variants)
- **Satī's self-immolation at Dakṣa's yajña creates the pīṭhas** — Satī, born as Dakṣa's daughter, immolates herself at his sacrifice; in the Śākta telling her scattered body creates the Śakti Pīṭhas _(actors: Satī, Dakṣa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/devi-bhagavata-upapurana.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata, skandha VII ch.30)
- **Shukra obtains the Mritasanjivani vidya from Shiva** — Through severe tapas Shukra obtains from Shiva the Mritasanjivani, the science of reviving the dead, with which he keeps resurrecting slain asuras against the devas _(actors: Shukra, Shiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/navagraha.md) — (Mahabharata; Matsya Purana)
- **Srikalahasti Kalamkari temple narratives** — Kalamkari pen-artists draw freehand Ramayana, Mahabharata and Krishna-lila friezes in vegetable dyes as temple cloth-hangings _(actors: Kalamkari artisans)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/chitra-kala-painting-traditions.md) — (Wikipedia, Kalamkari)
- **Surya's seven-horsed chariot turns northward (Ratha Saptami)** — On Magha Shukla Saptami the Sun-god's seven-horsed chariot turns decisively northward, marking Surya's birthday and awakening, observed with arka-leaf bathing _(actors: Surya)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Puranic/solar tradition; Drik Panchang)
- **Sāvitrī's curse turns the gods into the Pañcagaṅgā rivers** — Angered Sāvitrī curses the gods so that Viṣṇu, Śiva and Brahmā become the Kṛṣṇā, Kōynā and Veṇī streams _(actors: Sāvitrī, Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sacred-rivers-and-descent-myths.md) — (Marāṭhā sthala-purāṇa)
- **The Ānanda Tāṇḍava at Chidambaram** — For the sages Patanjali and Vyaghrapada, and after defeating the goddess Kali in a dance-contest, Shiva performs the Dance of Bliss in the golden hall, enacting the five cosmic acts of creation, preservation, destruction, concealment and grace _(actors: Shiva, Patanjali, Vyaghrapada, Kali)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancha-bhuta-sthalams.md) — (Chidambara Mahatmya; Tirumantiram)
- **The Skandapurāṇa Vindhyavāsinī war-cycle** — In the early Skandapurāṇa the goddess takes the Vindhya as her seat, gathers fierce attendants, and wages war on the buffalo/Śumbha-class demons—an oldest full Goddess-war account feeding the Devī Māhātmya _(actors: Vindhyavāsinī, Mātṛkās/Kālī emanations, the asura host)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vindhyavasini.md) — (Skandapurāṇa (early recension) Vindhyavāsinī-cycle)
- **Tripurāntaka — destruction of the three flying cities** — Śiva mounts a cosmic chariot built from all the gods and looses a single arrow that incinerates the three indestructible flying cities (Tripura) of the asura brothers at the one aligned instant _(actors: Śiva, Tārakākṣa, Vidyunmālī, Kamalākṣa, Maya)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa Rudra-saṃhitā; Liṅga Purāṇa; Matsya Purāṇa; Padma Purāṇa; Mahābhārata Karṇa Parva)
- **Vishnu svayam-vyakta seat at Pushkara** — The Pushkara lake-tirtha, foremost where all gods reside, is counted a Vishnu self-manifest seat (Varaha/Veni-Madhava) despite its Brahma dominance _(actors: Vishnu (Varaha/Venimadhava); Brahma)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-svayamvyakta-vishnu-kshetras.md) — (Padma Purana (Srishti/Patala Khanda); Pushkara-mahatmya)
- **Viṣṇu's war on the rākṣasas; slaying of Mālī and flight to Pātāla** — Grown arrogant from Brahmā's boons, the three brothers harry the devas; Viṣṇu wars on them, kills Mālī with his discus, and Mālyavān and Sumālī flee to Pātāla, abandoning Laṅkā to Kubera _(actors: Viṣṇu, Mālī, Mālyavān, Sumālī)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/rakshasas.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa Uttara Kāṇḍa)
- **Birth of Dridhasyu** — Agastya and Lopamudra beget the son Dridhasyu (Idhmavaha), who chants the Vedas from the womb, releasing the ancestors from limbo _(actors: Agastya, Lopamudra, Dridhasyu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/agastya.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva)
- **Birth of Ganesha and the elephant head** — Parvati fashions Ganesha from her body to guard her door; Shiva beheads him in fury and replaces his head with an elephant's, making him lord of the ganas and remover of obstacles _(actors: Parvati, Ganesha, Shiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Rudra-samhita, Kumara-khanda))
- **Cheriyal scrolls of the caste-Puranas** — Nakashi painters create vertical narrative scrolls of the Markandeya-purana and epic episodes for bard-communities who sing them village to village _(actors: Nakashi painters, Kunapuli/Gauda bards)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/chitra-kala-painting-traditions.md) — (Wikipedia, Cheriyal scroll painting)
- **Cyavana shares the fate of the netted fish** — The sage Cyavana, caught in a fisherman's net, insists on sharing the fate and value of the fish, teaching the worth of all life and the dharma of the Niṣādas _(actors: Cyavana, the Niṣāda fishermen)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/13-anushasana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 13)
- **Divakara Muni and the Mischievous Boy at Ananta-kadu** — A divine boy defiles the sage Divakara Muni's shalagrama and vanishes into Ananta-kadu forest, where an ilappa tree falls and transforms into the colossal reclining Vishnu of Padmanabhasvami _(actors: Divakara Muni, Vishnu (boy form))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Brahmanda Purana)
- **Durga beheads the shape-shifting Mahishasura** — Mahishasura morphs through buffalo, lion, man, and elephant forms; Durga pins the buffalo, pierces its neck, and beheads the demon as he emerges from its mouth, becoming Mahishasuramardini _(actors: Durga, Mahishasura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 3)
- **Durga slays Mahishasura (Madhyama-carita)** — The gods' combined splendor coalesces into Durga, who rides her lion and destroys the buffalo-demon Mahishasura — the Mahishasuramardini episode _(actors: Durga (Devi), Mahishasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/markandeya-purana.md) — (Markandeya Purana (Devi Mahatmya chs. 2-4))
- **Gautama Brings Down the Godāvarī** — Sage Gautama, atoning for the accidental death of a cow, uses a tuft of Ganga's hair (or Shiva's gift) to bring down the Godavari at Tryambakeshvar _(actors: Gautama)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sapta-puri-and-sacred-cities.md) — (Brahma Purana (Gautami-mahatmya))
- **Kaikasī sent to seduce Viśravas** — Sumālī sends his daughter Kaikasī to win the sage Viśravas to breed a champion who could overthrow Kubera, producing Rāvaṇa and his siblings _(actors: Sumālī, Kaikasī, Viśravas)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/rakshasas.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa Uttara Kāṇḍa)
- **Kali springs from Chandi's brow to slay Raktabija** — Kali springs from the wrathful Chandi's furrowed brow and drinks the blood of Raktabija _(actors: Kali, Chandi, Raktabija)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 7-8; Mahanirvana Tantra)
- **Kamakhya's annual menstruation (Ambubachi Mela)** — The Kamakhya Shakti Peetha (where Sati's yoni fell) closes for three days marking the goddess's annual menstruation, then reopens, the rite sacralizing menstruation as cosmic fertility _(actors: Kamakhya, Sati)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Wikipedia, Ambubachi Mela)
- **Kanyā-tīrtha in the Mahābhārata pilgrimage-tour** — The Mahābhārata's tīrtha-yātrā names a Kanyā/Kumārī tīrtha at the far southern shore among the holy bathing-places, attesting the virgin-goddess cape's antiquity _(actors: the Pāṇḍavas; (narrator of the tīrtha-list))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-kanyakumari.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva (tīrtha-yātrā))
- **Ketu Granted Planetary Status at Keezhaperumpallam** — The headless serpent-body Ketu worships Shiva at Keezhaperumpallam and is blessed with planetary status, fixing the spot as the Ketu parihara _(actors: Ketu, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/navagraha-temples.md) — (Sthala-purana)
- **Kālāntaka — Mārkaṇḍeya saved from Yama** — Śiva bursts from the liṅga as Ender-of-Death to strike down Yama and grant the boy-devotee Mārkaṇḍeya deathless eternal youth at sixteen _(actors: Śiva (Kālāntaka), Mārkaṇḍeya, Yama, Mṛkaṇḍu)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Skanda Purāṇa, Liṅga Purāṇa, Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa)
- **Left as protector of Parikshit at the Great Departure** — As the Pandavas renounce the world and install Parikshit, Yuyutsu the senior Kuru survivor is left as steward-protector of the young king's realm, bridging Dvapara's end into the Kali age _(actors: Yuyutsu, Parikshit, the Pandavas, Kripa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/yuyutsu.md) — (Mahabharata, Mausala/Mahaprasthanika Parva)
- **Manifestation of Mahā-Gaṇapati against the obstacle-yantra** — To counter Viṣukra's paralyzing Jaya-vighna yantra, the Goddess manifests Mahā-Gaṇapati, who shatters the obstacle and clears the army's path _(actors: Lalitā, Kāmeśvara, Mahā-Gaṇapati, Viṣukra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna))
- **Marriages of Kārtikeya to Devasenā and Vaḷḷi** — Skanda weds the army-bride Devasenā given by Indra and the hill-tribe love-bride Vaḷḷi, wooed in disguise with Gaṇeśa's help _(actors: Kārtikeya, Devasenā, Vaḷḷi, Indra, Gaṇeśa)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kanda Purāṇam)
- **Murugan Imprisons Brahma and Teaches Om to Shiva at Swamimalai** — Having imprisoned Brahma for failing to explain Pranava (Om), Murugan expounds Om to Shiva himself, becoming the guru of his own father as Swaminatha _(actors: Murugan, Brahma, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Kanda Puranam; murugan.org)
- **Nala loses his kingdom to Kali's possession** — Possessed by the demon Kali, Nala gambles away his kingdom to his brother Pushkara and is exiled with Damayanti _(actors: Nala, Damayanti, Kali, Pushkara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/03-vana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva 58-59)
- **Narration framed by Śiva to Pārvatī** — The whole discourse is transmitted as Śiva narrating the secret Rāma-story to Pārvatī, who reports Rāma's words to Lakṣmaṇa _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rama-gita.md) — (Adhyātma Rāmāyaṇa, narrative frame)
- **Parable of the dog transformed into ever-fiercer beasts** — A ṛṣi's pet dog is successively transformed into fiercer beasts for protection until it turns on its master and is reduced again to a dog, a parable against raising the base to high station _(actors: a ṛṣi, his dog)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/12-shanti-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 12.116-117)
- **Pradyumna as the third Pāñcarātra vyūha** — In Pāñcarātra theology Pradyumna is the third of the four eternal vyūhas (Vāsudeva-Saṅkarṣaṇa-Pradyumna-Aniruddha), correlated with manas and the creative principle _(actors: Pradyumna (vyūha), Vāsudeva, Saṅkarṣaṇa, Aniruddha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/pradyumna.md) — (Nārāyaṇīya (Mahābhārata Śānti Parva); Bhāgavata Purāṇa 11.5.29-30; Pāñcarātra Āgamas)
- **Pramlocā and the 907-year tapas of Kandu** — Sent to break rishi Kandu's world-scorching penance, Pramlocā lives with him 907 years; when banished, her foetus exudes as sweat onto the trees, becoming Māriṣā, later mother of Dakṣa _(actors: Pramlocā, Kandu, Māriṣā)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/apsaras.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.15; Brahma Purāṇa)
- **Raibhya Ṛṣi's Penance and Naming of Rishikesh** — The sage Raibhya performs severe tapasya on the Gaṅgā bank; pleased, Nārāyaṇa appears on a mango branch and boons to remain there as Hṛṣīkeśa Nārāyaṇa, giving the place its name Rishikesh _(actors: Raibhya Ṛṣi, Viṣṇu (Nārāyaṇa))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/haridwar-rishikesh-ganga-dwara.md) — (Skanda Purana, Kedar-Khanda; tirthayatra.org)
- **Recovery of self-manifest Badarinarayana at Badari** — The self-formed shaligrama Badarinarayana murti, tapas-seat of Nara-Narayana, is disclosed/recovered from the Narada-kunda in the Alaknanda _(actors: Vishnu as Badarinarayana; Nara-Narayana; (Shankaracharya in legend))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-svayamvyakta-vishnu-kshetras.md) — (Bhagavata Purana; Skanda Purana Badari-mahatmya)
- **Sanatkumāra teaches Nārada the Bhūman** — The sorrowing Nārada, knowing only texts, asks Sanatkumāra to lead him across; Sanatkumāra ascends a ladder of faculties to the infinite Bhūman, the bliss that is the Self _(actors: Nārada, Sanatkumāra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/chandogya-upanishad.md) — (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 7.1–7.26)
- **Shiva Destroys Tripurasura as Bhima at Bhimashankar** — Shiva manifests as Bhima to destroy the demon Tripurasura tormenting the devotee-king Kamarupeshwar, and the sweat of the fierce battle becomes the Bhima river, founding the Bhimashankar jyotirlinga _(actors: Shiva (Bhima), Tripurasura, Kamarupeshwar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/12-jyotirlingas.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Shiva Worships Ganesha Before Destroying Tripurasura at Ranjangaon** — Shiva worships Ganesha at Ranjangaon before marching to destroy the three flying cities of Tripurasura, inverting the usual order with the great god propitiating his own son _(actors: Shiva, Ganesha, Tripurasura)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashtavinayaka.md) — (cf. Tripurantaka myth)
- **Shiva Worships Ganesha Before Fighting Tripurasura** — At Ranjangaon Shiva worships the powerful Mahaganapati form of Ganesha before going to battle the demon Tripurasura _(actors: Shiva, Ganesha, Tripurasura)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/temple-networks-and-circuits.md) — (Ashtavinayaka sthala-purana)
- **Six disciples question Pippalāda** — Six seekers question the sage Pippalāda on creation, prāṇa, sleep, the syllable Oṃ, and the person of sixteen parts _(actors: Pippalāda)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/principal-upanishads-detail.md) — (Praśna Upaniṣad)
- **Tarakasura's boon of restricted death** — The asura Tarakasura wins from Brahma that he can be slain only by a true son of Shiva, counting on the widowed ascetic Shiva never fathering one _(actors: Tarakasura, Brahma, Shiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Skanda Purana; Shiva Purana)
- **The Devī Gītā delivered to Himavat** — The Goddess Bhuvaneśvarī instructs Himavat and the gods, revealing her Virāṭ universal form and expounding nondual Brahman, māyā, yoga, and her worship — the Śākta analogue of the Bhagavad Gītā _(actors: Bhuvaneśvarī, Himavat)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/devi-bhagavata-upapurana.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata, skandha VII chs.31–40)
- **The Kacha-Devayani episode** — Brihaspati's son Kacha infiltrates as Shukra's disciple to learn the vidya; killed repeatedly and finally fed to Shukra in wine, he learns the mantra from inside Shukra's belly, bursts forth and revives Shukra, then mutual curses follow including Devayani's curse to marry a non-brahmin _(actors: Kacha, Shukra, Devayani)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/navagraha.md) — (Mahabharata, Adi Parva 71-76)
- **The knower-of-the-body and the internal fires** — The text closes by turning from the body's anatomy to the kṣetrajña (knower within) and the internal fires that sustain life, framing embryology as self-knowledge _(actors: the indwelling Self (kṣetrajña); Pippalāda)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/garbha-upanishad.md) — (Garbha Upaniṣad 5; colophon)
- **The Pralaya Deluge Engulfs the World** — The cosmic dissolution suddenly floods all worlds as the seven oceans merge, leaving Mārkaṇḍeya the sole survivor tossed on endless waters _(actors: Mārkaṇḍeya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/markandeya.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 12.9; Mahabharata Vana Parva)
- **Tāpī created from Sūrya's own heat** — Sūrya fashions his daughter Tāpī from his excessive heat to provide a cooling presence; she becomes the westward river _(actors: Sūrya, Tāpī/Tāpatī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sacred-rivers-and-descent-myths.md) — (Tāpī-māhātmya; Skanda Purāṇa)
- **Vishnu becomes Mohini and serves nectar to the devas** — The asuras seize the amrita, but Vishnu assumes the enchantress Mohini, bewitches them into surrendering the pot, and serves the nectar only to the devas _(actors: Vishnu/Mohini, the asuras, the devas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/samudra-manthana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 8.9; Mahabharata, Adi Parva)
- **Viṣṇu deludes the Tripura asuras** — Before the Tripura war Viṣṇu, as a heretic teacher or Buddha-like preacher, seduces the Tripura asuras away from dharma to make them vulnerable to Śiva's arrow _(actors: Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa; Matsya Purāṇa)
- **Vrishabha slays the netherworld demons** — Shiva assumes a bull-form to descend into Patala and slay the degenerate sons who terrorized the netherworld _(actors: Vrishabha (Shiva))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Yama, First Mortal to Die, Becomes Lord of the Dead** — Yama, the first mortal to die, becomes the path-finder of the dead and benevolent lord of the ancestral realm (Pitrloka), gathering the dead to a heavenly pasture _(actors: Yama)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedic-deities-and-suktas.md) — (Rigveda)
- **Yoga Upaniṣads bridge classical Upaniṣads to Haṭha/Tantra** — Their chakra, nāḍī, kuṇḍalinī, bandha, mudrā and nāda system, rooted in Kaṭha/Śvetāśvatara/Maitrī and the Yoga darśana, becomes the direct scriptural ancestor of the Haṭha manuals and Tantric subtle-body theory _(actors: Patañjali (Yoga-sūtra); later Haṭha/Tantra compilers)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/yoga-upanishads-overview.md) — (Kaṭha, Śvetāśvatara, Maitrī Ups.; Yoga-sūtra; Haṭha-yoga-pradīpikā; Gheraṇḍa-saṃhitā; Śiva-saṃhitā)
- **Ahimsa Paramo Dharmah spoken to Ruru** — A serpent-brahmin teaches the sage Ruru that non-violence is the highest dharma, the maxim recurring across the Mahabharata _(actors: Ruru, serpent-brahmin)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/ahimsa-and-hindu-ethics.md) — (Mahabharata, Adi Parva 1.11.12)
- **Avatara of Vakratunda slaying Matsarasura** — Ganesha descends as Vakratunda to slay Matsarasura, the demon personifying envy, an allegory of conquering jealousy _(actors: Ganesha (Vakratunda), Matsarasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/ganesha-and-mudgala-upapuranas.md) — (Mudgala Purana)
- **Beheading of Svarbhanu and origin of Rahu and Ketu** — The asura Svarbhanu disguises as a deva to drink the nectar; Surya and Chandra expose him and Mohini severs his head with the Sudarshana Chakra, but the nectar-touched head and body become the immortal eclipse-causers Rahu and Ketu _(actors: Svarbhanu (Rahu/Ketu), Surya, Chandra, Mohini/Vishnu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/samudra-manthana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 8.9-8.10; Vishnu Purana; Mahabharata)
- **Bhadrakali born from Shiva's wrath at the Daksha-yajna** — The variant Kali form Bhadrakali is born from Shiva's wrath at Daksha's sacrifice _(actors: Bhadrakali, Shiva, Daksha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Devi Mahatmya tradition)
- **Birth of Kartikeya and slaying of Tarakasura** — Shiva's fiery seed, passed through Agni and Ganga to the Krittikas, is born as the six-faced Kartikeya/Skanda, who as war-general slays the invincible demon Tarakasura _(actors: Shiva, Agni, Ganga, Krittikas, Kartikeya, Tarakasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Rudra-samhita, Kumara-khanda))
- **Birth of Skanda and slaying of Taraka (Vamana telling)** — Shiva's seed borne by Agni, Ganga and the Krittikas generates six-faced Skanda, consecrated commander of the gods, who slays the boon-protected Taraka _(actors: Shiva, Agni, Ganga, Krittikas, Skanda, Taraka)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vamana-purana.md) — (Vamana Purana (adhyaya ~31))
- **Curse and Disappearance of the Sarasvatī River** — The Sarasvati is cursed to sink into the desert and goes underground, reappearing only at named tirthas such as Prabhasa and Kurukshetra _(actors: Sarasvati)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sapta-puri-and-sacred-cities.md) — (Mahabharata; Rigveda 7.95)
- **Devi slays Shumbha, Nishumbha and Raktabija (Uttama-carita)** — The Goddess campaigns against Shumbha and Nishumbha and their generals Dhumralochana and Chanda-Munda, and Kali drinks the blood of Raktabija, capped by the Devi's declaration of supremacy _(actors: Devi, Kali, Shumbha, Nishumbha, Dhumralochana, Chanda, Munda, Raktabija)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/markandeya-purana.md) — (Markandeya Purana (Devi Mahatmya chs. 5-13))
- **Durga slays Mahishasura (Navaratri/Dussehra)** — Durga, forged from the combined tejas of the devas, battles the buffalo-demon Mahishasura over nine nights and beheads him, commemorated by Navaratri and Vijayadashami _(actors: Durga, Mahishasura)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Devi Mahatmya)
- **Fall of Viṣukra and Viṣaṅga and the demon army** — Vārāhī and the Nityās overcome Bhaṇḍāsura's brothers and forces, defeating their illusion-engines and revived-asura weapons with superior Daśāvatāra-astras _(actors: Vārāhī, the Nityās, Viṣukra, Viṣaṅga)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna))
- **Indra and Virocana learn the Self from Prajāpati** — Gods and demons send Indra and Virocana to Prajāpati, who tests them over a 101-year discipleship through four answers; Virocana settles for the bodily self while Indra persists to the true bodiless Self _(actors: Indra, Virocana, Prajāpati)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/chandogya-upanishad.md) — (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 8.7–8.12)
- **Indra assumes many forms through his mayas** — In the Rigveda, Indra wields maya as wonder-working power, assuming many forms, the earliest Vedic sense of maya as creative potency _(actors: Indra)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/maya-and-avidya.md) — (Rigveda 6.47.18; Brihadaranyaka Upanishad 2.5.19)
- **Krishna revives Parikshit; the line preserved** — Krishna revives the dead-born child Parikshit, who succeeds to Hastinapura and continues the Kuru dynasty into the Kali Yuga; Ashvatthaman is cursed to deathless wandering _(actors: Krishna, Uttaraa, Parikshit, Ashvatthaman)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/virata-and-his-children.md) — (Mahabharata, Sauptika & Ashvamedhika Parvas; Bhagavata Purana 1.8-1.12)
- **Krishna's dehotsarga (departure from the world)** — Krishna casts off his body at the Prabhasa Triveni (Dehotsarga), returning to his transcendent abode and ending the Dvapara Yuga at the Dvapara-Kali hinge _(actors: Krishna, Jara)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/prabhasa-somnath-tirtha.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 11.30-31; Mahabharata Mausala Parva 4)
- **Kundalini revealed by Shiva to the Goddess** — The subtle-body anatomy of nadis, chakras, and the coiled serpent-power is revealed by Shiva to the Goddess as the body's eternal map _(actors: Shiva, Devi)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/chakras-and-kundalini.md) — (Shakta tantras; Shat-chakra-nirupana)
- **Kāśyapa-gotra as universal fallback** — In gotra-tradition anyone without a known gotra is assigned the Kāśyapa gotra, since Kaśyapa is the father of all beings _(actors: Kaśyapa, the gotra-less)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kashyapa.md) — (gotra-pravara tradition / Dharmaśāstra usage)
- **Manasā Tends Śiva After the Hālāhala** — The serpent-goddess Manasā, destroyer of poison, is assimilated to Śaiva myth as the one who tended Śiva after he drank the Hālāhala poison _(actors: Manasā, Śiva)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nagas-and-serpents.md) — (Purāṇas; Manasā-maṅgala tradition)
- **Mataris­van brings fire to humans** — When fire was hidden from humans, Matarisvan stole/fetched Agni from heaven and gave him to the Bhrigus, who established sacrificial fire among the clans of men _(actors: Matarisvan, Agni, the Bhrigus)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/agni.md) — (Rg Veda 1.60; 3.9; 6.8)
- **Music section and Pāśupata-Śaiva lore** — Early Purāṇic treatise on music (svaras, grāmas, mūrcchanās, tāla) plus the Lakulīśa/Māheśvara yogeśvaras, the Śiva incarnations founding the Pāśupata school _(actors: Śiva, Lakulīśa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vayu-purana.md) — (Vāyu Purāṇa; cf. Liṅga Purāṇa)
- **Nachiketa's Three Boons from Yama** — Given to Death by his father's angry word, the boy Nachiketa waits three nights at Yama's house and receives three boons, including Yama's teaching of the secret of the Atman beyond death _(actors: Nachiketa, Yama, Vajashravas)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/yama.md) — (Katha Upanishad 1-2)
- **Nalinika seduces Isisinga (Buddhist Jataka parallel)** — In the Nalinika Jataka the horned ascetic Isisinga, born of a doe, is seduced and his ascetic power broken by the king's daughter Nalinika disguised as a young ascetic, sent by Sakka _(actors: Isisinga, Nalinika, Sakka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rishyashringa.md) — (Nalinika Jataka (no. 526))
- **Nārada as bhakti author and Purāṇa namesake** — Tradition ascribes to Nārada the Nārada Bhakti Sūtra defining devotion as supreme love of God, and casts the Nārada Purāṇa as his Vaiṣṇava discourse _(actors: Nārada)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada.md) — (Nārada Bhakti Sūtra; Nārada Purāṇa)
- **Nārada initiates Dhruva and counsels Vyāsa** — As guru-bhakta he initiates Dhruva into Viṣṇu-worship and counsels Vyāsa to compose the Bhāgavata, embodying the nāma-saṅkīrtana ideal _(actors: Nārada, Dhruva, Vyāsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 1.5–6, 4.8; Nārada Bhakti Sūtra)
- **Parvati scorches Kubera's eye** — In a folk Shaiva variant, Kubera gazes enviously at Parvati and her glance scorches one of his eyes yellow, making him Ekakshipingala, the one tawny-eyed _(actors: Kubera, Parvati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/kubera.md) — (Folk Shaiva tradition)
- **Pichwai hangings behind Shrinathji at Nathdwara** — Large cloth pichwai are hung behind the child-Krishna Shrinathji icon, their subjects fixed by the Vallabha Pushtimarga festival-calendar _(actors: Pichwai painters, Shrinathji (Krishna), Pushtimarga sampradaya)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/chitra-kala-painting-traditions.md) — (Wikipedia, Pichhwai)
- **Reunion of Nala and Damayanti** — Transformed by the naga Karkotaka into the dwarf Bahuka, Nala exchanges dice-lore for horse-lore with Rituparna, expels Kali, and reunites with Damayanti, winning back his kingdom _(actors: Nala, Damayanti, Rituparna, Karkotaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/03-vana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva 66-79)
- **Rāvaṇānugraha-mūrti iconographic tradition** — The episode becomes a canonical Śaiva sculptural form — Śiva and Pārvatī atop Kailāsa with Rāvaṇa straining beneath — famously at Ellora and Elephanta _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Rāvaṇa (sculptural))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ravana-kailasa-ravananugraha.md) — (Śaiva Āgama/Śilpa iconography; Ellora & Elephanta reliefs)
- **Sage Gautama Brings Down the Godavari at Trimbakeshwar** — Falsely accused of killing a maya-cow, sage Gautama brings the Ganga down as the Godavari to purify the sin, and Shiva remains as the three-faced Trimbakeshwar jyotirlinga _(actors: Gautama, Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/12-jyotirlingas.md) — (Shiva Purana; Brahma Purana)
- **Sage Shvetashvatara Teaches the Upanishad** — The eponymous sage Shvetashvatara, by the power of his austerity and God's grace, sees and declares the supreme purifying knowledge to the assembly of renunciate seers, transmitting it as a guarded teaching _(actors: Shvetashvatara, the rishis)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/svetashvatara-upanishad.md) — (Shvetashvatara Upanishad 6.21-6.23)
- **Shiva burns Kama and is won by Parvati's tapas** — The devas send Kama to fire desire at the meditating Shiva; Shiva burns him to ash with his third eye, but Parvati's long tapas eventually wins him _(actors: Shiva, Kama, Parvati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Shumbha and Nishumbha usurp heaven** — The asura brothers Shumbha and Nishumbha seize the cosmos and strip the gods of their shares of sacrifice _(actors: Shumbha, Nishumbha, the gods)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 5)
- **The Child Vishnu on the Banyan Leaf** — Amid the flood Mārkaṇḍeya sees a luminous infant (Bāla-Mukunda) resting on a banyan leaf, sucking his toe _(actors: Mārkaṇḍeya, Bāla-Mukunda)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/markandeya.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 12.9; Mahabharata Vana Parva)
- **The Yama-Yami Dialogue** — The twins Yama and Yami hold their famous incest dialogue, with Yama refusing his sister's advances _(actors: Yama, Yami)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedic-deities-and-suktas.md) — (Rigveda 10.10)
- **Tāpatī weds Saṃvaraṇa and bears Kuru** — The river-goddess Tāpatī marries king Saṃvaraṇa, united by Vasiṣṭha, and bears Kuru, eponym of the Kuru dynasty _(actors: Tāpatī, Saṃvaraṇa, Vasiṣṭha, Kuru)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sacred-rivers-and-descent-myths.md) — (Mahābhārata, Ādi Parva; river-māhātmya)
- **Uddalaka teaches Shvetaketu 'Tat Tvam Asi'** — The teacher Uddalaka instructs his son Shvetaketu nine times that 'That Thou Art,' delivering the great Samavedic mahavakya _(actors: Uddalaka, Shvetaketu)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/atman-brahman-and-the-six-darshanas.md) — (Chandogya Upanishad 6.8.7-6.16)
- **Śukra's penance to Śiva and rebirth as semen** — Śukra performs extreme tapas to Śiva at Kāśī; pleased, Śiva swallows him and expels him as semen (śukra), fixing his name and granting him the Mṛtasañjīvanī mantra _(actors: Śukra, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/the-gurus-brihaspati-and-shukra.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa Rudra-saṃhitā 2.5.50)
- **Variant disputes over the boundary member** — Recensions differ over Naimisha vs Salagrama as the northern pole and over substituting Pushkara, while the Garuda-Padma roster keeps both/all _(actors: Purana redactors; Sri Vaishnava commentators)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-svayamvyakta-vishnu-kshetras.md) — (Garuda Purana; Padma Purana; Vaishnava mahatmya recensions)
- **Varuna teaches Bhrigu to know Brahman (Bhrigu Valli)** — Sage Varuna gives his son Bhrigu the definition of Brahman, and through tapas Bhrigu realizes Brahman successively as anna, prana, manas, vijnana, and finally ananda _(actors: Varuna, Bhrigu)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/pancha-koshas.md) — (Taittiriya Upanishad 3.1-3.6)
- **Vishvamitra reveals the Gayatri Mantra** — The seer Vishvamitra perceives and reveals the Gayatri Mantra addressed to Savitr, the most revered Vedic mantra _(actors: Vishvamitra, Savitr)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/mantra-japa-and-murti-puja.md) — (Rigveda 3.62.10)
- **Vyāsa as chiranjīvi and Kuru counsellor** — A deathless chiranjīvi, Vyāsa advises the Kurus, grants Sañjaya divine sight and Gāndhārī her hundred sons, and raises the war-dead from the Gaṅgā for one night _(actors: Vyāsa, Gāndhārī, Kuntī, Sañjaya, Dhṛtarāṣṭra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyasa.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi & Āśramavāsika Parvas)
