- **Yatinatha tests the Bhil devotees** — As an ascetic guest Shiva tests the hospitality of the tribal Ahuka and Ahuka, rewarding their sacrifice of self for a guest _(actors: Yatinatha (Shiva), Ahuka, Ahuka)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Agastya Brings the Kāverī to the South** — Sage Agastya brings the Kaveri south by releasing her from his kamandalu, often via Ganesha-as-crow tipping the pot, sourcing the river at Talakaveri _(actors: Agastya, Ganesha, Kaveri)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sapta-puri-and-sacred-cities.md) — (Skanda Purana; Agastya legend cycle)
- **Agni hides in the waters and plants** — Weary of his consuming role, Agni hides in the waters and plants; the gods search and he is betrayed by aquatic creatures and revealed, whereupon he curses those who disclosed him _(actors: Agni, the gods, fish/frogs/elephant)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/agni.md) — (Mahabharata Vana/Anushasana strands; Brahmana myths)
- **Alambusa seduces Isisinga (Buddhist Jataka parallel)** — In the Alambusa Jataka Sakka sends the nymph Alambusa to seduce Isisinga; he falls but then recovers his ascetic resolve _(actors: Isisinga, Alambusa, Sakka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rishyashringa.md) — (Alambusa Jataka (no. 523))
- **Ashvins Rescue Bhujyu and Rejuvenate Cyavana** — The twin physician-horsemen Ashvins rescue the shipwrecked Bhujyu and restore the aged sage Cyavana, performing their characteristic healing and rescue miracles _(actors: Ashvins, Bhujyu, Cyavana)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedic-deities-and-suktas.md) — (Rigveda)
- **Avadhuta humbles Indra** — The naked wandering renunciate form of Shiva humbles Indra, who blocked his path, by paralyzing the king of gods to teach ego-dissolution _(actors: Avadhuta (Shiva), Indra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Avatara of Ekadanta slaying Madasura** — Ganesha descends as Ekadanta to slay Madasura, demon of arrogance/conceit _(actors: Ganesha (Ekadanta), Madasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/ganesha-and-mudgala-upapuranas.md) — (Mudgala Purana)
- **Bhīmā springs from Śiva's sweat after the Tripura slaying** — The Bhīmā river rises at Bhīmāśaṅkar from Śiva's sweat after he destroys the demon Tripura/Bhīma _(actors: Śiva, Bhīmā/Candrabhāgā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sacred-rivers-and-descent-myths.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa; sthala-tradition)
- **Birth of Hariharaputra (Ayyappa) to Slay Mahishi** — Because the demoness Mahishi can be killed only by a child of both Hari and Hara, Vishnu takes the Mohini form, unites with Shiva, and Hariharaputra (Ayyappa) is born _(actors: Shiva, Vishnu-Mohini, Mahishi, Ayyappa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Bhutanatha-gita / Shasta tradition)
- **Birth of Kaushiki from Parvati's sheath** — From Parvati's bodily sheath emerges the radiant Ambika/Kaushiki, while Parvati's remainder darkens into Kalika (Kalaratri) _(actors: Kaushiki/Ambika, Parvati, Kalika)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 5)
- **Chamunda slays Chanda and Munda** — Kali earns the name Chamunda by slaying the asura generals Chanda and Munda _(actors: Chamunda/Kali, Chanda, Munda)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 7)
- **Charter of Tulsi-Vivaha and the city Jalandhar** — The myth grounds the Tulsi-Vivaha festival, daily Tulsi/shalagrama worship, and the naming of Jalandhar as a Shakti Pitha _(actors: Tulsi (Vrinda), Vishnu, Sati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/jalandhara-vrinda.md) — (Padma Purana; festival and sthala-purana tradition)
- **Dawn of the New Satya Yuga** — With the wicked gone, survivors' minds turn crystal-pure; Satya returns when Moon, Sun and Jupiter enter Pushya together _(actors: Kalki, survivor seed-population)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/README.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 12.2.23-24, Vishnu Purana 4.24)
- **Gajāsura-saṃhāra** — Śiva slays the elephant-demon Gajāsura, son of Mahiṣāsura, and wears his flayed hide, becoming Kṛttivāsas, the hide-clad one _(actors: Śiva, Gajāsura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Liṅga Purāṇa, Kūrma Purāṇa)
- **Mārkaṇḍeya Inhaled into the Cosmic Body** — The child inhales Mārkaṇḍeya, who sees the entire universe within, then is exhaled back onto the cosmic ocean _(actors: Mārkaṇḍeya, Bāla-Mukunda)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/markandeya.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 12.9; Mahabharata Vana Parva)
- **Origin of Ganesha (Vamana telling)** — The elephant-headed son of Parvati is given an origin-account and made lord of obstacles and chief of the ganas _(actors: Parvati, Ganesha)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vamana-purana.md) — (Vamana Purana)
- **Origin of Jagannatha at Puri** — King Indradyumna founds the worship of Jagannatha at Puri from the daru-brahman (log-deity), whose images are left unfinished _(actors: King Indradyumna, Jagannatha)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/skanda-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Skanda Purana (Vaishnava-khanda, Purushottama-kshetra-mahatmya))
- **Pampā weds Śiva as Virūpākṣa (Pampā-kṣetra naming)** — The river-goddess Pampā/Pampāvatī performs tapas on Hemakūṭa hill and wins Śiva as Virūpākṣa; from her the place is named Pampā-kṣetra, whence 'Hampi' — the Śaiva-Śākta layer beneath the Rāmāyaṇa geography _(actors: Pampā/Pampāvatī, Śiva (Virūpākṣa), (Brahmā))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kishkindha-hampi-anegundi.md) — (Pampā-māhātmya; Hampi sthala-purāṇa [regional])
- **Shiva's seed carried to the Sharavana reeds** — Shiva's fiery seed, too potent for any womb, is carried by Agni then Ganga, who deposits it in a thicket of Shara reeds _(actors: Shiva, Agni, Ganga)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva 224-232; Shiva Purana)
- **Slaying of Bhaṇḍāsura and burning of Śūnyaka** — Lalitā looses the Mahā-Kāmeśvarāstra, reducing Bhaṇḍāsura and his City of the Void to ash _(actors: Lalitā, Bhaṇḍāsura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna); Lalitā Sahasranāma)
- **The cat Lomaśa and mouse Palita's expedient alliance** — A wise mouse and a cat caught in a hunter's snare form a temporary alliance against common enemies; the mouse frees the cat at the last instant, teaching expedient alliance with an enemy under necessity _(actors: Palita the mouse, Lomaśa the cat)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/12-shanti-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 12.136-138)
- **Tripura-dahana (burning of the three cities)** — Three asura brothers win three indestructible flying cities; mounting a cosmic chariot built of the gods, Shiva incinerates all three Tripura with a single arrow _(actors: Shiva, Tarakaksha, Vidyunmali, Kamalaksha)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Rudra-samhita, Yuddha-khanda))
- **Union of Shiva and Mohini producing Ayyappa** — In a sequel Shiva asks to see Mohini and, enchanted, their union produces Ayyappa/Hariharaputra (Shasta) _(actors: Shiva, Mohini/Vishnu, Ayyappa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/samudra-manthana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 8.12; South Indian/Shaiva traditions)
- **Yājñavalkya–Gārgī debate at Janaka's court** — At King Janaka's court the philosopher Gārgī Vācaknavī challenges Yājñavalkya in debate over the ground of all being _(actors: Yājñavalkya, Gārgī Vācaknavī, Janaka)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/principal-upanishads-detail.md) — (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.6, 3.8)
- **Ajamila saved by uttering 'Narayana'** — The fallen brahmana Ajamila is saved at death by calling his son's name 'Narayana,' the locus classicus for the power of the divine name _(actors: Ajamila, Viṣṇudutas, Yamadutas)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/bhagavata-purana-skandha-map.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6)
- **Andhaka-vadha** — Andhaka, born of Shiva's sweat, lusts after Parvati; Shiva impales him on the trident while the Matrikas drink his cloning blood, and the chastened Andhaka becomes the gana Bhringi _(actors: Shiva, Andhaka, Matrikas, Parvati)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Rudra-samhita, Yuddha-khanda); cf. Kurma/Linga Purana)
- **Andhakāsura-vadha** — Śiva impales the blind demon Andhaka, who lusted after his unknowing mother Pārvatī; the Mātṛkās drink his cloning blood and he is redeemed as the gaṇa-chief Bhṛṅgi _(actors: Śiva, Andhaka, Pārvatī, the Mātṛkās)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Matsya Purāṇa, Kūrma Purāṇa, Liṅga Purāṇa, Padma Purāṇa, Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Atri Rescues Surya** — The sage Atri rescues the sun Surya in the legend associated with the Atri lineage of Rigvedic seers _(actors: Atri, Surya)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/rigveda.md) — (Rigveda (Mandala 5 / Atri legend))
- **Avatara of Mahodara slaying Mohasura** — Ganesha descends as Mahodara to slay Mohasura, demon of delusion/confusion _(actors: Ganesha (Mahodara), Mohasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/ganesha-and-mudgala-upapuranas.md) — (Mudgala Purana)
- **Bhrigu's curse making Agni all-devouring** — Sage Bhrigu curses Agni to become eater of everything pure and impure; distressed, Agni withdraws until the gods coax him back with the boon that fire purifies whatever it touches _(actors: Bhrigu, Agni, the gods)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/agni.md) — (Mahabharata)
- **Birth of Kartikeya from the reeds** — In the reed-bed the seed becomes six infants suckled by the six Krittikas; when Parvati embraces all six they fuse into the six-faced Shanmukha (Kartikeya) _(actors: Kartikeya, the Krittikas, Parvati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva; Skanda Purana; Shiva Purana)
- **Burning of Kāma by Śiva** — At the Aṅga site Viśvāmitra recounts how Śiva reduced Kāma to ash, making him Ananga, the bodiless _(actors: Śiva, Kāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/1-bala-kanda.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 23)
- **Devapi and Maru Restore Dharma** — Two immortals, Devapi (lunar line) and Maru (solar line), re-emerge from Kalapa to re-found the kshatriya dynasties _(actors: Devapi, Maru)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/README.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 12.2.37-38, Vishnu Purana 4.24)
- **Devi's vow to marry only her conqueror** — When Shumbha's envoy demands she marry him, the Goddess refuses, vowing to wed only one who defeats her in battle, inviting the war _(actors: Devi, Shumbha, Chanda, Munda)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 5)
- **Indra slays Diti's womb-son, creating the Maruts** — To forestall an Indra-killer, Indra slays Diti's gestating son in the womb, and the fragments become the Maruts, the storm-gods and Indra's allies _(actors: Indra, Diti, the Maruts)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/kamadeva-and-minor-deities.md) — (Ramayana; Puranas)
- **Śiva disrupts Dakṣa's sacrifice (Bhaviṣya Parva)** — In the heterogeneous Bhaviṣya Parva, sectarian myth recounts Śiva's disruption of Dakṣa's sacrifice and Hari's combat with him — a Vaiṣṇava retelling among the alternate cosmogonies and hymns _(actors: Śiva, Dakṣa, Hari)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/harivamsha.md) — (Harivaṃśa, Bhaviṣya Parva)
- **Kālāntaka — Śiva conquers Death for Mārkaṇḍeya** — When Yama comes to claim the Śiva-devotee boy Mārkaṇḍeya at his fated death at sixteen, Śiva bursts from the liṅga as Kālāntaka, strikes down Yama, and grants the boy deathless eternal youth _(actors: Śiva, Mārkaṇḍeya, Yama)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa; Skanda Purāṇa; Liṅga Purāṇa; Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa; Ṛgveda 7.59.12)
- **Lalita emerges from yajna fire to slay Bhandasura** — Lalita Tripurasundari emerges from the fire of a yajna and slays the demon Bhandasura, who was born of Kama's ashes, commanding the Sri Chakra battle-array _(actors: Lalita Tripurasundari, Bhandasura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Brahmanda Purana, Lalitopakhyana; Lalita Sahasranama)
- **Manikantha Slays Mahishi and Founds Sabarimala** — The divine infant Manikantha, raised by King Rajashekhara of Pandalam, fetches tiger's milk, defeats Mahishi, reveals his divinity, and asks a shrine be built where his arrow lands at Sabarimala _(actors: Manikantha-Ayyappa, Rajashekhara, Mahishi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Shasta oral tradition)
- **Narasiṃha as fourth Daśāvatāra** — Narasiṃha takes his place as the fourth of the ten classical avatāras within the Satya-yuga descent group, linking forward to Vāmana via Prahlāda's grandson Bali _(actors: Viṣṇu (Narasiṃha), Prahlāda, Bali)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/prahlada-narasimha.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa; standard daśāvatāra lists)
- **Puranas Recited to the Sages at Naimisaranyam** — At Tiru Naimisaranyam, the forest where Suta recites the Puranas to the assembled sages _(actors: Suta, sages)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/108-divya-desams.md) — (Puranas)
- **Revival of Kāmadeva and restoration of the worlds** — From the victorious fire (or at Rati's plea) Kāmadeva is restored to bodily form and reunited with Rati; desire and fertility return, the devas regain their stations, and Lalitā returns to Śrīpura as Rājarājeśvarī _(actors: Lalitā, Kāmadeva, Rati, the devas)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitopākhyāna))
- **Shani's gaze severs Ganesha's head** — In a folk variant, the reluctant Shani is pressed to look at baby Ganesha and his destroying glance severs the child's head, leading to the elephant-head substitution _(actors: Shani, Ganesha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/navagraha.md) — (Puranic/folk tradition)
- **Shared Hindu–Buddhist Siddha membership** — Matsyendra/Lūipa, Gorakṣa, Jālandhara, Kāṇha/Kṛṣṇacārya, Caurangī and Saraha recur in both Nāth and Vajrayāna lists, marking a single trans-sectarian Siddha milieu and the fountainhead of haṭha-yoga, alchemy and Sahaja Tantra _(actors: Lūipa, Saraha, Nāgārjuna (Siddha), Virūpa, Tilopa, Nāropa, Matsyendra, Gorakṣa)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nath-sampradaya-and-the-siddhas.md) — (Caturaśīti-siddha-pravṛtti; Wikipedia (Mahasiddha, Natha Sampradaya))
- **Shiva Frees Supriya and Slays Daruka at Nageshwar** — Shiva frees his devotee Supriya and fellow prisoners held by the demon Daruka in their undersea city, destroying the demon and remaining as Nagesha, lord of serpents, in Darukavana _(actors: Shiva (Nagesha), Supriya, Daruka)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/12-jyotirlingas.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Shiva Rescues Supriya and Slays Daruka at Nageshvara** — Shiva manifests as the Nageshvara jyotirlinga near Dvaraka in connection with rescuing his devotee Supriya and the legend of the demon Daruka _(actors: Shiva, Supriya, Daruka)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dwarka-bet-dwarka-saurashtra.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Shiva slays Andhaka and Andhaka becomes Bhringin** — Shiva in Bhairava form impales the demon Andhaka on the trident; from his blood emerge the Ashta-Bhairavas and the planet Mangala, and the purified Andhaka becomes the gana Bhringin _(actors: Shiva, Andhaka, Chamunda, Ashta-Bhairavas)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vamana-purana.md) — (Vamana Purana; cf. Matsya/Kurma/Linga Purana)
- **Shiva Slays Yama to Save Markandeya (Kalantaka)** — When Yama casts his noose at the boy Markandeya clinging to a Shivalinga, Shiva bursts forth as Kalantaka and slays the god of death, then grants Markandeya eternal youth _(actors: Shiva, Markandeya, Yama)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/yama.md) — (Skanda, Linga, Padma Puranas)
- **Sureshvara tests Upamanyu** — Shiva appears as Indra to test the boy-sage Upamanyu's devotion, then reveals himself and grants boons _(actors: Sureshvara (Shiva), Upamanyu)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **The fowler converted by the self-immolating pigeon** — A bird-catcher caught in a storm is shown selfless hospitality by a captured pigeon and its mate, who immolate themselves for their guest; moved, the fowler renounces violence and takes to asceticism _(actors: a fowler, a pigeon and its mate)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/12-shanti-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 12.143-149)
- **The nectar-strengthened devas win the Amrita-manthana war** — Strengthened by the nectar, the devas with Vishnu defeat the asuras, Indra is restored to Svarga, and Lakshmi's fortune returns _(actors: the devas, Vishnu, Indra, the asuras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/samudra-manthana.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 8.10-8.11; Mahabharata)
- **Tāmraparṇī drips from Agastya's pot on Pothigai** — The pearl-bearing Tāmraparṇī is said to issue from Agastya's water-pot on the Pothigai (Malaya) hill _(actors: Agastya, Tāmraparṇī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sacred-rivers-and-descent-myths.md) — (Tamil sthala-purāṇa)
- **Vishnu's Self-Revelation to Mārkaṇḍeya** — The boy reveals himself as Nārāyaṇa, source and end of all worlds, who reabsorbs and re-emits creation, telling the sage he alone may witness it _(actors: Nārāyaṇa, Mārkaṇḍeya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/markandeya.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva)
- **Avatara of Gajavaktra slaying Lobhasura** — Ganesha descends as Gajanana/Gajavaktra to slay Lobhasura, demon of greed _(actors: Ganesha (Gajavaktra), Lobhasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/ganesha-and-mudgala-upapuranas.md) — (Mudgala Purana)
- **Battle of the Ten Kings (Dasarajna)** — With the seer Vasishtha as his priest, King Sudas defeats a confederation of ten kings in the Dasarajna war on the Parushni river _(actors: Sudas, Vasishtha, ten kings)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/rigveda.md) — (Rigveda 7.18; 7.33; 7.83)
- **Birth of Kārttikeya to slay Tāraka** — Viśvāmitra narrates the cosmic birth of Skanda from Agni and the Kṛttikās to kill the asura Tāraka, alongside the births of Gaṅgā and Umā _(actors: Kārttikeya, Agni, Kṛttikās)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/1-bala-kanda.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 35–37)
- **Birth of the Three-Breasted Minakshi** — Born of a yajna to the childless Pandya king Malayadhvaja, the girl Minakshi has three breasts, with a prophecy that the third will vanish when she meets her destined husband _(actors: Malayadhvaja, Minakshi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Tiruvilaiyadal Puranam)
- **Devi burns Dhumralochana to ash** — The Goddess reduces the general Dhumralochana to ashes with a single humkara, and her lion mauls his army _(actors: Devi, Dhumralochana)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 6)
- **Hanuman shields devotees from Shani** — In folk tradition Hanuman frees or defeats Shani, who then grants that devotees of Hanuman are spared his worst affliction _(actors: Hanuman, Shani)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/navagraha.md) — (Folk tradition)
- **Jalandhara-vadha and the Vrinda deception** — Vishnu disguised as Jalandhara tricks the chaste Vrinda into breaking her vow, destroying the asura's invincibility; Shiva slays Jalandhara, and Vrinda immolates herself to be reborn as Tulasi _(actors: Jalandhara, Vishnu, Vrinda, Shiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Rudra-samhita, Yuddha-khanda))
- **Jālandhara-vadha & the Vṛndā deception** — Viṣṇu tricks the chaste Vṛndā into breaking her vow so Śiva can slay her invincible ocean-born husband Jālandhara with a toe-spun discus; Vṛndā curses Viṣṇu and immolates herself _(actors: Śiva, Jālandhara, Vṛndā, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa Rudra-saṃhitā Yuddha-khaṇḍa, Padma Purāṇa)
- **Kripa counted among the eight Chiranjivis and future Saptarshi** — Kripa is named in the chiranjivi-smarana verse as a deathless one and is held to become a Saptarshi of the future Savarni Manvantara _(actors: Kripa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/kripa.md) — (Chiranjivi tradition (cirañjivi-smarana stotra); Puranas)
- **Revelation of the Lalitā Sahasranāma and Śrī Vidyā charter** — The eight Vāg-devīs reveal the thousand names encoding the myth; the Lalitopākhyāna and Tripurā-rahasya become the foundation legend and inner allegory of the Śrī Vidyā Śrīkula cult _(actors: Lalitā, the eight Vāg-devīs, Hayagrīva, Agastya)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/lalita-bhandasura.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa (Lalitā Sahasranāma); Tripurā-rahasya)
- **Shatakshi/Shakambhari slays Durgama** — When the asura Durgama seizes the Vedas and causes a hundred-year drought, the Goddess manifests as the hundred-eyed Shatakshi and herb-bearing Shakambhari, nourishes the world, and destroys Durgama _(actors: Shatakshi/Shakambhari, Durgama)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vamana-purana.md) — (Vamana Purana; cf. Devi-Bhagavata)
- **Shiva reclaims Kashi from King Divodasa** — Through the 64 Yoginis and Ganesha, Shiva reclaims the city of Varanasi from King Divodasa, establishing Kashi as the supreme moksha-kshetra _(actors: Shiva, King Divodasa, 64 Yoginis, Ganesha)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/skanda-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Skanda Purana (Kashi-khanda))
- **Shiva Restores Yama to Life** — The gods plead and Shiva revives the slain Yama so the cosmic order of death can continue, earning Shiva the epithet Mrityunjaya _(actors: Shiva, Yama, the gods)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/yama.md) — (Skanda, Linga, Padma Puranas)
- **Shiva's Closing Boons to Mārkaṇḍeya** — After the vision vanishes, Shiva and Pārvatī appear and grant Mārkaṇḍeya undying fame, freedom from age and death, and mastery of the Purāṇas _(actors: Shiva, Pārvatī, Mārkaṇḍeya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/markandeya.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 12.10)
- **Skanda made general of the gods** — Even as a boy Skanda is anointed Devasenapati, commander of the celestial army, by Indra and armed by Shiva with the Vel _(actors: Skanda, Indra, Shiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva; Skanda Purana)
- **Spilled amrita founds the Kumbh Mela sites** — In popular tradition drops of amrita fall at Prayag, Haridwar, Ujjain, and Nashik during the contest over the pot, the origin legend of the Kumbh Mela _(actors: devas, asuras)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/samudra-manthana.md) — (Puranic/folk tradition)
- **Svaha unites with Agni (Karttikeya conception)** — Svaha, in love with Agni, takes the forms of six of the seven saptarshi wives to unite with him, a strand woven into the birth of the war-god Karttikeya _(actors: Svaha, Agni)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/agni.md) — (Skanda/Puranic Karttikeya-birth strands)
- **The Rāmopākhyāna retelling** — Mārkaṇḍeya narrates the compressed Daṇḍaka sequence to Yudhiṣṭhira in the Pāṇḍavas' Dvāpara exile-forest _(actors: Mārkaṇḍeya, Yudhiṣṭhira)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dandakaranya-rama-forest.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana-parva 262–263)
- **Uma-Haimavati reveals Brahman to the gods** — Uma-Haimavati appears to the gods in the Kena Upanishad to reveal Brahman, the earliest named appearance of the Goddess as teacher _(actors: Uma-Haimavati, the gods)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Kena Upanishad 3-4)
- **Viṣṇu slays Hayagrīva and seizes the Sudarśana disc** — In a Rāmāyaṇa-variant origin, Viṣṇu seizes the Sudarśana discus after slaying the dānava Hayagrīva atop Mt. Cakravāna _(actors: Viṣṇu, Hayagrīva)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/divyastras-and-divine-weapons.md) — (Sudarshana Chakra (Wikipedia); Rāmāyaṇa)
- **Vṛtrasura battles Indra** — The demon-devotee Vṛtra battles Indra; the Narayana-kavaca mantra-armor figures in the episode _(actors: Vṛtra, Indra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/bhagavata-purana-skandha-map.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6)
- **Vyāsa instructs his son Śuka, who attains liberation** — The renunciate Śuka, having mastered the Vedas, questions Vyāsa on the highest good; Vyāsa teaches the path to Brahman and Śuka attains liberation, merging into the elements _(actors: Vyāsa, Śuka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/12-shanti-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 12.224-250)
- **Annapurna feeds Shiva proving food is real** — Parvati as Annapurna, goddess of nourishment, feeds Shiva at Kashi to prove that food is real after he declared the world illusory _(actors: Annapurna/Parvati, Shiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Annapurna Upanishad tradition; Kashi sthala-purana)
- **Avatara of Lambodara slaying Krodhasura** — Ganesha descends as Lambodara to slay Krodhasura, demon of anger/wrath _(actors: Ganesha (Lambodara), Krodhasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/ganesha-and-mudgala-upapuranas.md) — (Mudgala Purana)
- **Bhairava's Kāpālika penance and the skull's fall at Kāśī** — Bhairava wanders as a naked mendicant begging with the skull-bowl until the brahmahatyā sin is expiated and the skull finally drops at Kapāla-mocana in Kāśī, making Vārāṇasī the supreme place of liberation _(actors: Bhairava)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa Śatarudra-saṃhitā; Skanda Purāṇa; Matsya Purāṇa)
- **Citraketu cursed by Parvati to become Vṛtra** — King Citraketu is cursed by Parvati to be born as the demon Vṛtra, linking the two skandha-6 episodes _(actors: Citraketu, Parvati)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/bhagavata-purana-skandha-map.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6)
- **Descent of the Narmada (Reva)** — The river Narmada (Reva) originates from Shiva's tapas on Mt. Maikala, gaining such power that the mere sight of her equals bathing in the Ganga _(actors: Shiva, Narmada)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/skanda-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Skanda Purana (Avantya-khanda, Reva-khanda))
- **Śiva gifts the Sudarśana Cakra to Viṣṇu** — In the Śaiva variant, Viṣṇu worships Śiva with a thousand lotuses, offers his own eye for the flower Śiva hid, and Śiva gifts him the discus — hence 'Sudarśana.' _(actors: Viṣṇu, Śiva)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/divyastras-and-divine-weapons.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Koṭirudra-saṃhitā)
- **Kali springs from the brow and is named Chamunda** — Facing Chanda and Munda, Kali leaps from the Goddess's brow, devours the asura army, beheads the two generals, and is named Chamunda _(actors: Kali, Chandika, Chanda, Munda)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 7)
- **Markandeya's vision of pralaya** — The deathless sage survives the cosmic dissolution and beholds Vishnu as a divine infant on a banyan leaf, entering the child to see the universe within _(actors: Markandeya, the child Vishnu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/03-vana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva 186-188)
- **Marriage of Minakshi and Sundareshvara** — The warrior-queen Minakshi storms Kailasa; facing Shiva her third breast vanishes, recognizing her lord, and Shiva comes to Madurai as Sundareshvara to wed her _(actors: Minakshi, Shiva-Sundareshvara)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Tiruvilaiyadal Puranam)
- **Shiva and Vishnu jointly slay Jalodbhava** — The water-born demon Jalodbhava is destroyed by Shiva and Vishnu together; in the episode Shiva gifts the Sudarshana-cakra to Vishnu and sacred rivers are materialized _(actors: Shiva, Vishnu, Jalodbhava)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vamana-purana.md) — (Vamana Purana)
- **Shiva Revives Ghushma's Son at Grishneshwar** — The devotee Ghushma keeps faith after her co-wife murders her son and throws him in the tank where she immerses her daily lingas, and Shiva revives the boy and manifests as Grishneshwar, the last jyotirlinga _(actors: Ghushma (Kusuma), Shiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/12-jyotirlingas.md) — (Shiva Purana)
- **Skanda slays Tarakasura** — Skanda leads the devas and slays Taraka with the Vel, fulfilling his birth-purpose and ending the asura's terror of the three worlds _(actors: Skanda, Tarakasura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Skanda Purana; Matsya Purana)
- **Slaying of Shankhachuda** — Shankhachuda, protected by his wife Tulasi's chastity, loses his power when Vishnu in disguise breaks her vow, and is then slain by Shiva's trident _(actors: Shankhachuda, Tulasi, Vishnu, Shiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Rudra-samhita, Yuddha-khanda))
- **Sulabhā enters King Janaka's mind and out-argues him** — The female ascetic Sulabhā, doubting Janaka's claimed liberation-while-ruling, enters his mind by yogic power and out-argues him in a famous debate on whether one truly free can remain a king _(actors: Sulabhā, King Janaka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/12-shanti-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 12.308)
- **Surya's Marriage (Wedding Hymn)** — The wedding of Surya (the sun-maiden) is narrated in the archetypal Vedic marriage hymn, the model for human wedding rites _(actors: Surya, Soma)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/rigveda.md) — (Rigveda 10.85)
- **Ajamila Saved by the Name** — The fallen brahmana Ajamila is saved at death by calling his son 'Narayana,' demonstrating the glory of the divine Name in the Vishnuduta-Yamaduta debate _(actors: Ajamila)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavata-tales-catalog.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6.1-3)
- **Avatara of Vikata slaying Kamasura** — Ganesha descends as the peacock-mounted Vikata to slay Kamasura, demon of lust/desire _(actors: Ganesha (Vikata), Kamasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/ganesha-and-mudgala-upapuranas.md) — (Mudgala Purana)
- **Durgā Slays Mahiṣāsura** — The buffalo-demon Mahiṣa, unkillable by god or man, conquers heaven until the gods pool their tejas into Durgā, who beheads him mid-shapeshift with her triśūla, earning the name Mahiṣāsuramardinī _(actors: Durgā, Mahiṣāsura)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/asura-wars-and-roster.md) — (Devī Māhātmya 2-4; Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa)
- **Gajāsura-saṃhāra — slaying the elephant-demon** — Śiva impales the elephant-demon Gajāsura on his trident, dances upon him, and flays his hide to wear as a garment, becoming Kṛttivāsas, the hide-clad one _(actors: Śiva, Gajāsura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa; Liṅga Purāṇa; Kūrma Purāṇa)
- **Markandeya and Kalantaka (conquest of death)** — The boy-sage Markandeya, fated to die at sixteen, embraces the linga; when Yama's noose snares it, Shiva bursts forth as Kalantaka, strikes down Yama, and grants Markandeya deathless youth _(actors: Markandeya, Yama, Shiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Shatarudra/Koti material); cf. Rigveda 7.59.12)
- **Murugan splits Surapadman** — In the Tamil cycle Murugan hurls the Vel and splits Surapadman in two; the halves become his peacock vahana and rooster banner _(actors: Murugan, Surapadman)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Kanda Puranam)
- **Shiva's 64 Sacred Games (Tiruvilaiyadal) at Madurai** — Shiva performs sixty-four lilas across Madurai (carrying sand for pittu, the poetry contest with Nakkirar), making the whole city the kshetra of his sacred sports _(actors: Shiva, Nakkirar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Tiruvilaiyadal Puranam)
- **Soma cursed by Daksha and restored at Prabhasa** — The Moon-god Soma, cursed by his father-in-law Daksha to wane, regains his light by worshiping Shiva at Prabhasa, the etiology of the Somnath ('Lord of Soma') jyotirlinga _(actors: Soma, Daksha, Shiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/skanda-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Skanda Purana (Prabhasa-khanda))
- **The Dharma-vyādha (righteous butcher) teaches the proud Brahmin** — The righteous butcher of Mithilā teaches a proud Brahmin that dharma lies in doing one's own duty without attachment, even in a low trade _(actors: Dharma-vyādha, a Brahmin)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/12-shanti-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 12.261-264)
- **The eclipse vendetta of Rahu and Ketu** — In revenge for exposing him, Rahu and Ketu periodically swallow Surya and Chandra, who pass through the severed throat and reappear, causing eclipses _(actors: Rahu, Ketu, Surya, Chandra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/navagraha.md) — (Puranas)
- **The Seven Mothers emerge** — The shaktis of the major gods burst forth as the Saptamatrika, each a feminine mirror of her god with his vahana and weapons _(actors: Brahmani, Maheshvari, Kaumari, Vaishnavi, Varahi, Narasimhi, Aindri)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 8)
- **Yaksheshvara humbles the proud devas** — After the devas grow proud of defeating the asuras, the mysterious Yaksha-form of Shiva humbles them by setting a blade of grass none can burn or cut _(actors: Yaksheshvara (Shiva), the devas)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Shiva Purana; cf. Kena Upanishad)
- **Ajamila Saved by the Name (Yamaduta encounter)** — The Yamadutas are driven off by Vishnu's Vishnudutas when the dying sinner Ajamila cries 'Narayana,' establishing that devotion overrides Yama's jurisdiction _(actors: Ajamila, Yamadutas, Vishnudutas)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/yama.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6.1-3)
- **Andhakāsura-vadha — slaying the blind demon** — Śiva impales the lustful demon Andhaka on his trident and holds him aloft until his pride burns away, while the Mātṛkās drink his cloning blood; the chastened Andhaka becomes the gaṇa-leader Bhṛṅgi _(actors: Śiva, Andhaka, the Mātṛkās)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Matsya Purāṇa; Kūrma Purāṇa; Liṅga Purāṇa; Padma Purāṇa; Śiva Purāṇa)
- **Avatara of Vighnaraja slaying Mamasura** — Ganesha descends as Vighnaraja, mounted on the serpent Shesha, to slay Mamasura/Mamatasura, demon of attachment/possessiveness _(actors: Ganesha (Vighnaraja), Mamasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/ganesha-and-mudgala-upapuranas.md) — (Mudgala Purana)
- **Birth of Skanda to slay Taraka** — Agni and the Krittikas rear the war-god Skanda, born to slay Taraka, who becomes commander of the gods' army _(actors: Skanda, Agni, the Krittikas, Taraka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/03-vana-parva.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva 213-221)
- **Hanuman identified as a Rudra-amsha** — The Shatarudra-samhita enumerates Shiva's manifestations and identifies Hanuman as a partial incarnation (amsha) of Rudra _(actors: Shiva, Hanuman)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Shatarudra-samhita))
- **Kali drinks Raktabija's blood** — As Chandika strikes the cloning demon Raktabija, Kali drinks every falling drop and devours the spawned clones before they touch ground, killing him bloodless _(actors: Kali, Chandika, Raktabija)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 8)
- **King Sukeshi and the flying city** — The Shiva-devoted rakshasa king Sukeshi is granted invincibility and a flying city, and teaches dharma so that the demons become virtuous _(actors: Sukeshi, Vidyutkeshi, Shiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vamana-purana.md) — (Vamana Purana)
- **Kālī Slays Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa (Cāmuṇḍā)** — The generals Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa of Śumbha-Niśumbha are slain by Kālī, who springs from Durgā's brow and is renamed Cāmuṇḍā _(actors: Kālī/Cāmuṇḍā, Caṇḍa, Muṇḍa)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/asura-wars-and-roster.md) — (Devī Māhātmya 7)
- **Mankanaka's dance and Shiva's humbling** — Vegetable sap flows from the sage's wound and he dances the world into dancing, until Shiva humbles his pride by showing ash from his own thumb _(actors: Mankanaka, Shiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata-minor-episodes.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva, Tirtha-yatra)
- **Marriage of Kartikeya to Devasena** — Kartikeya weds Devasena, given to him by Indra as his army-bride at Tiruparankunram after the demon-war _(actors: Kartikeya, Devasena, Indra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Skanda Purana; Kanda Puranam)
- **Mothers emanated to fight Raktabija** — The Sapta/Ashta Matrikas, shaktis of the male gods, are emanated to fight the demon Raktabija _(actors: The Matrikas, Raktabija)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 8)
- **Shiva and Parvati Follow Kartikeya to Srisailam** — After the fruit-quarrel with Ganesha, Kartikeya sulks off to Srisailam, and his parents follow to settle on the hill as Mallikarjuna and Bhramaramba _(actors: Kartikeya, Shiva, Parvati)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Skanda Purana, Shrishaila-khanda)
- **Tulādhāra teaches the penance-proud Jājali** — A heavenly voice declares the merchant Tulādhāra greater in righteousness than the austerity-proud Jājali; Tulādhāra teaches that true dharma is universal harmlessness and honest livelihood, not ritual _(actors: Tulādhāra, Jājali)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/12-shanti-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 12.253-256)
- **Viśvāmitra reveals the Gāyatrī mantra** — As Ṛgvedic seer of Maṇḍala 3, Viśvāmitra is the draṣṭṛ of RV 3.62.10, the Sāvitrī/Gāyatrī, the most sacred Vedic verse _(actors: Viśvāmitra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vishwamitra-vasishtha.md) — (Ṛgveda 3.62.10)
- **Avatara of Dhumravarna slaying Abhimanasura** — Ganesha descends as the blue-horse-mounted Dhumravarna to slay Abhimanasura/Ahankarasura, demon of pride/egotism _(actors: Ganesha (Dhumravarna), Abhimanasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/ganesha-and-mudgala-upapuranas.md) — (Mudgala Purana)
- **Bhramaramba Slays the Bee-Demon Arunasura** — The Goddess takes the form Bhramari and emits swarms of six-legged bees to destroy the demon Arunasura, who held a boon against death by any two- or four-legged being _(actors: Bhramaramba/Devi, Arunasura)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Srisailam sthala-purana)
- **Death of Nishumbha** — Chandika duels and fells Nishumbha, decapitating the being that tries to emerge from his body _(actors: Chandika, Nishumbha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 9)
- **Gajendra-moksa** — The elephant-king Gajendra, seized by a crocodile, is liberated when he surrenders to Viṣṇu, a touchstone of saranagati _(actors: Gajendra, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/bhagavata-purana-skandha-map.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 8)
- **Indra splits Diti's foetus into the Maruts** — Indra, to abort the asura who would kill him, splits the sleeping Diti's foetus into the forty-nine Maruts _(actors: Indra, Diti)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/1-bala-kanda.md) — (Vālmīki Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla Kāṇḍa 46–47)
- **Jālandhara-vadha and the Vṛndā deception** — To defeat the invincible ocean-born Jālandhara, Viṣṇu impersonates him and breaks the chastity of his wife Vṛndā; Śiva then slays Jālandhara with a discus, and the betrayed Vṛndā curses Viṣṇu and immolates herself, reborn as Tulasī _(actors: Śiva, Jālandhara, Viṣṇu, Vṛndā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa Rudra-saṃhitā Yuddha-khaṇḍa; Padma Purāṇa)
- **Kalantaka conquers Death for Markandeya** — Shiva conquers Death by kicking down Yama to save the boy-devotee Markandeya, the root of the Mrityunjaya conqueror-of-death form _(actors: Kalantaka (Shiva), Yama, Markandeya)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Skanda Purana)
- **Murugan woos and weds Valli** — Murugan woos Valli, daughter of a Tamil hunter chieftain, in disguise (aided by Ganesha as an elephant) and weds her as his love-bride _(actors: Murugan, Valli, Ganesha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Kanda Puranam; Tamil tradition)
- **Raktabīja Slain, Blood Drunk by Kālī** — The asura general Raktabīja, whose every spilled blood-drop spawns a clone, is ended when Kālī drinks his blood mid-air and the Mātṛkās devour the duplicates _(actors: Raktabīja, Kālī, Mātṛkās)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/asura-wars-and-roster.md) — (Devī Māhātmya 8)
- **The worm reborn upward to godhood** — A worm taught by a sage to meditate on the supreme is reborn upward through the orders of being to godhood, a parable of devotion and the soul's ascent _(actors: a worm, a sage)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata/12-shanti-parva.md) — (Mahabharata 12.286-287)
- **Viśvāmitra parts the rivers for Sudās** — As purohita of King Sudās, Viśvāmitra persuades the rivers to lower their waters so the Bharata army can cross _(actors: Viśvāmitra, the rivers Vipāś and Śutudrī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vishwamitra-vasishtha.md) — (Ṛgveda 3.33)
- **Citraketu Cursed to be Born Vritra** — The grieving king Citraketu becomes a vidyadhara, mocks Shiva-Parvati, and is cursed by Parvati to be born as the demon Vritra _(actors: Citraketu, Parvati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavata-tales-catalog.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6.14-17)
- **Devi reabsorbs all goddesses into herself** — When Shumbha taunts that she fights with others' help, the Devi declares she alone exists and reabsorbs the Matrikas and Kali into her single self _(actors: Devi, the Matrikas, Kali, Shumbha)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 10)
- **Goddess Slays Niśumbha and Śumbha** — The asura brothers Śumbha and Niśumbha, who seized the three worlds, are slain in sequence by the Goddess (Kauśikī/Ambikā), who reabsorbs Kālī for the final duel, ending the war _(actors: Śumbha, Niśumbha, Goddess (Kauśikī))_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/asura-wars-and-roster.md) — (Devī Māhātmya 9-10)
- **Markandeya survives the cosmic deluge** — The deathless sage adrift on the dissolution-ocean enters and exits the mouth of the child Vishnu on the banyan leaf, beholding cyclic time _(actors: Markandeya, the child Vishnu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata-minor-episodes.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva, Markandeya-Samasya Parva)
- **Parvati manifests as Annapurna at Kashi** — When Shiva declares the world maya, Parvati withdraws and food vanishes from the cosmos; she manifests as Annapurna at Kashi, feeding even Shiva, establishing the Goddess as sustaining material reality _(actors: Parvati (Annapurna), Shiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/sati-daksha-and-parvati.md) — (Annapurna Upanishad / Sthala-purana)
- **Skanda teaches Pranava to Shiva at Swamimalai** — The child Skanda imprisons Brahma for failing to explain Om, briefly takes over creation, and expounds the syllable to Shiva himself, becoming Swaminatha _(actors: Skanda, Brahma, Shiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Kanda Puranam; Skanda Purana)
- **Tripurantaka destroys the three cities** — Shiva, with a single arrow from his bow Pinaka, destroys the three flying cities (Tripura) of the asuras _(actors: Tripurantaka (Shiva), the Tripura asuras)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Shiva Purana Rudra Samhita; Mahabharata Karna Parva)
- **Two clocks for the sectarian Upaniṣads** — Academically a late CE layer post-dating Pāñcarātra/Pāśupata/Śrīvidyā; traditionally eternal apauruṣeya Śruti fixed at the ~3102 BCE Kali junction _(actors: ṛṣis (traditional); modern text-critics (academic))_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/vaishnava-shaiva-shakta-upanishads-overview.md) — (Muktikā Upaniṣad; Adyar editions; scholarly dating)
- **Death of Shumbha** — The Goddess pierces Shumbha through the heart with her trident; with the demon-kings gone the gods regain heaven and sing the Narayani Stuti _(actors: Devi, Shumbha, the gods)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 10-11)
- **Diti's Vow and the Birth of the Maruts** — Diti performs penance to bear an Indra-killer, but Indra enters her womb and cuts the embryo into the forty-nine Maruts _(actors: Diti, Indra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavata-tales-catalog.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 6.18-19)
- **Gajasuravadha — Shiva slays the elephant-demon** — Shiva slays and dances within the flayed hide of the elephant-demon Gajasura, taking the elephant-skin-clad terror form _(actors: Shiva, Gajasura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Kurma Purana)
- **Kartikeya departs south after the race** — Losing the fruit-race to Ganesha, the proud Kartikeya abandons Kailasa and settles in the Tamil south as Murugan _(actors: Kartikeya)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ganesha-and-kartikeya.md) — (Skanda Purana; Tamil tradition)
- **Andhakasura-vadha — Shiva impales Andhaka** — Shiva impales the blind asura Andhaka on his trident, drinking each drop of his blood to prevent new demons, finally converting him into the gana Bhringi _(actors: Shiva, Andhaka)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Matsya/Kurma/Linga Puranas)
- **Ascent of Kundalini and union of Shakti with Shiva** — The roused Kundalini-Shakti uncoils, rises through sushumna piercing each chakra, and reunites with Shiva at the crown, producing the nectar of liberation _(actors: Kundalini-Shakti, Shiva)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/chakras-and-kundalini.md) — (Hatha Yoga Pradipika; Shat-chakra-nirupana)
- **Birth of Skanda and slaying of Taraka** — Skanda is born from Agni and the Krittikas, becomes generalissimo of the divine host, and slays the demon Taraka _(actors: Skanda, Agni, the Krittikas, Taraka)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata-minor-episodes.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva, Markandeya-Samasya Parva)
- **Brahma Distills the Natya Shastra from the Four Vedas** — Brahma creates the dramatic/aesthetic science (Gandharvaveda/Natya Shastra) as a 'fifth Veda' by drawing recitation from Rig, song from Sama, gesture from Yajur, and rasa from Atharva _(actors: Brahma, Bharata Muni)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upavedas-vedangas-darshanas.md) — (Natya Shastra 1.1-1.5)
- **Devi foretells future incarnations** — In the Narayani Stuti the Devi promises to descend again, listing future forms including Shakambhari, who feeds the world in a drought, and Bhramari, who slays Aruna _(actors: Devi)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 11)
- **Dharmavyadha teaches Kaushika (Vyadha-gita)** — The righteous fowler Dharmavyadha instructs the brahmin Kaushika that ahimsa is the highest dharma, a renunciant ideal _(actors: Dharmavyadha, Kaushika)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/ahimsa-and-hindu-ethics.md) — (Mahabharata, Vana Parva 3.198.69)
- **Forging of Indra's Vajra from Dadhīci's bones** — The sage Dadhīci gives up his body so the gods can forge the Vajra thunderbolt from his spine/bones — the one weapon able to slay the asura Vṛtra _(actors: Dadhīci, Indra, the Devas)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/divyastras-and-divine-weapons.md) — (Ayudhapurusha (Wikipedia))
- **Mahakaleshvara linga emerges (slaying of Dushana)** — Shiva bursts forth at Ujjain to destroy the demon Dushana threatening a devotee-boy, remaining as the south-facing svayambhu Mahakaleshvara jyotirlinga _(actors: Shiva, Dushana)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/shiva-purana.md) — (Shiva Purana (Kotirudra-samhita))
- **Vaishnavi Beheads Bhairavnath at Trikuta** — The divine girl Vaishnavi, pursued by the lustful tantric Bhairavnath at Shridhar's feast, flees to the Trikuta cave and beheads him at the cave-mouth _(actors: Vaishnavi, Bhairavnath, Shridhar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Vaishno Devi folk legend)
- **Devi grants Suratha and Samadhi their boons** — After three years of worship the Goddess grants Suratha rebirth as the future Manu Savarni and Samadhi spiritual liberation, closing the frame _(actors: Devi, Suratha, Samadhi)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-mahatmya-and-the-great-goddess.md) — (Devi Mahatmya 13)
- **Jalandharavadha — Shiva slays Jalandhara** — Shiva slays the asura Jalandhara, born of his own brow-fire, by cutting him with a chakra drawn from his toe _(actors: Shiva, Jalandhara)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Shiva Purana Rudra Samhita Yuddha Khanda)
- **Naciketa's journey to Yama-loka** — The boy Naciketa, sent to the abode of death by his angry father Uddalaka, traverses Yama's world, sees the rewards of dharma and punishments of sin, and returns to describe it _(actors: Naciketa, Uddalaka, Yama)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/varaha-purana.md) — (Varaha Purana (Dharma-samhita); cf. Katha Upanishad)
- **The Boon to Bhairavnath** — Dying repentant, Bhairavnath is forgiven and granted that no Vaishno Devi pilgrimage is complete until the devotee also visits his shrine above the cave _(actors: Vaishnavi, Bhairavnath)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Vaishno Devi folk legend)
- **Transmission of Ayurveda from Brahma to Sages** — The science of medicine descends through a divine chain — Brahma to Daksha Prajapati to the Ashvins to Indra to Dhanvantari/Bharadvaja and finally to human sages — founding Ayurveda _(actors: Brahma, Daksha, Ashvins, Indra, Dhanvantari, Bharadvaja)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upavedas-vedangas-darshanas.md) — (Charaka Samhita; Sushruta Samhita)
- **Bhikshatana seduces the Daruka-vana sages' wives** — As a naked beautiful mendicant wandering in penance for severing Brahma's head, Shiva seduces the wives of the Daruka forest sages _(actors: Shiva (Bhikshatana), the Daruka-vana sages)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Shaiva Agamas; Suta Samhita)
- **Śiva burns the three cities of Tripurāsura with the Pināka** — Śiva uses his bow Pināka (and in some tellings the Vijaya bow made by Viśvakarmā) to destroy the three flying cities of Tripurāsura, earning the epithet Pinākī _(actors: Śiva, Tripurāsura)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/divyastras-and-divine-weapons.md) — (Pinaka (Wikipedia); Vijaya (bow))
- **Kanya Kumari's Sabotaged Wedding to Shiva** — To keep the Goddess virgin so she can kill Banasura, the gods sabotage her dawn wedding to Shiva (Narada crows like a cock), so she remains kumari forever _(actors: Devi-Kumari, Shiva, Narada)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Bhagavata & Skanda references)
- **Matrikas born from Vaishnavi's distracted mind** — In the Varaha Purana's unusual account, the eight Mother-Goddesses arise from the goddess Vaishnavi losing concentration during austerity, with Yamuna as seventh and Yogeshvari as eighth _(actors: Vaishnavi, Matrikas, Yogeshvari)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/varaha-purana.md) — (Varaha Purana; cf. Devi-Mahatmya/Markandeya P.)
- **Nahusha's Fall** — Becoming interim Indra, Nahusha grows arrogant, kicks Agastya while demanding the saptarshis carry his palanquin, and is cursed to fall as a python, later liberated by Yudhishthira _(actors: Nahusha, Agastya, Yudhishthira)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/suryavamsha-and-chandravamsha.md) — (Mahabharata Vana/Udyoga Parva; Bhagavata VI/IX)
- **Shiva Reveals Rama's Secret to Parvati** — As the Adhyatma's outer frame, Shiva — supreme jnanin and devotee — discloses the esoteric inner (Brahman) meaning of Rama's life to Parvati in an Uma-Maheshvara dialogue _(actors: Shiva, Parvati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/adhyatma-ramayana.md) — (Adhyatma Ramayana, opening)
- **Durga slays Mahishasura (Varaha telling)** — The Varaha Purana carries the Goddess's destruction of the buffalo-demon Mahishasura and allied demons _(actors: Durga, Mahishasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/varaha-purana.md) — (Varaha Purana)
- **Kapila Reborn as Avatara of Vishnu (Samkhya Founder)** — The mythic sage Kapila, said to be an avatara of Vishnu, founds the Samkhya enumeration of Purusha and Prakriti, establishing the dualist darshana _(actors: Kapila)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upavedas-vedangas-darshanas.md) — (Bhagavata Purana; Samkhya-karika)
- **Kumari Slays Banasura** — The Goddess incarnates as the virgin Kumari and slays the demon Banasura, who held a boon that only a virgin girl could kill him, with her cakra _(actors: Devi-Kumari, Banasura)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Bhagavata & Skanda references)
- **Sudarśana cuts Satī's corpse into 51 pieces** — Viṣṇu's Sudarśana Cakra cuts the corpse of Satī into pieces that fall to earth as the 51 Śākta pīṭhas _(actors: Viṣṇu, Satī, Śiva)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/divyastras-and-divine-weapons.md) — (Sudarshana Chakra (Wikipedia))
- **Durgā receives her lion mount from Himavān** — The Himālaya (Himavān) gives Durgā her lion/tiger mount, which the warrior-goddess rides into battle against Mahiṣāsura _(actors: Durgā, Himavān, Mahiṣāsura)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/vahanas-and-celestial-vehicles.md) — (Devī Māhātmya; Hindu-Blog)
- **Priyavrata enlightened by Narada** — The king Priyavrata gains past-life wisdom and heaven through bhakti after instruction by Narada _(actors: Priyavrata, Narada)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/varaha-purana.md) — (Varaha Purana)
- **Pārvatī gives the Vel spear to Kārttikeya** — Pārvatī gifts the divine spear Vel to Kārttikeya/Murugan, the emblem with which he slays Tāraka/Sūrapadma _(actors: Pārvatī, Kārttikeya)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/divyastras-and-divine-weapons.md) — (Astra (weapon) (Wikipedia))
- **Rudrākṣa born of Śiva's tears** — The Rudrākṣa seeds are born of Śiva's tears of compassion (over Tripura / over the world), strung into mālās for japa _(actors: Śiva)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sacred-symbols-and-emblems.md) — (Japamala, Wikipedia; Rudraksha-Ratna)
- **Yama Dvitiya / Bhai Duj Charter** — Yama visits his sister Yamuna who honors him with a feast, and he grants that brothers who receive their sister's tilaka or bathe in the Yamuna escape untimely death _(actors: Yama, Yamuna)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/yamuna-and-river-goddesses.md) — (Bhavishya & Skanda Puranas)
- **Ashvashira's liberation** — The king Ashvashira learns Narayana's omnipresence and that detached performance of duty (nishkama-karma) leads to liberation _(actors: Ashvashira)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/varaha-purana.md) — (Varaha Purana)
- **Devi Mutes and Slays Mukasura at Kollur** — The Goddess robs a tapas-performing demon of speech, making him Muka; when the mute demon harasses sage Kola, she slays him and stays on as Mukambika at Kola's request _(actors: Devi-Mukambika, Mukasura, Kola Maharshi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Kollur sthala-purana)
- **Pippalada Answers the Six Questions** — The sage Pippalada is approached by six pupils who put six questions to him on creation, prana, Om, sleep, and the sixteen kalas of the Purusha, delivering the Prashna Upanishad's teaching _(actors: Pippalada, six pupils)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/atharvaveda.md) — (Prashna Upanishad)
- **The pigeon's self-sacrifice for the hunter** — A male pigeon immolates himself in his own fire to feast the hunter who had snared his mate, the supreme tale of guest-hospitality _(actors: a pigeon, a fowler)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata-minor-episodes.md) — (Mahabharata Santi Parva, Apad-dharma)
- **Vṛndā curses Viṣṇu to become the Śālagrāma stone** — After Viṣṇu breaks Vṛndā's chastity by impersonating her asura husband Jālandhara so Śiva can slay him, the heartbroken Vṛndā curses Viṣṇu to become stone (the Śālagrāma) and immolates herself, rising as the Tulsī plant _(actors: Vṛndā, Viṣṇu, Jālandhara, Śiva)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sacred-flora-and-fauna.md) — (Padma/Skanda/Śiva Purāṇas; Tulasi in Hinduism (Wikipedia))
- **Establishment of the manvantara administration** — At each manvantara turnover Brahma renews the cosmic administration — a new Manu, Indra, Devas, Saptarishis, Manu's sons, and a manvantara-avatara of Vishnu _(actors: Brahma, Manu, Indra, Saptarishis, Vishnu)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/manvantara-system.md) — (Vishnu Purana 3.1-2, Bhagavata Purana 8.1)
- **Gautama kills the crane-king Rajadharman** — The ungrateful brahmin kills and eats his benefactor the crane-king and is destroyed, a fable on ingratitude _(actors: Gautama, Rajadharman)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/mahabharata-minor-episodes.md) — (Mahabharata Santi Parva, Apad-dharma)
- **Markandeya's pralaya vision of Bala-Mukunda** — Granted to behold Vishnu's maya, the deathless Markandeya survives the cosmic deluge, sees the infant Vishnu on a banyan leaf, is inhaled into him to witness the whole universe, and exhaled back _(actors: Markandeya, Vishnu (Bala-Mukunda/Narayana))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/markandeya.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 12.8-12.10; Mahabharata Vana Parva)
- **Sarasvati River Dries Up at Vinashana** — The mighty Vedic river Sarasvati dries up and sinks underground at Vinashana, surviving as the hidden third stream at Prayag _(actors: Sarasvati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/yamuna-and-river-goddesses.md) — (Mahabharata; Matsya Purana)
- **Tulasī becomes the Gaṇḍakī river and Tulsī plant** — In the Śaṅkhacūḍa variant, Tulasī does penance to win Viṣṇu and is married to the asura Śaṅkhacūḍa; after Śiva slays him, Viṣṇu decrees her body becomes the Gaṇḍakī river and her hair the Tulsī plant _(actors: Tulasī, Viṣṇu, Śaṅkhacūḍa, Śiva)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sacred-flora-and-fauna.md) — (Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa; Tulasi in Hinduism (Wikipedia))
- **Uddalaka Aruni Teaches Shvetaketu 'Tat Tvam Asi'** — The sage Uddalaka Aruni instructs his son Shvetaketu with the great mahavakya 'tat tvam asi' (That thou art), a foundational teaching of Vedanta delivered in the Chandogya _(actors: Uddalaka Aruni, Shvetaketu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/samaveda.md) — (Chandogya Upanishad)
- **Mārkaṇḍeya sees the infant Viṣṇu on the Akṣaya-vaṭa** — During the cosmic dissolution, Mārkaṇḍeya sees the infant Kṛṣṇa/Viṣṇu floating on the leaf of the imperishable banyan (Akṣaya-vaṭa) on the pralaya-waters _(actors: Mārkaṇḍeya, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sacred-flora-and-fauna.md) — (Sacred tree (Wikipedia))
- **Naciketas Questions Yama on Death and the Self** — The boy Naciketas confronts Yama, god of death, and through a dialogue receives the teaching on the immortal Self and the 'razor's edge' path, recorded in the Katha Upanishad _(actors: Naciketas, Yama)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/yajurveda.md) — (Katha Upanishad)
- **Bhrigu Progressively Realizes Brahman** — Taught by his father Varuna, the sage Bhrigu ascends through anna, prana, manas, vijnana to ananda, progressively realizing Brahman in the Bhrigu-valli of the Taittiriya Upanishad _(actors: Bhrigu, Varuna)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/yajurveda.md) — (Taittiriya Upanishad (Bhrigu-valli))
- **Descent of the Godavari (Gautami Ganga)** — To expiate the accidental killing of a phantom cow, the sage Gautama propitiates Shiva, who releases a strand of Ganga into the Deccan as the Godavari _(actors: Gautama, Shiva, Godavari)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/yamuna-and-river-goddesses.md) — (Brahma & Skanda Puranas)
- **Gajendra-mokṣa — Viṣṇu liberates the elephant-king** — The elephant-king Gajendra, seized in a lake by the crocodile Hūhū and struggling for a thousand years, surrenders utterly with a raised lotus; Viṣṇu flies in on Garuḍa, slays the crocodile with the Sudarśana cakra, and liberates him _(actors: Gajendra, Hūhū, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sacred-flora-and-fauna.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8.2–8.4)
- **The Matsya flood — saving of Vaivasvata Manu** — Vishnu as the fish Matsya warns Vaivasvata of a coming deluge; the Manu's boat carries the seven sages, the seed of all creatures, and the Vedas across the pralaya _(actors: Vishnu (Matsya), Vaivasvata Manu, the Saptarishis)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/manvantara-system.md) — (Matsya Purana, Bhagavata Purana 8.24, Shatapatha Brahmana 1.8.1)
- **Surya reveals the Surya Siddhanta to Maya** — The Sun-god Surya reveals the Surya Siddhanta, the cornerstone of Hindu mathematical astronomy, to the asura Maya, framing the science of time as divinely given _(actors: Surya (Sun-god), Maya (asura))_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/jyotisha-and-the-zodiac.md) — (Surya Siddhanta)
- **Naraka(sura) of Kamarupa** — The Kalika Purana records the legend of the demon-king Naraka of Pragjyotisha/Kamarupa, from whom the historical Assamese dynasties claimed descent _(actors: Narakasura)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/upapuranas-overview.md) — (Kalika Purana)
- **Sati Takes Sita's Form to Test Rama** — In the Manas frame, Parvati (as Sati) doubts Rama's divinity and assumes Sita's form to test him, prompting Shiva to instruct her in true devotion _(actors: Sati/Parvati, Shiva, Rama)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/ramcharitmanas-tulsidas.md) — (Ramcharitmanas, Bal Kand)
- **Śukra wins the Mṛtasañjīvanī vidyā** — Bhṛgu's son Śukra, guru of the asuras, obtains from Śiva the Mṛtasañjīvanī science to revive the slain _(actors: Śukra, Śiva)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/rishi-lineages-and-gotras.md) — (Mahābhārata)
- **Cyavana rejuvenated by the Aśvins** — The aged sage Cyavana, married to princess Sukanyā, is rejuvenated by the Aśvins (the origin of Cyavanaprāśa), and in return secures them a soma-share _(actors: Cyavana, Aśvins, Sukanyā)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/rishi-lineages-and-gotras.md) — (Mahābhārata Vana Parva; Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 4.1.5)
- **Kakabhushundi Recounts Rama's Glory to Garuda** — The immortal crow-sage Kakabhushundi expounds Rama's glory to Garuda, who had been confused by Rama's human-seeming bondage in the war _(actors: Kakabhushundi, Garuda)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/ramcharitmanas-tulsidas.md) — (Ramcharitmanas, Uttar Kand)
- **Atyantika Pralaya — moksha of the individual soul** — The individual soul's permanent merger with Brahman through liberating knowledge; ego is destroyed and samsara ends forever for that being _(actors: the liberated jiva, Brahman)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/kalpa-and-pralaya.md) — (Vishnu Purana 6.5, Bhagavata Purana 12.4.34-36)
- **Markandeya survives the cosmic flood** — The deathless sage Markandeya survives the cosmic dissolution-flood and sees the whole cosmos in the infant resting on a banyan leaf _(actors: Markandeya)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/bhagavata-purana-skandha-map.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 12)
- **Bhikṣāṭana and the Dāruka-vana (pine-forest) myth** — Śiva wanders the Dāruka pine-forest as the naked, beautiful, ash-smeared mendicant Bhikṣāṭana to humble a colony of arrogant Mīmāṃsaka/atheist ṛṣis who hold that ritual karma alone governs the cosmos; he seduces their wives while Viṣṇu-as-Mohinī deludes the sages, and when the enraged ṛṣis hurl conjured weapons (tiger, serpent, the Apasmāra dwarf, fire, ḍamaru, deer, axe) he subdues each and takes it as a permanent attribute, finally dancing the tāṇḍava on the dwarf — the etiological charter for his entire core iconography _(actors: Śiva (as Bhikṣāṭana), Viṣṇu (as Mohinī), the Dāruka-vana Mīmāṃsaka/heretical ṛṣis and their wives, the Apasmāra/Muyalaka dwarf)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa; Kūrma Purāṇa; Vāmana Purāṇa; Śiva Purāṇa; Sūta Saṃhitā; Tamil Śaiva sthala-purāṇa)
- **Indra slays Vritra after drinking soma** — Empowered by drinking vast quantities of the immortality-giving soma, Indra slays the demon Vritra _(actors: Indra, Vritra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/chandra-soma.md) — (Rg Veda 8.48; Rg Veda 9)
- **Kaṅkālamūrti — bearing the skeleton-staff of Viṣvaksena** — In the continuous penitential form linked to the Bhairava/brahmahatyā wandering, Śiva roams as a begging ascetic bearing on his trident the kaṅkāla (skeleton/bone-staff) of Viṣvaksena, Viṣṇu's gatekeeper-commander whom he slew when barred entry; the bone-staff becomes the defining emblem of the form as he expiates the killing _(actors: Śiva (as Kaṅkālamūrti/Bhairava), Viṣvaksena (Viṣṇu's gatekeeper-commander))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Kāraṇāgama; Śaiva Āgama / śilpa tradition; sthala-purāṇa)
- **Chandra weds the 27 nakshatra daughters of Daksha** — Chandra marries the twenty-seven daughters of Daksha Prajapati who personify the lunar mansions, visiting one wife per night — the calendrical core of the myth _(actors: Chandra, Daksha, the 27 nakshatras)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/chandra-soma.md) — (Vishnu, Bhagavata, Skanda Puranas)
- **Daksha curses Chandra with wasting disease** — Enraged by Chandra's neglect of all wives but Rohini, Daksha curses him with kshaya-roga (consumption), causing the Moon to dwindle — the etiology of the lunar phases _(actors: Daksha, Chandra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/chandra-soma.md) — (Vishnu, Bhagavata, Skanda Puranas)
- **Kapila transmits Sankhya through the lineage** — Kapila teaches Sankhya at the dawn of the present cosmic order, transmitting it through Asuri and Pancasikha to a disciple-lineage later versified by Isvarakṛṣṇa _(actors: Kapila, Asuri, Pancasikha, Isvarakṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/darshanas/samkhya-karika.md) — (Sankhya-karika 69-71)
- **Markandeya Beholds the Pralaya** — The deathless sage Markandeya beholds the cosmic dissolution and the child on the banyan-leaf, and resists Kamadeva _(actors: Markandeya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavata-tales-catalog.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 12.8-10)
- **Narayana reveals Pancaratra over five nights** — The Lord Narayana reveals the Pancaratra doctrine over five nights to five sages, the originating revelation of the Vaiṣṇava Agama stream _(actors: Narayana, five sages)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/agamas-tantras-dharmashastras.md) — (Pancaratra Samhitas)
- **Shiva grants Chandra periodic waxing and waning (Somanatha origin)** — The dying Chandra worships Shiva at Prabhasa; Shiva places the crescent on his head and grants that he will wane and wax forever rather than die — founding the Somanatha jyotirlinga _(actors: Chandra, Shiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/chandra-soma.md) — (Skanda/Matsya Puranas)
- **Chandra abducts Tara** — After arrogance bred by his Rajasuya sacrifice, Chandra abducts Tara, wife of Brihaspati, and refuses every plea to return her _(actors: Chandra, Tara, Brihaspati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/chandra-soma.md) — (Vishnu Purana; Bhagavata 9.14)
- **Śeṣa abandons Kadrū's curse-plot** — Revolted by his kinsmen's deceit in clinging to Uccaiḥśravas's tail to cheat Vinatā, the eldest nāga Śeṣa abandons the serpent-society to undertake penance _(actors: Śeṣa, Kadrū)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/shesha-ananta.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi/Āstīka Parva)
- **The Tarakamaya War** — The dispute over Tara escalates into a cosmic war pitting Brihaspati, Indra and Rudra against Chandra, Shukra and the asuras, threatening universal destruction until Brahma mediates _(actors: Brihaspati, Indra, Rudra, Chandra, Shukra, asuras, Brahma)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/chandra-soma.md) — (Vishnu Purana 4.6; Bhagavata)
- **Birth of Budha to Tara and Chandra** — Tara returns pregnant; pressed before the assembly she declares Chandra the father, and the radiant son Budha (Mercury) is born _(actors: Tara, Chandra, Budha, Brihaspati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/chandra-soma.md) — (Vishnu Purana; Bhagavata 9.14)
- **Śeṣa's great austerity** — Śeṣa performs terrible tapas, subsisting on air, at Gandhamādana, Badarī, Gokarṇa, Puṣkara and the Himavat foothills _(actors: Śeṣa)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/shesha-ananta.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva)
- **Siva reveals the Agamas as dialogue** — The Agama revelation is framed as a dialogue of Siva (or Viṣṇu/Devi) with consort or disciple, the originating frame of Tantric/Agamic scripture for Kali Yuga _(actors: Siva, Devi)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/agamas-tantras-dharmashastras.md) — (Saiva, Vaiṣṇava, Sakta Agamas)
- **Brahmā's boon charging Śeṣa to uphold the earth** — Pleased by his tapas, Brahmā grants Śeṣa his boon and charges him to steady the unstable, quaking earth _(actors: Brahmā, Śeṣa)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/shesha-ananta.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva)
- **Śeṣa passes through the earth to become its axis** — At Brahmā's word Śeṣa passes through a crevice clear through the globe and emerges below, bearing the earth steadily on his hood as its foundation _(actors: Śeṣa)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/shesha-ananta.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva)
- **Sati surrounds Shiva as the Ten Mahavidyas** — Forbidden by Shiva to attend Daksha's sacrifice, Sati assumes a terrifying form and multiplies into ten goddesses occupying the ten directions; whichever way Shiva turns a fierce form blocks him until he recognizes her supremacy — the origin of the Dasha Mahavidya _(actors: Sati, Shiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dasha-mahavidya.md) — (Mahabhagavata Purana; Brihaddharma Purana)
- **Sundareśvara's sixty-four Tiruviḷaiyāḍal at Madurai** — Śiva-Sundareśvara performs sixty-four 'holy sports' (tiruviḷaiyāḍal) across Madurai — carrying earth for the pittu cake, the Nakkīrar poetry-contest, selling bangles, animating a stone elephant to eat sugarcane, turning jackals to horses — making the whole city the kṣetra of his play _(actors: Śiva (Sundareśvara); Nakkīrar; the old woman Vandhi; the Pāṇḍya king)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Tiruviḷaiyāḍal Purāṇam (Parañcōti Munivar); Hālāsya Māhātmya)
- **Chinnamasta severs her own head** — Bathing with her hungry attendants, the Goddess severs her own head to feed them with three jets of her own blood — the origin myth of the self-decapitated Mahavidya Chinnamasta _(actors: Chinnamasta, Dakini, Varnini)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dasha-mahavidya.md) — (Pranatoshini / Narada-Pancharatra tradition)
- **The 64 Līlās of Śiva — Tiruviḷaiyāḍal of Sundareśvara at Madurai** — The Tamil Śaiva cycle of sixty-four sacred sports in which Śiva as Sundareśvara, consort of Mīnākṣī, repeatedly intervenes in the Pāṇḍya capital Madurai — carrying sand for a cake of piṭṭu, the Tamil poetry-contest with Nakkīrar, selling bangles, the stone elephant eating sugarcane, opening/convening the Tamil Sangam, turning jackals into horses — foundational to Mīnākṣī-Sundareśvara temple theology and the Madurai sthala-purāṇa _(actors: Śiva (as Sundareśvara), Mīnākṣī, the Pāṇḍya king, the poet Nakkīrar, the old woman Vandi, the people of Madurai)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-myth-cycle.md) — (Tiruviḷaiyāḍal Purāṇam (Paranjothi Munivar; Perumpaṟṟappuliyūr Nampi); Hālāsya Māhātmya / Skanda Purāṇa; Tamil Śaiva tradition)
- **Mahālakṣmī settles at Karavīra and slays Kolhāsura** — Śrī-Lakṣmī leaves Vaikuṇṭha after estrangement from Viṣṇu (in the southern recension triggered by Bhṛgu's kick) and takes permanent earthly residence at Karavīra/Kolhapur, where she also slays the demon-king Kolhāsura who, dying, begs the city bear his name and the Goddess remain _(actors: Mahālakṣmī (Ambābāī); Viṣṇu; Bhṛgu; Kolhāsura)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Karavīra-māhātmya (Padma/Skanda Purāṇa); Veṅkaṭācala-Māhātmya)
- **Origin of Dhumavati from Sati** — Hungry and refused food at the yajna, Sati swallows Shiva himself and is cursed by him into widow's form (or emerges from the smoke of her self-immolation) — the origin of Dhumavati the widow-goddess _(actors: Sati, Shiva, Dhumavati)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dasha-mahavidya.md) — (Pranatoshini Tantra; Shaktapramoda)
- **End of Dvapara and onset of Kali Yuga** — Krishna's departure (traditionally 3102 BCE) ends Dvapara Yuga and marks the dawn of Kali Yuga _(actors: Krishna)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/03-dvapara-yuga.md) — (Surya Siddhanta; Vishnu Purana; Bhagavata 12.2)
- **Lalita slays Bhandasura** — The warrior form of Tripurasundari (Lalita) slays the demon Bhandasura, as told in the Lalitopakhyana _(actors: Lalita/Tripurasundari, Bhandasura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dasha-mahavidya.md) — (Lalitopakhyana (Brahmanda Purana))
- **Bagalamukhi halts a world-destroying demon** — The crane-headed goddess Bagalamukhi arises to halt a world-destroying storm/demon (Madanasura) by seizing his tongue and paralyzing him — the power of stambhana _(actors: Bagalamukhi, Madanasura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dasha-mahavidya.md) — (Bagalamukhi Tantra; Shaktapramoda)
- **Indra and the Ants (procession of past Indras)** — Indra's pride is humbled when a procession of ants is revealed to be a line of former Indras, asserting a beginningless succession of Brahmas and Indras across cycling universes _(actors: Indra, Vishnu/Brahmin boy)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/brahma-lifetime.md) — (Brahma Vaivarta Purana)
- **The Maruts aid Indra in slaying Vṛtra** — As Indra's war-band the Maruts lend their roar and force while he slays Vṛtra and releases the waters _(actors: Maruts, Indra, Vṛtra)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/the-maruts.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.85.9; 8.7)
- **Agastya mediates the Indra-Marut dispute** — In the Ṛgvedic quarrel between Indra and the Maruts over the offering-share, the sage Agastya mediates and reconciles them _(actors: Agastya, Indra, Maruts)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/the-maruts.md) — (Ṛgveda 1.165; 1.170–171)
- **Diti's vow for a son to kill Indra** — Grieving for her slain asura sons, Diti begs Kaśyapa for a son who will kill Indra, and is granted the powerful embryo on condition of a strict purity-vow _(actors: Diti, Kaśyapa, Indra)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/the-maruts.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa Bāla-kāṇḍa 46; Vāmana Purāṇa 71)
- **Indra splits Diti's embryo into the 49 Maruts** — Seizing on Diti's ritual lapse, Indra enters her womb and cuts the embryo with his vajra into seven and then seven-times-seven (49) crying fragments, telling them 'mā ruda' (do not weep), thus naming the Maruts _(actors: Indra, Diti, Maruts)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/the-maruts.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa Bāla-kāṇḍa 47; Vāmana Purāṇa 71)
- **Markandeya witnesses the cosmic deluge** — The long-lived sage Markandeya floats on the cosmic ocean between cosmic days and glimpses the infant Vishnu/Krishna who contains all worlds, witnessing repeated dissolution and re-creation _(actors: Markandeya, Vishnu (infant on banyan leaf))_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/brahma-lifetime.md) — (Mahabharata Vana Parva; Bhagavata Purana 12.8-10)
- **Diti's boon makes the 49 fragments into gods** — Waking and forgiving Indra, Diti asks that her 49 fragment-children be made devas ranged through the heavens, so the asura-born become gods _(actors: Diti, Indra, Maruts)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/the-maruts.md) — (Rāmāyaṇa Bāla-kāṇḍa 47; Purāṇas)
- **Holika burns and Prahlada is spared (Holika Dahan)** — Hiranyakashipu sends his fire-immune sister Holika to burn the Vishnu-devotee Prahlada on her lap; she burns instead and Prahlada survives, enacted by the Holi bonfire _(actors: Hiranyakashipu, Holika, Prahlada)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Puranic; prefiguring Narasimha)
- **Shiva instructs Parvati in the Guru Gita** — The Guru Gita is framed as Shiva instructing Parvati on the guru's nature, embedded in the Skanda Purana _(actors: Shiva, Parvati)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/guru-shishya-parampara.md) — (Guru Gita / Skanda Purana)
- **Citraketu cursed by Pārvatī to become Vṛtra** — The Vidyādhara king Citraketu, a former earthly king elevated to lordship, is cursed by Pārvatī to become the asura Vṛtra _(actors: Citraketu, Pārvatī, Vṛtra)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/vidyadharas-siddhas-and-spirits.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 6)
- **Dasaratha Exiles His Children (Dasaratha Jataka)** — In the Pali Dasaratha Jataka, King Dasaratha of Benares sends Rama-pandita, Lakkhana, and Sita into the forest for twelve years to avert a succession war — with no Ravana, abduction, or battle _(actors: Dasaratha, Rama-pandita, Lakkhana, Sita)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/jain-and-buddhist-ramayanas.md) — (Dasaratha Jataka (No. 461))
- **Indra slays Vritra with Dadhichi's bones** — Indra slays the great drought-demon Vritra, who held back the cosmic waters, using the vajra fashioned from sage Dadhichi's bones — the foundational deva-asura combat _(actors: Indra, Vritra, Dadhichi)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devas-asuras-and-the-lokas.md) — (Rigveda 1.32; Bhagavata Purana 6)
- **Durga slays Mahishasura** — The buffalo-demon Mahishasura, granted invincibility against all males, is slain by the goddess Durga as Mahishasuramardini _(actors: Durga, Mahishasura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devas-asuras-and-the-lokas.md) — (Devi Mahatmya / Markandeya Purana)
- **Rama-pandita's Equanimity at His Father's Death** — On hearing (initially false) news of his father's death, Rama-pandita remains composed and teaches that all that is born must die — the Jataka's sermon on impermanence and equanimity _(actors: Rama-pandita, Lakkhana, Sita)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/jain-and-buddhist-ramayanas.md) — (Dasaratha Jataka (No. 461))
- **Bhṛṅgi cursed to skin-and-bone** — The gaṇa-sage Bhṛṅgi, devotee of Śiva alone, is cursed to skin-and-bone and given a third leg for refusing to honour Pārvatī in his circumambulation — origin of the Ardhanārīśvara lesson _(actors: Bhṛṅgi, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/vidyadharas-siddhas-and-spirits.md) — (Śaiva folklore)
- **Kartikeya slays Tarakasura** — The asura Tarakasura, slayable only by Shiva's son, is killed by Kartikeya/Skanda _(actors: Kartikeya/Skanda, Tarakasura)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devas-asuras-and-the-lokas.md) — (Skanda Purana; Shiva Purana)
